## 2026-10-05 graph-native self-change rehearsal-to-commit lifecycle

Three source-locked workers each pass a paired full-copy/shared-page CoW lifecycle. The graph generates a threshold proposal with target UID withheld, branch outcomes beat both no-op and placebo, a disjoint canary passes, and Rust CAS commits once; fresh post-commit inputs pass. The graph selector receives threshold-sensitivity measurements and a fixed candidate grammar, so this is not cue-free discovery or open-ended induction. Three-pair timings show semantic parity but are too small for a speed claim. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_END_TO_END_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_END_TO_END_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

## 2026-10-05 graph-native blinded self-change baseline

The source-locked self-change evaluation passes 21 case runs across three workers. Graph rules choose proof-admissible candidate families, choose lower MDL over higher gain when multiple candidates pass, and issue an explicit abort when candidates fail control margins. Median evidence-selection tick is 0.090 ms, excluding boot, proposal construction, and measurement acquisition. Candidate clauses and outcomes are supplied by the harness; five of seven cases have only one candidate. This is a bounded graph-native selection/proof/abstention result, not open-ended induction, real task validation, patch adoption, or self-improvement. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_BLIND_BASELINE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_BLIND_BASELINE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

## 2026-10-05 live perception self-repair repeatability

Three fresh source-locked ARC episodes pass: the configured self-ID measure detects a live regression, the substrate auto-mints and applies its configured discriminator repair on frame 11, then keeps it after recovery. Median run time is 21.139 s. The native segmentation path now runs without OpenCV installed because the adapter imports OpenCV only for the Python fallback; fallback behavior remains untested. The task externally breaks the rule and configures target/correction on the CapabilityAxis, so this supports repeatable execution of a specified repair, not target discovery or autonomous structure learning. ## 2026-10-05 competing deferred rule edits and ambiguity

Three source-locked workers and nine isolated runs pass. Two one-shot producers each queue a `SetRuleClause`; receipts preserve ordered effect origins and revision transitions. Counterfactuals show the later writer uniquely determines the competing final behavior, while identical writes have no individually necessary producer. Duplicate live target names are refused: source events remain visible, no rule change commits, and branches retain the old behavior. The refusal reason is inferred from exact fixture state because the event receipt does not encode an explicit ambiguity code. This is synthetic queue-semantics evidence, not autonomous repair. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_COMPETING_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_COMPETING_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

## 2026-10-05 deferred `SetRuleClause` effect attribution and necessity

On the production `repair_apply_rule_clause` seed rule, the queued source effect survives drain as an exact origin and its receipt records the targeted `where` change (revision 0→1). Three workers and twelve isolated runs pass source-lock and primary-isolation gates. In the authored held-out probe, target tracing and the rule-change receipt identify the necessary effect in one omission branch; blind and pre-state-rematch baselines need two. No-cause rematch/receipt arms abstain. This is one uniquely sourced queue entry and harness-defined fault, not natural self-repair or broad causal diagnosis. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_DIAGNOSIS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_DIAGNOSIS_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

# Substrate capability map — initial inventory

## 2026-10-05 Thoughtexperiment checkpointed isolated observer cost v1.2.12

Three source-locked workers pass the ten-fixture combined-journal matrix. A phase-checkpoint branch independently matches total `conclusions` to the pre-hypothesis full diff and `derived_only` to the post-checkpoint full diff. Thirty-six fresh-process measurements compare three replicates per mode across small, same-event, and report-cap workloads. On report-cap saturation, median post-run RSS/PSS delta is 187,433 KiB combined vs 118,164 KiB event-only, about 69 MiB additional; median wall time is 481.5 vs 165.3 ms. This is an authored stress workload, and end-state RSS/PSS deltas do not capture transient peaks. The prior v1.2.11 751,016 KiB high-water values are withdrawn as contaminated by probe ordering. Same-event events remain endpoint-only; the value of per-effect evidence for hidden-outcome diagnosis is still open. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_2_12_RESULTS_20261005.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_2_12_AMENDMENT_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_2_12_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_12.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_12.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_12_lock_20261005.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_12_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_12_corrected_analysis_20261005.json).

## 2026-10-05 bounded per-effect write-target observer

The separate target-only API attributes effect indices to node IDs and edge keys without copying before/after values. Three source-locked workers and 27 isolated runs pass parity, attribution, zero-value-payload, report-cap, and visible-truncation checks. At report cap it records 16,384 targets and measures 326.1 ms / 163,672 KiB post-run RSS/PSS, versus endpoint-only 230.7 ms / 118,048 KiB and full value snapshots 600.2 ms / 276,292 KiB. It reduces full-trace overhead while retaining a measurable cost over endpoints. This is an authored stress case; production-rule diagnostic value and transient peak memory remain open. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EFFECT_TARGET_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EFFECT_TARGET_COST_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_effect_target_cost_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_effect_target_cost_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_effect_target_cost_v1_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_effect_target_cost_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

## 2026-10-05 per-effect observer cost and diagnosis capability

Per-effect rule snapshots are now separately opt-in. The [cost panel](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_EFFECT_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md) passes three source-locked workers and 45 isolated arms: the 32k-match report-cap workload adds 151,060 KiB RSS/PSS and raises median observer time from 223 ms endpoint-only to 560 ms with effect values. Its query deliberately omits rematching and full rule-source reconstruction; it establishes information exposed by the API, not natural diagnosis capability. ## 2026-10-05 Thoughtexperiment pre-clone admission and mutation routes v1.2.7

Three source-locked workers pass corrected analysis across nine fixtures. Attribution now admits recursively bounded payload before cloning before-images; oversized and 140-level nested values both fail closed, with zero retained pairs. The edge route replays ordered attribute replacement `{state: 0}→{state: 1}` and deletion to absence. Report-object truncation remains explicit at 16,384 values. On the edge fixture, the payload counter is 96 bytes and serialized event JSON is 866 bytes; on report saturation they are 524,288 and 1,652,753 bytes. Whole-worker RSS is 268,776–338,180 KiB, including setup and all modes. The original analyzer's known target-selection check fails; the corrected analyzer passes. Admission covers the attribution logger; concurrently active delta observers retain their own full before-image behavior. These are bounded mutation-route results, not general memory guarantees or diagnosis/self-repair evidence. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_2_7_RESULTS_20261005.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_2_7_AMENDMENT_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_2_7_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_7.py), [corrected analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_7_correction.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_7_lock_20261005.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_7_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_event_value_attribution_v1_2_7_corrected_analysis_20261005.json).

## 2026-10-05 Thoughtexperiment report-object cap v1.2.4

A source-locked three-worker workload with 33 rules writing 512 nodes each reaches the report ceiling of 16,384 before/after objects exactly: 32 events retain complete snapshots and the 33rd fails closed with its truncation flag. Traced, plain, and exact modes agree on final state and closure summary. The retained snapshot estimate is 1,915,904 bytes. With 512 objects/event, 16,384 objects/report, and a 64 KiB/event cap, no more than 2 MiB of representation can be counted before the object ceiling binds; the configured 4 MiB report byte ceiling is unreachable under current accounting. This is not an RSS bound. The original analyzer repeats the v1.2.3 target-selection error; its separately hashed correction passes. ## 2026-10-05 repeated-variable incremental matcher correction

An expanded four-rule differential found that semi-naive driver seeding could emit false self-loop witnesses after adding non-self relation edges when a pattern reused one variable at both endpoints. The source check now requires endpoint equality when both pattern endpoints share a variable, and inequality when they are distinct. The fresh 32-worker v3 gate passes exact oracle parity for directed forward/reverse joins, repeated-variable loops, bound-attribute predicates, decoy relations, and add-only incremental updates. The preceding v2 failure is preserved, not counted as a pass. Deletion/retraction and other matcher routes remain open. See [v3 results](SUBSTRATE_BOUND_EDGE_MATCHER_DIFFERENTIAL_V3_RESULTS_20261005.md), [v3 protocol](SUBSTRATE_BOUND_EDGE_MATCHER_DIFFERENTIAL_V3_PREREG_20261005.md), [v2 amendment](SUBSTRATE_BOUND_EDGE_MATCHER_DIFFERENTIAL_V2_AMENDMENT_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/bound_edge_matcher_differential_v3.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/bound_edge_matcher_differential_v3_analysis_20261005.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/bound_edge_matcher_differential_v3_20261005.json), and [source](../../../runners/dsl/src/substrate.rs).

All runtime and analyzer checks pass. This supports the fully bound membership path exercised by the scaling and query panels; deletion/retraction, broader repeated-variable shapes, and other effect/matcher routes remain unverified. ## 2026-10-05 exact bound-edge matcher fast path

The matcher now uses exact `TypedGraph::has_edge` membership when both endpoints are already bound. In the source-locked [v3 query eligibility scale panel](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_QUERY_ELIGIBILITY_SCALE_V3_RESULTS_20261005.md), eligible-set and selected-node parity hold at all five sizes; median eligibility closure improves 5.2–54.7× over the prior source-locked capture from 512 through 8,192 probes. At 8,192 probes, median closure falls from 852 ms to 15.6 ms. This is a graph-level synthetic rule result across separately timed captures; broad matcher correctness, end-to-end task cost, and natural-task prevalence remain open. ## 2026-10-05 native query-eligibility join-order comparison

The paired [v2 protocol](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_QUERY_ELIGIBILITY_SCALE_V2_PREREG_20261005.md) compares two authoring orders across five populations and five paired replicates. All 50 workers and independent parity checks pass, but there is no consistent timing improvement under the default install-time match-edge reordering. Source inspection identifies a candidate engine cost in fully bound match edges: the current matcher enumerates source neighbors before filtering the bound target. This is a plausible explanation, not yet a measured attribution. ## 2026-10-05 native query-eligibility scale

The source-locked [eligibility scaling protocol](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_QUERY_ELIGIBILITY_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md) measures the native v1.3 rule shape over five population sizes and five replicates per size. Native eligibility sets and once-per-query selected nodes match an independent oracle in all 25 workers. From 512 to 8,192 probe nodes, median eligibility closure grows from 3.45 ms to 852 ms; native selection grows from 0.074 ms to 0.852 ms. ## 2026-10-05 native candidate-adequacy query selector v1.3

The source-locked [v1.3 protocol](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_CANDIDATE_ADEQUACY_QUERY_V1_3_PREREG_20261005.md) and [results](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_CANDIDATE_ADEQUACY_QUERY_V1_3_RESULTS_20261005.md) measure graph-native candidate-positive query ordering on 40 fresh paired panels. The selector now anchors once per query; native and host query orders and final decisions match across all pairs. Median native query-selection closure falls from 686.7 ms in v1.2 to 20.2 ms in v1.3, while eligibility closure remains 75.3 ms. Native worker CPU median is 375 ms versus 281 ms for the host arm. The candidate menu and numeric tasks are supplied, and three false accepts are too few to estimate general query value. ## 2026-10-06 persistent attribute-index fork profile

`TypedGraph::attr_indices` now uses nested `CowShardedMap`s over `(node_type, attr_key)` and attribute values. The source-locked [v2 profile](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_PROFILE_V2_RESULTS_20261006.md) verifies exact queries with one distinct value per node at 7,164, 16,384, and 50,771 nodes. Shallow attribute-index clone medians are 30/30/40 ns versus 0.530–5.326 ms for explicit deep copies. This measures index clone only; first-write cost, mutation isolation, full-Substrate branching, and application closure remain open. See [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_PROFILE_V2_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_attr_index_profile_v2.py), [native probe](../../../runners/dsl/src/bin/typed_graph_cow_attr_index_profile_v2.rs), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_attr_index_profile_v2_lock_20261006.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_attr_index_profile_v2_20261006.json).

The source-locked [v3 mutation panel](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_MUTATION_V3_RESULTS_20261006.md) passed exact mutation and parent/sibling isolation checks and exposed full value-bucket scans during delete/retype. The v4 [targeted-unindex panel](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_TARGETED_UNINDEX_V4_RESULTS_20261006.md) removes those scans and cuts shared delete/retype first-write time by 96.3–98.3% against v3. V5 adds an order-preserving COW-paged by-type index; typed order, compaction, NodeID reuse, and branch-isolation gates pass. The separate v4/v5 captures show no material delete/retype latency improvement at 50,771 nodes, so remaining cost attribution is open. See [v5 results](TYPED_GRAPH_ORDERED_TYPE_INDEX_COW_V5_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_ORDERED_TYPE_INDEX_COW_V5_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/ordered_type_index_cow_v5.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_ordered_type_index_cow_v5.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/ordered_type_index_cow_v5_lock_20261006.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/ordered_type_index_cow_v5_raw_20261006.json).

The follow-up [live GenInfer scope-index lifecycle protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPE_INDEX_LIFECYCLE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), amended in [v1.1](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPE_INDEX_LIFECYCLE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261005.md), passes traced-work, output, branch, mutation, and restore checks over 36 fresh workers. It eliminates GenInfer's typed-population visits through 10,000 synthetic codebook decoys, while parsed parts/candidate work remain equal and untraced full-closure medians remain effectively equal. See [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPE_INDEX_LIFECYCLE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_scope_index_lifecycle_v1_1_lock_20261005.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_scope_index_lifecycle_v1_1_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_scope_index_lifecycle_v1_1_analysis_20261005.json). This remains harness-authored frame scope on one route, not evidence of natural-task tractability.

Post-hoc attribution of that trace finds that ShapeDecode, TransformDecode, and DimInfer still account for 75% of the helper population visits at all three sizes; indexed GenInfer removes the remaining quarter. Search-candidate work is unchanged. See the [residual-work analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_scope_helper_residual_v1_analysis_20261005.json) and [reproducible summarizer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_geninfer_scope_helper_residual_v1.py). This attribution guides the next gate but does not establish which work dominates wall time.

An exact rational solver prototype tests a possible full-rank route beyond the kernel's then-current 2-D limit. Authored 1-D/2-D/3-D cases all recover their training matrix, predict held-outs, and abstain on repeated-origin rank-deficient controls; the 3-D exhaustive envelope at the same data-derived bound is 387,420,488 candidates. This establishes algorithm feasibility only. Subsequent feature-gated native integration is reported below ([feasibility results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_MATRIX_SOLVER_FEASIBILITY_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_MATRIX_SOLVER_FEASIBILITY_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1_20261006.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1_analysis_20261006.json)).

The opt-in native full-rank solver is now integrated for one- and three-coordinate cases. The initial 20-worker comparison was exploratory because its corrected analyzer followed capture. A preregistered v1.1 replication with frozen analyzer passes all 96 workers across three repetitions, three distinct 3-D matrices, and rank-deficient, nonlinear, and ambiguous-ID controls. It acquires and reuses every positive 1-D/3-D matrix with receipts, preserves the 2-D projection, and abstains on every negative control. Median 3-D training closure is 1.46 ms with the solver versus 0.65 ms for feature-off refusal on these small authored frames. Keep the feature opt-in. Identity alignment currently scans the target rows for each source row, so O(N²) relation matching is the next performance gate. Natural prevalence, dimensions above three, and rank-deficient induction remain open ([v1 results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_NATIVE_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [v1.1 results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_NATIVE_COMPARISON_V1_1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [v1.1 protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_NATIVE_COMPARISON_V1_1_PREREG_20261006.md), [v1.1 runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1.py), [v1.1 analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1.py), [v1.1 capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1_20261006.json), [v1.1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1_analysis_20261006.json), [initial v1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_analysis_initial_failure_20261006.json)).

An experimental fused GenInfer candidate validator combines its structural checks and slot comparison traversal. The source-matched 88-worker [comparison](GENINFER_FUSED_CANDIDATE_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md) passes exact semantic and counter parity over fullseed positive/corruption controls and linear/coupled-quadratic transfer fixtures. It does not reduce audited GenInfer term units, and fullseed audit-off training medians are effectively equal (fused/baseline 0.997). Keep the feature disabled by default: no stable speedup was measured. Next profile candidate work by rejection point and seek a directly counted reduction before revising this optimization; higher-dimensional inference and natural-workload prevalence remain open.

The captured v1.4 live graph has 410 ShapeSignature nodes and none carries `scope_id` or `frame_id`; all three worker graph digests reproduce. The [runtime provenance analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_live_codebook_frame_provenance_v1.py) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_codebook_frame_provenance_v1_analysis_20261005.json) show that this route cannot be frame-filtered by `obs.scope_id` without first assigning codebook ownership. Separately, a source-locked [ShapeDecode name-aperture baseline](SHAPEDECODE_NAME_APERTURE_BASELINE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md) measures 24 workers and 33–145 effective codebook parts. This singleton-scope route makes exactly 2P² population visits (2,178–42,050); median untraced closure grows from 15.9 to 86.7 ms. See [preregistered protocol](SHAPEDECODE_NAME_APERTURE_BASELINE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/shapedecode_name_aperture_baseline_v1.py), [independent analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_shapedecode_name_aperture_baseline_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/shapedecode_name_aperture_baseline_v1_lock_20261005.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/shapedecode_name_aperture_baseline_v1_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/shapedecode_name_aperture_baseline_v1_analysis_20261005.json). This is one synthetic, current-scan route; no indexed arm, natural prevalence, or general ShapeDecode complexity claim follows.

The paired [parsed-codebook cache experiment](SHAPEDECODE_NAME_APERTURE_CACHE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md) then passes all 12 checks over 80 fresh workers: visits fall 96.97–99.31%, with 4.32–8.71× median closure speedups on this authored singleton-name workload and exact output/coarse-work parity. A separate [semantic control panel](SHAPEDECODE_NAME_APERTURE_SEMANTICS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md) passes 12 checks over 14 fresh workers for casing, exact and case-fold-equivalent duplicates, absent and empty scopes, and reversed requested-name order. Both use current source and retain source locks, captures, and independent analyzers. Surface-valued attributes, malformed out-of-scope records, exclusion filters, mutation lifecycle, production-derived workloads, and natural-task prevalence remain open; neither result establishes general tractability.

## 2026-10-04 EvalCache checkpoint coverage

The checkpoint implementation supports neighbour, filter, argmax, and holds entries. The live replay exercises nonempty neighbour and filter caches; argmax and holds are empty on that route, while reachable and SD-operation entries remain unsupported and fail closed. The source-shape and invalidation crosswalk is in the [EvalCache checkpoint audit](SUBSTRATE_EVAL_CACHE_CHECKPOINT_COVERAGE_AUDIT_20261004.md), with dynamic primary-route evidence in the [v8 replay results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_ENTRY_REPLAY_V8_RESULTS_20261004.md).

The Thoughtexperiment fork inventory v3 now classifies all 76 declared `TypedGraph` and `Substrate` fields, including selective EvalCache checkpoint behavior and branch-local rule-audit identity. See its [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_state_inventory_v3.py) and [source-hashed capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_state_inventory_v3.json).

A focused codec probe confirms exact schema V6 restore/re-export for synthetic nonempty neighbour, filter, argmax, and holds entries; natural primary replay currently populates only neighbour/filter. See [codec results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_CACHE_CODEC_V1_1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

A separate live rule fixture naturally populates neighbour/argmax/holds entries and matches exact subsequent work and state across restored stores. It covers one small authored rule and does not establish natural prevalence or scale. See [live Argmax/Holds results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_CACHE_LIVE_ARGMAX_HOLDS_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

The source-authored `vision_eye.ph0_wunit_winner` rule now supplies a nonempty evaluator-generated Argmax cache entry. Two independent restores match checkpoint state and subsequent aggregate/per-rule work and graph/scheduler state exactly. This transfers cache replay evidence to one actual seed rule but does not establish cache speedup or natural frequency. See [vision-eye Argmax replay results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_CACHE_VISION_EYE_ARGMAX_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_CACHE_VISION_EYE_ARGMAX_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/scheduler_cache_vision_eye_argmax_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/scheduler_cache_vision_eye_argmax_v1_lock_20261004.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/scheduler_cache_vision_eye_argmax_v1_20261004.json).

A separate source-locked scale panel varies WUnit population from 4 to 256 on the same authored phase-0 rule family. Candidate work grows 51.4×, Term work 48.5×, and median closure latency 41.9× for a 64× population increase, with exact parity between uninstrumented timing and audited twins. This is bounded one-host scaling evidence, not an asymptotic claim. See [WUnit scale results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_WUNIT_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_WUNIT_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/vision_eye_wunit_scale_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/vision_eye_wunit_scale_v1_lock_20261004.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_wunit_scale_v1_20261004.json).

The confirmatory V2.5 fixture covers multiple outer bindings, two-node Holds keys, and pass-boundary invalidation after a dependency mutation; exact replay work and state pass. See [V2.5 results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_CACHE_LIVE_ARGMAX_HOLDS_V2_5_RESULTS_20261004.md).

A locked census of authored executable seed rules finds 327 Argmax occurrences and 33 Holds occurrences, with no rule containing both; runtime cache transfer should use separate subsystem fixtures. See [census results](SUBSTRATE_CACHE_OPERATOR_SEED_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

A follow-up source-locked static census classifies 204/327 Argmax sites as structurally eligible for the current memoization whitelist and identifies common exclusions. It guides workload selection but does not imply runtime firing or benefit. See [eligibility census results](SUBSTRATE_ARGMAX_ELIGIBILITY_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ARGMAX_ELIGIBILITY_CENSUS_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/cache_argmax_eligibility_census_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/cache_argmax_eligibility_census_v1_lock_20261004.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cache_argmax_eligibility_census_v1_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 sequential multi-task episode ownership

Two source-locked fresh workers reproduce the five-task, single-graph sequence
after excluding only eight observed diagnostic `ts`/`runtime_s` differences.
Only two prompts have explicit Goals (both `report_self`, including a
scene-description misroute); three adjacent-prompt `responds_to` edges cross
the disjoint owner projections. The accumulated episode therefore exposes
task-routing interference and non-disjoint ownership, while the analyzer does
not establish task correctness or complete dependency scopes. See [results](SUBSTRATE_MULTI_TASK_EPISODE_OWNERSHIP_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[protocol](SUBSTRATE_MULTI_TASK_EPISODE_OWNERSHIP_V1_PREREG_20261004.md),

and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/multi_task_episode_ownership_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 operational cuts vs explicit ownership

On the retained two-request graph, conservative Message/Goal/Event relations
recover 5/8 and 1/3 nodes from the two harness-supplied operational cuts
(Jaccard 0.625 and 0.333). The cuts also contain lifecycle artifacts, and two
edges cross between their disjoint owner sets. This weakens the interpretation
of small declared cuts as graph-resident task ownership and still does not
specify complete inference dependencies. See [crosswalk results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_FRAME_EXPLICIT_OWNERSHIP_CROSSWALK_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[preregistration](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_FRAME_EXPLICIT_OWNERSHIP_CROSSWALK_V1_PREREG_20261004.md),

and [analysis record](../../../evaluation/reports/live_frame_explicit_ownership_crosswalk_v1_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 coverage-aware policy and duplicate-Term audit

The evidence-only policy replay covers 25 frozen authored numeric panels.
An audit found that its five class-return panels each contained four IDs for
one identical canonical Term. They establish duplicate grouping, not
distinct-hypothesis equivalence. Exact Term deduplication removes 39/90 pool
rows, changes all five class decisions to exact-term decisions, preserves
finite-holdout correctness, and raises total labels from 60 to 70 (median 2
to 3). Candidate acquisition is inherited from the upstream fixed pool; this
is not natural-task transfer or persistent self-improvement. See
[results](COVERAGE_AWARE_CLASS_RETURN_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[deduplication results](TERM_DEDUPLICATION_CLASS_RETURN_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),

[deduplication protocol](TERM_DEDUPLICATION_CLASS_RETURN_V1_PREREG_20261004.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/coverage_aware_class_return_v1.py),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/coverage_aware_class_return_v1_lock_20261003.json),
[capture](../../../evaluation/reports/coverage_aware_class_return_v1_20261003.json),
and [independent replay analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/coverage_aware_class_return_v1_1_analysis_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 coverage-policy boundary falsification

The current policy returns `(ax == -ex) AND (ax < 4)` in a class with two
exact opposition Terms after zero labels, even though its probes stop at
`ax=3` and registered holdout at `3.5`. Wider scoring finds a median 741
errors per panel for the restricted candidate. The fixed coverage rule is
therefore insufficient to certify off-support equivalence. See [results](PROBE_COVERAGE_FALSE_EQUIVALENT_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),

[capture](../../../evaluation/reports/probe_coverage_false_equivalent_v1_20261004.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/probe_coverage_false_equivalent_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 candidate-boundary probe repair

Parsing `ax < 4` creates probes around its threshold; the native selector
chooses `[4, 0, -4, 0]` and one oracle label removes the false candidate in
all five panels. Independent wider scoring finds zero remaining false-safe
returns, compared with five under fixed coverage. This repair is specific to
the authored opposition equation and candidate form. See [results](BOUNDARY_DIRECTED_PROBE_REPAIR_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),

and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/boundary_directed_probe_repair_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 exact Term deduplication ablation

A source-locked rerun removes 39 exact duplicate Terms from 90 frozen

## 2026-10-04 CoW class-receipt transport

Five upstream-safe decisions were represented by graph receipts inside
shared-page CoW branches and returned at the primary's captured revision.
Independent graph-diff replay confirms exact member links, no pre-return
primary mutation, branch/primary parity after adoption, unchanged rule state,
and stale-revision rejection. A term audit shows each four-member receipt
contains one canonical Term, so this demonstrates duplicate-entry transport,
not diverse-class transport. The candidate set and safety decision are
inherited, with no rule activation or persistent adoption. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COVERAGE_CLASS_RECEIPT_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[deduplication audit](TERM_DEDUPLICATION_CLASS_RETURN_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),

[capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_coverage_class_receipt_v1_20261004.json),
and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_coverage_class_receipt_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 prompt-neighborhood cut sensitivity

The five-graph structural probe does not robustly establish cheap cuts around
task prompts. Prompt-root cut edges per frame node were below the broader
degree-ranked non-Message control median in 9/60 graph/settings comparisons,
while below the small same-type Message census median in 51/60. The latter
contains only 2–8 other Messages per graph and has substantial degree gaps;
the broader arm is type-confounded. Radius/hub sensitivity is large, and the
sample is five authored prompts rather than a natural-task prevalence set.
This is structural diagnostic evidence, not frame discovery or a treewidth
result. See [v2 results](LIVE_GRAPH_PROMPT_NEIGHBORHOOD_CUTS_V2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](LIVE_GRAPH_PROMPT_NEIGHBORHOOD_CUTS_V2_PREREG_20261004.md), [evaluator](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_live_graph_prompt_neighborhood_cuts_v2.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_graph_prompt_neighborhood_cuts_v2_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_graph_prompt_neighborhood_cuts_v2_20261004.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_graph_prompt_neighborhood_cuts_v2_analysis_20261004.json), and [analysis lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_graph_prompt_neighborhood_cuts_analysis_v2_lock_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 Thoughtexperiment touched-object audit cost and guarantee

At 10.5k/100.5k/200.5k total nodes, the exact selected-branch/returned-primary
comparison takes median 128 ms/1.33 s/2.64 s, while reconstructing and comparing
the complete state of the one or two journal-touched nodes takes 0.09–0.13 ms.
All 18 workers preserve full branch/oracle parity and the touched-state check
passes each residual. Six injected unrelated-node mutations also pass this
touched check, while exact full-graph digests detect them. The cheap method is
therefore a journal-scoped consistency check, not a replacement for global
parity without a stronger mutation-coverage guarantee. It fails closed for
edge or add/remove residuals. The v1 fixture/instrumentation failure and v1.1
analyzer assumption error are preserved and disclosed. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TOUCHED_OBJECT_PARITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TOUCHED_OBJECT_PARITY_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [amendments](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TOUCHED_OBJECT_PARITY_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261004.md), [analysis amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TOUCHED_OBJECT_PARITY_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_touched_object_parity_v1_1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_touched_object_parity_v1_1_1_analysis_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 Thoughtexperiment journal mutation coverage

The follow-up source audit found that public `TypedGraph::node_mut` needed a
direct case in addition to setter-based mutations. A locked 17-case current
source matrix now matches every journal residual to a whole-graph diff,
including live/fresh/tombstoned identity restoration, node and edge mutation
paths, repeated writes, and checkpointed derived-only state. The deliberate
post-capture write remains visible only to the global oracle, preserving the
known guarantee boundary. This covers fixed single-threaded calls through the
enumerated public methods, not retained mutable references, concurrency, or
all caller paths. See [v1.2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_2_PREREG_20261004.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_2_lock_20261004.json), [evaluator](../../../runners/dsl/src/bin/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_2.rs), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_2_20261004.json).

V1.3 stores complete journal and whole-graph diffs for every case. An
independent analyzer recomputes all 17 equalities from raw JSON and passes
eight integrity and semantic checks. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_3_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_3_PREREG_20261004.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_3_lock_20261004.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_3_20261004.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_3_analysis_20261004.json).

V1.4 extends the matrix to 20 cases and independently checks mutation revision behavior. All 20 capture-point journal diffs match whole-graph oracles; the scope transition advances revision with no serialized fixture change when all fixture edges remain visible, and derived-index declaration has no semantic change. A narrowing scope that hides existing edges remains uncovered and is a known residual risk for journal-based exact validation. See [v1.4 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_4_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_4_PREREG_20261004.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_4_lock_20261004.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_4_20261004.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_4_analysis_20261004.json).

V1.5 resolves the narrowing-aperture oracle ambiguity with a 21-case source-locked matrix. In the new case, scoped serialization hides two edges, while the semantic journal and full-state diff after clearing the read restriction agree on only the node attribute write. All 21 cases and independent analysis checks pass. This supports treating the journal as a semantic residual and the scoped graph as an observation projection for the tested sequence. See [v1.5 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_5_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_5_PREREG_20261004.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_5_lock_20261004.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_5_20261004.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_5_analysis_20261004.json).

V1.6 adds five restricted-aperture edge mutation cases and corrects before-image capture for hidden-edge replacement and deletion. All 26 semantic residuals match the full underlying-state oracle; a fresh static audit also passes the 23 public mutable graph methods against the corrected source. This is finite direct-operation evidence, not a concurrency or arbitrary-interleaving guarantee. See [v1.6 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_6_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_6_PREREG_20261004.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_6_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_6_20261004.json), [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_6_analysis_20261004.json), and [current-source mutation audit](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_tracked_mutations_v2_20261004.json).

workers across path, star, clustered-star, and random-recursive-tree noise
layouts pass candidate, isolation, oracle-parity, stale-return, and quiescence

descriptive. ## 2026-10-03 Predecessor closure inside CoW thought experiments

Across six discourse cases and five execution arms, direct, full-copy branch,
shared-page branch, and scoped branch closure agree on canonical graph state
and exact correction evidence. The two positive cases return one changed
interpretation node; four controls return no graph changes. Primary graph and
rule state remain unchanged until revision-checked residual application. On
this tiny fixture, shared-page branch fork medians are about 0.20 ms versus
0.49–0.53 ms full-copy; complete branch totals range from 0.90 to 1.32 ms and
show no stable backend/mode winner. No broad speedup or scaling result
follows. This establishes one integration path for held
predecessor rules, isolation, and residual return. ## 2026-10-03 Thoughtexperiment frame-anchor topology comparison

Across 72 paired correction-trace cells, monolithic closure, generic scoped
closure, and declared-anchor scoped closure preserve exact evidence and
canonical graph parity. Independent discourse states increase target-rule
anchor use from 3 to 24 evaluations at eight pairs, but reduce total Candidate
and Term work by only 5.1% and 5.4%; rule-enumeration counts are unchanged and
no end-to-end speedup appears. This supports safe anchor execution on the
fixture, while showing whole-graph scoped matching remains the dominant cost.
It is not evidence for learned frame discovery or natural-task prevalence.

## 2026-10-03 Native residual-to-cue execution in CoW branches

Across 13 locked cases, a branch-only native graph rule mapped raw actual/expected action residuals against four supplied opposite-action ontology facts. It emitted an existing `flip_comparison` cue for all four inverted-action cases, and the ordinary graph composer generated a held-out-correct `hop_descend_gradient` repair in 4/4. Eight non-inversion/ambiguity controls had zero false cues or proposals; the absent-target case derived a cue but abstained at composition. Baseline generic induction proposed nothing. In all 13 rows the primary graph/rules/revision stayed unchanged during exploration and exactly one compact `ExperimentResidual` returned. This shows native inference over supplied held structure and CoW lifecycle integration. The rule, relation table, cue vocabulary, and target class remain authored; it does not demonstrate that the substrate learned a rule or action geometry. See [results](RESIDUAL_TO_COMPOSER_NATIVE_CUE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md) and its protocol, lock, runner, and raw capture.

## 2026-10-03 Prompted A8 repair proposal and held-out utility

In a corrected four-worker staged replay, the graph-resident A8 composer used a `flip/comparison/gate` cue to propose the `flip_comparison` edit for the faulted `hop_descend_gradient` rule; all four proposals passed the two held-out directions. With only the common cue, it abstained in all four workers. The full result is qualified: the proposed rule digest did not match the host reference candidate under the preregistered name-only normalization because `content_hash`, `revision`, and `rule_uid` also differed, so the strict identity gate failed. Median graph composition plus one candidate trial (including shared task setup) was 133.20 ms versus 77.51 ms for the host default-order policy. This is evidence for prompted composition within a held edit vocabulary, not autonomous diagnosis; graph extraction/materialization remains partly host-side, and no cost advantage is shown. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_A8_PROPOSAL_BRIDGE_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md) and its linked lock/captures.

## 2026-10-03 Supervised numeric relation induction with term synthesis

The source-locked `domains.generator.synthesize` panel fits all five 16-example training draws, then is scored on a disjoint 1,296-case fractional grid. Only one draw generalizes exactly. Four return the shorter x-only predicate and generate 180 false positives each; the exact conjunction costs 1.19 s after 9,862 explored candidates, versus 5–68 ms for the underfit terms. This demonstrates relation induction within a fixed logical grammar and exposes training underdetermination and a long-tailed search cost. It does not show autonomous task/grammar discovery or integration into thoughtexperiment cue grounding. See [results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_RELATION_LEARNING_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_RELATION_LEARNING_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1.py), [corrected analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Coverage-directed numeric relation induction

After random training panels yielded only 1/5 exact hypotheses, five fresh synthesis workers received training sets with explicit x-only and y-only negative counterexamples. All five fit the training data and generalized exactly across 1,296 disjoint fractional vector pairs. Each discovered the same two-axis conjunction; median cost was 1.14 s and 6,587–10,638 explored terms. This supports supervised relation induction within the fixed grammar when examples separate dimensions, while quantifying a substantial cost increase over underfit short hypotheses. Small nonpaired sample; evaluator labels and grammar are supplied. The frozen Term is now integrated in an isolated native cue/composer replay. ## 2026-10-03 Numeric residual-to-cue transfer without action codebook

A 13-case locked replay replaces symbolic action-pair lookup with numeric observed/expected displacement fields. The native candidate branch has no action-pair ontology and no action labels on its residual nodes or induction input. It derives the authored held flip cue in four opposite-vector cases; all four held-out repairs pass. Eight ordinary controls abstain, while the absent-target case derives the cue and correctly emits no proposal. Median two-branch fork time is 0.247 ms and native concern induction is 6.23 ms. This establishes numeric residual-to-rule transfer, not learning: the evaluator encodes task outcomes as vectors, and the opposition formula and cue are authored. ## 2026-10-03 Varied-topology exact inference integration

A source-locked three-worker experiment covers 320 unique 16-node factor graphs per worker across two Qwen3 exports, four layer transitions, ten held-out inputs, and evaluator-imposed fan-in 1/2/4/8. All 960 rows pass independent separator recomputation, exact conditioned/JT energy and returned-sign parity, direct state rescoring, width certificates, source/topology checks, and no-mutation checks. Batch separator conditioning is 1.95–1.99× faster than the current Python monolithic junction-tree path for this fixed mixture. The effect varies by fan-in: about 1.06–1.11× at 1, 0.91–0.96× at 2, 0.32× at 4, and 2.20–2.27× at 8. This is generated, small-graph algorithm evidence; it does not establish natural cheap-cut prevalence, native inference speed, larger-graph scaling, or substrate-learned route selection. V1.1 and v1.2 solver failures remain disclosed in the [results and revision history](LLM_TRANSPORT_VARIED_TOPOLOGY_SOLVER_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md).

## 2026-10-02 Real-export exact inference census

Across all ten held-out inputs from one Qwen3-0.6B layer-12 mode export, three fresh workers rebuild a fixed connected 12-variable, 3-state mode-neuron graph. All 30 rows pass exact factor extraction and width-4 certificates; junction-tree MAP matches independent variable elimination and direct state rescoring exactly. Mean dominant-mode sign agreement is 0.75. This establishes small real-export integration, not task-level language capability, learned graph/cut construction, natural prevalence, or scale. ## 2026-10-02 Real-export layer-transport exact inference

On all ten held-out transitions for one learned Qwen3-0.6B L12→L13 transport, three fresh workers produce 60 connected 16-variable graphs at certified width 8. Exact MAP matches independent variable elimination and direct energy scoring in every case. The transport carries signal, but exact MAP did not improve over BP and is slower in this instrumented run (3.45 s median with allocation tracing active). ## 2026-10-02 Learned-transport topology fan-in

## 2026-10-02 Deferred framebuffer sample attribution

Three source-locked workers trigger the frame-sequence-gated look rule against a deterministic shared-memory frame. Each produces five WorldInstance nodes and two Regions through its single successful deferred `fb_sample`; ordinary, attributed, and exact-snapshot settlement agree, exact graph changes are covered, and no events drop. This establishes producer attribution for one static-frame path, not perception quality or continuous ingestion speed. Source path: [`pending_fb_samples`](../../../runners/dsl/src/graph.rs), [`Effect::FbSample`](../../../runners/dsl/src/rewrite.rs), [`apply_pending_fb_samples`](../../../runners/dsl/src/substrate.rs). See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_FB_SAMPLE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_FB_SAMPLE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_fb_sample_attribution_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_fb_sample_attribution_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_fb_sample_attribution_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Deferred RuleEdit attribution

In three source-locked workers, a graph rule queued one live `where`-clause change; deferred attribution named the producer, the target rule advanced from revision 0 to 1, and its newly enabled body made an exact-reconciled graph write. The original v1 fixture was rejected because its Boolean clause was malformed; v1.1 corrects the clause encoding and passes its gates. This is bounded evidence for one deferred `SetRuleClause` path, not autonomous self-modification. ## 2026-10-02 Raw residual-to-composer handoff boundary

A locked paired check runs the faulted navigation rule, observes wrong/expected actions (Down→Up or Right→Left), and passes only generic `actual`/`expected` fields to `induce_concern` without a name hint or target/operator cue. All four workers mint the same `induced_actual_expected` axis, but `ground_concern` refuses because those predicates map to no held structural relation. On a fresh matched context, the authored A8 cue builds `flip_comparison` for `hop_descend_gradient` in all four workers. Both paths preserve the primary rule during proposal construction. This isolates the handoff gap: observation ingestion and novel-axis minting work, while semantic grounding from an unlabelled outcome residual to a useful edit fails. The authored control's held-out utility is in A8 v1.4; this paired boundary check itself measures no new utility. See [protocol](RESIDUAL_TO_COMPOSER_GROUNDING_BOUNDARY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_residual_to_composer_grounding_boundary_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_residual_to_composer_grounding_boundary_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_to_composer_grounding_boundary_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_to_composer_grounding_boundary_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Current-native exact inference replay

After live verification found the earlier 2026-09-29 inference lock no longer matched the current native binary/build record, the 36-worker grid versus sparse partial q-tree MAP panel was rerun under a new lock. All ten integrity gates pass: exact model extraction, q-width certificates, min-fill/certificate MAP equality with an independent factor-table oracle, direct-energy agreement, profile reconciliation, no mutation, and pre/post source-lock equality. At q=5/6, min-fill falsely refuses the bound-q grid in 6/6 cases at each width while accepting all sparse partial q-trees. Median JT times (min-fill/certified order) are 112.5/135.4 ms for q=5 grids versus 38.0/35.1 ms for q-trees, and 199.9/279.3 ms for q=6 grids versus 51.5/57.1 ms for q-trees. This reconfirms exactness and topology-sensitive cost under the current native build; JT remains pure Python after graph extraction and only two synthetic binary pairwise families are tested. See [v1.3 protocol](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_3_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_3.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_3.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_3_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_3_20261002_analysis.json).

## 2026-10-02 Actor-identity topology follow-up

The initial matched-topology panel had two slower actor+verb hub trials among three. An eight-replicate-per-layout follow-up (32 trials) preserves the same 100k-node/400k-edge graph and proposal gate. Medians are 17.10 ms cyclic, 17.91 ms hub, 17.41 ms clustered, and 17.96 ms random-regular. The hub range extends to 25.71 ms and random to 20.68 ms, so there is occasional high-tail variation; the median suggests at most a modest hub effect in this sample. Every trial selects the actor+verb guard and preserves the primary rule store. See [follow-up protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_TOPOLOGY_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_structural_composer_topology_v1_1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_topology_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/structural_composer_topology_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_topology_v1_1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Composer sensitivity to unrelated edge density

At 100,000 unrelated nodes, a locked 4-family panel varies unrelated directed edges from 0 to 100,000 to 400,000 (0/1/4 edges per node). All 36 trials select the locked proposal and preserve primary rules. Median composition changes little by family from zero to four edges/node: recurrence 25.67→26.30 ms, reply 31.04→31.90 ms, actor relation 17.58→17.65 ms, and actor+verb identity 17.80→17.49 ms. The one noisy upper tail per recurrence/reply cell does not shift medians. Edge insertion raises current RSS by about 10.3 MiB per 100,000 edges in this graph representation. This only measures sensitivity to unrelated cyclic edges with indexed types; it does not establish inference cost, hub/clustering effects, or dense connectivity. See [protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_EDGE_DENSITY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_structural_composer_edge_density_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_edge_density_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/structural_composer_edge_density_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_edge_density_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Native graph build throughput and memory

A source-locked 2×3×3 benchmark measures native `substrate_rs.Substrate.add_node` and `add_edge_unchecked` independently at 100,000 and 1,000,000 nodes, with 0/1/2 edges per node. All 18 fresh-process captures report exact counts. Median node insertion is 1.05–1.08 million nodes/s across cells; edge insertion is 0.75–0.89 million edges/s. At one million nodes, peak RSS delta is 173.2 MiB without edges, 325.8 MiB at one edge/node, and 478.7 MiB at two edges/node. This uses the unchecked native storage API with one node type and cyclic offsets; it does not include edge grammar checking, cognitive inference, or wrapper costs. It provides a concrete native graph storage baseline and highlights edge count as a major memory driver. See [preregistration](NATIVE_GRAPH_BUILD_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_native_graph_build_scaling_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_native_graph_build_scaling_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/native_graph_build_scaling_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/native_graph_build_scaling_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Million-node composer checkpoint

The four-family panel reached 1,000,000 unrelated nodes in three fresh-process replicates per family. All 12 trials select the locked target/operator/site and preserve active primary rules. Median composition is 24.75 ms for recurrence, 30.35 ms for reply, 17.23 ms for the actor relation guard, and 16.62 ms for actor+verb identity. The reply family includes one 44.69 ms trial; its other two trials were 29.90 and 30.35 ms. RSS grew by 143.2–143.9 MiB during insertion of the one million nodes (about 150 bytes per inserted node in this specific representation/process measurement). This supports low composer-latency sensitivity to unrelated-node count through one million for the four prompted cases. It does not establish a full 10⁷–10⁸-node regime, exact graph-object memory cost, or natural-world frame locality. See [protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_FAMILY_SCALING_V1_3_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_3.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_3.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_3_lock_20261002.json), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_3_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Composer scaling extended to 100,000 nodes

The locked 4-family panel now extends to 100,000 unrelated nodes (48/48 trials pass the expected proposal and primary-rule isolation gates). Relative to zero noise, the 100,000-node median composition changes by +0.54 ms for recurrence, +0.84 ms for reply, −0.77 ms for the actor relation guard, and +0.78 ms for actor+verb identity. The negative shift is within observed runtime variation. Median insertion-phase RSS rises by about 13.2–13.9 MiB at 100,000 nodes, while low-size changes often remain within resident allocator pages. These measurements support low latency sensitivity through 100,000 unrelated nodes for these four prompted proposals; RSS remains coarse and no million-node or natural workload has been measured. See [protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_FAMILY_SCALING_V1_2_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_2_lock_20261002.json), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_2_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Four-family composer and RSS scaling

A source-locked 4-family × 3-graph-size × 3-replicate panel completed 36/36 expected proposal and primary-rule isolation gates. Median full composition ranges across 0/1,000/10,000 unrelated nodes are: recurrence 25.25/24.11/25.90 ms; repeated reply 29.40/30.47/30.92 ms; actor relation-only 15.82/16.20/16.87 ms; actor+verb identity 16.18/16.34/16.62 ms. The broader sample still does not measure autonomous residual discovery or capability quality beyond proposal gates. The full prompted structural composer now has a measured unrelated-node scaling point on the real actor-recipient identity repair. ## 2026-10-02 Graph-resident site materialization scaling

A locked 3×3 synthetic grid measures extraction plus `EditSite` insertion on fresh graphs with 0, 1,000, or 10,000 unrelated nodes, and target signatures of 8, 32, or 64 fields. Every cell produces the exact expected site set across five trials. At 32 and 64 fields, median time is essentially flat across unrelated graph sizes (max/min 1.019×); work is dominated by generating and inserting the candidate sites. At 8 fields, the 10,000-node cell is 1.54× the zero-noise timing, with sub-millisecond measurements. This supports low sensitivity to unrelated node count in this tested substrate path, not full composer scaling. See [preregistration](GRAPH_SITE_MATERIALIZATION_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_graph_site_materialization_scaling_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_graph_site_materialization_scaling_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/graph_site_materialization_scaling_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/graph_site_materialization_scaling_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Edge identity site extraction scaling

A source-locked single-host microbenchmark measures only `find_edge_identity_sites` over synthetic fresh-node signatures with 1–256 direct bound fields. All nine candidate counts match the predicted singleton/pair/full-signature formula. After replacing repeated list scans with a set-backed duplicate check, median extraction is 1.39 ms at 64 fields and 45.5 ms at 256 fields. This supports quadratic candidate-count growth and removes a substantial avoidable duplicate-check cost; it does not establish complete composer latency, memory use, graph-scale behavior, or natural-task prevalence. See [current-source scaling results](EDGE_IDENTITY_SITE_SCALING_CURRENT_SOURCE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md).

## 2026-10-03 Full edge-identity composer crossed scaling

The current-source full path crosses 2/4/8/16/32 identity fields with 0/1,000/10,000 unrelated nodes. ## 2026-10-02 Bidirectional edge-identity proposal transfer

The corrected four-worker v1.1 capture passes all 16 analysis gates. Native graph-Term selection chooses the correct scope for both recurrence idempotence and repeated conversation replies in all workers, with 8/8 held-out trials correct per family. The candidate generator is a hand-authored generic AST scan in the harness, so the evidence supports substrate-side selection/transfer, not autonomous proposal generation. ## 2026-10-02 Substrate-side edge-identity proposal generation

The general structural composer can now derive guard-site hypotheses from rule structure through the reusable edit-operator layer, expose those sites to the graph, and use a native Term to choose a unique operator/site. In four source-locked workers, proposals for recurrence and repeated replies each pass 2/2 training and 2/2 held-out episodes; no-overlap and tied-site controls abstain in all workers. The task cue and oracle remain external, and Python still extracts applicability and applies the selected edit. This is prompted proposal generation on two authored rules, not self-directed diagnosis or open-ended invention. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Edge-identity scope boundary on actor goals

The v1.1 extension tests `mint_actor_goal_recipient`, a real seeded rule that must make one goal per actor/verb. A generated guard that checks only whether the agent has any `has_actor_goal` edge passes single-verb training but suppresses the second distinct held-out verb in 8/8 episodes; the unmodified rule passes. Recurrence and repeated-reply transfer remain 8/8 held-out each. This identifies a missing proposal dimension: attribute identity for a freshly added node. The present system is prompted and does not discover the residual itself. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Attribute-scoped edge guard repair

The follow-up derives identity candidates from direct bound-attribute terms on freshly added nodes. The graph Term selects the `actor+verb` signature for `ActorGoal`; this repairs all eight held-out two-verb episodes while the relation-only control still fails all eight. Recurrence and repeated replies remain correct, and ambiguity controls abstain. The measured site vocabulary is O(m²) in a target's direct bound fields and does not cover computed expressions or autonomous cue discovery.
