## 2026-10-02 Recurrence generic structural-proposal boundary

Four locked workers select the recurrence rule and generic `add_match_guard`, but the fixed guard emits zero offers on both held-out candidates in every worker. A separately scored `dedup_identity_fix` reference passes all held-outs and reaches fixed point; receipt and primary isolation pass for both branches. This is negative transfer evidence: current target/operator-family selection does not specify the identity-aware idempotence semantics required by recurrence. The cue is externally supplied and names the target and guard family, so this does not measure autonomous diagnosis or operator invention. V1's command-dispatch failure produced no worker outcomes and is documented in the amendment. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_general_structural_proposal_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Recurrence compose-and-select integration

The failed generic composer proposal is added to the eight existing recurrence hypotheses. Across four source-locked workers, a native graph Term given only opaque candidate IDs and two training outcomes uniquely selects `dedup_identity_fix`; the winner passes all eight held-out episodes, while tie and all-wrong controls abstain. Evidence remains branch-local and primary rules are preserved. Median training scoring takes 8.02 s; the native selection step takes 0.568 ms. Candidate generation and task labels remain host-supplied, so this measures evidence-guided selection rather than new-operator invention. V1's held-out capture stopped before scoring due to a candidate-order replay mismatch; no outcome is claimed from that attempt. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_COMPOSE_AND_SELECT_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_COMPOSE_AND_SELECT_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_COMPOSE_AND_SELECT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_compose_and_select_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json), and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_compose_and_select_v1.py).

## 2026-10-02 Graph-resident evidence scoring and residual return

The `select_candidate_from_graph_evidence` API at [`contract_query.py`](../../../domains/contract_query.py) stores bounded proposal/evidence rows in an isolated substrate branch and uses a native Term to count matching labels and return only a unique positive-evidence winner. Four workers and 32 backend/alias conditions select the forward repair; tie, all-wrong, malformed coverage, and duplicate-namespace controls behave as specified. Eight compact residuals return to primary without candidate/evidence leakage or rule changes. Candidate enumeration, probes, predictions, and oracle labels are still host-supplied. The earlier v2 pilot is censored; see [v3 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V3_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V3_PREREG_20261002.md), [censored pilot](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V2_CENSORED_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_choice_v3_analysis_20261002.json).

Retrospective recurrence transfer: the same Term scorer uniquely selects `dedup_identity_fix` from eight descriptors across the three recorded workers using their two train episodes, then the selected candidate passes their two held-out episodes. The original preregistered six-per-worker count was wrong and is not claimed as met; this is API transfer on already visible outcomes, not independent replication ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_RETRO_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_RETRO_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [replay](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_graph_selection_retro_v1_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_graph_selection_retro_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json)).

Prospective recurrence gate: four workers each ran four training and four held-out episodes per candidate, and the graph Term selected `dedup_identity_fix` uniquely every time. It passes all held-outs and reaches fixed point. The original full protocol fails its all-candidate closure gate: five losing proposals hit the runner's 100-call cap in all 160 corresponding train/held-out episodes. A paired bounded-evaluation follow-up preserves pass/fail outcomes on all 256 candidate episodes and selects the same repair in every worker, while reducing mean panel runtime 20.6× and rule work 5.46× on this fixture through staged 200-call budgets and early rejection. This improves the thoughtexperiment harness, not general rule-closure semantics; a budget rejection is not proof of nontermination. A separately generated recurrence arity/budget panel preserves all candidate pass/fail outcomes and the graph-Term winner across 2/4/8 due candidates and 200/400/800-call budgets, with zero false rejections among reference-resolved held-out cases; savings are concentrated in proposals that exhaust at stage one ([calibration results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_BUDGET_CALIBRATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md)). Corrected baseline candidate episode means are 459 ms training and 467 ms held-out (the prior 115–117 ms figure was understated by four). See [early-rejection results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_EARLY_REJECTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_EARLY_REJECTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_early_rejection_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_early_rejection_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json), [original results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_EARLY_REJECTION_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md)).

Omniglot matcher transfer v1.1: graph-Term training evidence selects PointSetDist across three workers and transfers to alphabet-disjoint evaluation data at 60.4–66.8% accuracy (5% chance), best or tied-best against two alternative supplied Terms. This is real handwritten-glyph matcher selection, not open-ended visual learning; the corrected post-capture analyzer and earlier pilot gaps are disclosed ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OMNIGLOT_MATCHER_SELECTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_omniglot_matcher_selection_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json)).

FSDD matcher transfer v1.2: on six-speaker real speech data, the graph-Term selector uniquely chooses ordered DTW in two speaker-disjoint folds and correctly abstains on a training tie in the third. The selected matcher scores 43.0% and 64.5% on fresh held-out speakers against 10% chance, and is hindsight-best in both selected folds. The corrected analysis passes 9/12 gates; all three failures concern end-to-end selection under the fold-0 tie. Two earlier harness captures are disclosed as invalid pilots. This is transfer among three supplied Terms, not matcher invention or general speech understanding ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FSDD_MATCHER_SELECTION_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FSDD_MATCHER_SELECTION_V1_2_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fsdd_matcher_selection_v1_2.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fsdd_matcher_selection_v1_3_analysis_20261002.json)).

Generated-pool batch/stream scale v1.1: 54 paired fresh processes have exact hypothesis and held-out behavior parity between generator registration modes across size caps 5/6/7 and 4/8/12 unlabeled probes. At size 7, 8.6k–8.7k terms take 0.22–0.58 s; streaming reduces coarse peak RSS by 4–8% but has no stable wall-time win. The target behavior is absent at caps 5/6 and present at cap 7 in all replicates. This is one arithmetic family, not a broad tractability result; incomplete high-scale pools and incremental search remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATOR_STREAM_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generator_stream_scale_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json)).

Generated-pool stress scale v1.2: at size 9, both registration modes complete 88,069 explored terms and return identical 1,306-hypothesis pools in all three paired replicates. Streaming reduces process peak RSS by 25% (72 to 54 MiB) while taking about 7.0 s, essentially the same wall time as batch. The 10-second internal and 15-second parent caps were not reached. This is still one arithmetic family/anchor; size-11 cap behavior and cross-family scaling remain unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATOR_STREAM_SCALE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENERATOR_STREAM_SCALE_V1_2_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generator_stream_scale_v1_3_analysis_20261002.json)).

Generated-pool stress boundary v1.3: size 9 completes in all six runs; all six size-11 searches hit the 12-second internal timeout. At size 11, streaming examines about 12% more terms and reduces process peak RSS about 71% (338 to 99 MiB), but batch and streaming return different partial pools. Each partial pool contains finite-heldout-exact hypotheses, which does not establish completeness or adequacy. The 16-second parent and 2 GiB address-space limits were not reached. Cross-family behavior and incremental resume remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATOR_STREAM_SCALE_V1_3_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENERATOR_STREAM_SCALE_V1_3_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generator_stream_scale_v1_4_analysis_20261002.json)).

## 2026-10-02 Active choice among ordered edit sites

After the ordinary composer abstained on a direction-free cue, host-side generic enumeration supplied fault, forward-swap, and reverse-swap hypotheses. Native-Term active selection found a maximum-entropy probe in all 32 CoW/full-copy and alias-rate conditions. Once the oracle was released, only the forward edit survived; it passed all held-outs while both other hypotheses failed nonzero held-outs. This shows active evidence gathering among supplied candidates, while candidate enumeration, edit vocabulary, task fixture, and oracle remain outside the substrate. The literal one-bit criterion is impossible for three binary-output hypotheses; the corrected 0.9183-bit maximum and post-capture analyzer changes are disclosed. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_CHOICE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_CHOICE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_CHOICE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_choice_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Attribute-site cue-ablation boundary

Four source-locked workers choose the ordered swap when the cue names its direction, then abstain in every direction-omitted, source-only, and destination-only condition. Both opposing candidates were available and the primary rule stayed unchanged. This measures cue-conditioned selection and an honest abstention boundary; it does not show site discovery. The analyzer's exact-candidate-count defect and corrected subset check are disclosed in the result amendment. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_CUE_ABLATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_CUE_ABLATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_cue_ablation_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Fresh target with ordered attribute-site composition

The graph Term selected `classify_meter_delta`, `swap_term_leaf`, and `bad_score -> good_score` in four workers. All 32 isolated CoW/full-copy query conditions retained the proposal after oracle release, and all held-outs passed; full-copy fault controls failed nonzero cases. CoW branch creation was 1.95× faster locally, but end-to-end query time was slightly slower than full copy, so no full-loop speed advantage is supported. The ordered-site cue names the answer, and Python still authors/materializes the task. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_query_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_query_v1_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_query_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_query_v1_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_query_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Fresh-rule transfer with isolated CoW branches

On a newly authored binary threshold rule, the graph Term selected the target and `flip_comparison`; all 32 query selections across CoW/full-copy branches and four neutral rates selected the proposal after the oracle. Held-out utility, receipts, fixed points, and primary preservation passed. Snapshot-CoW fork averaged 14.0 µs versus 29.9 µs for full copy, while end-to-end query time was 64.56 ms versus 65.12 ms over 200 isolated predictions. This supports a local fork-cost advantage and exact CoW/full-copy semantics, not a general speed claim; candidate patch and prediction dominate. The earlier v1 query outcomes remain valid, but its condition timings were confounded by branch reuse. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FRESH_RULE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FRESH_RULE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [timing amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FRESH_RULE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fresh_rule_active_query_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fresh_rule_active_query_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fresh_rule_active_query_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Cue-conditioned shape-site selection

Four workers select the keyed attribute swap in the direction named by the cue, and abstain when the cue leaves both directions tied. The matched proposal passes all held-out shape cases; the reversed proposal fails. This is a diagnostic on a previously inspected fixture; Python still extracts sites/applies edits and the cue states the desired direction. A 255.77/38.96 ms composer timing asymmetry is unexplained. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_SHAPE_SITE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_SHAPE_SITE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_SHAPE_SITE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_shape_site_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_shape_site_v1_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_shape_site_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_shape_site_v1_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_shape_site_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Active probe selection

Across four source/native-locked workers, a native-Term selector chose a maximum-entropy input that separated supplied `key` and `signature` hypotheses in all 16 pools, including pools with 99% uninformative aliases. After the selection was frozen, the task oracle left only `key`, which passed 20 held-out discriminators per worker. Random probes discriminated in 0/4 trials at 90% and 99% alias rates. This is finite active query selection, not hypothesis or task generation: Terms, candidate pools, oracle, and scoring are host-supplied. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ACTIVE_PROBE_SELECTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ACTIVE_PROBE_SELECTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_active_probe_selection_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_active_probe_selection_v1_lock_20261002.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_active_probe_selection_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_active_probe_selection_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Composed proposal active-query integration

The prompted general-rule composer selected a comparator repair for the control-kind target; actual predictions came from isolated native rule branches. The active selector distinguished it from the fault in all 16 pools through 99% neutral names, and post-query labels selected the proposal. It passed both held-outs in four workers while the fault failed. Query-stage mean time increased from 10.62 ms to 208.66 ms as neutral cases increased, because each hypothesis was run on every candidate. This joins the proposal and query stages for one authored target; task/fault/cue/oracle and rewrite materialization remain Python-owned. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COMPOSED_PROPOSAL_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COMPOSED_PROPOSAL_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

V1.2 repeats the normal `ground_concern` control-kind result against the upgraded provenance path: four workers select the non-action classifier and flip comparison, pass both held-outs with receipts/isolation, and abstain without a cue. An opt-in graph-term picker reaches beyond action-writing rules: four blinded workers select `classify_kind_family` and `flip_comparison`, match the host reference candidate, and abstain with no cue. ## 2026-10-02 A8 quantum-route transfer

The corrected v1.3 portal oracle passes all integrity gates across four workers and six layouts; the two fresh held-outs H5/H6 pass in every worker. Source/fault controls discriminate, all host candidates are scored, and receipts/isolation hold. Graph selection remains slower than the host default (86.25 ms vs 49.16 ms median). This demonstrates cued repair transfer on one authored rule family, not general learning or self-improvement. The earlier v1.2 failed oracle capture is retained. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_A8_QUANTA_ROUTE_TRANSFER_V1_3_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_A8_QUANTA_ROUTE_TRANSFER_V1_3_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_a8_quanta_route_transfer_v1_3.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_a8_quanta_route_transfer_v1_3_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_a8_quanta_route_transfer_v1_3_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_a8_quanta_route_transfer_v1_3_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_a8_quanta_route_transfer_v1_3_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Navigation outcome-ranker integration

Three fresh source/native-locked workers integrated the production graph-resident correction ranker with six navigation candidates. Up/Left training outcomes moved `flip_comparison` ahead of the cold structural-cost choice for held-out Down/Right cases, reducing attempts from two to one versus cold rank. It tied the unchanged default order, which already selected the successful edit first. The independent review passes 13/13 outcome and integrity checks. This is within-family outcome use; host candidate generation and record transport remain, and the four-family blinded utility gate is still unmet. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_NAVIGATION_OUTCOME_RANK_INTEGRATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_NAVIGATION_OUTCOME_RANK_INTEGRATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_navigation_outcome_rank_integration_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_navigation_outcome_rank_integration_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_navigation_outcome_rank_integration_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [review](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_navigation_outcome_rank_integration_v1_analysis_review_20261002.json).

The runner omitted preregistered per-policy ordering latency. The capture amendment discloses the missing timing; no economic advantage is inferred from the trial counts alone. See [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_NAVIGATION_OUTCOME_RANK_INTEGRATION_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md).

## 2026-10-02 Residual-to-composer cue derivation

An explicit action-vector decoder maps opposite cardinal actions to the existing `flip_comparison` cue. Four direction workers propose the expected navigation repair and pass two held-out cases each; four valid non-inversions, four malformed/underdetermined residuals, and an absent-target control produce no candidate. This is a hand-authored bridge to a held operator vocabulary, not learned cue induction. Whole rule-store snapshots reveal 24 rules lazily initialized by induction on every fresh worker and five additional rules in cue-positive contexts. The follow-up runs induction and grounding in a CoW snapshot branch: 13/13 cases preserve the full primary graph/rule state during exploration, and each returns exactly one `ExperimentResidual`; all four positive proposals remain held-out useful and controls abstain. This closes the isolation/return boundary for the authored decoder, not target-blind cause discovery. See the [v1 results](RESIDUAL_TO_COMPOSER_CUE_DERIVATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [branch isolation results](RESIDUAL_TO_COMPOSER_BRANCH_ISOLATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [branch protocol](RESIDUAL_TO_COMPOSER_BRANCH_ISOLATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_residual_to_composer_branch_isolation_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_to_composer_branch_isolation_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_to_composer_branch_isolation_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

The follow-up passes 26/26 CoW/deep-copy task rows and returns post-induction deltas only after held-out-positive scoring. Revision-checked adoption reproduces each positive branch's 40-node/2-edge derived graph delta while preserving the host rule registry. Shared-graph-pages fork median is 0.246 ms versus 0.515 ms for full copy on the measured host; this small fixture does not establish a general speed advantage or full CoW across substrate state. The residual's independent usability and large-graph behavior remain open. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_RETURN_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_RETURN_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_residual_cow_return_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_cow_return_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_return_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

Candidate rehydration then passes in four fresh workers: each serialized proposal is read back from the host graph and reproduces exact held-out outcomes. A stale-revision attempt is rejected without a partial write, followed by a successful current-revision return. The harness packages this explicitly validated rule in a graph node; cue interpretation remains authored and branch rule vectors remain isolated. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_CANDIDATE_REHYDRATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_CANDIDATE_REHYDRATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_residual_cow_candidate_rehydration_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_cow_candidate_rehydration_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_candidate_rehydration_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

At a 100,000-node host and fanout 16, a three-repeat fresh-process capture finds 209.9 MiB median PSS growth for validated full-copy branches and 36.8 MiB for shared graph pages; branch-fork medians are 17.32 ms and 0.186 ms. All candidates pass held-out checks and one exact residual is returned. PSS stays elevated after branch drop, and this sequential fanout fixture does not measure concurrent throughput. It is evidence for this workload's partial-CoW memory benefit, not general performance or reclamation. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_FANOUT_MEMORY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_FANOUT_MEMORY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_residual_cow_fanout_memory_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_cow_fanout_memory_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_fanout_memory_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

The allocator follow-up reads glibc `mallinfo2` on the actual Python-loaded task: allocated/mapped bytes return from 340.9 MiB to 26.3 MiB after full-copy branch drop and from 64.4 MiB to 26.3 MiB after shared-page branch drop. `malloc_trim` then releases retained pages, confirming that high post-drop PSS was not equivalent to live glibc-accounted branch allocations in this workload. The allocator counters are process-wide and glibc-specific; trimming also freed prior baseline arenas. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_residual_cow_allocator_reclamation_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_cow_allocator_reclamation_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_allocator_reclamation_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Offline adequacy evidence-policy frontier

An offline, source-locked comparison replayed 6,480 independent/common-mode signed trials under unanimity, majority, and 80% agreement (19,440 decisions; 327/327 checks). With five independent providers and 10% error, majority raised correct eligibility but also raised false eligibility; at 50% error and under common-mode corruption, vote thresholds do not establish truth. This is sensitivity analysis over simulated labels, not an implemented policy or deployment recommendation. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_POLICY_TRADEOFF_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_POLICY_TRADEOFF_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_policy_tradeoff_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Common-mode adequacy label noise

A 4,320-trial common-mode mixture panel passes 84 independent checks on parity and threshold. With five simulated providers and 50% marginal error, false eligibility rises from 4/120 at independent errors to 31/120 at 50% mixture correlation and 55/120 at full common mode; additional IDs lose their benefit under shared errors. This is a stipulated mechanism model, not an estimate of real provider correlation. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_CORRELATED_NOISE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_CORRELATED_NOISE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_correlated_noise_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Stochastic signed-label noise

A 2,160-trial, two-task panel passed 48 independent gates across three fresh workers. Under the receipt's fail-closed rule, any provider disagreement becomes refusal; under simulated independent noise, false-accept probability for a wrong candidate falls as `p^m`, while correct-adoption probability also falls as `(1-p)^m`. Five providers at 10% noise yield theoretical 0.00001 false acceptance and 0.59049 correct acceptance; at 50% noise both fall to 0.03125. These are stipulated channel probabilities, not estimates of real-provider reliability or evidence that provider quorum proves truth. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_STOCHASTIC_NOISE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_STOCHASTIC_NOISE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_stochastic_noise_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Threshold task: domain shift and label-truth boundary

The signed selected-probe route transfers to an independently implemented three-input threshold oracle in a 15-worker source-locked panel (14/14 independent gates). Correct acquisition generalizes to 100 shifted held-outs; broad truthful probes reject the wrong candidate; alias-only probes and conflicting signed evidence are refused. A trusted false signer still induces wrong adoption in 3/3 runs, yielding 42/100 held-out errors: evidence authentication does not certify label truth. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_THRESHOLD_NOISE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_THRESHOLD_NOISE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_threshold_noise_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Selected-probe adoption transfer

The strict authenticated example-adoption route now requires a source-locked, recomputed nonzero-entropy probe over a supplied challenger and admissible domain. A preregistered transfer panel passed all 15 independent gates across 18 fresh workers: correct parity and three-argument clamp examples were adopted and passed held-outs; alias-domain false candidates were refused despite accepted receipts; distinguishing probes rejected false candidates. This is bounded transfer evidence, not autonomous challenger/domain generation or a natural-task success rate. See [transfer results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_SELECTED_PROBE_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_SELECTED_PROBE_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_selected_probe_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-01 Thoughtexperiment execution profile update

**Recurrence wave replication:** a second, independently authored thoughtexperiment family completes 24 source/native-locked workers with exact sequential/concurrent outcome parity and primary isolation. Shared graph pages deliver 1.72× concurrent/sequential throughput at fanout 8 (2.40× worker CPU/wall); eager full-copy concurrency remains slower than sequential. This supports backend- and workload-dependent parallelism, not a universal scaling claim. See [recurrence results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_WAVE_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_WAVE_V2_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_wave_v2_raw_20261001.json).
**Recurrence graph-size scaling:** across 48 locked workers, shared-page fanout-8 wall time rises 75→106 ms from 0 to 200k unrelated nodes, retaining 1.64–1.75× throughput over sequential; eager full-copy grows to 1.09 s and 507 MiB sampled ΔPSS at 200k. This is bounded evidence for one authored recurrence fixture; candidate count and task diversity remain fixed. See [scale results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_scale_v1_raw_20261001.json).
**Recurrence candidate-count scaling:** with a one-pass candidate index and 100k fixed noise nodes, shared-page fanout-8 moves from 0.65× sequential throughput at one candidate to 3.00× at 128, preserving all candidate oracles and primary isolation. The candidate set shares an authored repair structure, so autonomous induction remains untested. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_scale_v1_raw_20261001.json).
**Outcome ranking under verified negative evidence:** a three-worker locked policy panel passed 10/11 gates. After an independent [1,0] failure was recorded, both the current raw-count Program and a branch-local smoothed-mean Program selected the correct recurrence repair first; the proposed policy showed no gain. This supports responsiveness in one fixture, not autonomous utility adjudication or calibrated confidence. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CONFIDENCE_RANK_POLICY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CONFIDENCE_RANK_POLICY_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_confidence_rank_policy_v1_raw_20261001.json).

**Concurrency evidence update:** native fork and edge-scope bindings now release the GIL around isolated branch setup. The same-oracle 12-process replay passes all gates and changes scoped full-copy fanout-8 waves from 3.07 s/0.98× CPU-wall to 1.62 s/2.00×; shared-page concurrent waves remain near 1.21 s/1.01×. This is bounded evidence of backend-dependent parallelism. See [v3 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_CONCURRENCY_PROFILE_V3_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [source-locked raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_concurrency_profile_v3_raw_20261001.json).
**Branch mutation evidence update:** the native active-rule-by-name path passes exact outcome parity with the existing Python rule-store export path in 24 locked processes, cuts concurrent rule-patch phase 6.5–7.5×, and shortens whole waves 14–19%; shared-page CPU overlap remains unresolved. See [v4 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_RULE_PATCH_V4_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_rule_patch_v4_raw_20261001.json).
The native patch route is now the default after a 36-process semantic panel matched replacement, removal, absent-target, mixed, later-slot, and two-rule edits across both backends. The legacy Python route remains available as an explicit control. See [semantic parity results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_NATIVE_RULE_PATCH_SEMANTICS_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_native_rule_patch_semantics_v1_raw_20261001.json).
**Nested prediction update:** native branch rule-store summaries remove full-store JSON export from native-fork prediction setup. A 12-process profile cuts the shared-page eight-branch wave 55–56% and raises concurrent CPU/wall to 1.09×, with semantic and primary-isolation gates intact. See [profile results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PYTHON_NATIVE_PROFILE_V3_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_python_native_profile_v3_raw_20261001.json).
**Scaling update:** the positive/negative shape-decoding workload passes all 9 gates across 0–200k unrelated nodes. At 200k, full-copy gains 1.77× concurrent batch speed over sequential with 2.74× CPU/wall; shared pages keep lower absolute latency but concurrent execution remains slower than sequential. See [scale results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_scale_v1_raw_20261001.json).
**Aperture-boundary update:** at 100k noise nodes, observation-spoke edges increase the shared-page sequential wave from 311 to 9,132 ms at almost unchanged edge count; concurrent execution reaches 6.47× worker CPU/wall. Disconnected path/star/grid/random cases remain cheaper. See [topology results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_TOPOLOGY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_topology_v1_raw_20261001.json).
**Matched boundary curve:** holding 100,771 nodes and 100,743 edges fixed, increasing focus spokes from 0% to 99% raises shared-page sequential wave latency 305→5,851 ms, while concurrent latency grows 407→1,154 ms and worker CPU/wall reaches 5.81×. See [boundary-sweep results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_BOUNDARY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_boundary_v1_raw_20261001.json).

The shape-decoding rehearsal now has source/native-locked, 12-process evidence for isolated Python/native CPU attribution and unprofiled concurrent CPU/phase behavior. Sequential profiles pass semantic and primary-isolation gates; fanout-8 concurrent work still runs at approximately one CPU-ms per wall-ms. This is evidence about one bounded rehearsal path, not general substrate tractability. See [profile results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PYTHON_NATIVE_PROFILE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PYTHON_NATIVE_PROFILE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PYTHON_NATIVE_PROFILE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md).

## Rung 3/4 activation update (2026-09-26)

The clean-boot runtime census resolves one integration question for MetaShape growth: the default core (17 seeds) loads neither `shape_decode_loop` nor `meta_shape_growth`; the selected conversation profile (70 seeds) loads the shape loop but not growth rules, and two `teach()` observations remain pending. Explicitly loading the growth dependency closure processes those observations as residual→recognized and mints one symbolic `MetaShape`. All inspected Gen/Time/Depth/Blink activation flags are absent in each clean profile. This is one authored activation fixture and does not establish natural residual frequency or general concept-learning capability. The profile/call-site audit and held-out false-mint/transfer measurement remain open. See the [runtime results](SUBSTRATE_INDUCTION_RUNTIME_ACTIVATION_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_induction_runtime_activation_census_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_induction_runtime_activation_census_v1_20260926.json).

**Daemon-profile correction:** the default fresh daemon mode is `full` (`load_all_seeds`), not core-only; it loads both growth seeds and processes the repeated teaching fixture, adding one symbolic MetaShape. The daemon `chat` and `embodied` fresh boots omit the growth seeds and leave the fixture pending. Full boot has 11 failures across 740 selected manifests. The selected Phase 7 conversation evaluation profile also omits MetaShape growth. See the [daemon census results](SUBSTRATE_INDUCTION_DAEMON_ACTIVATION_CENSUS_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_induction_runtime_activation_census_v2.py), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_induction_runtime_activation_census_v2_20260926.json).

**Evidence-to-learning boundary:** daemon teacher dispatch writes `TeacherEvidence` and candidate graph nodes but does not call `teach()` or `meta_shape.observe`; separately, daemon experiment close routes graph-marked ready conclusions and eligible adoptions through `teach()`. Thus a gated Rung 4 route exists for experiment closure, while teacher evidence has no demonstrated adoption bridge. Neither route's natural activation rate or downstream value has been measured ([teacher route trace](SUBSTRATE_INDUCTION_DAEMON_ACTIVATION_CENSUS_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md#production-route-source-trace), [close-action result](SUBSTRATE_DAEMON_EXPERIMENT_CLOSE_METASHAPE_BRIDGE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md)).

**Conversation-path probe:** one source-locked full-profile run sent four repeated ordinary statements through `WorldAdapter.converse` and observed no added `ShapeObservation`, `MetaShape`, `TaughtHint`, or `TeacherEvidence` nodes. The shape learner was installed, but this input path did not reach it for the fixture. Untraced turn times were 76–255 ms in this one run; the call-traced v1 timings are excluded as instrumentation-contaminated. [Results](SUBSTRATE_DAEMON_CONVERSATION_METASHAPE_BRIDGE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/daemon_conversation_metashape_bridge_v1_1_lock_20260926.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_daemon_conversation_metashape_bridge_v1_1_20260926.json).

**Production close-action bridge:** `jabberwock_daemon._close_tick` calls the experiment conclusion/adoption closer, which routes eligible results through `teach()` and thus MetaShape observation. In a controlled source-locked probe, two eligible conclusions became recognized observations with residual `axis_tag`/`topic` fields; the growth rules minted one support-2 MetaShape. The original strict status-order preregistration failed and is preserved; a separately locked fresh-process replication of the recognized-plus-residual pattern passed. This establishes an available gated route, not natural activation rate, evidence validity, or downstream value. [Initial result](SUBSTRATE_DAEMON_EXPERIMENT_CLOSE_METASHAPE_BRIDGE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [replication](SUBSTRATE_DAEMON_EXPERIMENT_CLOSE_METASHAPE_BRIDGE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_daemon_experiment_close_metashape_bridge_v1_1_20260926.json).

**Held-out transfer and fixed-field control:** two independent source-locked runs show a support-2 experiment-summary MetaShape recognizing a third supported summary with new values in the abstracted fields, while refusing a refuted summary whose fixed `answer` differs; no extra mint occurs. This is a narrow positive/negative pair, not a broad error rate or evidence validation. [Results](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_EXPERIMENT_HELDOUT_DISCRIMINATION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [v1 capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_experiment_heldout_discrimination_v1_20260926.json), [v1.1 replication](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_experiment_heldout_discrimination_v1_1_20260926.json).

**Multi-schema boundary:** a locked four-schema panel and fresh-process replication show the weaker keyword-prior rung can attribute observations to MetaShapes despite fixed-field changes; a preexisting `spatial_dynamics` prior also dominates one intended new schema. Only 1/4 designed negative controls remained residual. The report separates `recognized` from `recognized_prior`; it does not interpret these structural outcomes as semantic false-positive prevalence. [Results and per-family captures](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_MULTISCHEMA_TRANSFER_PANEL_RESULTS_20260926.md), [v1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_multischema_transfer_panel_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [v1.1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_multischema_transfer_panel_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Fixed-value sensitivity:** a 28-control sweep and fresh-process replication classify each observation by rung. Twelve controls were attributed to their target MetaShape by `recognized_prior` despite changed fixed values; seven MotionRule controls were attributed to `spatial_dynamics`; three extra-field examples were structurally recognized; six controls remained residual. No control caused an additional mint. This is controlled pattern behavior, not semantic precision. The next capability gate pairs these outputs with a downstream decision oracle. [Results](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_PRIOR_FIXED_VALUE_SENSITIVITY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [v1 capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_prior_fixed_value_sensitivity_v1_20260926.json), [v1.1 capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_prior_fixed_value_sensitivity_v1_1_20260926.json).

**Downstream filing decision:** the actual `teach()` consumer `categorise_by_shape` was evaluated before/after MetaShape growth on 20 locked synthetic examples and exactly replicated. Coverage rose 25%→75%; accuracy fell 65%→55%; abstention recall fell 90%→30%, as two new labels over-filed negative controls and one minted shape was not returned by the keyword consumer. This shows recognition/mint evidence alone overstates task utility. No natural-domain performance claim follows. [Protocol and results](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_CATEGORISATION_UTILITY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [v1.1 capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_categorisation_utility_v1_1_20260926.json), [v1.2 replication](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_categorisation_utility_v1_2_20260926.json).

**Default-filing reachability probe:** three actor-goal codebook helpers make 19 runtime classifier calls on the default boot, all abstaining because it loads zero MetaShapes. Loading only `shape_vocabulary` changes one item to `spatial_dynamics`: an epistemic signal whose own note describes a stylistic account. Replication is exact, but no independent label establishes this as an error. The helpers have no discovered non-test repository caller, so this does not estimate production frequency. [Default results](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_DEFAULT_FILING_RUNTIME_RESULTS_20260926.md), [vocabulary-arm results](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_DEFAULT_FILING_RUNTIME_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_metashape_default_filing_runtime_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Status:** inventory in progress. This map records implementation paths and existing evidence pointers; it is not a claim that the listed capabilities are general, complete, or production ready.

## Capability acquisition: spectral I/O contract follow-up (2026-09-29)

An independent direct-DFT oracle on three fixed signals confirmed six valid forward spectral artifacts and exposed six false acceptances when the same forward term was labeled as magnitude-only signal recovery. A source-locked follow-up adds requested-primitive and declared-port checks: the six forward cases remain accepted and match the oracle, while all six non-identifiable inverse requests are refused as `PrimitiveRequest(recover_signal)`. This closes one constructed failure mode only; general semantic-verification soundness, other primitive families, and natural-task error rates remain unmeasured ([v1.4 baseline](SUBSTRATE_SPECTRAL_IO_ORACLE_V1_4_RESULTS_20260929.md), [v1.5 correction](SUBSTRATE_SPECTRAL_IO_ORACLE_V1_5_RESULTS_20260929.md), [v1.5 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/spectral_io_oracle_v1_5/analysis.json)).

**Cross-family scope constraint:** an AST source audit finds only one buildable computational family (`spectral_value`) in `substrate_extension.build_primitive`, with a forward constructor and a negative-control twin. Thus the spectral correction has no within-extension second family replication yet. The audit does not cover the separate shape/dimension learners or authored rule-minting pathways ([audit result](SUBSTRATE_EXTENSION_CONSTRUCTION_REGISTRY_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [machine-readable output](../../../evaluation/reports/extension_construction_registry_v1.json)).

**Current-source replay:** after the v1.5 lock drifted in four files, a new v1.6 lock captures the current extension, orchestration sources, and native binary. Its amended independent analysis evaluates the returned Term tree natively and matches an external direct-DFT oracle for all 18 signal checks across six forward cases; six magnitude-only inverse requests are refused with a graph-resident request. The raw capture and analysis-only validator correction are both retained. This confirms the fixed recipe on current source, not substrate selection or invention of that recipe ([v1.6 results](SUBSTRATE_SPECTRAL_IO_ORACLE_V1_6_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_SPECTRAL_IO_ORACLE_V1_6_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_SPECTRAL_IO_ORACLE_V1_6_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/spectral_io_oracle_v1_6_raw_20261001.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/spectral_io_oracle_v1_6_analysis_20261001.json)).

## Exact inference scaling update (2026-09-29)

A fresh 54-worker binary-grid sweep varies known column separator q=1–6 and length m=32/128/512. Exact junction-tree outputs under min-fill and supplied column orders match an independent column-DP oracle in every worker. At fixed q, 16× length yields about 16–18× inference time. At m=512, q=1→6 raises min-fill-order inference 42.6 ms→6.02 s as node count rises 6×. Min-fill overestimates known width from q=5 upward (q=5→6; q=6→8), and its ordering cost reaches 8.48 s at q=6,m=512. This separates the inference envelope from decomposition cost, but does not show natural cheap cuts ([results](CAN_GRID_BOUNDARY_WIDTH_SCALE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260929.md), [protocol](CAN_GRID_BOUNDARY_WIDTH_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20260929.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_grid_boundary_width_scale_v1_2_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_grid_boundary_width_scale_v1_2_20260929_analysis.json)).

**Production gate relabelling:** 72 additional fresh workers randomize node IDs and edge insertion order on q×32 grids. The default bound-4 exact solver accepts all 48 q≤4 cases and refuses all 24 q=5/6 cases; all accepted results match the independent DP, refused calls return no state, and no graph mutation occurs. Width estimates remain stable across 12 relabellings per q (q5→6, q6→8). This is one regular topology family and does not measure natural graph distributions ([results](CAN_PRODUCTION_TREEWIDTH_GATE_RELABELLING_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_production_treewidth_gate_relabelling_v1_1_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_production_treewidth_gate_relabelling_v1_20260929_analysis.json)).

**Matched-width topology stress:** 144 relabelled N=120 graphs compare grids with path, shared-core, and random q-tree families. Min-fill returns exact width on every q-tree; on grids it overestimates at q=5 in 11/12 cases and q=6 in 12/12. Thus production's safe upper-bound gate can refuse certified tractable-width graphs based on topology, while a uniform grid-only result would miss the heuristic's behavior on other structures. Ordering time also varies across same-q families, confounded by their differing edge counts/degrees. Synthetic evidence only; natural graph structure remains unmeasured ([results](CAN_MATCHED_WIDTH_TOPOLOGY_ENVELOPE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_matched_width_topology_envelope_v1_1_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_matched_width_topology_envelope_v1_1_20260929_analysis.json)).

**Edge-matched topology control:** 72 additional cases pair each N=120 grid with a sparse partial q-tree having exactly the same edge count and certified width. Both are flagged non-chordal by an exploratory MCS check. Min-fill retains the prior pattern: 11/12 false refusals at q=5 and 12/12 at q=6 for grids, versus 0/12 for the sparse partial q-trees at either width. This rules out edge count and chordality alone as explanations in these fixtures, while degree profile and separator placement remain confounds ([results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_WIDTH_TOPOLOGY_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_width_topology_v1_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_width_topology_v1_20260929_analysis.json)).

**Paired exact-inference cost:** 36 fresh graph workers evaluate min-fill and supplied width-q elimination orders against an independent factor-table variable-elimination oracle; all outputs match. At q=4/5/6, grid min-fill JT medians are 56.6/107.5/192.2 ms versus 28.1/38.3/51.8 ms for edge-matched sparse partial q-trees (instrumented Python timings). Grids use more traced Python allocation. However, on q=5/6 grids the supplied narrower-maximum-bag order is slower than min-fill, showing maximum bag width alone does not explain runtime; v1.2 profiles now measure separators, bag sizes, child counts, and source-aligned work. The exact solver is pure Python after native graph extraction, so per-call traced allocations and whole-worker RSS are reported separately; a native JT allocator metric does not apply ([v1.2 results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_2_RESULTS_20260929.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_2_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_2_20260929_analysis.json)).

**Matched degree-profile control:** a corrected 54-worker study adds a hub-concentrated partial q-tree alongside grids and sparse partial q-trees, all at N=120 and matched E/q. Exact inference matches the independent oracle throughout. Median hub maximum degree is 119 versus 33.5–36.5 for the sparse q-tree; grid min-fill JT medians are 57/112/194 ms versus 30/39/54 ms for sparse q-trees. Hub message-entry counts rise at q=5/6, but its latency remains close to the sparse q-tree in these six-replicate cells. This shows degree maximum and total message entries alone do not predict latency across these fixtures. The first v1 harness mislabeled the hub generator as both q-tree families and is excluded from the degree claim ([v1.1 results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_DEGREE_PROFILE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [amendment](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_DEGREE_PROFILE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260929.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_degree_profile_v1_1.py), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_degree_profile_v1_1_20260929.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_degree_profile_v1_1_lock_20260929.json)).

**Runtime-derived exact inference scale:** 48 fresh workers settle native CAN rules on post-closure paths and bandwidth-2 graphs at N=32/64/128/256 in 2- and 3-state modes. All width-1/2 JT energies match an independent table-based VE oracle. JT time grows about 7.7–8.6× as N increases 8×; traced Python allocation is similarly near-linear. Native closure instead grows from 15–24 ms at N=32 to 8.8–21.5 s at N=256, with low single-digit/teen progress counts. The workloads are fixture-generated and do not estimate natural graph prevalence ([results](CAN_RUNTIME_GENERATED_EXACT_INFERENCE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [protocol](CAN_RUNTIME_GENERATED_EXACT_INFERENCE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20260929.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_runtime_generated_exact_inference_scale_v1.py), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_runtime_generated_exact_inference_scale_v1_20260929.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_runtime_generated_exact_inference_scale_v1_lock_20260929.json)).

**Runtime closure cost attribution:** 16 paired audit workers show that doubling N from 128 to 256 multiplies candidate pairs by 10.4–10.8× on two topology/mode comparisons. Across the panel, Hebbian reweight, Hebbian mint, and coactivation account for 99.96% of Term evaluations; audited and uninstrumented runs preserve progress and final state in all cells. This localizes the closure bottleneck for this CAN workload, not for other seeds or domains ([results](CAN_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [protocol](CAN_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_PREREG_20260929.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_runtime_closure_attribution_v1.py), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_runtime_closure_attribution_v1_20260929.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_runtime_closure_attribution_v1_lock_20260929.json)). Next evaluate a matcher/index optimization against parity, invalidation, and instrumentation-off timing gates.

**Runtime clause-order sensitivity (2026-09-29):** the two-rule CAN sparse-first intervention reproduces on held-out replicates 2/3: all eight parity/source gates pass, candidate work falls 26–66×, and instrumentation-off closure time falls 3.50–4.04×. A second authored family, the four-edge anaphora task, passes 293/293 checks with exact task and canonical graph parity; its 24 requested permutations realize eight installed orders with 8–16 candidate tests. The latter is audit-on and supports work sensitivity only, not a wall-time speedup. Evidence remains fixture- and family-specific; order discovery overhead and natural-task prevalence are open ([CAN held-out results](CAN_RUNTIME_MATCH_ORDER_SENSITIVITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [CAN held-out protocol](CAN_RUNTIME_MATCH_ORDER_SENSITIVITY_V1_1_HELDOUT_PREREG_20260929.md), [CAN raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_runtime_match_order_sensitivity_v1_1_20260929.json), [anaphora results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_JOIN_ORDER_V1_2_CURRENT_SOURCE_RESULTS_20260929.md), [anaphora protocol](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_JOIN_ORDER_V1_2_CURRENT_SOURCE_PREREG_20260929.md), [anaphora analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_derived_anaphora_join_order_v1_2_analysis_20260929.json), [anaphora raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_derived_anaphora_join_order_v1_2_workers_raw_20260929.json)). Next measure more task-derived rule families, including instrumentation-off parity and costed order selection.

**Full inference-profile replication:** a fresh 36-worker replay adds complete bag-size, separator-size, and child-count profiles for both orders. The profile's inner-loop proxy explains the measured timing direction on q=5/6 grids: supplied-order estimates exceed min-fill (14,564 vs 12,732 and 28,644 vs 22,284), and the supplied-order JT call is slower despite its lower maximum bag. `junction_tree_map` itself is pure Python after model extraction; Python traced allocation and whole-worker RSS have distinct scopes, with no native JT allocator claim ([v1.2 results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_2_RESULTS_20260929.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_2_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_2_20260929_analysis.json)).

## Source-backed engine access coverage update (2026-09-28)

The rule-engine inventory now has a mechanical crosswalk from every `TermNode` variant to its evaluator arm, recursive child-walk case, and access-collection classification. The crosswalk covers 237 variants; all have evaluator and child-walk entries. It identifies 36 unconditional wildcard readers, five finite-key readers, five conditional finite-or-wildcard readers, and 191 variants that reach the generic child-walk fallback. A depth-limited source scan finds 29 direct/transitive graph-reader helper paths; each detected path has an explicit access declaration. Three external dependency boundaries are identified: `ReadFile`, `UseTool`, and `ProgCallExtern`.

This improves the source linkage for the **Terms and value evaluation** and **Rule matching, effects, fixpoint** rows below, but does not close their access-fidelity evidence. The helper scan is lexical and depth-limited; a generic child walk does not prove purity, and the three external boundaries are not behaviorally audited by this crosswalk. The next inventory action is to review the 191 fallback evaluator arms and classify their graph, host, and dynamic-evaluation dependencies; then select runtime fixtures that exercise the unresolved classes. See the [crosswalk results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_ACCESS_DEPENDENCY_COVERAGE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [generator](../../../scripts/audit_term_access_dependency_coverage_v1.py), and [machine-readable inventory](../../../evaluation/reports/term_access_dependency_coverage_v1_20260928.json).

**Fallback helper triage:** a follow-up source scan resolves the 23 helper paths linked from 21 of the 191 generic-fallback variants. None contains the scan's direct graph-access markers; six recursively evaluate child Terms, six read the interner, and one reads the derived-operator registry. This leaves 170 fallback variants without a detected crate-qualified helper and does not prove the reviewed subset graph-pure or externally side-effect-free. See [triage results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_ACCESS_FALLBACK_HELPER_REVIEW_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/audit_term_access_fallback_helpers_v1.py), and [machine report](../../../evaluation/reports/term_access_fallback_helper_review_v1_20260928.json).

**External boundary classification:** source review distinguishes unrestricted `ReadFile` (`std::fs::read_to_string` with no restriction in the evaluator arm), dynamic `UseTool` (a process-global registered Python callback with possible effects/reentry), and `ProgCallExtern` (a caller-declared name gate over an in-engine Rust oracle whitelist; unsupported names trap, with no dynamic host callback in the current interpreter). Of the 170 no-helper fallback arms, lexical markers find no direct graph/trace/context access and identify `ReadFile` as the only filesystem/callback marker; 120 arms recursively evaluate child Terms. This is static, lexical evidence only. Dynamic file/tool behavior, all registered handlers, and complete child-walk fidelity remain open. See [boundary results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_BOUNDARIES_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [boundary runner](../../../scripts/audit_term_external_boundaries_v1.py), and [fallback scan](../../../evaluation/reports/term_access_fallback_unlinked_v1_20260928.json).

**External dependency runtime probe:** three source-locked workers confirm that `ReadFile` changes its output with file contents while its rule reports only `edge:trigger`, `reads_any=false`, and no file trace event. In the one-match `UseTool` fixture, the callback fires twice per settle for one graph progress step; both dispatches appear in the trace. A missing handler still produces two dispatch records but zero successful calls and the `Int(0)` fallback. This demonstrates hidden external dependency state for files and callback work beyond progress counts in this synthetic fixture; it does not measure containment or natural frequency. See [results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_term_external_dependency_probe_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/term_external_dependency_probe_v1_analysis_20260928.json).

**Callback cardinality extension:** at M=1/2/4 matched bindings, with the `UseTool` effect first or last, callback/fire/trace counts are exactly 2M in all three workers; the reported settle progress remains 1 and final graphs match by effect order. This supports a linear count for this specific rule family and shows progress is not a callback-work measure. Other Term positions and callback side effects are unmeasured; timing is descriptive ([results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/term_external_dependency_probe_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json)).

**Predicate and nested-Term callback behavior:** direct predicate callbacks run twice per rule; with two rules the total is four (two per RuleUID). A false operand before `UseTool` short-circuits it; a false operand after `UseTool` still produces one callback despite the rejected candidate. A stateful callback returns true then false during a single settle, yet the first result's marker remains in the graph. This narrows the concern to predicate purity/replay semantics, not just host-effect cost. The first analyzer attempt had an inverted assertion and its raw capture was overwritten; the custody lapse is disclosed, and only the corrected three-worker capture is evidence ([results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_2_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/term_external_dependency_probe_v1_2_analysis_20260928.json)).

**Shared-callback order and replay:** a locked two-rule probe shows that a stateful callback returning true only on its first invocation makes the first inserted rule win; reversing insertion order reverses the effect. Resetting only callback-local state after settle, with no graph mutation, leads to zero callbacks and zero progress on the next settle. This is a specific graph-only scheduling result and demonstrates that external state does not invalidate the settled rule in this path ([results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_3_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_TERM_EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY_PROBE_V1_3_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_term_external_dependency_probe_v1_3.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/term_external_dependency_probe_v1_3_analysis_20260928.json)).

**As of:** 2026-09-29. The machine-readable mirror is generated from this map and validated with `scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py --check`. The linked-test AST audit finds candidate callable-name matches in 17 rows; 15 have one or more matches inside assert-bearing Python test functions. A same-file helper traversal finds 186 candidate source-call sites lexically reachable from tests. Five rows have only generic names and four rows have no linked-Python source-call match, including planning/action selection; five native P2 unit tests separately pass. These counts do not establish executed coverage, assertion relevance, independent oracle use, or freshness; broader Rust test linkage remains unaudited ([test-relevance audit](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_TEST_RELEVANCE_AUDIT_20260928.md)). **Plan:** [`SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md`](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md). **Programme dependencies and workstream owners:** [`SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_DEPENDENCY_OWNER_CROSSWALK.md`](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_DEPENDENCY_OWNER_CROSSWALK.md).

**Live event/Goal producer boundary (pre-integration baseline):** the earlier three-process goal-only `converse` probe created an inbound instruction event and source-linked Goal but no lifecycle events; Phase 4A `DeliberationExecutionTrace.for_goal` remained a distinct trace. That goal-only route still has no lifecycle producer; the held self-report route's later bounded integration is recorded in the capability row below. See [baseline results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_ACTION_EVENT_IDENTITY_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_action_event_identity_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Repeated live Goal origin identity:** a preregistered three-process, two-turn full-seed probe confirms that same-recipient repeated instructions produce distinct Goals, Messages, and inbound events, with each Goal comprehending and originating in only its own turn's records. No selection/execution/result events or Goal transitions appear. This confirms ingress provenance under repetition, not downstream lifecycle identity. See [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_MULTI_GOAL_ORIGIN_IDENTITY_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_MULTI_GOAL_ORIGIN_IDENTITY_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_multi_goal_origin_identity_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Lifecycle producer source audit (pre-integration):** the static audit found that ingress `record_event` lacked Goal identity and native transition rules used event order alone. These findings motivated the one-route producer integration reported below; other production action routes remain unaudited. See [source audit](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_LIFECYCLE_PRODUCER_SOURCE_AUDIT_20260926.md).

**Live held-faculty lifecycle route (pre-integration baseline):** before the producer integration below, three stable full-seed runs created distinct, correctly origin-linked Goals and returned self-report responses, but emitted no lifecycle events. The baseline v1 capture with source drift is retained and excluded. See [baseline results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_LIFECYCLE_PRODUCER_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [baseline analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_report_lifecycle_producer_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Goal-bound live lifecycle producer:** two conversation routes emit identity-linked selection/execution/result events and native rules require `for_goal`, resolve/close successful Goals, block/close failed self-report faculty calls, and reject unlinked lifecycle events. Self-report passes three source-current runs and five semantic controls under v1.6; attached-scene `describe_scene` passes three source-current repeated-request runs under v1.2. Native event-level interleaving passes, including missing-owner and multi-owner selection rejection. Without serialization, controlled two-thread overlap reproduced `RuntimeError: Already mutably borrowed` and lost B's reply in three workers. A per-adapter `RLock` now serializes public `WorldAdapter` methods; source-locked v1.2 then passes three workers with acquisition observed, sequential distinct Goal lifecycles, both baseline replies, and no thread errors. The adapter now serializes all public function-method calls through its per-instance RLock. A separate three-worker converse/tick capture confirms `tick()` waits for `converse()` to finish, then both complete with preserved Goal lifecycle/reply and no thread errors. The shared native-call facade now covers direct calls through adapter-owned `inner`, `s`, and `s._inner`; a separate three-worker four-contender capture passes `tick()` plus direct node reads through all three handles. A second native facade around the same PyO3 object now receives the identical lock; a source-locked five-contender capture passes in three workers, and a registry cleanup probe confirms owner-count cleanup. Raw handles retained before wrapping, complete adapters sharing Python-side indexes, autonomous workers, and all other schedules remain unverified. See [shared-facade results](SUBSTRATE_SHARED_NATIVE_FACADE_SERIALIZATION_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [cross-entrypoint results](SUBSTRATE_ADAPTER_API_SERIALIZATION_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [direct-access results](SUBSTRATE_DIRECT_NATIVE_ACCESS_SERIALIZATION_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), and the [surface audit](SUBSTRATE_CONCURRENCY_SURFACE_AUDIT_20260926.md). A 20-pair sequential full-turn comparison measured a +18.1 ms median guarded-minus-no-op delta around 1.7 s/turn, but paired deltas ranged from −67.1 to +109.2 ms; treat lock overhead as inconclusive under this evolving single-adapter workload. See [overhead results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_LOCK_OVERHEAD_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [cross-entrypoint results](SUBSTRATE_ADAPTER_API_SERIALIZATION_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md). See [self-report results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_LIFECYCLE_IDENTITY_INTEGRATION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [describe-scene v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_LIFECYCLE_IDENTITY_V1_2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [native interleaving results](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_GOAL_LIFECYCLE_INTERLEAVING_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [threaded failure results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_THREADED_INTERLEAVING_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [serialized confirmation](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_THREADED_INTERLEAVING_V1_2_RESULTS_20260926.md).

**Second live action route identity correction:** the initial three-worker probe found that repeated attached-scene `describe_scene` requests reused one parent Goal and emitted no lifecycle events. V1.1 adds request-scoped Scene views, a rule-authored parent-to-inbound-Message edge, unique new-owner selection, and parent-only lifecycle while retaining per-percept children. Three source-stable workers pass repeated sequential identity, exact event ownership, terminal closure, and reply parity with the earlier baseline. Native event-level interleaving passes; same-adapter threaded overlap initially failed with a PyO3 node-read borrow error; the per-adapter public-method RLock now serializes adapter calls. V1.2 confirms both replies and Goal identities across three source-stable workers, while raw native handle bypass, full-adapter shared-state access, and autonomous-worker concurrency remain unverified; the paired full-turn overhead estimate is inconclusive under observed timing variance. See [source audit](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_LIFECYCLE_PRODUCER_SOURCE_AUDIT_20260926.md), [v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_LIFECYCLE_IDENTITY_V1_2_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [threaded failure result](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_THREADED_INTERLEAVING_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [serialized confirmation](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_THREADED_INTERLEAVING_V1_2_RESULTS_20260926.md).

**Independent same-addressee task-identity replication:** the common-ground stress fixture reproduces cross-Goal lifecycle assignment under both temporal-only and addressee-only rules; explicit fixture links pass the two-sequence oracle. CommonGround and fixed-point gates remain intact. This synthetic operation sequence still has no production identity producer; the self-report route integration does not generalize to it. See [results](SUBSTRATE_COMMON_GROUND_TASK_IDENTITY_REPLICATION_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_common_ground_task_identity_replication_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Production Goal identity evidence:** the Phase 4A live-manifold materializer and native execution rule emit task-specific `for_goal` traces; full/scoped normalized task projections match at N=2/4/8. Scoped closure is slower and performs more matching (2.97× wall and 6.11× candidates at N=8). A locked follow-up across N=2/4/8 (nine pairs) shows typed-edge graph isomorphism after mapping by node type and attributes, despite differing raw IDs. Natural frame locality is not established. See [identity results](SUBSTRATE_PHASE4A_PRODUCED_GOAL_IDENTITY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [graph-isomorphism results](SUBSTRATE_PHASE4A_GOAL_IDENTITY_GRAPH_ISOMORPHISM_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_phase4a_goal_identity_graph_isomorphism_v1_analysis_20260926.json), and [raw graph capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_phase4a_goal_identity_graph_isomorphism_v1_20260926.json).

**Goal identity and owner scheduling scale:** three fresh replicates at N=16/32/64 pass task, exact checkpoint parity, notification, omission, and recovery gates. Operation counts reproduce near-quadratic growth for this generated fixture. The caller owner queue remains slower than full closure (1.73× median at N=64); identity links are supplied by the harness, so production integration and natural frame locality remain unmeasured. Current RSS deltas are descriptive, not allocation attribution. See [v4.2 results](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_GOAL_IDENTITY_REPLICATED_SCALE_V4_2_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_goal_identity_replicated_scale_v4_2_analysis_20260926.json).

**Latest scoped scheduling evidence:** a preregistered two-frame lifecycle probe verifies exact A/B owner notifications and full-closure projection parity for one rule-authored node birth and deletion cycle; it also confirms that an external intervention still requires the caller to seed its affected frame. See [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NODE_LIFECYCLE_PREREG_20260926.md), [result](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NODE_LIFECYCLE_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_node_lifecycle_probe_v1_20260926_analysis.json). This result is bounded to the fixture and does not establish scheduler completeness or natural-task frame locality.

**Latest scoped setup-cost evidence:** same-build snapshot filter/clone/allocation/drop timing is 86–95% of end-to-end scoped no-match calls across the measured synthetic matrix. Snapshot cost tracks much of the per-rule effect-payload increment. A source audit found `Substrate::rules` is publicly mutated by callers, so retained clone-cache invalidation is unsafe without encapsulating those paths. The next optimization probe uses borrow-based rule references instead. See [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_SNAPSHOT_CLONE_COST_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_SNAPSHOT_CLONE_COST_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_snapshot_clone_cost_v1_20260926_analysis.json).

The follow-up borrow-based implementation removes per-call rule-body cloning without a retained cache. Audit-off no-op slopes fall about 8×, while topology candidate counts stay unchanged and event/full work gaps persist. All no-op, topology, and per-rule parity gates pass, including tombstoned-slot attribution. See [borrowed-path results](SUBSTRATE_BORROWED_SCOPED_RULES_RESULTS_20260926.md) and the three v2 independent analyses linked there. This is an engine setup improvement, not evidence that event scheduling or natural frame partitions are tractable.

Matched chain/tree/star propagation probes through 64 frames pass event/full parity and exact owner notifications. They expose a strong scheduler-work gap: at N=64, event/full candidate ratios are 1.88× for the chain, 17.79× for the binary tree, and 68.61× for the star. Native audit counters and source indicate repeated scoped rule matching rather than tree/star depth exploitation. See [protocol](SUBSTRATE_FRAME_TOPOLOGY_MATCHED_SCALE_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_FRAME_TOPOLOGY_MATCHED_SCALE_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_frame_topology_matched_scale_v1_20260926_analysis.json). This evidence narrows the engine optimization target; it does not show that learned task frames have these shapes.

The v1 per-rule attribution run exposed missing scoped audit context; v1.1 adds original-slot keyed context and passes additive counter conservation. Per-rule results attribute most tree/star overhead to repeated propagation-rule matching, reaching a 127.5× event/full candidate ratio for star propagation-on at N=64. See [result and v1.0 failure disclosure](SUBSTRATE_FRAME_TOPOLOGY_COST_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_FRAME_TOPOLOGY_COST_ATTRIBUTION_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), and [v1.1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_frame_topology_cost_attribution_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json). Follow-up audit-on/off semantic parity passes 135/135 checks, and a no-match setup sweep finds 180–210 ns per active rule through 512 rules; see [audit parity](SUBSTRATE_SCOPE_AUDIT_ON_OFF_PARITY_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [setup-cost results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NOOP_SETUP_COST_RESULTS_20260926.md).
