Snapshot attribution finds no stable cost from equal tombstoned slots ([results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_RULE_SNAPSHOT_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS_20260926.md)); a factorial probe finds both effect payload and match-pattern size increase end-to-end scoped cost, with effect count the larger signal, but neither isolates cloning ([factorial results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_RULE_BODY_FACTORIAL_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_rule_body_factorial_v1_20260926_analysis.json)). These synthetic no-match and instrumented timing results do not establish natural-task tractability.

The ownership-seeded local-candidate path now reduces N=64 event/full candidate work to 0.080× on chains and 0.809× on binary trees, while star fanout rises to 15.89×. Corresponding audit-instrumented wall ratios are 0.357×, 3.077×, and 18.53×. All 606 amended correctness/provenance gates pass. The path is limited to single-edge, distinct-endpoint-variable rules before any graph edge deletion; other cases retain full matching. This narrows the optimization target to scheduler turns, hub notification fanout, and requeues, and is not evidence of natural-frame locality ([results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_LOCAL_CANDIDATES_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol amendment](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_LOCAL_CANDIDATE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_frame_topology_local_candidates_v3_1_20260926_analysis.json)).

The follow-up per-turn attribution passes 666/666 gates and finds zero duplicate notification suppressions, but one self-requeue per frame per transition followed by a no-progress turn. At N=64 star, peak queue depth is 64; the root accounts for 6,337 of 14,123 candidates and 48,329 of 106,927 Term evaluations. The next experiment tests self-requeue suppression against parity and omission controls; the separate root cost motivates high-degree adjacency batching ([results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NOTIFICATION_REQUEUE_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NOTIFICATION_REQUEUE_ATTRIBUTION_PREREG_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_notification_requeue_attribution_v1_20260926_analysis.json)).

A runner-only guard suppresses self-enqueue only when the owner Microtheory and direct `has_concept` set are unchanged across the scoped call. Five-repeat chain/tree/star runs match frozen baseline and full closure; an ownership-change control retains the notification, processes the new member, and passes parity. At N=64 candidates fall 29.3%/27.4%/5.4% and audit-on wall ratios are 0.619/0.621/0.760. A delayed-writer adversary also passes A→B→A after B notifies previously settled A. This remains synthetic evidence pending deletion/reuse, dynamic-boundary, deferred-effect, and feedback controls. A call-site audit finds no production `run_rules_scoped_delta_report` consumer or production cross-frame queue, so no current engine integration seam exists ([results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_SELF_REQUEUE_SUPPRESSION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [call-site audit](SUBSTRATE_SELF_REQUEUE_CALLSITE_AUDIT_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_SELF_REQUEUE_SUPPRESSION_PREREG_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_self_requeue_suppression_v1_20260926_analysis.json)).

The follow-up three-frame dependency probe passes full-closure parity for a node with two cross-frame dependencies, including exact A/B/C notification on birth and deletion. Its initial symmetric-binding pilot failed parity and remains preserved alongside the v1.1 role-guarded confirmation. See [result and failure disclosure](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_MULTI_FRAME_DEPENDENCY_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_multi_frame_dependency_probe_v1_20260926_analysis.json). This still does not establish arbitrary-degree notification or general scheduler completeness.

The five-repeat star-fanout scale probe extends owner-notification parity through 64 frames and passes every semantic/termination gate. Event queue cost rises faster than full closure in this synthetic topology: 13.05× slower at 64 frames, with 256 scoped report turns versus five full-closure progress steps. See [scale result](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NOTIFICATION_FANOUT_SCALE_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NOTIFICATION_FANOUT_SCALE_PREREG_20260926.md), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_notification_fanout_scale_v1_20260926_analysis.json). This is evidence against assuming a scheduler speedup from notification completeness alone.

**Scoped scheduler attribution (2026-09-26):** the preregistered chain/tree/star requeue capture passes all source/build, semantic, and raw-counter gates over 8–64 frames. At 64 frames, each topology has 256 median owner turns, 128 no-progress turns, 128 self-requeues, and zero duplicate suppressions; the star has 14,123 candidate tests and 106,927 term evaluations versus 2,591 and 18,515 for the chain. The synthetic attribution points to repeat owner-turn work and high fanout, not duplicate queue suppression. It does not establish natural task topology or batching safety. See [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NOTIFICATION_REQUEUE_ATTRIBUTION_PREREG_20260926.md), [result](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NOTIFICATION_REQUEUE_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_notification_requeue_attribution_v1_20260926_analysis.json). A semantics-preserving scheduling intervention remains unproven.

**Guarded self-requeue intervention (2026-09-26):** an opt-in harness policy suppresses the current owner’s enqueue only when its `has_concept` set is unchanged around local closure. All 60 paired cases pass full-projection and notification parity; a rule-authored membership insertion retains the self-requeue and processes the new member. At 64 frames, median event turns halve across chain/tree/star, while candidate-work reductions range from 5% (star) to 29% (chain). This experiment does not change default scheduling or establish natural workload gains. See [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_SELF_REQUEUE_SUPPRESSION_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_SELF_REQUEUE_SUPPRESSION_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_self_requeue_suppression_v1_20260926_analysis.json). Next check whether any real scoped caller has the same owner-saturation and ownership-invalidation contract before considering opt-in integration.

**Boundary-index measurement update (2026-09-26):** the Bigville dynamic-link fixture now has direct timings for live owner discovery, edge extraction, boundary-index construction, and incremental index update. At 100 five-Concept frames each measured stage is sub-millisecond; edge extraction plus construction is ~0.63 ms at baseline and ~0.93–0.95 ms with 100 links. Graph build plus initial partition is ~55–56 ms. All nine workers pass runtime owner/edge and incremental/full-rebuild checks. This is a 500-Concept single-fixture microbenchmark; it does not establish scaling, learned partition costs, natural-task cheap cuts, or production scheduler costs. See [results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_BOUNDARY_INDEX_COST_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_boundary_index_cost_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_boundary_index_cost_v1a_analysis_20260926.json).

**Bigville construction/partition scale (2026-09-26):** at 100/250/500 repeated five-Concept frames (500/1,250/2,500 Concepts), resident graph assembly medians are 5.40/12.80/24.61 ms and composed live partition passes are 0.969/2.471/5.140 ms. Twelve independent integrity gates pass across nine fresh workers. The repeated disconnected frame template limits interpretation; this establishes no natural-task locality or general scaling law. See [results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_PARTITION_SCALE_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_partition_scale_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_partition_scale_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Bigville runtime access census (2026-09-26, diagnostic):** three fresh-process arms on the previously measured 100-resident cast preserve identical graph state for full closure and caller-notified one-frame closure; the latter reduces candidate pairs 97.1% and Term evaluations 96.0% on one nonnoop mutation. Exact per-frame treewidth is 2. Single-run settlement is 3.81 ms vs 0.199 ms, but graph/partition setup is 52.7–55.0 ms; the observed 1.65 ms setup-plus-settlement difference is smaller than the 1.96 ms setup-time difference between arms, so total-cost benefit is unresolved. The partial native trace records 596 reads and five writes in the target frame; static `reads_any=true` remains, so absent cross-frame events do not certify locality. The trigger was selected after the first target was a no-op; this is diagnostic, not confirmatory or representative-distribution evidence. See [results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_TASK_ACCESS_CENSUS_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_TASK_ACCESS_CENSUS_PREREG_20260926.md), [raw record](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_task_access_census_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_task_access_census_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Conversation connected read-only boundary (2026-09-26):** a corrected 24-worker connected-chain probe keeps candidate state frame-local and shares immutable evidence read by the scoring rule. Full/scoped outcomes and projections match in all 12 pairs, but at eight tasks the generic scoped route is 7.67× the full median wall time, with 13.56× the candidate pairs and 10.91× the Term evaluations. This is one authored rule family; shared-evidence mutation notifications and broader topology remain untested. The earlier shared-mutable-candidate probe remains a separate semantic negative control. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_READONLY_BOUNDARY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_readonly_boundary_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_readonly_boundary_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Incremental mixed-dialogue task graph census (2026-09-26):** a new three-process trace of the ten-event conversational fixture yields three event/goal frames with sizes 5/1/6. Two `follows_event` edges cross frame boundaries; five shared nodes create 40 boundary incidences and a triangle interaction graph (exact width 2). Full closure after each event matches the task/common-ground oracle and fresh-process graph digests; total observed work is 135 candidate pairs and 558 Term evaluations. The partial trace sees 193 events but no direct cross-frame peer, despite the explicitly shared DiscourseState boundary. This is one authored task, not a representative distribution or partitioned-execution result. See [results](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_CONNECTED_TASK_CENSUS_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_CONNECTED_TASK_CENSUS_PREREG_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_connected_task_census_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_connected_task_census_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Live self-report boundary projection (2026-09-27, exploratory):** three runtime captures independently reproduce two task-owned projections (8 and 3 nodes), with induced-width upper bounds 2 and 1, but their typed boundaries have 36 and 18 incidences. Exact matcher attribution also repeats across selection/execution/result (8,031/8,002/7,998 tests each; zero unattributed). The strict source gate fails because `world_adapter.py` changed after capture, so this is source-drift-limited. The ownership is protocol-declared rather than learned; induced width covers only owned nodes and does not certify full inference or matcher locality. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_TASK_BOUNDARY_V2_RESULTS_20260927.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_report_task_boundary_v2_20260927.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_report_task_boundary_v2_analysis_20260927.json).

**Live self-report boundary replication (2026-09-27, source-stable):** v2.1 passes 51/51 gates under a separate lock and exactly reproduces the v2 graph and projection metrics. This confirms repeatability for the one authored fixture. The 8-node/36-incidence boundary and 3-node/18-incidence boundary are measured in full; the corresponding owned-width upper bounds are 2 and 1. The cuts remain protocol-defined, not learned, and do not bound the full dependency graph. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_TASK_BOUNDARY_V2_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_report_task_boundary_v2_1_20260927.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_report_task_boundary_v2_1_analysis_20260927.json).

**Vision `st_settle` cross-frame scale (2026-09-27, source-stable):** v1.1 passes 9/9 driver and 15/15 analyzer gates across 3–100 SUnits, with three seeds and workers. The current rule creates a complete activation dependency graph; at 100 units the exact global-fold counter is 20,100 `NodesOfType` items and 9,000 directed dependencies cross the imposed row-frame partition. Global and root-scoped single-sheet variants preserve one-sweep outputs, but root scoping leaves the within-sheet global factor intact. This is a task-family counterexample, not a general prevalence result; `Neighbours` traversal items are not fully counted. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SETTLE_SINGLE_SWEEP_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_settle_single_sweep_scale_v1_1_20260927.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_settle_single_sweep_scale_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json).

**Vision `st_settle` traced traversal replication (2026-09-27, source-stable):** v1.4 adds concrete typed-edge access counts and passes 10/10 driver plus 16/16 independent checks. At 100 SUnits the global arm reads 20,000 SUnit type-index items; the root-scoped arm instead traverses 20,000 `has_sunit` edges. Both traverse 1,368 local-neighbor edges, have 9,000 cross-row global-factor pairs, and return identical one-sweep outputs. This closes the earlier `Neighbours` item-count gap for the measured operation, showing equal quadratic population cardinality via different access paths rather than a root-scoping work reduction. It remains a one-rule, one-sheet fixture capped at 100 units. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SETTLE_SINGLE_SWEEP_SCALE_V1_4_RESULTS_20260927.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_settle_single_sweep_scale_v1_4_20260927.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_settle_single_sweep_scale_v1_4_analysis_20260927.json).

**Vision K=16 scope quality replication (2026-09-27):** three source-stable workers show exact global/root-scoped tuning and OSI parity after calibrated development/adaptation, with mean OSIs 0.225422/0.279704/0.378932 by seed. This supports scope invariance on three 4×4 single-sheet fixtures, not general visual competence. The preregistered within-arm readout weight-drift audit was omitted, so the complete preregistered gate is not passed; this limitation is explicit in the results and plan. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_K16_SCOPE_MULTISEED_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_k16_scope_multiseed_v1_1_20260927.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_k16_scope_multiseed_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json).

**Vision K=16 scope quality replication v1.2 (2026-09-27):** the full source-stable gate adds direct pre/post readout weight vectors. All six arms show zero weight drift; independent hashes reconcile, and the 16×12 tuning matrices and OSIs match exactly between global and root-scoped variants in all three seeds. This closes the registered measurement gap for a calibrated 4×4 single-sheet fixture, not general visual capability or speed. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_K16_SCOPE_MULTISEED_V1_2_RESULTS_20260927.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_k16_scope_multiseed_v1_2_20260927.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_k16_scope_multiseed_v1_2_analysis_20260927.json).

**Controlled nonzero-boundary exact inference (2026-09-26):** a source-locked 24-case Bigville experiment adds cross-resident factors with explicit boundaries k=0/2/4/6. BFS discovers interior components; conditional exhaustive MAP agrees with whole-graph junction-tree MAP throughout. At N=25,k=6 the direct conditional oracle checks 3,582,306 assignments and takes a 6.02 s median, while the global width-2 solver takes 7–9 ms. This validates conditional exactness under supplied separators and quantifies boundary exponential work; it does not show a partitioned speedup, native message passing, or natural cut prevalence. See [results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_CROSS_BOUNDARY_MAP_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_CROSS_BOUNDARY_MAP_PREREG_20260926.md), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_cross_boundary_map_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Exact boundary-message reuse (2026-09-26):** component messages conditioned on their local boundary interfaces preserve whole-model MAP across a second source-locked 24-case sweep. At N=25,k=6, counted assignments drop 650× (3.58M to 5,508; largest local interface size one), while message build plus boundary search is 22.28 ms versus 7.26 ms for global width-2 JT. The direct oracle's repeated-interior explosion is therefore avoidable on this structure, but no runtime win is shown; global boundary enumeration and Python overhead remain. This is an offline algorithmic control, not native frame execution or natural boundary evidence. See [results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_BOUNDARY_MESSAGE_REUSE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_BOUNDARY_MESSAGE_REUSE_PREREG_20260926.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_boundary_message_reuse_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Induced-boundary variable elimination (2026-09-26):** across 42 source-locked cases with k up to 12, min-sum elimination over the boundary factors exactly matches global JT. The controlled matching topology keeps boundary width at 1: at k=12, elimination processes 72 rows versus 531,441 exhaustive assignments. Message construction plus elimination still trails global width-2 JT in median time at N=25 (8.63 vs 7.61 ms), despite far lower combinatorial work than the direct conditional oracle. This supports a cost bound parameterized by induced width for this pattern, not a general boundary-locality or speed claim. See [results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_BOUNDARY_VARIABLE_ELIMINATION_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_BOUNDARY_VARIABLE_ELIMINATION_PREREG_20260926.md), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_boundary_variable_elimination_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Conversation boundary mutation/wakeup (2026-09-26):** after a shared read-only evidence value changes, notifying both dependent frame scopes matches full closure in all nine cases; omitting one causes a stale projection in all nine, and full catch-up recovers exact parity. At eight frames, required scoped work takes 0.704 ms versus 0.220 ms for full closure. This verifies only caller-supplied notification on one authored chain edge; there is no native delivery or general mutation-coverage claim. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_BOUNDARY_WAKEUP_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_boundary_wakeup_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_boundary_wakeup_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Conversation simultaneous-boundary owner mapping (2026-09-26):** a Python-side live reader-to-owner walk maps one to three changed shared evidence nodes to the expected frame sets in all 27 workers. Mapped scoped closure matches full closure in all nine pairs; omitting a dependent owner is detected and recoverable. At eight frames, mapping is 49 µs, five-frame reopening 126 µs, and scoped closure 1.159 ms versus 0.256 ms full. This checks one explicit relation and caller schedule only; general static/dynamic dependency extraction and native delivery remain unmeasured. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_MULTI_BOUNDARY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_multi_boundary_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_multi_boundary_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Native shared-write owner report (2026-09-26):** the scoped graph-delta observer reports both consumer Microtheories and the writer controller when a native conversation rule writes their shared evidence node. The reported write targets cover the exact snapshot delta; scheduling both consumers matches full closure in all nine pairs, and an omitted consumer is detected/recoverable. This is one native `SetAttr` path; the returned API flag remains `frame_notifications_complete=false`, so notification delivery is still caller-side. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_NATIVE_WRITE_OWNER_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_native_write_owner_v1_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_native_write_owner_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

The scoped owner report now also matches the full snapshot oracle across shared edge insertion/deletion, node attribute write, owned-node birth, and owned-node deletion (60 fresh workers; 10/10 independent gates). Owner endpoint coverage and unrelated-frame omission hold at 2/4/8 consumer frames. This remains a synthetic direct-effect matrix; deferred effects are out of scope, callers still schedule notifications, and `frame_notifications_complete` remains false ([protocol](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_SCOPED_OWNER_EFFECT_MATRIX_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_SCOPED_OWNER_EFFECT_MATRIX_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_native_scoped_owner_effect_matrix_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

For one deferred `TrackIdentity` effect, the scoped report surfaces `deferred_effects_pending` and its one-step budget exit; a scoped-only control has no identity assignments despite marking the writer done. A caller fallback to global closure matches ordinary full-closure identities in 6/6 pairs and reaches a zero-work terminal state (18 fresh workers; 12/12 gates). This supports a conditional fallback policy on this fixture only. Fallback cost and other deferred queues remain unmeasured ([protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_DEFERRED_FALLBACK_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_DEFERRED_FALLBACK_RESULTS_20260926.md), [v1.2 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_deferred_fallback_v1_2_analysis_20260926.json)).

A separate 120-worker cost sweep measures the same TrackIdentity fallback against ordinary global closure at 2–32 frame owners. State parity holds in every pair; fallback median settlement is 0.100–0.109 ms versus 0.082–0.089 ms full (median paired ratio 1.18–1.24×), with broad paired ranges that include parity at some sizes. This is a noisy sub-millisecond envelope for one fixture and does not establish general affordability ([cost protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_DEFERRED_FALLBACK_COST_PREREG_20260926.md), [cost results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_DEFERRED_FALLBACK_COST_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_deferred_fallback_cost_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A second queue-family probe covers deferred `NetCollapse`, which writes numerical `WeightTensor.data` and `ReflexNet.surprise`. Scoped settlement exposes pending/budget flags, the scoped-only control leaves consumed work without the queued weight update, and global fallback matches ordinary full state in 6/6 pairs (18 workers; 10/10 gates). This is a synthetic one-unit ReLU net mechanism result, not learning efficacy or general queue coverage ([protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NET_COLLAPSE_FALLBACK_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_NET_COLLAPSE_FALLBACK_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_net_collapse_fallback_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A third family exercises deferred graph-native `RulePatch`: fallback commits a CAS-checked rule edit and durable receipt, advances the rule revision, then reaches all dependent frame outputs. Scoped-only remains pending at the old revision; fallback matches full closure in 6/6 pairs (18 workers; 10/10 gates). This is one successful `where` patch and does not cover refusals, conflicting patches, or automatic notifications ([protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_RULE_PATCH_FALLBACK_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_RULE_PATCH_FALLBACK_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_rule_patch_fallback_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

Follow-up CAS controls cover a stale-at-submission patch and two patches racing on one revision/hash. Under global fallback, the stale patch is refused with no target/output change; in the same-revision pair, the first inserted patch commits and the second is refused stale, after which the new rule completes all frames. Full/fallback parity holds for 12/12 pairs (36 workers; 10/10 gates). The insertion-order observation is limited to this single-threaded two-patch fixture; it is not a concurrent-write or larger-queue guarantee ([protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_RULE_PATCH_CONFLICT_FALLBACK_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_RULE_PATCH_CONFLICT_FALLBACK_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scoped_rule_patch_conflict_fallback_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A mixed-queue probe puts RulePatch, TrackIdentity, and NetCollapse behind one scoped firing. One global fallback commits the patch, assigns all identities, updates the network's weight tensors/surprise, and reaches all frame outputs; its complete state matches ordinary full closure in 6/6 pairs (18 workers; 9/9 gates). The scoped-only control shows the initiating observation consumed while all three deferred outcomes remain unapplied. This is one fixed small graph and one writer firing; arbitrary orderings, multiple writers, other queue families, and cost remain open ([protocol](SUBSTRATE_MIXED_DEFERRED_QUEUE_FALLBACK_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_MIXED_DEFERRED_QUEUE_FALLBACK_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mixed_deferred_queue_fallback_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

The row-level JSON mirror is [`evaluation/substrate_capability/capability_map.json`](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/capability_map.json), generated from this table by [`scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py`](../../../scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py). Run `python3 scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py` after editing the table, and `python3 scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py --check` to detect a stale mirror. Validation checks row parity, source-path resolution, evidence-link existence, and required evidence/claim-boundary fields. It does not certify that a route is the default in every application or establish that historical results match the current checkout; see each row's activation and provenance limits.

The JSON mirror preserves `evidence_refs` and also classifies them into `test_refs`, `result_refs`, `protocol_refs`, `raw_record_refs`, `runner_refs`, and `supporting_refs`. Runner/analyzer links are followed from each cited result document and retain a `discovered_from` pointer, so the result-to-code path is inspectable. `test_mapping_status` distinguishes linked test files from rows with no test link; `linked_tests_present; coverage not audited` is deliberately not a coverage claim. Link categories are inferred from repository paths/names and should be reviewed if an artifact uses an unusual location.

## Evidence labels

- **Implemented:** source path exists; capability efficacy is unmeasured here.
- **Mechanism tested:** focused tests or a local result exercise the named mechanism.
- **Workload demonstrated:** an end-to-end result exists on a named workload.
- **Envelope measured:** size/condition sweep reports quality and resource scaling.
- **Replicated / generalisation:** reserved for frozen confirmatory replication or held-out task-family evidence. No row is assigned these grades solely from this inventory.

Evidence status follows the cited artifact’s actual scope. A preregistration is a plan, not a result. A result document is not assumed to match the current checkout until its source/build provenance is checked.

## Initial map

| Capability area | Main implementation surface | Ownership / path shape | Evidence found | Current envelope and open measurement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Production visual image ingestion and frame-scoped settling | `domains/wave_world.py::step`, `domains/wave_world.py::drive`, `seeds/vision_change.json`, `seeds/vision_settle.json`, `domains/complex_cells.py` | `WaveWorld.step(frame=...)` writes image luminance facts, then runs the graph's change, wave, settling and response rules. The two-sheet experiment supplies `Retina` ownership roots and steps target then sibling sequentially. | **Production-adapter workload demonstrated on three frozen adapted checkpoints.** All image pixels were delivered exactly across 256 calls. Across 24 comparisons root-scoped settlement preserved sibling state exactly while global settlement transferred state in all 24. Four-way dominant orientation varied across seeds and is descriptive, not accuracy ([v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_PRODUCTION_INGRESS_SCOPE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_production_ingress_scope_v1_1_corrected_analysis_20260929.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_production_ingress_scope_v1_1_20260929_raw.json)). A separate 288-cell explicit post-settlement `oo_sunit_rate` intervention across six adapted checkpoints and eight BSDS images passes all seven integrity gates with zero equation residual; root-scoped rates favor the 180° permutation in 45/48 pairs, while global rates do so in 0/48. This is a diagnostic readout intervention, not production scheduler behavior or semantic recognition ([v1.3.1 results](SUBSTRATE_NATURAL_IMAGE_RATE_REFRESH_INTERVENTION_V1_3_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/natural_image_rate_refresh_intervention_v1_3_1_20260929_analysis.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/natural_image_rate_refresh_intervention_v1_3_1_20260929_raw.json)). A held-transform 90° follow-up on the same six adapted states passes all seven gates; root-scoped maps favor the expected permutation in 47/48 pairs and global maps in 0/48. This is transform generalization within those states, not independent checkpoint or semantic-task generalization ([v1.4 results](SUBSTRATE_NATURAL_IMAGE_RATE_REFRESH_INTERVENTION_V1_4_RESULTS_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/natural_image_rate_refresh_intervention_v1_4_20260929_analysis.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/natural_image_rate_refresh_intervention_v1_4_20260929_raw.json)). Focused functional tests include [test_vision_waves_migrated.py](../../../tests/test_vision_waves_migrated.py) and [test_vision_change_migrated.py](../../../tests/test_vision_change_migrated.py); these cover vision rule mechanisms, not production frame-scoped ingress or natural-image accuracy. A source-locked six-seed dense-map morphology contrast acquires all nine trained sites for square and cross patches, then scores 288/288 versus 33/288 on held-out nearest-region classification; square wins in all six pairs. Both score 0/288 for exact held-out coordinate localization. This is an authored visual-map transfer result, not semantic image recognition ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_DENSE_MAP_MORPHOLOGY_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_phase0_dense_map_morphology_transfer_v1_20261005.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_phase0_dense_map_morphology_transfer_v1_analysis_20261005.json)).  A source-locked position-held-out orientation assay uses centered five-pixel horizontal/vertical bars at matched positions, with 12 train positions and four excluded corners. An external nearest-class-mean probe scores 31/48 after unsupervised learning versus 29/48 from the initial projection; three seeds improve, one declines, and two tie. Centroid/count controls score 24/48 and an exact translation-aware pixel template scores 48/48. A seed-level uncertainty analysis gives a two-sided sign-test p=0.625 and six-seed bootstrap interval −4.2 to +10.4 percentage points, leaving the observed 31/48 versus 29/48 difference unresolved.  A graph-native supervised association/readout has since been run on the same locked panel. It produces one native prediction for every probe and its learned association counts reconstruct from native training activations, but accuracy is 30/48 versus 31/48 for the external readout and 29/48 for the initial projection; predictions favor horizontal (20/24) over vertical (10/24). This demonstrates guided graph-resident association, not a held-out accuracy gain or independent rule invention ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_ORIENTATION_NATIVE_ASSOCIATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_ORIENTATION_NATIVE_ASSOCIATION_V1_1_PREREG_20261005.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_orientation_native_association_v1_1_20261005.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_orientation_native_association_v1_1_analysis_20261005.json)).The representation evidence is weak and does not establish invariant visual learning ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_POSITION_HELDOUT_ORIENTATION_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [uncertainty](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_position_heldout_orientation_v1_1_uncertainty_20261005.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_POSITION_HELDOUT_ORIENTATION_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_position_heldout_orientation_v1_20261005.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_position_heldout_orientation_v1_analysis_20261005.json)).A source-locked live corruption replay then completes across 12 workers: weights, clean readout, schedule, and deterministic masks reconcile. Native activations differ from weight-sum reconstruction by at most 1.11e-16; seven winner selections are one ULP below reconstructed argmax, so the original strict exact-max gate fails on 7/2,880 corruption probes (2,873/2,880 match exactly); a source-locked v1.4 post-hoc reconstruction puts all native winners within one ULP. Native single-winner accuracy differs from offline set-valued tie scoring on 32/2,880 probes. A source-locked v1.5 rule-oracle analysis confirms that all 2,880 live winners are exact maxima of captured native activations, and all 115 native activation ties select the first maximum in WUnit order. Thus seven one-ULP mismatches occur only when reconstructing activations from weights offline; they do not disagree with the live activation argmax. The post-hoc oracle remains exploratory because it was specified after observing the capture ([live results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_CORRUPTION_LIVE_REPLAY_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_CORRUPTION_LIVE_REPLAY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261005.md), [post-hoc protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_CORRUPTION_LIVE_REPLAY_V1_4_POSTHOC_ANALYSIS_PROTOCOL_20261005.md), [post-hoc analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_corruption_live_replay_v1_4_analysis_20261005.json), [rule-oracle protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_CORRUPTION_LIVE_REPLAY_V1_5_POSTHOC_ANALYSIS_PROTOCOL_20261005.md), [rule-oracle analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_corruption_live_replay_v1_5_analysis_20261005.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_corruption_live_replay_v1_20261005.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_eye_corruption_live_replay_v1_analysis_20261005.json)). | One synthetic grating family, three existing checkpoints, sequential inputs, and protocol-supplied roots. Median worker wall/CPU time is 0.390 s global versus 0.309 s scoped for eight calls; peak RSS is about 131 MiB. Exact internal rate-write phase and closure termination are not exposed by the adapter; frame-discovery cost, semantic natural-image accuracy, and learned cheap-cut prevalence remain unmeasured; the post-settlement intervention result is specific to a diagnostic rate readout and one 180° transform. Next prove a phase-local instrument equivalent to default `WaveWorld.step`, then measure independent image families and charge frame discovery separately. Replication under another grid/patch scale and explicit coordinate interpolation controls remain open. |

Six balanced timing pairs have median ratio 0.917 but a wide 0.856–1.095 range; the raw coordinator's stale three-pair aggregate predicate is false despite all six worker gates passing, independently checked ([`SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_ALL_TRANSITIONS_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_ALL_TRANSITIONS_RESULTS.md), [paired analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_problem_shape_all_transitions_paired_timing_v1_1_analysis_20260925.json)). Directed cross-frame wakeup is now measured on a controlled 12-frame CAN fixture: event scheduling matches full rescan and zeroes an independent residual for chain and both star orientations; omission controls retain residuals. The sensitivity-insensitive v1 attempt is preserved alongside the amended v1.1 result ([`SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_DIRECTED_FRAME_WAKEUP_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_DIRECTED_FRAME_WAKEUP_RESULTS.md), [v1.1 raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_directed_frame_wakeup_v1_1_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_directed_frame_wakeup_v1_1_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/bigville_directed_frame_wakeup_v1_1_lock_20260925.json)). Repeated directed boundary mutation (three cycles of delete/insert/weight replacement) matches full rescans at all 12 checkpoints, with zero independently recomputed residual; progress applications match while event scheduling uses fewer frame turns ([`SUBSTRATE_DIRECTED_BOUNDARY_REPLAY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_DIRECTED_BOUNDARY_REPLAY_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_directed_boundary_replay_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_directed_boundary_replay_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/directed_boundary_replay_lock_20260925.json)). A corrected 18-action directed-boundary plus node-lifecycle replay (three cycles of edge delete/insert/weight replacement and node birth/deletion) matches full rescan at all checkpoints with independent zero residuals; progress is equal and event scheduling uses 30% fewer frame turns. A preregistered connected conversation stress replay shares mutable candidate nodes across adjacent deliberations. A follow-up read-only boundary control gives each adjacent pair a shared evidence node while keeping candidates and selections frame-local; all 24 runs reached fixed points and all 12 full/scoped projections matched. A separate 36-worker native-rule probe propagates shared evidence values into frame-local state and explicitly resets them on retraction. A 108-worker topology/scale extension covers path, star, and balanced-tree graphs at N=8–64 across six insert/update/retract checkpoints; all 36 full/scoped pairs match and all 36 omitted-boundary controls detect the two stale outputs. A randomized dynamic-boundary extension runs 81 arms over 27 graph/replicate pairs at N=12/24/48. Full/scoped states match through insertion of non-tree edges, value updates, retraction/deletion, reinsertion, and final retraction; all omission controls detect the selected stale states. At N=48 scoped closure uses 176,036 candidates/1,492,452 Terms versus 1,934/16,862 full, while separately measured mutation work is ~1.19 ms. Scope membership and notifications remain caller-maintained; this is not native wakeup completeness ([`SUBSTRATE_SHARED_EVIDENCE_DYNAMIC_TOPOLOGY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_SHARED_EVIDENCE_DYNAMIC_TOPOLOGY_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_shared_evidence_dynamic_topology_v1_2_20260925.json), [audit](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_shared_evidence_dynamic_topology_v1_2.py)). At N=64, scoped work is 272,412 candidate pairs/2,258,928 Term evaluations versus 2,142/18,522 for full closure; candidate/Term counts are identical across topology at fixed N, and scoped wall is 65–69 ms versus 0.77–0.94 ms. This is a caller-driven fixed-rule benchmark, not natural locality or general complexity evidence ([`SUBSTRATE_SHARED_EVIDENCE_TOPOLOGY_SCALE_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_SHARED_EVIDENCE_TOPOLOGY_SCALE_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_shared_evidence_topology_scale_20260925.json), [audit](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_shared_evidence_topology_scale.py)). All 12 full/scoped pairs match across four checkpoints; an omitted-both-endpoints control leaves exactly the two affected outputs stale. At N=8 scoped work is 1,736 candidate pairs/14,896 Term evaluations versus 140/1,232 for full closure; this is caller-visited fixed-chain evidence, not automatic notification or natural locality ([`SUBSTRATE_SHARED_EVIDENCE_RULE_PROPAGATION_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_SHARED_EVIDENCE_RULE_PROPAGATION_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_shared_evidence_rule_propagation_v1_2_20260925.json), [audit](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_shared_evidence_rule_propagation_v1_2.py)). At N=8 scoped matching used 5,380 candidate pairs and 40,264 Term evaluations versus 312 and 2,584 for full closure. The native rules do not read the shared evidence edge, so this is overlapping-graph parity, not boundary propagation ([`SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_READONLY_BOUNDARY_SCALE_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_READONLY_BOUNDARY_SCALE_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_readonly_boundary_scale_20260925.json), [audit](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_conversation_readonly_boundary_scale.py)). All runs reach reported fixed points, but full/scoped projection parity holds only for the N=1 control and fails in all nine N=2/4/8 pairs; expected winner gates fail at larger sizes. This exposes global candidate-state aliasing and order sensitivity for this representation, not a general scheduler/locality result; cost is uninterpretable after semantic failure ([`SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_CONNECTED_FRAME_SCALE_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_CONNECTED_FRAME_SCALE_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_connected_frame_scale_20260925.json), [audit](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_conversation_connected_frame_scale.py)). A corrected native conversation-deliberation frame-scale replay also matches full/scoped normalized outcomes and fixed points in all 12 pairs over N=1/2/4/8 independent tasks. At N=8, generic scoped scheduling costs 17.2× the candidate enumerations, 15.6× the Term evaluations, and 4.5× median wall time of full closure. The v1 gate error and v1.1 correction are retained; see [`SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_FRAME_SCOPED_SCALE_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_FRAME_SCOPED_SCALE_RESULTS.md), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_frame_scoped_scale_v1_1_20260925.json), and [independent audit](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_conversation_frame_scoped_scale_v1_1.py). This single disconnected generated workload (N≤8) does not support broad frame-locality or asymptotic claims. The initial harness notification error is preserved and disclosed ([`SUBSTRATE_DIRECTED_BOUNDARY_LIFECYCLE_REPLAY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_DIRECTED_BOUNDARY_LIFECYCLE_REPLAY_RESULTS.md), [v1.1 raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_directed_boundary_lifecycle_replay_v1_1_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_directed_boundary_lifecycle_replay_v1_1_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/directed_boundary_lifecycle_replay_v1_1_lock_20260925.json)). The V1.7 same-corpus three-arm N=32 control reproduces append-placement increases of 1,536/2,094 Term evaluations in six unrelated rules; original-slot replacement leaves all non-ProblemShape counters unchanged. Whole-seed Term reductions remain below 0.5%, graph writes double, and timing has no resolved gain ([V1.7 results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RULE_ORDER_PRESERVATION_V1_7_RESULTS.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_problem_shape_rule_order_preservation_v1_7_analysis_20260925.json)). The seed-index cache cold/warm order defect is repaired under schema v5 and passed a focused frozen-corpus parity probe ([repair](SUBSTRATE_SEED_INDEX_CACHE_ORDER_FIX_V2_RESULTS.md), [probe](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_cache_order_probe_v2_20260925.json), [current-source application parity](SUBSTRATE_SEED_INDEX_COLD_WARM_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RESULTS.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SEED_INDEX_COLD_WARM_PROBLEM_SHAPE_PREREG_20260925.md), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_cold_warm_problem_shape_v1_20260925.json)). A current-source runtime capture over three `describe_scene` workers joins event-time native candidate work to rule patterns and retains one pre-action graph. The selected six producer closures have 337,540 global candidate tests per worker; their earlier per-rule arity breakdown is superseded by the follow-up thread-local attribution anomaly and is not validated. A follow-up captures exact outer lock-interval work: A=677,795 and B=842,748 candidate tests per worker, with per-rule and per-clause sums conserving arithmetically. However, clause-position counters exceed mapped match arities (41 anomalous rows for A, 28 for B per worker), so named-rule and arity attribution fails; a v1.1 Rayon-context rebinding replay leaves the same anomaly, so thread propagation alone is insufficient. Graph-resident and substrate RuleIdx namespaces overlap, and a generic batch path does not clear audit context before continuing. Only global request totals are valid until identities and context lifetime are repaired; the v1.1 comparison also records a changed world_adapter source hash, so only within-run graph parity is claimed. For `rule_intent_about_mentioned_concept`, independent enumeration predicts 32 candidates per pass from relation populations 2/8/22; a recorded native rule counter reports 64 over two scheduler passes, provisional pending serial-path/context validation. The v1.1 confirmatory capture preserves 36/36 global-integrity checks but fails attribution; see [results](SUBSTRATE_PARALLEL_RULE_AUDIT_CONTEXT_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_parallel_rule_audit_context_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json). The inherited request-A reply oracle fails in both captures; this is work/failure characterization, not successful task evidence ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_TASK_RUNTIME_JOIN_CAPTURE_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_task_runtime_join_capture_v1_2_analysis_20260927.json), [graph join](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_task_runtime_join_snapshot_v1_analysis_20260927.json)). Parallel live-task audit v1.3 validates matcher identity and exact attributed/unattributed counter conservation across three locked workers, but one worker's graph is two nodes/three edges smaller and candidate counts vary by 13.7%/15.7%; request A's exact-reply gate still fails ([results](SUBSTRATE_PARALLEL_RULE_AUDIT_CONTEXT_V1_3_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_parallel_rule_audit_context_v1_3_analysis_20260927.json)). A second source-locked live topology, the self-report lifecycle route, passes 72/72 identity/arity/conservation checks across three workers: selection/execution/result use 8,031/8,002/7,998 candidate tests with zero unattributed work. Its second-prompt route shortfall reproduces separately ([v1.9 results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_MATCHER_COST_V1_9_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_report_matcher_cost_v1_9_analysis_20260927.json)). A six-worker variance replay confirms the high-work describe-scene state; one earlier low-work outlier remains unexplained ([variance results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_TASK_GRAPH_VARIANCE_V2_RESULTS_20260927.md)). A second task-derived order replay on the linear anaphora rule passes 293/293 checks across 48 fresh workers; realized orders range 8–16 candidate tests with identical task output and canonical graphs ([results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_JOIN_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_derived_anaphora_join_order_v1_analysis_20260928.json)). The current-source context-scale replay adds 0/8/32 disconnected goal/event distractor components across 216 fresh workers; its analyzer passes 3,247/3,247 checks, with 36–139 candidate tests at D=0 and 49–59 at D=8/32. The native cardinality reorderer moves `for_goal` first in the augmented contexts and retains exact task outputs. A v1.1 reinstallation timing replay measures a 190–194 μs median target-rule add and 522–530 μs median for three lifecycle adds, but ordinary seed-load cost, uninstrumented closure latency, and natural prevalence remain open ([v1 results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_JOIN_ORDER_CONTEXT_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_JOIN_ORDER_CONTEXT_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [v1.1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/task_derived_join_order_context_scale_v1_1_analysis_20260929.json)). Paired counter-on/off timing on this fixture finds 1.23–1.37× median overhead from scale counters, so audit-on closure timings are not latency estimates ([overhead results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_JOIN_ORDER_AUDIT_OVERHEAD_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/task_derived_join_order_audit_overhead_v1_analysis_20260929.json)). A bare-adapter ordinary seed/task capture loads the registered discourse seed closure and runs ten counter-off native closures with task parity in 10 workers; seed load is 5.48 ms median, closures total 262 μs, while import and graph-readback overhead dominate this isolated harness ([results](SUBSTRATE_COMMON_GROUND_ORDINARY_SEED_TASK_COST_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/common_ground_ordinary_seed_task_cost_v1_analysis_20260929.json)). Default core boot and the production ingestion route remain unmeasured. A ten-worker default-boot two-turn anaphora capture now traverses `receive_utterance`/`comprehend`, lazily loads four conversation/intent seeds, preserves the held-out task oracle and terminal fixed point, and records zero scale counters; median boot is 30.84 ms and the two-turn span is 85.09 ms, with about 15 ms of harness readback unallocated ([results](SUBSTRATE_DEFAULT_BOOT_ANAPHORA_TASK_COST_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/default_boot_anaphora_task_cost_v1_analysis_20260929.json)). The ten-worker default-boot anaphora transfer panel covers a three-turn pronoun chain, this/that transfer, and two negative controls through 50 fixture-derived cases; all case oracles and graph-repeatability gates pass, with adapter initialization separated from receive/comprehend cost. This remains one integration-test family, not held-out or natural-task evidence ([results](SUBSTRATE_DEFAULT_BOOT_ANAPHORA_TRANSFER_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_DEFAULT_BOOT_ANAPHORA_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20260929.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_DEFAULT_BOOT_ANAPHORA_TRANSFER_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260929.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/default_boot_anaphora_transfer_v1_1_raw_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/default_boot_anaphora_transfer_v1_1_analysis_20260929.json)). The independently authored 11-case anaphora suite passes all semantic and repeatability gates over ten source-locked workers, testing two known concepts, recency, a three-turn chain, and negative controls. This is an assistant-authored micro-panel, not a blind or human-authored evaluation and not evidence of acquisition/general language competence ([results](SUBSTRATE_INDEPENDENT_AUTHORED_ANAPHORA_PANEL_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_INDEPENDENT_AUTHORED_ANAPHORA_PANEL_V1_PREREG_20260929.md), [suite](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/anaphora_independent_task_suite_v1.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/independent_authored_anaphora_panel_v1_lock_20260929.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/independent_authored_anaphora_panel_v1_raw_20260929.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/independent_authored_anaphora_panel_v1_analysis_20260929.json)). Human-authored/blinded tasks and natural-task prevalence remain unmeasured. A source-stable 48-worker live Percept × distractor factorial (12 cells, two fresh paired replicates per cell) passes all corrected gates. Its finite model records +146 candidate tests, +703 Term evaluations, and +165 selected accesses per synthetic distractor Concept at P=3, with interaction coefficients +43, +172, and +43 respectively per `(P−3)×Concept`; these are measured route-specific slopes, not asymptotic guarantees. Scheduler/deferred units stay fixed while matching work rises. The first capture is retained as source-drift-censored, and an offline analyzer invariant correction is disclosed. Natural frame locality, other task families, complete cost, learned structure, and scoped parity remain open. A separate visual task-family study reaches 8,192 SUnits and 268,961,824 global versus 134,760,480 root-scoped Term evaluations, with exact equation, global-coupling, and scoped-independence checks; its factors differ, so it is cross-family context rather than a matched replication ([vision scaling results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_TWO_SHEET_CURRENT_SOURCE_SCALE_V2_4_RESULTS_20260927.md)).  The 184-worker visual multi-frame v2.5.1 capture passes 14 checks through 6,912 SUnits: at three frames the root-scoped variant uses about one third the global Term work, and contrasting sibling state changes global but not scoped target outputs. A first capture is censored for native rebuild drift and preserved. This is synthetic domain-rule evidence, not learned locality or full visual-task capability; next evaluate production image ingestion/settling with task oracles and frame-discovery cost ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_FRAME_SCOPE_SCALE_V2_5_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_FRAME_SCOPE_SCALE_V2_5_PREREG_20260929.md), [capture amendment](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_FRAME_SCOPE_SCALE_V2_5_1_AMENDMENT_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_multi_frame_scope_scale_v2_5_1_analysis_20260929.json)). See [factorial results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_PERCEPT_NOISE_FACTORIAL_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [capture protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_PERCEPT_NOISE_FACTORIAL_V1_PREREG_20260929.md), [capture amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_PERCEPT_NOISE_FACTORIAL_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260929.md), [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_PERCEPT_NOISE_FACTORIAL_V1_1_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20260929.md), and [source-locked analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_describe_scene_percept_noise_factorial_v1_1_analysis_20260929.json).| The new mint cases remain small synthetics; they do not establish scheduler completeness for all rules, effects, imported/rewritten rules, or production task distributions. Dense candidate work increases with density; no general locality or end-to-end asymptotic claim follows. The report API overhead is measured only for one small synthetic cycle; representative workload performance, compiled-out instrumentation comparison, broader access-dependency coverage, and end-to-end paths remain open. A 17-case effect-progress matrix confirms state/progress parity for covered edge, node, attribute, and replacement effects. The `ReplaceValue` destination-collision undo gap was fixed using `EdgeAttrsSet`; direct native rollback probes pass for collision and non-collision cases, with repeated/crossing replacements still open ([`SUBSTRATE_EFFECT_PROGRESS_MATRIX_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EFFECT_PROGRESS_MATRIX_RESULTS.md), [progress JSON](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_effect_progress_matrix_20260925.json), [rollback JSON](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_replace_value_undo_probe_20260925.json)). A locked current-source mint-termination replay records budget-capped growth for unrestricted `add_node` and fixed-point closure for keyed `add_node_if_absent` on one recursive fixture; the v1.1 independent analyzer passes all gates. This confirms only the observed capped behavior, not nontermination or a weak-acyclicity theorem ([results](SUBSTRATE_MINT_TERMINATION_ENVELOPE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mint_termination_envelope_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mint_termination_envelope_v1_1_20260926.json)). A follow-up checker audit found empty non-list top-level rule/edge containers could be accepted as empty. The checker now rejects them; a locked 15-fixture plus native parity probe passes, while full accepted-grammar equivalence remains open ([results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CHECKER_HARDENING_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_checker_hardening_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json)). A locked 25-case generated grammar panel runs 15 checker-admitted rule programs through native closure and compares exact terminal graphs with an independent injective-binding interpreter; all 15 pass, and ten refusal controls remain refused. This is sampled syntax/runtime coverage, not exhaustive accepted-grammar equivalence ([results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_GENERATED_GRAMMAR_V1_2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_generated_grammar_v1_2_analysis_20260926.json)). The finite-fragment bound is a static analysis, not yet a construction-time guard or a proof of production scheduler termination. Explain the low-work graph replicate and repeat on an independent task before making a stable workload-cost claim. The current-source four-edge anaphora replay now verifies exact task and whole-graph parity across all 24 requested clause permutations (eight distinct installed orders), with 8–16 candidate tests; the held-out CAN sparse-first replay passes source/semantic parity and shows 3.50–4.04× instrumentation-off time reduction. These are two authored-family order-sensitivity results, not a general optimizer or workload-prevalence claim ([anaphora results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_JOIN_ORDER_V1_2_CURRENT_SOURCE_RESULTS_20260929.md), [CAN held-out results](CAN_RUNTIME_MATCH_ORDER_SENSITIVITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20260929.md)). A bounded estimator replay on this anaphora family passes 472/472 checks: it picks minimum exact work in 4/6 cells (median regret 1.00×; maximum 1.12×), while context growth collapses the 24 requests to one installed order at D=2/4. Charged planning+installation+closure is 19.20–22.68× source closure in median, with no winning cell; this rejects the planner for the tested small-closure envelope ([estimator results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_ORDER_ESTIMATOR_V1_3_RESULTS_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/anaphora_order_estimator_v1_3_analysis_20260929.json)). The current native `Substrate::add_rule` already applies a greedy relation-cardinality reorder; at D=2 it realizes 28 candidate tests versus 25 for untouched source order, exposing a local counterexample to cardinality as a sufficient cost proxy ([substrate install path](../../../runners/dsl/src/substrate.rs), [reorder algorithm](../../../runners/dsl/src/rewrite.rs)). Next measure this native heuristic on larger task-derived joins where its closure work can amortize installation, compare against exact candidate work, validate another rule family, explain the isolated low-work describe-scene state, and measure controlled frame-boundary growth with width and exact operations. Current-source finite-key closure v1.3 passes all 25 generated grammar fixtures (15 admitted native fixed points with exact oracle state, 10 refusals); scale v1.2 passes 90 source-locked cases through N=256. Dense M=4 median time grows 26 ms → 119 ms → 544 ms at N=64/128/256, with seven progress firings per case; this is one fixed rule family, not a general time bound ([grammar results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_GENERATED_GRAMMAR_V1_3_RESULTS_20261004.md), [scale results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_SCALE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [scale analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_scale_v1_2_analysis_20261004.json)).  Current-source topology/arity v2.2 passes 171 paired cases with exact independent closure states. On complete N=20 graphs, candidate tests are 760/15,200/289,560 and plain closure medians 0.317/5.157/173.199 ms for arity 1/2/3; sparse paths are much cheaper. This is one finite-key rule family, not a general cost exponent ([results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_TOPOLOGY_ARITY_V2_2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/mint_termination_topology_arity_v2_2_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_topology_arity_v2_2_20261004.json.gz), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_topology_arity_v2_2_analysis_20261004.json)).  A current-source 126-case three-rule positive interaction panel passes all exact independent closure, progress-bound, source-lock, and rule-order invariance checks through N=256 on paths and cycles; medians are 0.40–3.57 ms including setup/export. One sparse synthetic family only; not a general termination or scaling theorem ([results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_RULE_INTERACTION_V2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_RULE_INTERACTION_V2_PREREG_20261004.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_interaction_v2_analysis_20261004.json)).  The v3.2 current-source degree-scaling replay passes 180 exact independent closure comparisons and order-invariance checks across N=32–256, degree 1–16, and three rule orders. At N=256, median end-to-end time ranges 3.64–108.71 ms as analytical two-edge join bindings rise 256–65,536; timings are noisy and the count is not a native matcher counter. One regular sparse family only ([results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_RULE_INTERACTION_V3_2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_RULE_INTERACTION_V3_2_PREREG_20261004.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_interaction_v3_2_analysis_20261004.json)).  The native-counter v1.3 replay passes 180 audit-off/on pairs with exact independent closure parity and per-rule, clause, edge, and global counter conservation; audit-off counters are zero, IDs map unambiguously to rule names, and median paired timing ratio is 1.104. This validates attribution on one regular synthetic family, not universal counter coverage or natural prevalence ([results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_NATIVE_COUNTERS_V1_3_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_NATIVE_COUNTERS_V1_3_PREREG_20261004.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_native_counters_v1_3_analysis_20261004.json)).  The live runtime counter v1.3 diagnostic resolves all 4,788 observed RuleUIDs and reproduces task/graph outputs, but fails counter controls: the nominal audit-off arm records candidate work, and the second request has nine ambiguous rule IDs plus 51 unattributed tests per worker. Per-rule totals are unusable until global identity namespacing and off-mode isolation are repaired; the capture is diagnostic evidence ([report](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_COUNTERS_V1_3_DIAGNOSTIC_20261004.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_closure_counters_v1_3_reconciled_analysis_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_closure_counters_v1_3_20261004.json.gz)). A locked read-only census applies the conservative finite-key admission checker to the default `boot_core()` profile: 0/135 active rules are accepted individually, and the complete rule set is rejected. The active profile uses `where` clauses, `add_node`, `set_attr`, deletions, and other constructs beyond the fragment. This means the narrow finite-key termination bound does not cover the current default core; rejection does not show nontermination. Any termination guarantee needs a broader proved fragment or an explicitly restricted profile ([census results](SUBSTRATE_CORE_RULE_FINITE_KEY_ADMISSION_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CORE_RULE_FINITE_KEY_ADMISSION_CENSUS_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_core_rule_finite_key_admission_census_v1_20261005.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_core_rule_finite_key_admission_census_v1_analysis_20261005.json), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_core_rule_finite_key_admission_census_v1.py)). Root-profile follow-up: a locked census checks all 17 `CORE_SEEDS` roots with dependency closures; 13 are nonempty and none fits the finite-key fragment, while four empty profiles are excluded. Independent decisions agree on all 209 active rule instances. This does not imply nontermination ([root-profile results](SUBSTRATE_CORE_SEED_PROFILE_ADMISSION_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_core_seed_profile_admission_census_v1_analysis_20261005.json)). A post-census syntax tally finds 800 effect occurrences across those 209 active-rule instances, including 370 `set_attr`, 106 unconditional `add_node`, 28 `del_node`, and 11 `del_edge`; `set_attr_if_different` does not occur. Therefore admitting only the existing idempotent attribute effect would not increase core-seed coverage. This is descriptive syntax frequency and reinforces the need for a proved useful profile plus enforced admission ([triage](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md#2026-10-05-finite-key-termination-extension-triage)). A broader locked audit of all 117 registry IDs finds no individually admitted rule among 1,803 active rule instances and no admitted nonempty profile among 82; five IDs remain explicitly unavailable due to missing manifests. Thus no registered root supplies a ready finite-key restricted mode ([v1.2 results](REGISTERED_SEED_PROFILE_ADMISSION_CENSUS_V1_2_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](REGISTERED_SEED_PROFILE_ADMISSION_CENSUS_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/registered_seed_profile_admission_census_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_registered_seed_profile_admission_census_v1_2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/registered_seed_profile_admission_census_v1_2_lock_20261005.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/registered_seed_profile_admission_census_v1_2_20261005.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/registered_seed_profile_admission_census_v1_2_analysis_20261005.json)). An explicit two-rule transitive reachability profile is admitted and exactly matches native `ReachableFrom` plus independent DFS in 36 cases through N=64. Its apparent Q=8 crossover failed replication. At N=128/Q=256, v1.3 shows modest synthetic all-source savings (paired medians 0.941/0.955) but neither cell meets the preregistered consistency threshold. A corrected native rule-path comparison then finds the existing opt-in same-graph `ReachableFrom` cache reduces R=16 total cost by 9–35% versus cache-off, while explicit profile materialization is 1.28–1.54× slower than cache-on across all tested cells. Cache benefit is specific to repeated keys inside native rule execution; host `PySubstrate.evaluate()` uses a fresh cache per call. Browser-profile v1.3 also reports 7.46–12.81× quieter N=128 ticks on synthetic DOM records, not live-page or natural-prevalence evidence. The derived profile is not registered/enforced ([profile v1.3](FINITE_KEY_REACHABILITY_PROFILE_V1_3_RESULTS_20261005.md), [cache comparison](REACHABILITY_CACHE_MATERIALIZATION_COMPARISON_V1_2_RESULTS_20261005.md), [cache analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/reachability_cache_materialization_comparison_v1_2_analysis_20261005.json), [browser cache](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_PROFILE_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_3_RESULTS_20260928.md)). |
| Rule dependency and scheduling | `runners/dsl/src/rewrite.rs::RewriteRule::derive_access`, `runners/dsl/src/rewrite.rs::run_rules_opts`, `runners/dsl/src/rewrite.rs::run_rules_opts_scoped`, `runners/dsl/src/area_scheduler.rs::derive`, `runners/dsl/src/wave_stencil.rs::admissible`, `runners/dsl/src/wave_stencil.rs::stencil_tick`, `runners/dsl/src/stage_a.rs::rule_is_derivable`, `runners/dsl/src/py.rs::PySubstrate::run_rules_settle`, `runners/dsl/src/py.rs::PySubstrate::rule_areas` | Rust derives global `Edge`/`Type`/`Attr` read/write sets plus conservative any-access flags and offers multiple scheduler/batching paths; not all are default. | **Implemented; selected mechanism evidence.** Static resources/flags match on 16 CAN/conversation rules. Three typed-edge wakeup cases pass direct graph oracles after a cursor invalidation fix. A seven-term runtime matrix covers typed `Edges`, `Neighbours`, `InNeighbours`, `ReachableFrom`, `MapNeighbours`, any-type edges, and `EdgeAttr` in `Sum`; all cases refresh outputs and settle. A fresh-build 18-case mutation matrix adds insertion/deletion, edge-weight replacement, node-attribute writes, and node birth; 17/18 outputs match direct graph oracles. The remaining effect-only `Attr` consumer stays stale under the scheduler’s explicit suppression policy, while a predicate `Attr` consumer refreshes ([`SUBSTRATE_DYNAMIC_ACCESS_RECALL_MATRIX_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_DYNAMIC_ACCESS_RECALL_MATRIX_RESULTS.md)). A follow-up full-rescan probe finds `set_attr` replay correct and edge replay updating in place in the controlled case, but fresh `add_node` replay duplicates the minted node; the initial probe found an edge-attribute replacement that mutated graph state without progress accounting; the current source now counts that replacement, records `EdgeAttrsSet` for undo, and Stage A carries it as an edge write. The rebuilt probe confirms progress accounting on this path; the single-binding edge and mint cases, plus a two-binding edge-only case, each passed two Stage A derive/undo/commit comparisons with no divergence or unsoundness. A two-binding `set_attr`+`add_edge` case refreshes both outputs against the oracle without edge duplication, but Stage A recorded zero compared drains for that mixed shape; an edge-plus-fresh-mint case exercises both undo types with no divergence. A separate six-binding one-variable self-loop `SetAttr`+`AddEdge` case is admitted to Stage A compare and yields one compared drain, zero divergence, zero unsoundness, and oracle-matching outputs. A new source-locked probe seeds prior node and edge attrs and tests their mixed overwrite at 1/2/6/16/32 bindings: exact full-graph/progress parity, one compared drain per case, and zero divergence/unsoundness. This establishes that independent one-variable mixed overwrites are admitted; contended multi-variable shapes remain excluded by contention analysis ([`SUBSTRATE_STAGE_A_MIXED_EFFECT_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_STAGE_A_MIXED_EFFECT_RESULTS.md), [mixed-overwrite results](SUBSTRATE_STAGE_A_MIXED_OVERWRITE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw JSON](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_stage_a_mixed_effect_probe_20260925.json)). A source-locked disjoint-pair probe additionally covers two-variable bindings that overwrite pre-existing cross-edge attrs at 1/2/6/16/32 bindings: 15/15 cells pass exact graph/progress parity with one compared drain each and zero divergence, unsoundness, or runtime conflict fallback. The static gate still excludes multi-variable rules that write a matched node, even when fixture pairs are disjoint ([preregistration](SUBSTRATE_STAGE_A_DISJOINT_PAIR_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_STAGE_A_DISJOINT_PAIR_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_stage_a_disjoint_pair_v1_analysis_20260926.json)). A source-locked runtime-overlap probe confirms the conservative guard falls back once for shared-hub fanouts 2/6/16/32 (and not at fanout 1); all 15 Stage A-enabled versus ordinary-closure cells have exact graph/progress/oracle parity. The tested hub writes are semantically independent by target, so this is correct fallback behavior with a potential batching cost, not a speedup result ([preregistration](SUBSTRATE_STAGE_A_OVERLAP_FALLBACK_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_STAGE_A_OVERLAP_FALLBACK_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_stage_a_overlap_fallback_v1_analysis_20260926.json)). The source-locked named CAN eligibility census yields zero Stage-A-derivable drains across 18 N=16/32 path/banded2/complete cells (434 excluded drains), so its fallback rate is undefined and compare-mode divergence is vacuous; ordinary and enabled executions nevertheless match exact graph/progress and independent fixed-point checks ([preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CAN_STAGE_A_FALLBACK_ENVELOPE_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_CAN_STAGE_A_FALLBACK_ENVELOPE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_can_stage_a_fallback_envelope_v1_analysis_20260926.json)). The authored `event_reasoning` chain also supplies an eligible workload: six counterbalanced workers over N=1/4/16/64/256 achieve 100% measured drain admission, zero fallback, exact graph/progress/oracle parity, and proposal counts of 3N. Enabled-path wall time is 2.57× ordinary at N=256 (six-run median ratio; small workload, descriptive only), so admission alone does not demonstrate an optimization ([preregistration](SUBSTRATE_EVENT_REASONING_STAGE_A_ENVELOPE_PREREG_20260926.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_EVENT_REASONING_STAGE_A_ENVELOPE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_event_reasoning_stage_a_envelope_v1_analysis_20260926.json)). A shared-source one-binding family and independent-writer families at 2, 4, 8, 16, and 32 bindings were each measured under six rule orders; four orders in every family settled with stale outputs, while the mismatch fraction falls as bindings grow. A 96-case follow-up exhausts rule orders for one, two, and three competing writers at 8, 64, and 128 reader bindings; every case settles, stale reads remain, and the stale-order share rises from 1/2 to 4/6 to 18/24 with writer count in the fixed generator ([`SUBSTRATE_REPLAY_ORDER_SCALE_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_REPLAY_ORDER_SCALE_RESULTS.md), [raw JSON](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_replay_order_scale_20260925.json)). A guarded derived edge persists after its predicate becomes false and after full rescan, while paired explicit `del_edge` rules retract it in both tested orders. Native `EdgeData` has no producer/justification field, consistent with an explicit-deletion contract rather than automatic truth maintenance. The effect-only standalone attribute-set and the two-variable mixed set-plus-edge replay cases were excluded from Stage A comparison ([`SUBSTRATE_EFFECT_ONLY_ATTR_REPLAY_SAFETY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EFFECT_ONLY_ATTR_REPLAY_SAFETY_RESULTS.md)). A 50-worker fan-out sweep measures required term recomputation, with treatment/control wall time reaching 4.25× at 64 bindings; this is not pure invalidation overhead. A 320-worker paired audit-on/off closure control has median ratios near 1 but broad ranges, so small instrumentation cost is unresolved. A first independent-frame pilot exactly factors the disconnected control (12/12) but connected frame copies usually fail full-graph stability (3/36 pass state and residual checks). A shared-graph `run_rules_scoped` pilot preserves live boundary reads: all 48 scoped outcomes are full-graph fixed points, with exact state matches in 29/48. A CAN-specific static dependency queue reproduces round-robin outcomes in 48/48 and all results pass the full-graph fixed-point check. Guarded matcher scope pushdown for the single `has_concept` edge reduces scoped candidate pairs by 4× at N=16 and 8× at N=32, and Term evaluations by 3.6–7.2×, with all 48 states unchanged between pre/post builds. A dynamic CAN wakeup probe matches full rescans after cross-frame edge insertion/deletion, edge-weight replacement, and a field change (4/4), given explicit notifications of directly affected frames; an omitted-notification control correctly detects a stale frame. A scale pilot covers N=8/16/32/64 (16/16 parity and residual checks), and a broader amended discovery matrix covers eight mutations at N=8/16/32 (24/24 parity/residual checks; 3/3 omitted-notification controls sensitive). Its original v1 fixture failure is preserved; v1.1 is post-result discovery, progress counts are equal, and no cost reduction is measured. A fresh preregistered v2 varies fixture parameters across three seeds and confirms all 72 event/full pairs plus nine negative controls, with an independent analyzer and current-source provenance; progress counts remain equal. V2 still uses fixed path topology, fixed contiguous frames, caller notifications, one SetAttr update rule, and one birth or deletion per cell. A project-authored Bigville cast replay derives 100 five-Concept components from live causal adjacency and `has_concept`; ten sequential evidence updates match full rescans and independent residuals. Candidate work is 95 versus 7,545 pairs and 4,461 versus 336,039 Term evaluations, while progress is equal. The cast has no cross-resident edges and the result is diagnostic. A dynamic-link follow-up retains those 100 runtime-derived frames while adding/removing/reweighting ring, star, and random connected cross-resident links. Across nine topology/replicate pairs, event and full states match with zero independently recomputed residual at seven checkpoints; omitted evidence notification is sensitive in all nine controls. Candidate work falls 61–68% and Term evaluations 61–66% under a caller Python queue, which supplies endpoint frames explicitly. Native effect-to-frame notification remains unimplemented/unmeasured ([`SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_RUNTIME_PARTITION_DYNAMIC_LINKS_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_RUNTIME_PARTITION_DYNAMIC_LINKS_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_runtime_partition_dynamic_links_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_runtime_partition_dynamic_links_analysis_20260925.json)). These are caller-notified Python queue results, not connected-frame boundary propagation, native effect-to-frame notification, natural-world locality, production GALS, repeated node-lifecycle sequences, or mint-idempotence evidence.
