A source-locked synthesis-to-query integration on two coupled-quadratic scalar functions retains a held-out-exact member in 20/20 entropy-query runs per task. Entropy uses one oracle label versus 1.1–1.3 under shuffled fixed/random ordering, at 0.381/0.572 ms median and 72 candidate evaluations versus 0.012/0.016 ms and two evaluations for baselines. This is a small finite integration result with a measurable label/compute trade-off; query-domain discovery and natural-task transfer remain open ([v1.2 results](GENERATED_QUADRATIC_QUERY_TRANSFER_V1_2_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](GENERATED_QUADRATIC_QUERY_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [amendment](GENERATED_QUADRATIC_QUERY_TRANSFER_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261005.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_generated_quadratic_query_transfer_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_generated_quadratic_query_transfer_v1_2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/generated_quadratic_query_transfer_v1_2_20261005_lock.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/generated_quadratic_query_transfer_v1_2_20261005_raw.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/generated_quadratic_query_transfer_v1_2_20261005_analysis.json)).| Maintain a result ledger by task family; measure grammar/candidate/fuel budgets, held-out accuracy, cost, false acceptance, acquisition cost, and transfer. Separate execution from induction and the bounded source reader from program synthesis. Do not summarize as general induction capability. |
| Memory and persistence of experience | `runners/dsl/src/memory.rs::MemoryFaculty::observe_and_bound` / `consolidate` / `shuffle` / `recall_by_shape`, `runners/dsl/src/py.rs::PySubstrate::memory_observe_and_bound` / `memory_consolidate` / `memory_index_disk_record` / `memory_unindex_disk_record` / `memory_recall_by_key` / `memory_recall_by_shape`, `substrate/boot_core.py::boot_core`, `seeds/episodic_working_memory_core.json`, `domains/long_term_memory.py::LongTermMemory::consolidate` / `recall_by_shape` / `recall`, `substrate/graph.py::TypedGraph::node` | Episodic/working-memory policies and episodes are graph-resident Rust Substrate state operated by graph-authored rules; episodic rules are included in the default `CORE_SEEDS` assembly. A distinct native `MemoryFaculty` is lazy, per-PySubstrate process state for budgeting, disposable-ring management, hot/cold tracking, and the long-term shape index. Consolidation writes record-attribute JSON files. The optional Python long-term adapter projects feature vectors, scans durable JSON files on first bind to rebuild native ShapeIndex entries and `_known`, and calls native exact-key or shape recall. | **Selected mechanisms have focused evidence; lifecycle limits are now measured.** [`test_episodic_working_memory_migrated.py`](../../../tests/test_episodic_working_memory_migrated.py) exercises graph-rule binding, retrieval/replay, capacity, gates, and expiry. Rust unit tests in `memory.rs` cover selected salience, retention-score, and shape-index properties. A three-repeat, 1/8/32/64-record current-extension sweep page-outs and pages in every record's attributes; median page-in grows from 0.018 to 0.4536 ms and consolidation from 0.0585 to 1.7795 ms over these small synthetic clusters. With interleaved allocations, none of the original IDs are reused (the one-record no-interleave control reuses its ID); no anchor or chain edges return. An initial two-process probe found an empty reader index and a stubbed `LongTermMemory.recall`. Locked fresh-process probes verify complete header indexing and representative exact-key attr recall through 50,000 records. The adapter has a three-value category signature, so rank-shape clusters are bounded by 3! = 6; these tests measure per-cluster occupancy and record-scan cost, not cluster-count growth. At 50,000 records median bind takes 1.62 s and peak reader RSS is 71.8 MiB including interpreter baseline. A separate integrity probe rejects duplicate keys by leaving them unfindable and counts malformed JSON/missing-attrs records as skipped; unrelated unique keys still recall correctly. Readers restore no edges ([`SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTART_RECALL_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTART_RECALL_RESULTS.md), [`SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTART_SCALE_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTART_SCALE_RESULTS.md), [`SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTART_SCALE_EXTENDED_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTART_SCALE_EXTENDED_RESULTS.md), [`SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTORE_INTEGRITY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_RESTORE_INTEGRITY_RESULTS.md), [integrity raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_memory_restore_integrity_20260925.json), [extended raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_memory_restart_scale_extended_20260925.json)). Historical [`jabberwock_online_plan.md`](../agent_loop/jabberwock_online_plan.md) claims same-ID page-in with edges intact through a `domains/paged_memory.py` hook, but that module is absent; current probes contradict edge restoration and show ID behavior depends on allocation conditions. | Measure episodic provenance/interference and long-term recall quality/latency separately. Measure cold/warm caches, larger payloads, truncated-but-valid records, and concurrent-writer behavior; decide whether the adapter needs a richer category signature before evaluating cluster-count sensitivity; implement relationship-preserving page-in only if required by the record contract; and measure restart/corruption/recovery before broad persistence claims. A locked shape-quality pilot restores 36 records in fresh readers and verifies ordinal-rank recall on all 12 queries: 100% precision for the represented rank class but 50% for a magnitude label intentionally erased by rank encoding; exact-key controls return 12/12. This is a synthetic representation-boundary result, not task utility ([`SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_SHAPE_QUALITY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_SHAPE_QUALITY_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_memory_shape_quality_pilot_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_memory_shape_quality_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/memory_shape_quality_lock_20260925.json)). Measure byte-estimation error, eviction quality, memory pressure, and lifecycle cost; compare memory/no-memory/reset-memory conditions.  A separate graph-authored episodic cue path passed a pre-locked synthetic task pilot: 18/18 exact unique-cue recalls across 6/24/78 Episode records; 6/6 same-cue collisions produced confidence 0.5 and abstained at threshold 0.75; unknown, reset-memory, and no-memory controls behaved as expected. Single-run median tick latency was 0.17/0.35/1.98 ms across the three unique-cue load arms. This is cue-rule task evidence, not long-term ShapeIndex or natural semantic retrieval ([`SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_MEMORY_TASK_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_MEMORY_TASK_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_memory_task_pilot_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_memory_task_pilot_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/episodic_memory_task_pilot_lock_20260925.json)).  A follow-on pre-locked cue-form probe found 12/12 exact-key target recalls, including 6/6 with lexical near-neighbors, and 0 candidates plus explicit insufficient-information for all 12 truncated and 12 paraphrased forms. This confirms that the current episodic request path is exact-key at this boundary; it is not fuzzy or semantic retrieval ([`SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CUE_ROBUSTNESS_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CUE_ROBUSTNESS_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_cue_robustness_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_cue_robustness_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/episodic_cue_robustness_lock_20260925.json)).  A separately locked two-slot working-memory probe accepted two same-task inputs then refused a third; replaced the lowest-priority foreign-task slot while preserving the higher-priority item; and expired an unmaintained slot at epoch 9 versus a maintained slot at epoch 16, releasing content on expiry. This is a selected working-memory gate mechanism result, not useful eviction or long-term episodic retention ([`SUBSTRATE_WORKING_MEMORY_CAPACITY_TTL_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_WORKING_MEMORY_CAPACITY_TTL_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_working_memory_capacity_ttl_amended_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_working_memory_capacity_ttl_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/working_memory_capacity_ttl_amended_lock_20260925.json)).  A fresh-process graph-checkpoint probe restored six Episodes, all memory rules, and context/schema/event edges; intact recall passed 6/6. Removing one recorded-episode link caused an explicit miss, while malformed JSON raised `JSONDecodeError`. A valid-JSON summary mutation was silently returned as recalled content with confidence 0.85, and `WorldAdapter.close()` after load raised `AttributeError` for an uninitialized vision field. This is WorldAdapter graph checkpoint evidence, not LongTermMemory/MemoryFaculty record durability ([`SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CHECKPOINT_RECOVERY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CHECKPOINT_RECOVERY_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_checkpoint_recovery_amended_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_checkpoint_recovery_analysis_20260925.json), [v3 lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/episodic_checkpoint_recovery_amended_v3_lock_20260925.json)).  The post-load close defect was fixed by initializing `_vision_illumination_nodes`; a v5 locked fresh-process rerun closed all restored adapters successfully while reproducing the retrieval/corruption outcomes. Valid-JSON semantic alteration remains silently trusted ([`SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CHECKPOINT_RECOVERY_POSTFIX_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CHECKPOINT_RECOVERY_POSTFIX_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_checkpoint_recovery_postfix_v5_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_checkpoint_recovery_postfix_v5_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/episodic_checkpoint_recovery_postfix_v5_lock_20260925.json)).  A separate pre-locked full graph checkpoint scale sweep at N=6/24/78/192 achieved 600/600 exact recalls across two distinct-process replicates per size. At N=192 median save/load were 22.5/16.5 ms and per-key query tick was 12.0 ms (p95 12.4 ms), compared with 0.168 ms at N=6; the 192-key reader workload totaled 2.31 s. This is a small synthetic warning envelope, not an asymptotic claim; no operation attribution or natural-task graph was included ([`SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CHECKPOINT_SCALE_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_CHECKPOINT_SCALE_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_checkpoint_scale_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_checkpoint_scale_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/episodic_checkpoint_scale_lock_20260925.json)).  Native per-rule attribution on the graph-authored episodic path isolates the repeated collision-detection rule as a quadratic candidate source: `2*N*(N+1)` candidate tests on one query at N=6/24/78/192, 98.5% of all candidate tests at N=192. It runs over all episode pairs even when cues are unique; uninstrumented median query tick reaches 12.0 ms at N=192. This is a rule-specific measured work envelope, not a substrate-wide complexity class ([`SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_QUERY_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_EPISODIC_QUERY_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_query_attribution_20260925.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_episodic_query_attribution_analysis_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/episodic_query_attribution_lock_20260925.json)). |

Main twin runs are deterministic, but current task identities have substantial drift from the prior seed-v1.1.0 result; the current seed is v1.14.0. This is a known-suite recheck, not fresh-domain evidence ([`SUBSTRATE_P6_CURRENT_RECHECK_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_P6_CURRENT_RECHECK_RESULTS.md), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_p6_current_recheck_20260925.json), [transition analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_p6_recheck_analysis_20260925.json)). Each arm reproduced exactly; current-vs-historical pass IDs drift substantially under seed v1.14.0 versus historical v1.5.1, so the increase is not causally attributable ([`SUBSTRATE_MBPP_ARC3_CURRENT_RECHECK_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_MBPP_ARC3_CURRENT_RECHECK_RESULTS.md), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mbpp_arc3_current_recheck_20260925.json), [transition analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mbpp_arc3_recheck_analysis_20260925.json), [pre-run lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/mbpp_arc3_current_recheck_lock_20260925.json)). P3-R0 is preregistered local readiness scaffolding with mandatory no-adoption; its preregistration states no P3-E1 result or independent evaluator custody ([`PREREGISTRATION.md`](../../../evaluation/p3/PREREGISTRATION.md)). A separate locked 18-task P4 diagnostic on a previously inspected contrast-string family returned 11 accepted routes and 5 seven-row passes; each fresh-process worker showed zero TermArena length growth, while budget-gap routes had a larger observed RSS envelope. This is diagnostic resource evidence, not independent capability or scaling evidence ([`SUBSTRATE_P4_ISOLATED_TASK_MEMORY_RESULTS.md`](SUBSTRATE_P4_ISOLATED_TASK_MEMORY_RESULTS.md), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_p4_isolated_task_memory_20260925.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/p4_isolated_task_memory_lock_20260925.json)). It strengthens provenance, not the learning envelope or natural-task transfer claim ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MEASUREMENT_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MEASUREMENT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_genmint_measure_v1_1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_measurement_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_measurement_v1_1_20260926.json)). [Results](SUBSTRATE_GAP_GROUNDER_ROLE_WIRING_V1_3_RESULTS_20260929.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GAP_GROUNDER_ROLE_WIRING_V1_3_PREREG_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/gap_grounder_role_wiring_v1_3/analysis.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/gap_grounder_role_wiring_v1_3/capture.json), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/gap_grounder_role_wiring_v1_3.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/gap_grounder_role_wiring_v1_3_lock_20260929.json). The preregistered GenMint process-restart panel completes 90 sequences. The exact learned graph/grammar and semantically equivalent native rules restore in fresh processes; the intended generator is acquired in 25/30 enabled sequences, and the 400 held-out calls routed through that training-acquired generator are exact. Five shearB/adjacent acquisition misses remain despite recognized status. End-to-end restart costs about 410 ms over continuous enabled use and has only a modest, family-dependent paired advantage over disabled induction, so this establishes bounded durable reuse rather than broad learning benefit ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_PROCESS_RESTART_REUSE_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_PROCESS_RESTART_REUSE_V1_PREREG_20260930.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_PROCESS_RESTART_REUSE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260930.md), [runner](../../../scripts/genmint_process_restart_reuse_v1.py), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/genmint_process_restart_reuse_v1_20260930.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/genmint_process_restart_reuse_v1_1_analysis_20260930.json)). A source-locked ten-worker code-write acquisition panel teaches one new construction from each external parity/clamp function, then exactly transfers to all 305 held-out inputs in both native Terms and emitted Python. The starter requests abstain before teaching; the same request selects the new shape afterward. This is example-supervised codebook growth, not autonomous proposal generation. Full-seed boot costs 12.19 s median and peaks near 1.68 GiB RSS ([results](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_EXAMPLE_ACQUISITION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_EXAMPLE_ACQUISITION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_code_write_example_acquisition_v1_raw_20261001.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_code_write_example_acquisition_v1_analysis_20261001.json)). The existing [`test_code_growth_migrated.py`](../../../tests/test_code_growth_migrated.py) is linked mechanism coverage; it was not run as part of this panel. The bounded native-Term query selector achieves a measured gain when informative inputs are sparse: on an eight-hypothesis family it identifies the hidden candidate in three selected queries versus 7.496 mean random queries; on three easy families random is nearly as good. Hypotheses and labels remain harness/oracle supplied, so this is not autonomous hypothesis induction ([results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SELECTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SELECTION_V1_1_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_selection_v1_1_analysis_20261001.json)). Scaling to 128 finite binary-coded hypotheses gives a 178.59 ms median loop on this host; direct contradictions are refused, but observational aliasing can still produce a false singleton (15 predicted vs 115 held-out oracle). Candidate generation and input choice remain bounded/harness-authored ([results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALING_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_scaling_v1_analysis_20261001.json)). Independent factor sweeps measured 1.30 s at H=512, 304 ms at Term depth 69, and 467 ms over 500 candidate inputs on the same finite coded family; these are workload-specific, two-repeat measurements ([results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALE_FACTORS_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALE_FACTORS_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_scale_factors_v1_analysis_20261001.json)). A 24-worker memoization intervention preserved exact query histories and reduced native calls 1.96–2.59×, yet identity-cache wall time was 1.51–1.66× slower than uncached; fingerprint and JSON caches were slower still. Keep memoization opt-in and test batched prediction/partition updates next ([results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_MEMOIZATION_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_MEMOIZATION_V1_2_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_memoization_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json)). The sequence-local pure-Term matrix then preserved the exact selector histories and reached 1.78–1.87× median speedup at H=128/512 and H=32/N=500 with approximately unchanged peak RSS; it preserves the known off-grid false singleton ([results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_BATCHED_PREDICTIONS_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_BATCHED_PREDICTIONS_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_BATCHED_PREDICTIONS_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_batched_predictions_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json)). This speed result is from the eager matrix revision. A follow-up introduced lazy survivor-first evaluation and passed exact parity across eight pure-Term operators and partial `{x}`/`{x,y}` inputs, selecting 12 partial queries and refusing `Holds` before evaluation. In these small one-query cases the lazy version reduces calls but is slower (0.832 vs 0.634 ms median). A 36-worker crossover study then measured 1.51–1.99× speedups with 3–9 follow-up queries, while already-singleton cases were slower (0.63–0.75×); the prior off-grid false-singleton remains ([results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_BATCHED_PARTIAL_DOMAIN_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [crossover protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_LAZY_MATRIX_CROSSOVER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [crossover results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_LAZY_MATRIX_CROSSOVER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_lazy_matrix_crossover_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A task-generated transfer panel then exercised pools from the actual bounded synthesizer on triangular, square, and Euler tasks (H=9/1/34; three workers each). Independent replay confirmed exact query/survivor parity and held-out correctness; lazy selection reduced calls 18.8%/39.4% on the two nontrivial pools, with timing tied for the one-query triangle and 1.30× faster for two-query Euler. Pool generation (46–683 ms) dominates selection (0.9–3.6 ms), so no end-to-end learner speedup follows ([generated-pool transfer results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_POOL_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_POOL_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_pool_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A three-worker follow-up on a fresh cubic task generated complete H=10 pools and found the correct survivor in one query on five held-outs; lazy selection reduced calls 18.75% while selector latency improved only 0.016 ms. Pool synthesis took 49.39 ms. This is small authored-family transfer evidence, not broad generalization ([cubic results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_cubic_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A nine-worker size-7 follow-up found H=66 broad-grid and H=27 one-point-grid cubic pools, both resolved exactly in one query. An arithmetic-only H=9 pool for `absolute-value` was eliminated by its first selected probe in all workers, providing a refusal signal for this out-of-vocabulary target. Lazy selection reduced calls, while latency remained essentially tied and pool generation dominated. This is a single controlled contradiction result; adversarial grid aliasing and calibrated refusal rates remain open ([grid/misspecification results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_GRID_MISSPEC_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_GRID_MISSPEC_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_grid_misspec_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A dedicated one-point cubic alias control then reproduced a generated-pool false singleton in all three workers: `x` survives as the sole hypothesis after matching `x³` at the known/candidate inputs, then scores 0/4 held-out. This directly demonstrates that held-out/model-adequacy checks are necessary before singleton adoption; selector parity does not imply the target survived pool construction ([alias results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_ALIAS_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_ALIAS_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_cubic_alias_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A post-selection audit replay on 24 locked outcomes shows one fresh independent label rejects each of the three cubic alias false singletons; 18 represented controls pass and three empty out-of-grammar pools stay refused. With zero audit examples the three aliases pass vacuously. This supports a cheap finite validation guard but remains harness logic using external labels, not a general correctness certificate or production adoption path ([adequacy-gate results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_INDEPENDENT_ADEQUACY_GATE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_INDEPENDENT_ADEQUACY_GATE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_independent_adequacy_gate_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A source/input-locked API pilot now binds candidate-Term hashes and acquisition traces to deterministic fresh-probe requests and provenance-bearing residual receipts. It accepts 18/18 represented controls, rejects 3/3 false singletons, preserves 3/3 empty-pool refusals, and distinguishes ambiguous/incomplete/invalid evidence across six controls. This remains caller-declared provenance and harness-supplied labels, with no adoption-path integration ([receipt results](SUBSTRATE_PROVENANCE_BOUND_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_PROVENANCE_BOUND_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_provenance_bound_adequacy_receipt_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A corrected 720-cell sparse-witness probe study finds maximum-entropy challenger selection reduces one-label false acceptance from 56/60 to 0/60 while preserving 30/30 correct adoptions; alias-only domains still falsely accept 30/30, and marginal decision time rises from about 0.0009 ms to 0.4 ms (hash-order preprocessing excluded from the timed decision). Earlier no-fresh-input and integer-overflow captures are disclosed as diagnostic ([selection results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROBE_SELECTION_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROBE_SELECTION_V1_2_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_probe_selection_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_probe_selection_v1_2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_probe_selection_v1_2_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_probe_selection_v1_2_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_probe_selection_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json)). This is a supplied-model, authored scalar result, not a production adequacy guarantee. A follow-up source/native-locked 1,440-cell early-stop panel transfers the same tradeoff to two-variable arithmetic: entropy selection rejects all broad-domain wrong candidates with one label in both families while preserving correct controls; alias-only domains remain false accepts. At budget 4 the mean hash label use is 3.53/3.65 versus one for entropy, with higher entropy-selector compute cost. The task families and labelers remain authored, and the challenger set is supplied ([early-stop transfer results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_EARLY_STOP_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_EARLY_STOP_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_early_stop_transfer_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_early_stop_transfer_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_early_stop_transfer_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_early_stop_transfer_v1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_early_stop_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A full-seed integration panel runs the selector through candidate-bound audit requests, receipts, and the actual write-shape `teach()` boundary: correct square adoption transfers 4/4 held-outs in 3/3 workers, broad identity-as-square is refused in 3/3, and singleton aliasing falsely adopts in 3/3. The receipt binds the candidate and selected descriptor but does not authenticate selector code or label-provider identity ([integration results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_SELECTOR_RECEIPT_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_SELECTOR_RECEIPT_ADOPTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_selector_receipt_adoption_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_selector_receipt_adoption_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_selector_receipt_adoption_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_selector_receipt_adoption_v1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_selector_receipt_adoption_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A matched provider-spoof panel then shows that a forged expected value with the same provider ID and copied oracle hash passes receipt verification and is adopted in 3/3 fresh full-seed runs; the honest value is rejected in 3/3. Thus the receipt checksum proves internal consistency, not source authenticity. An opt-in stdlib HMAC wrapper now authenticates labels before the same `teach()` boundary: 36/36 tamper, replay, and unknown-provider controls are rejected; unsigned forgeries are blocked. A trusted signer can still sign an incorrect value and cause false adoption, keyring ownership/isolation is unresolved, and the legacy receipt-only route remains callable ([provider-auth results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_AUTH_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_AUTH_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [wrapper](../../../domains/adequacy_provider_auth.py), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A v1.1 follow-up adds the zero-probe case: the core receipt accepts an empty request vacuously but the authenticated wrapper rejects it. All 48 v1.1 tamper/replay/unknown-provider/empty-probe controls fail closed; trusted-but-incorrect signatures remain accepted, and registry ownership remains unresolved ([v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_AUTH_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_AUTH_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_auth_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json)). The live `WorldAdapter` now offers opt-in strict code-example acquisition: provider keys are copied into adapter-owned construction-time configuration, strict policy survives checkpoint save/load, and secrets are not serialized. The current-source adoption-surface panel adopted exactly one typed code-write shape in 3/3 valid-signature runs, refused 3/3 tampered signatures despite internally accepted receipts, refused 3/3 direct receipt-only calls on strict adapters, and refused all 6/6 curriculum function attempts without evidence (9/9 analyzer checks; [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_ADOPTION_SURFACE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_adoption_surface_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_adoption_surface_v1_2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_adoption_surface_v1_2_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_adoption_surface_v1_2_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_adoption_surface_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json)). Strict enforcement is opt-in and does not cover lower-level direct `teach()` or non-strict agents. A live trusted-signer challenge then showed that a valid HMAC can still carry a false label: identity was adopted in 3/3 strict-route trials and failed all 9 held-out square cases, while the correct square control passed all 9 (7/7 checks; [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_TRUSTED_SIGNER_LIVE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_trusted_signer_live_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_trusted_signer_live_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_trusted_signer_live_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_trusted_signer_live_v1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_trusted_signer_live_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A 15-process current-source provider/domain panel (10/10 checks) separates the failures: conflicting signed labels are refused; two registered IDs agreeing on a false label still cause 3/3 false adoptions; and two truthful providers on an alias-only probe also cause 3/3 false adoptions. This shows ID multiplicity and signatures do not certify source independence or probe adequacy ([results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_AGREEMENT_AND_DOMAIN_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_AGREEMENT_AND_DOMAIN_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_agreement_domain_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_provider_agreement_domain_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_agreement_domain_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_agreement_domain_v1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_agreement_domain_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). A source-locked selected-probe gate then recomputed challenger disagreement and refused the zero-entropy alias domain in 3/3 strict live-route runs even though receipts were accepted; the truthful square control generalized 9/9, while a trusted false label still caused adoption in 3/3 and failed 9/9 held-outs (12/12 analyzer checks; [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_SELECTED_PROBE_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_SELECTED_PROBE_ADOPTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_selected_probe_adoption_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_selected_probe_adoption_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_selected_probe_adoption_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_selected_probe_adoption_v1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_selected_probe_adoption_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). The provider/key trust boundary is not deployment-ready ([spoof results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_SPOOF_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROVIDER_SPOOF_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_spoof_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_adequacy_provider_spoof_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_adequacy_provider_spoof_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_spoof_v1_raw_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_provider_spoof_v1_analysis_20261002.json)). | Five synthesized-hypothesis active-query workers retain the exact target in 9–10-member version spaces and all max-entropy, fixed-first, and seeded-random arms finish exact on held-outs. Maximum entropy uses a median of two labels versus one fixed-first and four random, with higher selector latency; the v1 fixed-first list put designed separating probes first, so this result is order-specific ([results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [amendment](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_analysis_20261003.json)). The order-balanced v1.1 follow-up gets exact singleton identification in 150/150 entropy blocks and 111/150 sequential randomized-order blocks, with higher selector latency and only one numeric family ([results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_PREREG_20261003.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json)). Keep toy rule learning, graph-authored domain growth, and source-level self-modification as separate capabilities. Measure held-out gain, false mint/adoption, forgetting, negative transfer, and full acquisition cost per path. P3-E1 learning/adoption claims remain gated; local P3-R0 readiness is not learning efficacy or independent evaluation. |
| Planning and action selection |`runners/dsl/src/p2_bounded.rs::revise_belief` / `simulate_one_action` / `select_all_actions`, `runners/dsl/src/p2_native.rs::worker_reply` / `evaluator_reply`, `runners/dsl/src/p4_readiness.rs::advance_p4_r0`, `domains/operational_planning.py::run_plan`, `domains/ls20_planning_seed.py`, `domains/spatial_sense.py::quanta_flood_step`, `worlds/arc_ls20_scenario.py::run_episode| P2-B1 selection is pure bounded Rust; P2-Native exchanges JSON proposals and a separate evaluator reconstructs expected output. Neither path grants or executes actions. The older operational-planning path is Python/graph orchestration that can dispatch configured capabilities; P4-R0 is readiness protocol state, not a planner. |**Partial; bounded mechanism and evaluator protocols exist.** Rust unit coverage includes `p2_bounded.rs::preserves_equal_minima_as_tie`, `::fails_closed_for_incomplete_prediction_set`, and `::maximum_intervention_removes_over_budget_candidates`, plus `p2_native.rs::mechanics_control_real_transition_and_not_submitted_witnesses` and `::maximum_intervention_excludes_over_budget_actions_in_worker_and_evaluator` ([bounded unit tests](../../../runners/dsl/src/p2_bounded.rs#L338), [native unit tests](../../../runners/dsl/src/p2_native.rs#L735)). The linked Python protocol/controls/runtime files validate staged P2 proposal and runtime-closure artifacts; they do not call the bounded planner functions directly ([`test_p2_controls.py`](../../../tests/evaluation/test_p2_controls.py), [`test_p2_runtime.py`](../../../tests/evaluation/test_p2_runtime.py), [`test_p2_protocol.py`](../../../tests/evaluation/test_p2_protocol.py)); the five native unit cases pass under exact-name Cargo filters with source/build/environment provenance ([v1.4 results](SUBSTRATE_PLANNING_NATIVE_UNIT_AUDIT_V1_3_RESULTS_20260928.md), [manifested capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_planning_native_unit_audit_v1_4_20260928.json)). These are mechanism checks, not evidence of long-horizon held-out planning. The staged capability boundary and required later experiments are specified in [`GENERAL_INTELLIGENCE_EXPANSION_PLAN.md`](GENERAL_INTELLIGENCE_EXPANSION_PLAN.md). A current-source source-locked offline ls20-shaped task panel now pairs the graph-rule curiosity planner with greedy/quanta navigation on 150 worlds: quanta succeeds 30/30 on all four 16×16/32×32 walled cells, versus greedy 0–1/30, and exactly matches greedy on all 30 open controls. Independent transform/station/reachability checks and source/build lock gates pass. Quanta median episode wall time is 1.12–1.23 s at 16×16 and 3.49–3.65 s at 32×32; this one synthetic task does not measure general planning or natural prevalence ([current-source scale results](SUBSTRATE_LS20_MAPNAV_CURRENT_SOURCE_SCALE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/ls20_mapnav_current_source_scale_v1_2_independent_analysis.json)). Focused functional tests include [test_operational_planning.py](../../../tests/test_operational_planning.py) and [test_arc_ls20_mapnav_migrated.py](../../../tests/test_arc_ls20_mapnav_migrated.py); these cover configured planning/navigation routes, not the bounded Rust P2 selector’s long-horizon capability.|P2-B1 fixes horizon/branching to 1, caps actions at 4 and nodes per action at 8, and evaluates a sealed toy transition vocabulary. No open-ended search envelope, held-out-world success, replanning, calibration under shift, or baseline advantage is measured. Keep proposal selection separate from action execution and host I/O; evaluate long-horizon planning only after the plan's earlier custody, perception, and learning gates. A screening panel found conservative recognition refusals under pixel noise, then a source-locked four-seed replication across 640 worlds held quanta routing fixed and balanced all six transforms. At sigma 8, both station counts succeed 80/80; at sigma 10/12 they succeed 76/80 and 64/80 (one station), 71/80 and 59/80 (six); at sigma 14 they fall to 1/80 and 5/80. All recognition errors are refusals; no wrong transform key is observed. The final amended independent audit passes all integrity gates, with 32/32 operator blocks balanced. This is an offline LS20-shaped result, not a general planning or visual-robustness claim ([v2 results](SUBSTRATE_LS20_DECISION_AMBIGUITY_NOISE_V2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [final analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/ls20_decision_ambiguity_noise_v2_2_analysis.json)). A source audit finds TransformDecode/DimInfer expose no runner-up score or candidate margin; `checked` is work count, while ShapeDecode `total_fit` is a search statistic, not confidence ([observability audit](SUBSTRATE_RECOGNITION_CONFIDENCE_OBSERVABILITY_AUDIT_20260928.md)). Next measure held-out shape-family transfer; if adding candidate diagnostics, preserve winner/status parity, measure instrumentation overhead, and calibrate selective risk on separate data before any confidence claim. Continue long-horizon planning and tail-cost gates.|

### Native matcher order evidence (2026-09-28)

Two source-locked, task-shaped join-order panels now compare different four-edge patterns. The cyclic common-ground `discourse_goal_select` rule showed a 3.86× candidate-work spread across realized orders with exact graph parity. The linear anaphora rule showed a smaller 2× range (8–16 native candidate tests) across eight realized orders; all 48 workers returned the tested `it → question` result, reached a fixed point, and had matching canonical graph digests. Both used opt-in counters, and the anaphora input is one selected two-turn example. These observations do not estimate natural workload frequency, uninstrumented speed, or a general join policy. See [anaphora replay and raw evidence](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_JOIN_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_JOIN_ORDER_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_derived_anaphora_join_order_v1_analysis_20260928.json), and [run manifest](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_derived_anaphora_join_order_v1_20260928.json).

## What this inventory establishes

The substrate is not one algorithm. It is a Rust native core, Python loading/orchestration/adapters, graph-authored rules, domain-specific components, and multiple execution paths with different evidence levels. Capability claims must name the path and ownership split. Results from one subsystem cannot automatically be generalized to another.

The current evidence is strongest where the operation has a named, bounded workload and a result artifact—for example, low-treewidth CAN exact inference and selected shape/program-synthesis experiments. It is much weaker for shared envelopes spanning multiple domains, general induction economics, cross-domain transfer, or large-scale end-to-end resource claims.

GenInfer's selected-null probe established a semantic caveat: it refuses predicate and relation-coverage mismatches in the tested fixtures, but returns `explained=true` with the same matrix for coordinate-perturbed observations. A repeated output-field audit confirms that these cases carry a predicate-local `shift(+1)` residual while exact positives carry no residual. Thus `explained` means accepted generator-plus-residual decomposition, not matrix-only exact fit. Both failed null gates are retained, and residual precision/recall beyond this perturbation remain unmeasured ([noise results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_NOISE_REFUSAL_RESULTS_20260926.md), [residual results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RESIDUAL_SEMANTICS_RESULTS_20260926.md), [residual analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_residual_semantics_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [implementation](../../../runners/dsl/src/shape_decode.rs)).

**Relation transfer on fresh signatures (2026-10-03):** A 30-case source-locked full-seed capture tests three 2-D maps, disabled controls, identity controls, and corrupted heldouts. Only `[2,1,1,1]` is acquired before heldout insertion and then attributed on heldout in 3/3 repeats. `[1,0,1,1]` appears only after heldout insertion; `[1,1,0,1]` routes through pre-existing `cycle4` even when GenInfer is disabled. Thus only 3/9 clean enabled cases count as learned transfer, and the strict three-family gate fails. No corrupted heldout exactly matches (0/9), and disabled controls acquire no target generator. This supports a narrow bounded acquisition-and-reuse capability, with route aliases and heldout-time induction as explicit confounds; it does not show open-ended relation induction ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RELATION_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RELATION_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261003.md)).

**Prospective relation transfer v2 (2026-10-03):** After the v1 alias failures, a preregistered 30-case panel uses three distinct maps selected through disclosed one-run task-development probes. All 9/9 enabled clean runs acquire their target matrix before heldout insertion and reuse the same generator on a separate signature; per-map transfer is 3/3. Disabled acquisition is 0/9, identity minting 0/3, and corrupted exact matches 0/9. Median training closure is 9.84 ms and heldout closure 0.74 ms. This supports bounded data-derived integer-matrix induction and reuse on synthetic 2-D relations, with the search family/seed/task authored in advance; it does not establish autonomous goal setting, unbounded operator induction, or improvement of the learner ([v2 results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RELATION_TRANSFER_V2_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RELATION_TRANSFER_V2_PREREG_20261003.md)).

**Full-seed GenInfer scaling (2026-10-03):** An 84-worker locked sweep crosses relation counts 4–64 with bounds B=3–6. All 60 positive cases acquire and transfer; disabled and predicate-corrupt training controls acquire no generator (0/12 each). Coordinate corruption is accepted as generator-plus-local-shift in 60/60 cases but exact matrix-only matches are 0/60; the residual is explicit. Training closure grows approximately with `N×(2B+1)^4`, reaching 1.78 s median at N=64/B=6; heldout reuse at that cell is 4.67 ms. Process HWM delta is only coarsely measured (median 960 KiB above post-boot baseline). This sharpens the bounded one-frame tractability profile while leaving multi-frame, high-dimensional, startup, and natural-task scaling unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FULLSEED_SCALING_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FULLSEED_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261003.md)).

**Shared-codebook versus isolated-frame scaling (2026-10-03):** A source-locked comparison matches 93/93 frame outputs in both conditions. For K=16, one shared closure costs 1.053 s versus 164 ms summed across 16 one-part isolated closures (6.42×); the pure search diagnostic is 614,400 candidate checks shared versus 38,400 isolated. At K=1, the closures are comparable. This supports a frame-locality cost opportunity, while exposing the implementation gap: `frame_id` is ignored and isolation was supplied by separate full-seed graphs. Boot, scheduling, frame discovery, and setup overhead were excluded, so no deployed speedup is claimed. Next implement and benchmark an explicit frame/codebook scope with its selection/maintenance costs included ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FRAME_ISOLATION_COMPARISON_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FRAME_ISOLATION_COMPARISON_V1_1_PREREG_20261003.md)).

A larger residual-envelope audit confirms global numeric shifts, symbolic substitution, the tested `max_dims` boundary, and refusal of one conflicting symbolic map. It also finds a repeated-predicate numeric case where inconsistent residuals cause a stable alternative matrix to be selected. Generator recovery therefore needs its own metric and falsification suite; `explained` alone cannot support a learning-accuracy claim ([envelope results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RESIDUAL_ENVELOPE_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_residual_envelope_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A repeated-predicate robustness surface (three layouts, three corruption magnitudes, five repeats) finds all positives exact, while only 5/45 corrupted cases return the generating matrix. Twenty corrupted cases are accepted, 15 with alternative matrices, and 25 are refused. The outcome is geometry-sensitive and non-monotonic in corruption magnitude; the smallest candidate bound (624 vs 6,560) corresponds to other layouts and different acceptance behavior. This is a narrow synthetic result, but it directly shows that matrix recovery, decomposition acceptance, and search envelope need separate scorecard fields ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_REPEAT_PREDICATE_ROBUSTNESS_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_repeat_predicate_robustness_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

Source review and candidate-bound checks point to the mechanism in the tested cases: the true matrix is always inside the enumerated cube, but GenInfer independently sorts held and observed relations, aligns them positionally, and accepts predicate-local residual shifts. The residual-count then matrix-L1 Occam tie-break can favor a lower-norm alternative matrix. This is a fixture-level explanation; preserving relation identity or measuring alternative-matrix selection under other layouts remains open ([source-linked results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_REPEAT_PREDICATE_ROBUSTNESS_RESULTS_20260926.md), [implementation](../../../runners/dsl/src/shape_decode.rs)).

A paired stable-ID ablation rejects the prediction that identity fields eliminate the ambiguity: accepted corruptions fall from 20/45 to 15/45, but the accepted stable-ID cases all return alternative matrices. A source-derived candidate enumerator then matches native outputs in all 18 corrupted cells. It shows that stable IDs can make the true matrix ineligible under predicate-local residual consistency, while no-ID paired-square +2 has 25 eligible decompositions and the L1 tie-break selects an alternative (L1=3) over the true matrix (L1=4). The generating matrix remains inside every search bound. This explains the tested failures, not general noise behavior ([ablation results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RELATION_IDENTITY_ABLATION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [ranking reconstruction results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_CANDIDATE_RANKING_RECONSTRUCTION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_candidate_ranking_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A full-seed follow-up runs 240 fresh-core sequences (two observations, five repeats, enabled/disabled controls). The seed's held rungs often resolve observations before GenInfer. Among enabled corrupted first members, 25 acquire a linear generator (5 exact and 20 alternate); all 120 later clean members are recognized transformed, but the 20 alternative-first cases grow an additional generator. Enabled runs use 55 quanta versus 180 disabled in this bounded sequence, with an enabled M-squared closure p90 of 221.8 ms and 252.2 ms maximum. This is end-to-end authored-seed evidence with a visible latency tail, not a natural-task estimate ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_RESIDUAL_ROBUSTNESS_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_residual_robustness_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A repeated-predicate factorial then separates matrix recovery from decomposition acceptance across three geometries and five perturbations: all 15 positives recover the known matrix, but only 4/12 perturbed cells do so, while 11/12 return `explained=true`. A held-out-relation probe independently scores withheld rows and confirms that a returned predicate-local residual applies systematically to that predicate: clean training predicts clean holdouts exactly, while shifted training predicts only equally shifted holdouts. Both are small direct-evaluator diagnostics ([recovery results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_REPEATED_PREDICATE_RECOVERY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [held-out results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_HELDOUT_RELATION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_heldout_relation_v1_20260926.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_heldout_relation_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A paired full-seed follow-up (180 sequences) shows that clean training observations can mint `[1,1,0,2]`, re-decode without a CapabilityAxis quantum, and then use that newly acquired generator to recognize a separate clean held-out base in all 15/15 positive sequences. Uniformly shifted training also transfers the matrix to clean heldouts in all 15 matched sequences. Inconsistent training acquires the expected matrix in only one of three predicate layouts. The two-row held-out target is ambiguous with a pre-existing `scale2`: every status is `recognized_transformed`, including the 90 disabled controls, and shifted heldouts always choose `scale2`. Route-aware amended scoring separates learned-generator use from other held-dimension recognition. Median clean-train closure is 32.79 ms with GenInfer enabled vs 1.21 ms disabled in this single capture, so acquisition has a substantial measured cost before amortization. This is evidence for the authored mechanism, not a held-out capability gain over the full seed ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_HELDOUT_TRANSFER_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_HELDOUT_TRANSFER_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_heldout_transfer_v1_20260926.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_heldout_transfer_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json)).

A v2 confirmation expands the held-out base to four relations and adds a paired induction-disabled control. All 15 clean enabled cases use the training-acquired matrix and exactly predict all four held-out rows; all 15 clean disabled controls instead mint a quantum at training and heldout stages. Uniform-shift training also transfers to clean heldouts in 15/15 cases. Inconsistent training acquires the matrix in 10/30 enabled sequences. Median clean-train closure is 24.69 ms enabled vs 0.80 ms disabled, indicating a material acquisition cost in this single authored fixture. The corrected analysis distinguishes training-acquired generators from alternate or later-minted routes. The initial analyzer source was not retained despite its hash being captured; this provenance failure is recorded, and the v2.1 route-aware analysis is the authoritative scorer ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_HELDOUT_TRANSFER_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_HELDOUT_TRANSFER_V2_PREREG_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_HELDOUT_TRANSFER_V2_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_heldout_transfer_v2_20260926.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_heldout_transfer_v2_1_analysis_20260926.json)).

An unregistered clean matrix/layout fixture-selection pilot then acquired the prior matrix and `shearA` in all three label layouts but failed to acquire `shearB` in one layout even though the observation ended `recognized_transformed`. This one-run result is only a preregistration aid; it reinforces that generator acquisition needs matrix-level scoring and that layout interactions belong in the next confirmatory matrix family ([exploratory results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MATRIX_LAYOUT_EXPLORATORY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_matrix_layout_exploratory_v1_20260926.json)).

A preregistered 360-worker full-seed matrix/layout/reuse sweep confirms the pilot interaction: 40/45 enabled training sequences acquire the expected matrix, and `shearB × adjacent_01` again fails in all five replicates despite recognized status. Every one of 280 held-out calls routed to a matrix acquired during training predicts all four rows exactly (1,120/1,120). The measured K=0/1/2/4 cumulative median closure is 3.80/4.61/5.64/7.61 ms enabled versus 0.87/1.73/2.78/5.64 ms disabled; there is no median crossover through K=4, although enabled sequences avoid the matched disabled arm's median 1/2/3/5 CapabilityAxis quanta. This establishes an authored transfer mechanism with acquisition cost, not runtime amortization or natural-task prevalence. Next extend the reuse horizon, isolate stage costs and add matrix/geometric diversity; separately measure frame separator distributions and factorized execution on representative workloads ([results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MATRIX_REUSE_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MATRIX_REUSE_PREREG_20260926.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/genmint_matrix_reuse_lock_20260926.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_matrix_reuse_v1_20260926.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_matrix_reuse_v1_analysis_20260926.json)).

An extended 540-worker sweep through 16 held-out applications separates training closure from application closure. In 8/9 matrix/layout cells, all training-acquired-matrix routes predict every four-row held-out task correctly (1,240 calls; 4,960/4,960 rows across K prefixes). Median application-only closure at K=16 is 27.4 ms enabled vs 42.0 ms disabled, offsetting a median training premium of about 2.9 ms. The pooled matched runtime crossover occurs at K=16; among the 40/45 cases that acquired the intended matrix during training, it appears at K=8. Prior-matrix layouts do not cross within this horizon. The `shearB × adjacent_01` miss defers acquisition: its first held-out call costs 548–595 ms, followed by 0.8–1.6 ms reuse calls, a substantial latency-tail failure. Thus amortization is demonstrated only on selected authored families and is matrix/layout dependent; it does not support natural prevalence or general tractability. The v2 first analyzer's 90-vs-108 cell-count error and corrected separate v2.1 analysis are both retained. Next expand transform/geometry diversity and measure the deferred-acquisition distribution, while keeping the representative workload separator/factorisation census as a separate gate ([extended results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MATRIX_REUSE_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MATRIX_REUSE_V2_PREREG_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_MATRIX_REUSE_V2_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_matrix_reuse_v2_20260926.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_genmint_matrix_reuse_v2_1_analysis_20260926.json)).

The map now provides categorized source and test links, but it does not map every callable to its tests or audit test coverage. Baseline manifests, unified metric records, and confirmation that all historical results correspond to the current checkout remain explicit Phase A tasks below.

For TermArena lifecycle, the public JSON shape APIs no longer retain temporary Terms; seven malformed scratch-operation paths preserve logical arena length, and malformed `add_rule` / late `import_rules` failures preserve TermArena length, rule count, and symbol allocation sequence. Codec manifest installation now stages the full rule list before committing; three successful-path replicates install 20 rules identically, while malformed-manifest failure remains source-reviewed because the manifest is embedded. General reclamation remains constrained by persistent rule, grammar, concept, and cache references ([shape results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_ARENA_SHAPE_CLEANUP_RESULTS.md), [failure-path results](SUBSTRATE_TERM_ARENA_FAILURE_ROLLBACK_RESULTS.md), [rule-import results](SUBSTRATE_RULE_IMPORT_ATOMICITY_RESULTS.md), [codec result](SUBSTRATE_CODEC_MANIFEST_INSTALL_RESULTS.md), [ownership audit](SUBSTRATE_TERM_ARENA_OWNERSHIP_AUDIT.md)).

Native access tracing now includes a concrete old-value comparison event for both branches of `SetAttrIfDifferent` and a named `UseTool` dispatch event; three fresh paired workers pass the mechanism controls and independent analysis. This closes only those two trace visibility gaps. Callback internals and scheduler/deferred queue work outside a RuleUID remain incomplete ([probe results](SUBSTRATE_ACCESS_TRACE_ATTR_COMPARE_TOOL_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [current coverage inventory](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_runtime_access_trace_coverage_v1_20260926.json)). Deferred queue instrumentation now records producer RuleIdx, enqueued/drained item counts, batches, and drain time by family. In one mixed fixture, RulePatch, TrackIdentity, and NetCollapse counters conserve across full and scoped-plus-fallback closure, with exact terminal state parity; the scoped-only arm exposes pending work. Scheduler slot scans and two queue families remain unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_DEFERRED_QUEUE_AUDIT_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_deferred_queue_audit_v2_1_analysis_20260926.json)). For cross-frame scheduling, native dependent-rule dirtying exists, while dependency-component frame scheduling remains unverified. Opt-in `run_rules_delta_report` logs graph mutation targets and directly affected Microtheories; `run_rules_snapshot_delta_report` is the exact graph-state oracle; `run_rules_scoped_delta_report` reports one scoped settlement. Target parity passed on eight direct effect families and deferred TrackIdentity; owner mapping passed four lifecycle/cross-owner fixtures. A scoped cross-owner write returned both owner IDs, and a queued deferred effect was explicitly reported pending. On sparse edge closure the caller-visible report cost 2.4–2.9× ordinary settlement at 64–2,048 matches. A locked 27-worker Bigville replay then used native affected-Microtheory IDs to drive a runtime-frame event queue: all event/full pairs matched exact projected state and had zero independent full-graph residual at all seven checkpoints; event settlement used 354–454 turns versus 1,100–1,300 full turns. Frame construction and initial host mutation seeds remain external; deferred-effect families, dependency-component grouping, and natural frame locality remain open ([implementation](../../../runners/dsl/src/graph.rs), [delta report result](SUBSTRATE_RULE_GRAPH_DELTA_REPORT_OVERHEAD_RESULTS.md), [Bigville protocol](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_NATIVE_OWNER_NOTIFICATION_PREREG_20260925.md), [Bigville result](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_NATIVE_OWNER_NOTIFICATION_RESULTS.md), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_native_owner_notification_20260925.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_NATIVE_FRAME_NOTIFICATION_PROTOCOL_20260925.md)).

## Phase A completion checklist

- [x] For each row, identify exact callable entrypoint(s), default/opt-in status, and source line anchors. The row-level JSON records normal/alternate paths, activation notes, and source lines; the generator now rejects a row without an entrypoint, owner note, or resolvable callable anchor.
- [ ] Link the strongest result, preregistration, and known counterevidence; mark stale/unverified provenance.
- [x] Record linked test files separately from workload results, preregistrations/locks, and raw reports. The machine-readable status says `coverage not audited`; it does not infer test coverage from a linked filename.
- [x] Record canonical state owner and any host shadow/cache for each path in the machine-readable entrypoint audit; remaining persistence and adapter completeness limits stay listed per row.
- [x] Add capability rows for previously unmapped public native-module families and supporting surfaces; all 90 top-level public modules now link to at least one capability row. This is source coverage, not proof of completeness or quality ([audit](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/native_module_inventory_audit_20260928.json)).
- [x] Generate a machine-readable counterpart and validate that every claim row has an evidence label and claim boundary.

## Initial priorities from the map

1. **Unify provenance and counters:** the repo has many strong local result documents, but a shared run manifest and metric vocabulary are prerequisites to compare them.
2. **Audit the real execution path:** several capabilities mix Rust, Python, seeds, adapters, and simulators; measure the active path rather than the intended architecture.
3. **Measure closure and matching cost:** rule execution under fresh-node growth and candidate explosion is the central engine-scale unknown.
4. **Measure structural locality:** derive frame/separator statistics on representative workload graphs; do not infer locality from sparse edge counts.
5. **Separate operation from acquisition:** report held-structure application separately from residual induction, minting, evaluation, and maintenance.
6. **Carry negative evidence forward:** local minima, failed transfer/generalisation, false recognition/minting, and host-boundary counterexamples should remain visible in the capability map.

## Current tractability evidence update

**Monolithic scheduler slot-work v1 (2026-09-26):** a locked 12-worker sparse no-match sweep at 1/8/32/128 rules passes 220/220 analyzer checks. Exact counters show linear scan work: first-call external polling and dirty-vector selection each visit `R` rules; warm fixed-point calls visit `R` external gates and probe `R` dirty bits, selecting none. Timings are audit-on microseconds and exclude queue drains/rule execution. Positive matching, scoped scheduling, and natural workloads remain unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_MONOLITHIC_SCHEDULER_WORK_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_monolithic_scheduler_work_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_monolithic_scheduler_work_v1_workers_raw_20260926.json)).

**Scheduler/deferred operation-work trace v1 (2026-09-28):** a source-locked three-worker synthetic replay adds per-`run_rules` operation IDs and a separate work trace. Scheduler pass/slot totals and enqueue/batch/item counts for `rule_edits` and `fb_samples` reconcile exactly with process-global scale-audit counters for this single-substrate fixture. This does not establish scheduler recall or scaling ([results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_WORK_TRACE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scheduler_work_trace_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_WORK_TRACE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md)).

**Live scheduler-work scope diagnostic v1 (2026-09-28):** a three-worker `describe_scene` request reproduces 21 operation IDs and exact operation-work totals, but the original counter-parity gate fails: graph-scoped rows show 37 scheduler passes versus 39 process-global audit passes, and 16 global framebuffer drain batches have no corresponding rows in the selected graph scope. The audit counters have no graph-scope key, unlike the work trace. This exposes an attribution boundary; it does not establish complete request work or task locality. The report preserves the failed gate and identifies per-scope counters and canonical state comparison as next requirements ([amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SCHEDULER_WORK_TRACE_V1_AMENDMENT_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_live_scheduler_work_trace_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_scheduler_work_trace_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/live_scheduler_work_trace_v1_20260928.json)).

**Live scheduler-work scoped parity v2 (2026-09-28):** a new scope-keyed scale snapshot exactly matches operation-work rows for all five scheduler measures and every enqueue/drain metric across all five queue families in three fresh workers. The same request has 21 repeatable operation IDs and the expected reply. The process-global audit still shows 16 framebuffer drain batches absent from the selected graph scope; those batches are not yet assigned to another substrate instance. This closes per-scope counter reconciliation for one route, not whole-request attribution, trace recall, locality, or scaling ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SCHEDULER_WORK_TRACE_V2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_scheduler_work_trace_v2_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_live_scheduler_work_trace_v2.py), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/live_scheduler_work_trace_v2_20260928.json)).

**All-scope operation work and framebuffer drain attribution v4 (2026-09-28):** with work-only tracing enabled across graph scopes and typed IDs for `run_rules`, `apply_rule_once`, and `apply_rule_batch`, three workers reproduce six scopes and 67 operations. Every scope reconciles, and scope sums equal all process-global scheduler and five-family queue counters. The 16 framebuffer drain batches are assigned to two scopes. This closes counter attribution for named operation families on one dialogue route; matcher/Term work, other host entrypoints, trace overhead, frame locality, and scaling remain unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SCHEDULER_WORK_TRACE_V4_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_scheduler_work_trace_v4_analysis_20260928.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SCHEDULER_WORK_TRACE_V4_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_live_scheduler_work_trace_v4.py), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/live_scheduler_work_trace_v4_20260928.json)).

**All-scope work-trace overhead pilot v1 (2026-09-28):** in three paired blocks on the same live route, trace-on adds median 3.66% request wall time and 3.67% process CPU with scale audit enabled in both arms; ranges are 2.75–4.29% and 2.73–4.52%. Replies, task-level observables, and integer work counters match. This is a small route-specific estimate, not a general runtime overhead bound; trace-only/audit-off, workload-size, memory, and energy comparisons remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_WORK_TRACE_OVERHEAD_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/work_trace_overhead_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_WORK_TRACE_OVERHEAD_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_work_trace_overhead_v1.py), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/work_trace_overhead_v1_20260928.json)).

**Positive-match dirty-density scheduler v1 (2026-09-26):** 54 fresh locked workers pass 219/219 checks over 8/32/128 rules, three dirty counts and prefix/suffix positions. Plain/audit graph and report parity holds; each selected positive one-edge rule produces one derived edge. Fixed-point probes show first-dirty-position sensitivity (`R+1` prefix total versus `2R-k+1` suffix total); external polling and dirty-vector construction each remain full-vector scans per applicable pass. This is exact scheduler accounting for one authored mutation family. Multi-edge joins, repeated requeues, scoped costs, deferred queue coverage and natural task workloads remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_DIRTY_DENSITY_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_scheduler_dirty_density_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_DIRTY_DENSITY_V1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md)).

**Deferred queue family coverage v1 (2026-09-26):** all five instrumented families now have dynamic full-closure enqueue/drain evidence across v2 and v1. This follow-up covers `rule_edits` and `fb_samples` at 1/2/4/8 writer counts; 75/75 checks confirm queue conservation, per-rule producer attribution, audit-on/off whole-graph/report parity, target edits, and framebuffer grid output. Earlier v2 scoped-pending/global-fallback results cover only RulePatch, TrackIdentity and NetCollapse. Scoped behavior for RuleEdit/FbSample and natural queue prevalence remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_REMAINING_DEFERRED_QUEUE_FAMILIES_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_remaining_deferred_queue_families_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/remaining_deferred_queue_families_v1_lock_20260926.json)).

**All-family deferred fallback coverage (2026-09-26):** a separate 12-worker, 207-check evaluation confirms scoped settlement leaves `rule_edits` and `fb_samples` pending, then global fallback drains them and reaches exact whole-graph parity with full closure. Alongside v2, all five queue families now have fixture-level scoped-pending/global-fallback evidence. Natural prevalence, concurrent producers, mixed arbitrary queue interactions, and production locality remain unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_REMAINING_DEFERRED_QUEUE_FALLBACK_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_remaining_deferred_queue_fallback_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_remaining_deferred_queue_fallback_v1_workers_raw_20260926.json)).

**Positive two-edge join × queue multiplicity v1 (2026-09-26):** across 27 locked cells and 165/165 checks, full-closure queue items scale exactly with `R×M`, per-writer attribution matches successful join multiplicity, and audit-on/off graph/report states match. Paired-workload candidate tests (writer plus its target rule) rise 6→24→276 at multiplicity 1→4→16; two tests per writer/target pair come from the fixed target rule. This is one authored pattern, not a general complexity exponent or natural-task estimate. End-to-end time/memory and larger/skewed joins remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_JOIN_MULTIPLICITY_DEFERRED_QUEUE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_join_multiplicity_deferred_queue_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_join_multiplicity_deferred_queue_v1_workers_raw_20260926.json)).

**Positive join clause attribution v1.1 (2026-09-26):** across 60 locked cells and 545/545 checks, output and queue work are unchanged by the two authored orders. `export_rules()` confirms installation reorders singleton trigger/game edges first, then the equal-cardinality path edges in an order affected by tie handling. At `M=64`, paired-workload tests are 4,164 vs 132; writer-only work is 4,162 vs 130, with the difference on the final path edge (4,096 vs 64). This measures authoring-order sensitivity through the existing greedy reorderer, not matcher behavior with reordering disabled, and gives no general complexity or total-time result ([results](SUBSTRATE_JOIN_MULTIPLICITY_CLAUSE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_JOIN_MULTIPLICITY_CLAUSE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_1_PREREG_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_join_multiplicity_clause_attribution_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_join_multiplicity_clause_attribution_v1_1_workers_raw_20260926.json)).

**Two-edge join degree skew v1 (2026-09-26):** 126 locked cells pass 1,013/1,013 checks at fixed successful multiplicity with 0/4/16 decoy-edge ratios, three incidence splits, two authored orders, and three replications. Installed-order readback confirms the current global edge-count reorder handles unequal relation populations consistently; equal-cardinality ties retain author-order sensitivity. At `M=64` with 1,024 balanced decoys, work is 332,354 vs 642 writer candidate tests, while right-only placement gives 130 under both orders. Three-replicate plain closure medians are 2.257 ms vs 0.557 ms in the balanced pair, a noisy fixture-specific signal. No natural-task distribution, memory bound, general complexity, or optimizer effect is established ([results](SUBSTRATE_JOIN_DEGREE_SKEW_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_JOIN_DEGREE_SKEW_V1_PREREG_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_join_degree_skew_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_join_degree_skew_v1_workers_raw_20260926.json)).

**Endpoint-degree exact order oracle v1 (2026-09-27 local / 2026-09-26 UTC):** across 54 locked cells and 491/491 checks, an endpoint-specific cost model exactly predicts native writer candidate counts for both orders in this two-edge schema. Candidate work falls 2.2×–518×; after charging Python planning and writer-rule installation, combined wall cost is materially better only at the largest/highest-decoy point (2.355 ms→0.924 ms) and loses on six of nine settings. This is an exact schema-specific oracle, not a general optimizer; it establishes that an amortization/skip threshold is required before broadening the policy ([results](SUBSTRATE_ENDPOINT_DEGREE_JOIN_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_ENDPOINT_DEGREE_JOIN_ORDER_V1_PREREG_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_endpoint_degree_join_order_v1_analysis_20260926.json), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_endpoint_degree_join_order_v1_workers_raw_20260926.json)).

ProblemShape global per-rule attribution confirms large local work reductions but sub-one-percent whole-seed closure reductions on two authored N=32 workloads. The attempted rule-order control V1.3 is invalidated: the transformed seed inputs were live symlinks, corpus drift occurred during setup, and a reproducibility mismatch remains unexplained. No causal claim is supported by that partial pair. A valid follow-up requires an immutable identical seed snapshot for both arms, per-worker digest checks, and full ordered rule/load diagnostics before repeated comparisons ([measurement record](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RULE_ORDER_PRESERVATION_V1_3_DIAGNOSTIC.md), [global attribution](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_GLOBAL_RULE_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS.md)).

V1.4's locked same-corpus order-preservation comparison passed all 12 semantic/order pairs. At N=32, eventized rules retained in their original slots reduced their own work without changing any non-ProblemShape rule counters; whole-seed Term evaluations fell 0.48%, candidate tests 0.96%, with unchanged enumeration counts and doubled graph writes. Timing showed no resolved benefit. V1.7 subsequently supplied the same-snapshot three-arm control and resolved the append-order collateral mechanism; the historical V1.4 “next” statement is superseded ([V1.4 results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RULE_ORDER_PRESERVATION_V1_4_RESULTS.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_problem_shape_rule_order_preservation_v1_4_analysis_20260925.json), [V1.7 results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RULE_ORDER_PRESERVATION_V1_7_RESULTS.md)).

A randomized same-corpus three-arm control now identifies rule-placement collateral on two N=32 authored ProblemShape workloads: appending replacements adds 1,536/2,094 Terms to six unrelated rules, while in-place replacement leaves all non-ProblemShape counters identical.
