The whole-seed savings remain under 0.5% in Terms, enumeration counts are unchanged, graph writes double, and timing has no resolved benefit. The measurement also uncovered a loader reproducibility defect: cold and warm seed-index caches over identical bytes produced different seed order/rule indices. A schema-v5 repair now preserves cold/warm state on a frozen 833-file corpus. A V3 follow-up confirms exact cold/warm equality for full serialized graph state (24,784 nodes, 11,090 edges), grammar, rule store, rule access sets, active-rule order, and seed-load order; 2,289 rules and the same 11 seed failures appear in both loads. Lifecycle probes confirm baseline-equivalent state after v4-schema, malformed-JSON, and bad-payload-digest rebuilds, two simultaneous cold builders, final warm load, and a valid-JSON source-byte change that changes the cache source digest while preserving boot state. A current-source application comparison now confirms task/work parity for recognition and recurrent-residual N=32 mixes; broader order-sensitive workload and corpus coverage remain open ([repair result](SUBSTRATE_SEED_INDEX_CACHE_ORDER_FIX_V2_RESULTS.md), [V2 probe](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_cache_order_probe_v2_20260925.json), [V3 runner](../../../scripts/substrate_seed_index_cache_state_probe_v3.py), [V3 report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_cache_state_probe_v3_20260925.json), [lifecycle runner](../../../scripts/substrate_seed_index_cache_lifecycle_probe_v1.py), [lifecycle report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_cache_lifecycle_probe_v1_20260925.json), [source-invalidation runner](../../../scripts/substrate_seed_index_source_invalidation_probe_v1.py), [source-invalidation report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_source_invalidation_v1_20260925.json), [application parity result](SUBSTRATE_SEED_INDEX_COLD_WARM_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RESULTS.md), [three-arm result](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RULE_ORDER_PRESERVATION_V1_7_RESULTS.md)).

The seed-index cache now preserves manifest traversal order across cold and warm loads using cache schema v5; v4 cache files are rejected and rebuilt. V3 confirms full boot-state equality across cold and warm cache paths on the frozen corpus; lifecycle and source-invalidation probes confirm selected cache recovery, simultaneous cold-writer parity, and digest-triggered rebuilds. A locked current-source application probe adds cold/warm parity on two authored N=32 ProblemShape mixes: all four workers pass semantics/fixed-point gates, and task outcomes, rule/load order, and operation-count projections match within each pair. Boot takes 24–29 s versus 0.8–1.1 s for summed task observations in this single-run-per-arm measurement; this is descriptive and makes loader-stage attribution and repeated timing the next scale question. Broader corpus and order-sensitive application coverage remain open ([repair result](SUBSTRATE_SEED_INDEX_CACHE_ORDER_FIX_V2_RESULTS.md), [V3 probe report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_cache_state_probe_v3_20260925.json), [lifecycle report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_cache_lifecycle_probe_v1_20260925.json), [source-invalidation report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_index_source_invalidation_v1_20260925.json), [application parity result](SUBSTRATE_SEED_INDEX_COLD_WARM_PROBLEM_SHAPE_RESULTS.md), [implementation](../../../substrate/boot_all.py)).

A four-process cProfile follow-up finds warm index lookup at 0.37–0.39 profiled seconds, compared with 3.63–4.38 seconds cold. Recurring costs include 748 native seed-manifest installs (9.83–11.82 cumulative seconds) and the boot-time self-model provenance mirror (12.51–13.86 seconds, including overlapping export/JSON work). The profile preserves full boot-state equality but includes instrumentation overhead; no optimization or latency claim follows. Evaluate the provenance mirror's consumers/completeness before proposing lazy construction ([profile results](SUBSTRATE_SEED_BOOT_PROFILE_RESULTS.md), [raw data](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_seed_boot_profile_v1_20260925.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SEED_BOOT_PROFILE_PREREG_20260925.md)).

**Conversation scoped owner wakeup (2026-09-26):** a preregistered four-frame diagnostic on one authored connected dialogue matched native full closure at all ten checkpoints when the caller followed `affected_microtheories`; omitting those cross-frame wakeups left the goal-origin receipts stale after remote resolution, and final full closure restored exact graph parity. This confirms useful owner reports on one task path. It does not establish an automatic production scheduler or locality prevalence. The caller-side queue was slower than global closure in this small single run. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_SCOPED_OWNER_WAKEUP_RESULTS_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_conversation_scoped_owner_wakeup_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_conversation_scoped_owner_wakeup_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Interleaved conversation v2 diagnostic (2026-09-26):** a second authored fixture with two interlocutors and two goals preserved exact full-closure state under the caller queue at all ten checkpoints; suppressing remote notifications left receipts stale after their result checkpoints, with exact global recovery. Temporal-only goal transitions are in the benchmark-loaded experimental seed; an AST source audit found no domain call sites for the host transition helper. A benchmark-only addressee-guard variant matches this fixture's intended task assignment and preserves owner-queue parity, but it does not solve same-addressee concurrent-goal identity. See [v2 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_SCOPED_OWNER_WAKEUP_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [candidate v3 results](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_GOAL_ASSOCIATION_V3_RESULTS_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_discourse_goal_association_v3.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_goal_association_v3_analysis_20260926.json).

**Same-addressee Goal identity scale (2026-09-26):** over 2/4/8 concurrent Goals, a benchmark-only explicit `event -> for_goal -> Goal` guard matches the intended transition oracle where temporal and recipient-only guards fail. Following affected-owner notifications matches full closure at every checkpoint; omitting them leaves result receipts stale until global recovery. At eight Goals, the caller queue uses 1.30× candidate pairs and 2.35× wall time versus full closure in one small capture. The identity edge is supplied by the harness; no production schema or general performance claim follows. See [results](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_GOAL_IDENTITY_SCALE_V4_RESULTS_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_discourse_goal_identity_scale_v4.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_goal_identity_scale_v4_analysis_20260926.json).

**Same-addressee Goal scale extension (2026-09-26):** explicit task-link and owner-queue parity pass at N=16/32/64 (448 checkpoints total); temporal and recipient-only controls still fail task assignment. Candidate-pair and Term counts grow about 3.9–4.0× per doubling from 16 to 64 in this generated workload. At N=64 the owner queue uses 1.27× candidates, 1.19× Terms, and 1.59× wall time versus full closure in one capture. These are workload-specific operation counts and single-run timing, not a formal bound or production claim. See [results](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_GOAL_IDENTITY_SCALE_V4_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_discourse_goal_identity_scale_v4_1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_goal_identity_scale_v4_1_analysis_20260926.json).

**High induced-boundary width (2026-09-26):** 72 exact chain/cycle/clique cases pass through k=8. At k=8, boundary widths are 1/2/7 with 66/174/9,840 elimination rows. A v1.1 source-locked rerun independently verifies all 72 cases, the canonical manifest, parent log, and worker stdout hashes; at N=20,k=8, dense clique message+VE takes 51.26 ms versus 35.71 ms for global JT. The retained initial harness undercoverage is disclosed; the corrected replication passes. This confirms width sensitivity in a controlled factor family, not natural task boundary distributions or an execution speedup. See [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_HIGH_BOUNDARY_WIDTH_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_HIGH_BOUNDARY_WIDTH_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [manifest-bearing raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_high_boundary_width_v1_1_20260926.json), [current-source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/bigville_high_boundary_width_v1_1_lock_20260926.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_high_boundary_width_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json). Original v1 artifacts remain preserved.

**Connected conversation boundary panel (2026-09-26):** a 12-case native census over one authored common-ground stress sequence and generated same-addressee concurrent goals (N=2/4/8) passes fixed-point, exact goal-provenance, graph-replication, and structural reconstruction checks. At N=2/4/8, direct cross-frame edges are 3/7/15, shared boundary incidences 8/16/32, and pairwise frame projection widths 1/3/7. The latter clique-expands global shared nodes and does not imply independent pairwise interactions. Candidate pairs rise 119/386/1,364; this measures global closure, not partitioned speed. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONNECTED_CONVERSATION_BOUNDARY_PANEL_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONNECTED_CONVERSATION_BOUNDARY_PANEL_PREREG_20260926.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_connected_conversation_boundary_panel_v1_analysis_20260926.json).


## Follow-up capability audit (2026-09-26)

**RatioInduce:** native direct-call within-frame ratio detection is implemented and the existing synthetic probes recover their five planted integer ratios; tested controls are refused. A source-semantics replay demonstrates that 3:2 emits matrix `[1,0,0,3]`, which changes the extent ratio to 9:2 under `apply_linear_once`. The returned aspect ratio also does not encode the population’s between-frame scale factors. No `seeds/*.json` consumer exists. Treat scale-invariant representation, faithful map application, seed integration, population collection, durable minting, and held-out reuse as open rung-3 items. A v1.3 direct-call cost pilot on one exact positive family passes 11/11 semantic/counter cells: medians scale 49.16× for 64× more frames and 48.22× for 64× more relations/frame. This supports only a narrow already-materialized operator envelope; semantic validity and production integration remain open. See [`SUBSTRATE_RATIOINDUCE_COST_PILOT_V1_3_RESULTS_20260926.md`](SUBSTRATE_RATIOINDUCE_COST_PILOT_V1_3_RESULTS_20260926.md). The formant audio probe also floors. Details and correction are in [`SUBSTRATE_RATIOINDUCE_CAPABILITY_AUDIT_20260926.md`](SUBSTRATE_RATIOINDUCE_CAPABILITY_AUDIT_20260926.md).


**Closure termination capability:** a conservative analyzer and native verification path cover a restricted finite-key monotone insertion fragment. With fixed admitted rules, complete finite initial state, no external mutations during closure, and only literal-key node deduplication plus empty-attribute edge insertion, the possible graph fact universe is finite and the progressing firing bound is polynomial in initial node count. One native recursive fixture reaches fixed point within the bound. General rule closure with unrestricted fresh-name invention remains unbounded/unresolved. See [formal scope, proof, captures, and next gates](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_TERMINATION_20260926.md).


**Restricted closure replication:** expanded the native finite-key fragment check to 18 cases over one/two/four minted signatures, sparse cycles and dense initial graphs, and three substrate constructions per cell. Exact closure and analyzer-derived progress bounds pass throughout; tested closures settle in three passes. This strengthens evidence for the fragment but does not broaden its formal admission syntax or establish general weak acyclicity. See [theorem and boundaries](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_TERMINATION_20260926.md) and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_families_v1_analysis_20260926.json).


**Restricted closure scale:** the finite-key insertion fragment passes an amended 54-case native sweep through 64 initial nodes for sparse-cycle/dense graphs and 1/2/4 authored signatures, including an explicit bool/int key-coalescing case. Exact terminal graphs and fixed-point/bound checks all pass. Static syntactic signature count is a safe upper bound when native equality coalesces keys (e.g. `true == 1`). Evidence is one positive rule schema and does not establish parser equivalence for every admitted program. See [scale results and limitations](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md).

**Restricted closure cost scale:** a fixed recursive insertion schema on directed cycles was measured at N=16–1,024 and M=1/2/4 mint signatures, with exact terminal graph hashes and termination bounds independently checked in two processes. Median closure time rises roughly with final edge count for each fixed M; at N=1,024 it is 1.3–1.4 ms (M=1), 4.0–4.2 ms (M=2), and 13.8–17.0 ms (M=4). This is a narrow cost envelope for one topology/rule schema, not a general CPU-time guarantee. See [results and limitations](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_SCALE_COST_RESULTS_20260926.md), [v1.1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_scale_cost_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json), and [protocol](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_SCALE_COST_PREREG_20260926.md).

**Restricted closure join/topology envelope:** the fresh-process replicated 210-case matrix varies positive join arity and path/cycle/layered graph structure under exact finite-key checks. The N=128 layered cases have 4,095 input edges and take 2.1–6.1 ms, compared with about 0.1–0.2 ms for the sparse path/cycle cases; because edge count and degree distribution change together, this is a density/branching stress rather than a topology-only result. Match-binding counts come from a separate enumerator, not a native candidate counter. See [results and limits](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_JOIN_TOPOLOGY_RESULTS_20260926.md), [paired analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_join_topology_v1_1_pair_analysis_20260926.json), and [protocol](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_JOIN_TOPOLOGY_PREREG_20260926.md).

**Restricted closure work attribution:** on the same locked cases, two source-complete audit captures replicate native candidate expansion and per-rule totals exactly. For the N=128 layered q=3 case, the engine enumerates 32,004 candidate pairs over intermediate joins for 3,969 complete path bindings; q=2 has 24,066 candidates for 7,938 bindings. Later joins use bound-node local expansion; edge-only rules produce no Term evaluations. This explains the measured cost difference at the counted-work level but remains one positive finite-key schema and authored topology. See [work attribution results](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_JOIN_WORK_ATTRIBUTION_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_join_work_attribution_v1_3_analysis_20260926.json), and [source-complete amendment](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_JOIN_WORK_ATTRIBUTION_V1_3_AMENDMENT_20260926.md).


**Restricted closure rule interactions:** expanded the finite-key fragment evidence to native three-rule programs with one-/two-edge positive joins and five edge types. Across 36 cases, an independent interpreter matches exact graph closure and all statuses/progress bounds pass; rule order alters pass counts but not final semantics. This remains one generated program family and does not establish analyzer-runtime equivalence for every admitted syntax. See [interaction results and next limits](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_RULE_INTERACTION_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md).


**Restricted closure self-joins:** 72 native/reference cases pass for repeated-variable loops, reciprocal-edge joins, and three-edge cycle rules across positive/null graph strata and rule orders. The independent interpreter matches exact closure and all runs stay within the finite-fact bound. A versioned correction narrows E to initially present or rule-emitted edge types. This is selected-syntax evidence, not full analyzer/runtime grammar equivalence. See [results and claim boundary](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_SELF_JOIN_V1_1_RESULTS_20260926.md).

**Cheap-cut complexity conditions (2026-09-26):** exact finite-domain inference has a linear-in-frame-count bound only when full induced width (including local interiors and factor scopes), domain size, local evaluation work, and interface width are bounded. Closure equivalence additionally needs complete fair cross-frame notification; a linear work bound requires bounds on fan-out and rework, not merely small cuts. Current owner-scoped reports explicitly do not certify complete notifications, and task-derived partition construction/maintenance remains a measured cost. See [sufficient conditions and gates](SUBSTRATE_CHEAP_CUT_SUFFICIENT_CONDITIONS_20260926.md) and the [Phase C evidence audit](SUBSTRATE_CHEAP_CUT_GATE_AUDIT_20260926.md).

**Authored-capture cut ranking (2026-09-26):** a retrospective independent analysis ranks three cuts on the existing Bigville (500 task nodes) and connected-dialogue (12 task nodes) captures. Authored, balanced-ID, and dependency-min-fill arms have identical cut metrics on both snapshots. Dialogue exposes 40 graph incidences between task-owned and global nodes. This does not execute candidate partitions or satisfy mutation/parity gates; fresh confirmatory work remains required. See [results and limits](SUBSTRATE_AUTHORED_CAPTURE_CUT_RANKING_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_authored_capture_cut_ranking_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Bigville cut mutation (2026-09-26):** six fresh workers capture insertion/retraction/reweight with trace-on/off parity; independent graph, residual, and cut checks pass. All candidate partitions refuse on `reads_any`. The selected single edge has zero closure effect, so this is structurally complete but semantically insensitive and cannot support omitted-boundary parity. Dialogue mutation remains unsupported. See [results and boundaries](SUBSTRATE_AUTHORED_BIGVILLE_CUT_MUTATION_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_authored_bigville_cut_mutation_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Bigville dynamic-link cut ranking (2026-09-26):** across 9 fresh workers and 63 checkpoints, current-source complete graph captures pass independent residual and metric reconstruction. At ring/star/random-connected insertion, authored and balanced-ID groups have 198–200 directed causal crossings, while contiguous min-fill groups have 592–680 despite the same induced-width bound of 2. This identifies a limitation of min-fill order as a partition heuristic on these generated graphs. Alternative partitions still refuse because the active CAN rule reads globally; no execution-cost claim follows. See [results](SUBSTRATE_BIGVILLE_DYNAMIC_LINK_CUT_CAPTURE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_bigville_dynamic_link_cut_capture_v1_analysis_20260926.json).

**Finite-key closure degree sensitivity (2026-09-26):** the corrected v1.1 edge-count-matched matrix measures an authored injective positive-join family on bidirected paths, stars, and balanced trees. At N=128, all have 254 directed edges, yet star q=2 costs 33,020 candidate-pair visits and 8.50 ms median, compared with 1,520/0.235 ms for paths and 1,768/0.291 ms for balanced trees. A star q=3 negative control has no complete injective matches but still visits 48,768 candidates. This establishes degree/topology sensitivity for this matcher fixture; it does not establish natural graph frequency, isolate degree as the only cause, or demonstrate tractability on task-derived frames. See [results and claim limits](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_DEGREE_MATCHED_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mint_termination_degree_matched_v1_1_analysis_20260926.json), and [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_finite_key_degree_matched_v1_1.py).

**Retrospective task-derived graph/work audit (2026-09-26):** an independent reanalysis of one prior ten-event connected dialogue capture confirms identical 261-node/235-edge graphs across three execution arms, 12 task-owned nodes in three frames, two direct cross-frame edges, and 40 frame-to-global boundary incidences. Whole-graph weak degree has median 1, p95 6, and maximum 35 on an unframed Lexicon node; recorded native closure made 135 candidate visits and 558 Term evaluations per ten-event run. This separates sparse task-frame width from global high-degree seed structure, but the run is historical and the original dirty-worktree materializer/`py.rs`/native binary source identities are not fully recoverable. It is not a current-rule or natural-task estimate. See [retrospective results and limits](SUBSTRATE_RETROSPECTIVE_RUNTIME_TASK_CENSUS_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_retrospective_runtime_task_census_v1_20260926.json), and [protocol](SUBSTRATE_RETROSPECTIVE_RUNTIME_TASK_CENSUS_PREREG_20260926.md).

**Current task event-owner integration (2026-09-26):** a source-locked replication first confirms that directly materialized, unowned lifecycle events are rejected by current identity-guarded rules, leaving Goals proposed. The amended fixture routes six lifecycle events through the production `_record_live_goal_event` helper; all three process arms and independent task/structure checks pass, resolving both Goals to their own event sequences. The 263-node/243-edge task graph has 40 frame-to-global boundary incidences and reported frame-interaction width 2. Combined work accounting, including helper-internal closures, is 97 candidate visits/316 Term evaluations per ten-event fixture. This validates one helper path on a harness-supplied inbound envelope, not natural runtime prevalence or task locality across conversations. See [v2.1 results and limits](SUBSTRATE_DISCOURSE_CONNECTED_TASK_CENSUS_V2_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/discourse_connected_task_census_v2_1_lock_20260926.json), [combined work analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_connected_task_census_v2_1_work_attribution_20260926.json), and [preserved v2 failure](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_discourse_connected_task_census_v2_20260926.json).

**Full-seed live lifecycle cost (2026-09-26):** the amended v1.4 threaded `describe_scene` capture locks the full seed manifest globs and relevant Python source trees and reproduces all six live helper calls across three workers. Per two-request schedule, the helper runs account for 337,522 candidate-pair visits and 1,686,456 Term evaluations; each event kind's two calls use about 112,500 candidates/562,000 Terms, with median helper elapsed time 42–46 ms per call. These counts cover the entire active full-seed rule set and event preparation plus native closure. This is a controlled single-route schedule and not evidence of natural workload frequency, scoped-closure benefit, or general concurrency safety. The narrower v1.3 source lock is excluded from the headline. See [v1.4 results and limitations](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_THREADED_INTERLEAVING_V1_4_RESULTS_20260926.md), [full source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_describe_scene_threaded_interleaving_v1_4_lock_20260926.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_threaded_interleaving_v1_4_analysis_20260926.json), and [protocol amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_THREADED_INTERLEAVING_V1_4_AMENDMENT_20260926.md).

**Source-matched live action route work (2026-09-26):** describe-scene v1.5 and self-report v1.7 use the same full source closure (1,669 files), native binary/build, Python version, and platform. Each two-request schedule has six helper calls and exact work replication across three processes. Candidate/Term counts are 337,522/1,686,456 for describe-scene and 49,888/528,489 for self-report; these fixture-specific ratios (6.77×/3.19×) are descriptive because task content, graph history, and threaded-versus-sequential scheduling differ. The work records include the entire loaded rule set and helper preparation. See [comparison results and limits](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_ACTION_ROUTE_WORK_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_action_route_work_comparison_v1_20260926.json), [describe-scene v1.5 result](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_THREADED_INTERLEAVING_V1_5_RESULTS_20260926.md), and [self-report v1.7 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_report_lifecycle_identity_integration_v1_7_analysis_20260926.json).

**Self-identity route, Goal, and reply ownership (2026-09-26):** three full-source-locked workers pass 21 v3.1 gates for two action forms, two question forms, one external-object negative, and one injected faculty failure per worker. Successful action replies have one resolved identity Goal; the released receipt commits both Goal and exact outbound Message, and the chat transcript matches the returned surface. On failure, the Goal blocks, the graph candidate is suppressed from outbound readback, and the explicit error is the sole transcript surface. This closes duplicate Goal and output/readback inconsistencies for these forms. It does not establish broader language coverage, answer quality, or tractability. See [v3.1 results and limits](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_IDENTITY_REPLY_RELEASE_V3_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_IDENTITY_REPLY_RELEASE_V3_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_identity_reply_release_v3_1_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_self_identity_reply_release_v3_1_lock_20260926.json).
**Held-topic evidence and answer release (2026-09-26):** three source-locked workers pass 31 gates for five exact answers over teacher-supplied copper, basalt, and hexagon evidence, across three comprehension routes. Each answer is grounded in a same-event Goal/evidence path and released with a matching receipt; unknown unobtainium remains unresolved without leaking a known fact. Full-seed boot is 12.07 s median and held-answer turns take 123–285 ms, with 53.6k–67.7k candidate enumerations and 542k–631k Term evaluations across all active rules. The unknown no-result topic grounder runs twice per turn; topic Goals also remain status `active` after either answer or unresolved outcome. Task projections expose 9–10 counted boundary incidences, not complete separators/treewidth. This is a fixed six-turn capability probe, not a coverage, natural workload, or tractability estimate. See [v1.2 results and limits](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_HELD_TOPIC_EVIDENCE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_2_20260926.json), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_2_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_held_topic_evidence_v1_2_lock_20260926.json).

**Held-topic grounding work attribution (2026-09-26):** a v1.3 diagnostic repeats the three-worker held-topic panel with per-call stacks and graph/request snapshots. On each unknown turn, the first no-result call is `_answer_topic_reply`'s inline local attempt; the second is `scan_and_sync_ground` fulfilling the same graph-minted GroundRequest. No graph nodes or edges are added between calls, while that request transitions from requested to running and Goal outcome from unset to pending. This identifies a duplicate local-check candidate, but the inline call can also return a local answer before dispatch; local-hit and miss controls must establish behavior before changing it. [V1.3 report](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_HELD_TOPIC_EVIDENCE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_HELD_TOPIC_EVIDENCE_V1_3_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw trace](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_3_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_held_topic_evidence_v1_3_lock_20260926.json).

**Held-topic local-hit/miss integration (2026-09-26):** three source-locked workers pass 35 gates. A controlled hit injected at `_ground_local` traverses ordinary `ground_topic`/`learn_from_teacher`, creates supported held evidence, resolves and releases the exact answer, and supersedes both graph requests after one local attempt. The unknown miss uses two attempts (inline then request fulfillment), both requests fail, and the unresolved/no-leak answer is preserved. The hit is an integration fixture, not a real Wikipedia hit-rate sample; turn timing/work comparisons are not causal due to graph-history differences. See [v1.4 results and limits](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_HELD_TOPIC_EVIDENCE_V1_4_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_4_20260926.json), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_4_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_held_topic_evidence_v1_4_lock_20260926.json).

**Same-turn topic local-miss reuse (2026-09-26):** three source-stable workers pass 36 gates after the adapter records a completed local-only miss on its exact same-turn GroundRequest. Dispatch consumes the mark instead of invoking the local phrase grounder again; GroundRequest status/error, independent SearchRequest failure, unresolved answer, and no-leak release remain intact. Unknown calls drop from 2 to 1 while native candidate/Term counts match the prior control. Wall-time comparison is noncausal across differing source closures. This is one controlled miss path, not a natural retrieval-rate or broad tractability result. See [v1.5.1 results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_HELD_TOPIC_EVIDENCE_V1_5_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_5_1_20260926.json), [raw workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_5_1_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_held_topic_evidence_v1_5_1_lock_20260926.json).

**Live held-topic full graph cut (2026-09-26):** three source-locked workers capture every typed edge incident to the declared answer-task nodes. Across five positive answer turns, the induced operational subgraphs contain 11 nodes/11 internal edges with min-fill width upper bound 2, but their complete direct cut has 41–46 crossing incidences and 26–30 unique external neighbors. The earlier selected-edge projection counted only 9–10 incidences. Shared Agent/Channel/Discourse/Intent/Mention boundary nodes mean these counts are not independent separator dimensions; this manually selected evidence path is not a learned frame or natural cheap-cut estimate. See [v1.6 structural results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_HELD_TOPIC_EVIDENCE_V1_6_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_6_20260926.json), [raw graph cuts](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_6_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_held_topic_evidence_v1_6_lock_20260926.json).

**Held-topic prospective pre-outcome frame capture (2026-10-05):** three current-source workers record seven authored prompts at the pre-closure ingress boundary. The frozen input projection is 6–9 nodes with state-graph width upper bound 2 and 8–10 crossing incidences, but the following native trace touches 7,204–11,814 distinct nodes outside that cut (123,816–440,129 access events per turn). Capture integrity passes; the positive held-answer release check fails on all three “Can you tell me about copper?” turns. This is an outcome-blind ingress-frame method and evidence of broad downstream native graph access on one authored family, not natural-task prevalence, complete factor scope, or a tractability estimate. See [results](SUBSTRATE_PREOUTCOME_TASK_FRAME_CAPTURE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_PREOUTCOME_TASK_FRAME_CAPTURE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/preoutcome_task_frame_capture_v1_20261005/capture.json.gz), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/preoutcome_task_frame_capture_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

**Held-topic native access hotspot attribution (2026-10-05):** a hash-linked analysis confirms identical per-prompt access profiles across all three workers and matches the relevant seed sources to the capture lock and Rust operation sources to the native build manifest. Three resident rules account for 345,701 first-turn outside-cut access events: `slang_variant_bind_denotes` performs 15 `AddNodeIfAbsent` Concept-population scans (7,164 candidates each), while `curiosity_targets_ambivalent_nodes` and `gr_condition_lapse` continue population work on each held-topic turn. The first-turn ShapeDecode ladder adds 57,057 accesses. This separates first-pass lexicon/decoding work from repeated ambient population work; it does not prove the latter can be deferred without semantic change. The opt-in per-area settlement API is not used by conversation, and it has no request-priority lane/residual continuation. Next compare global closure with unlimited barrier/async area settlement on isolated branches, then add a priority/residual interface only if full-state parity holds. See [results](SUBSTRATE_PREOUTCOME_TASK_FRAME_HOTSPOT_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_preoutcome_task_frame_hotspots_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/preoutcome_task_frame_hotspots_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

**Held-topic paired trace/capability replay (2026-09-28):** on the actual workspace, three counterbalanced baseline/combined-trace process pairs pass all 24 independent source, work-accounting, frame-schema, and semantic-parity checks. The full local `data/` corpus is content-locked as well as Python/seed/native inputs. Each arm passes 9/10 fixed outcomes: all teaching calls and six of seven query forms succeed; “Can you tell me about copper?” remains unresolved and asks for grounding information. The meta held-answer prompts, table query, unknown-topic no-leak control, and injected local-hit control pass. Frames have 10–13 nodes and induced-width upper bounds 1–2, but their complete cuts have 40–51 incidences. These manually selected frames do not establish cheap-cut prevalence or automatic frame discovery ([results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_TRACE_SEMANTIC_PARITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [raw paired capture](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_trace_semantic_parity_v1_1_20260928.json), [corrected independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_trace_semantic_parity_v1_1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [source/data lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/held_topic_trace_semantic_parity_v1_1_lock_20260928.json)). The earlier [isolated v1 capture](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_STRUCTURAL_TRACE_CAPTURE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md) is retained as snapshot-specific history only.

**Live self-report full graph cut (2026-09-26):** a second independent live family repeats two successful self-report actions and injected action failure in three source-locked workers. The operational owned sets have 7 nodes, 15/13 internal edges, complete cuts of 29–31 incidences, 15–17 external neighbors, and induced-width upper bound 2–3. Compared with held-topic's 11-node/41–46-incidence/width-2 sets, the direct cuts vary by family. Shared Agent/Channel/Interlocutor/discourse hubs make incidence counts distinct from separator dimensions; neither manually declared set is a learned frame or full rule-dependency graph. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_IDENTITY_BOUNDARY_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_identity_boundary_v1_20260926.json), [raw cuts](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_identity_boundary_v1_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_self_identity_boundary_v1_lock_20260926.json).

**Three-family runtime task-boundary panel (2026-09-26):** complete typed-edge cuts over manually declared held-topic, self-report, and concurrent `describe_scene` owned nodes show 29–46 direct incidences per task and induced owned-width upper bounds 2–3. In the scene pair, one direct A/B `responds_to` edge coexists with six shared external nodes. This demonstrates task-family-specific boundary structure and why an edge allowlist or scalar cut count is insufficient; it does not discover natural frames or measure full rule-dependency treewidth. See [cross-family results](SUBSTRATE_RUNTIME_TASK_BOUNDARY_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [scene analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_boundary_v1_20260926.json), [self-report analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_self_identity_boundary_v1_20260926.json), and [held-topic analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_held_topic_evidence_v1_6_20260926.json).

**Rule-attributed runtime work across the scene task boundary (2026-09-26):** a source-locked traced replay preserves the two-request lifecycle and complete cuts while capturing 1.87M selected native events per worker across 410 rules. A-owned nodes are touched by 5,562 events and B-owned nodes by 3,011; 1.862M touch neither set, with 96.3% of all events classified as attribute reads. This shows that small operational cuts can coexist with broad global closure work in this fixture. The partial trace aggregates both serialized requests and has known operation-family blind spots; it does not prove this work is unnecessary or safely partitionable. See [trace results and limits](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw trace](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v1_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_describe_scene_access_trace_v1_lock_20260926.json).

**Per-request traced/untraced work and cross-owner accesses (2026-09-26):** three fresh-process pairs reproduce exact replies, lifecycle, task cuts, and normalized whole-graph state. The separate A/B intervals record 951,567/919,200 native events across all 410 loaded rules; B touches 2,081 nodes assigned to A and 3,011 nodes assigned to B. Attribute reads dominate both traces. The paired timing difference suggests noticeable instrumentation overhead for B, but three pairs do not estimate it reliably; raw graph hashes also include volatile diagnostic times excluded from the semantic signature. See [v2 results and limits](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw paired workers](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [input lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_lock_20260926.json).

**Current-source recheck of per-request closure work (2026-09-26):** after `WorldAdapter` acquired public-method serialization and post-turn heap reclamation, a separately source-locked v2.1 replay repeats the same 951,567/919,200 events and A/B cross-owner contacts, with exact semantic graph, reply, lifecycle, and boundary parity in all three traced/untraced pairs. A's median timing is unchanged within noise; B is 22–25% slower with tracing in this small panel. This is a current-source workload result, not an estimate for other tasks or complete access coverage. See [v2.1 results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_1_AMENDMENT_20260926.md), [raw pairs](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_1_workers_raw_20260926.json), and [current-source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_1_lock_20260926.json).
**Live task per-rule touch matrix:** independent analysis of the current-source A/B trace reproduces across three workers: in B's request interval, 65 rules touch A-owned vertices, 71 touch B-owned vertices, and 63 touch both across events; 12 events directly span the two sets. The incomplete trace cannot prove absent rules independent or safely removable. See [analysis results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_TASK_RULE_TOUCH_MATRIX_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md) and [capture analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_task_rule_touch_matrix_v1_20260926.json).

**Native access-trace coverage audit (2026-09-26):** source inspection maps the existing typed trace events and four remaining limits: Python tool callbacks, unchanged `SetAttrIfDifferent` reads, rejected matcher edge identities, and operations with no active RuleUid. `sd_cache_key` now records each typed-population query and fingerprint node read under an active rule UID. The one-versus-two computed-effect probe records 2 versus 4 query markers per codebook type with traced/untraced semantic parity; see [cache-key results](SUBSTRATE_SD_CACHE_KEY_TRACE_PROBE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_SD_CACHE_KEY_TRACE_PROBE_V1_PREREG_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_sd_cache_key_trace_probe_v1.py), and [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_sd_cache_key_trace_probe_v1_20260926.json). Paired probes confirm that a `UseTool` callback fires once without a tool trace event and that `SetAttrIfDifferent` emits a target write event only when the value changes; see [blind-spot results](SUBSTRATE_ACCESS_TRACE_BLINDSPOT_PROBE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md). A 1-to-9 candidate sweep records 1-to-9 candidate tests and predicate reads but only one successful pattern-edge event, confirming rejected edge identities are omitted ([matcher results](SUBSTRATE_MATCHER_REJECTED_CANDIDATE_TRACE_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_MATCHER_REJECTED_CANDIDATE_TRACE_V1_PREREG_20260926.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_matcher_rejected_candidate_trace_v1.py), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_matcher_rejected_candidate_trace_v1_20260926.json)). Direct/out-of-rule traces remain unattributed, and cache hit/miss is not explicitly labeled. Live totals remain partial by construction; zero dropped rows only indicates the event-key cap did not overflow. See [coverage results](SUBSTRATE_RUNTIME_ACCESS_TRACE_COVERAGE_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20260926.md), [audit runner](../../../scripts/substrate_runtime_access_trace_coverage_audit_v1.py), and [machine-readable source audit](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_runtime_access_trace_coverage_v1_20260926.json).

**Two-sheet vision tractability and task contrast (2026-09-27):** a source-locked v1.3 capture on a 5,969-node/16,313-edge/170-rule shared substrate finds global full-rescan adaptation at 1,580.1 s versus Retina-root-scoped at 144.8 s (10.9× in this one fixture); the global arm has one presentation requiring 238,132 cumulative rule steps after a 200,000-step budget. Both scopes pass terminal-status, exact blank-replay, and zero readout-weight-drift checks. A's tuning response is exactly invariant to B's registered grating in both scopes, so the predicted global interference was not observed. This is one visual fixture, not general task prevalence or global scope safety. B retinal input/channels were verified, but B SUnit activity was not separately measured. The original driver's only failed fixture bit was its incorrect positive-tag-count requirement for the zero-executable-rule `vision_compose` manifest; independent source audit validates the corrected condition. See [results and limits](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_3_RESULTS_20260927.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_3_20260927.json), and [independent audit](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_3_audit_20260927.json).

**Two-sheet vision B-stack activity follow-up v1.4 (2026-09-27):** in the same 5,969-node paired fixture, B receives 128 retinal bursts but its 16 SUnits have zero rate/activation at the end of A's readout under both global and root-scoped settling. A separate B-only K=16 sweep has nonzero tuning (max 1.7744, mean OSI 0.2968), proving response somewhere in the swept stimulus space, not to the exact paired phase-zero frame. The raw capture passes source, topology, calibration, replay, settled-status, and weight-stability gates; the driver's overall pass is false only because its positive joint-B-activity gate fails. Silence is not specific to global scope, and its cause is unresolved. Next compare exact fixed-frame B-only and paired A/B conditions from the identical prepared snapshot before attributing a shared-sheet interaction ([v1.4 result and limits](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_4_RESULTS_20260927.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_4_20260927.json), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_4_AMENDMENT_20260927.md)).

**Two-sheet vision fixed-frame diagnostic v1.5 (2026-09-27):** four root-scoped conditions restore one prepared graph snapshot. The exact B phase-zero grating evokes positive `act` in all 16 B SUnits (sum 295.1451), with all 128 B retinal bursts present; the per-unit vector is exactly unchanged when A's grating is added. B `rate` remains zero in both grating conditions, while both-blank and A-only controls have zero B `act`. Thus the v1.4 zero-`rate` endpoint does not demonstrate B inactivity or A-to-B suppression. Nine independent checks pass, including source stability, common prepared state, terminal closure statuses, and the blank/stimulus controls. Next trace `act` and `rate` writers/readers pass-by-pass and reconcile the K=16 `cell_stats` sequence with the fixed endpoint; use that field audit to preregister subsequent task/scale measures ([v1.5 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_5_RESULTS_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_5_PREREG_20260927.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_5.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_5_analysis_20260927.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_5_20260927.json)).

**Two-sheet vision SUnit rate-threshold audit v1.6 (2026-09-27):** the root-scoped exact-frame rerun records per-unit `act`, `theta`, `rate`, and update stamps. All 16 B activations are 17.688–19.119, thresholds 20.282–20.739, and the independent `max(act-theta,0)` equation matches observed zero rate in all 32 B-only/both-grating unit observations. All update stamps equal the schedule-implied endpoint step 23,010. This resolves v1.4/v1.5's apparent silent-stack discrepancy for this endpoint: zero firing rate is expected even with positive activation. It does not establish global-scope equivalence or general task capability. Next replicate the four conditions under global settling, then test a preregistered small orientation/phase panel with equation checks before broadening the task or scaling ([v1.6 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_6_RESULTS_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_6_PREREG_20260927.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_6.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_6_analysis_20260927.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_6_20260927.json)).

**Matched-scope two-sheet vision endpoint v1.7 (2026-09-27):** eight integrity checks pass after restoring the exact root-scoped-adapted graph into an isomorphic global-rule twin. On the same starting state, root-scoped B activation is identical for B-only and A+B grating (295.1451); global-rule B-only activation is 590.2902, while A+B ends at zero B activation. This is a cross-sheet coupling signal for this fixture, conditional on root-scoped learning history. However, three root-scoped conditions and global B-only terminate on repeated graph states, not fixed points. Across 256 unit endpoints, 76 final rates differ from `max(final_act-theta,0)` despite current update stamps. Static guards explain a plausible time-slice mismatch: `oo_sunit_rate` fires once per Retina step, then same-step settling may update `act`. Do not promote the coupling signal to a converged inference claim. Next trace rule firing and unit fields at closure-pass boundaries, enumerate the full repeated-state cycle, and define whether task response means pre-settle rate or post-settle activation before widening scale/prevalence ([v1.7 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_7_RESULTS_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_7_PREREG_20260927.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_7_20260927.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_7_analysis_20260927.json)).

**Two-sheet vision closure-pass trace v1.8 (2026-09-27):** seven independent checks pass. Full closure and one-pass-sliced execution match exactly in terminal graph, termination class, progress work, and pass count for a root-scoped B-only cycle and a global both-gratings fixed point. The full panel gives exact cycle periods: root-scoped B-only 8, A-only 6, both 24 passes; global B-only 8, with global A-only and both-gratings fixed points. `act`/`act_step` first change on pass 2; `rate`/`rate_step` first change on pass 3 and never update again, while act continues changing through later settling passes. Export order places settling before `oo_sunit_rate`; this explains why current-step stamps do not identify the closure pass of the measured rate. The engine has fixed-point and exact-cycle outcomes on the same learned graph depending on scope/condition. Next define a response-phase contract for drive, settled activation, rate, and termination class, then widen to multi-seed, independently authored visual tasks before prevalence or scale claims ([v1.8 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_8_RESULTS_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_8_PREREG_20260927.md), [raw trace](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_8_20260927.trace.jsonl), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_8_analysis_20260927.json)).

**Orientation/phase vision response panel v1.9 (2026-09-27):** the phase-aware frozen-state panel passes 10/10 integrity checks across 50 conditions on one matched prepared graph. All input bursts match their frames; all rate-write snapshots satisfy `rate=max(act-theta,0)` with zero mismatches. B-only rate-write sums are identical across rule scopes at the eight tested gratings (0, 0, 18.956, 14.518, 0, 0, 19.946, 10.897 for orientations 0/45/90/135 crossed with phases 0/π/2). Termination differs: root-scoped has 5 fixed points and 20 exact cycles; global has 8 fixed points, 9 cycles, and all 8 both-sheet grating cases hit the 512-pass cap while all 32 SUnit `act` values still change at the final pass. Capped outcomes are unresolved horizons, not nontermination. This is a scope-locality/convergence signal for one root-adapted visual state, not natural prevalence or scale evidence. Next extend the eight capped global cases and repeat the phase-aware panel across at least three independently adapted seeds and independent task families; retain response, termination, and cost as distinct outcomes ([v1.9 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_9_RESULTS_20260927.md), [response-phase contract](SUBSTRATE_VISION_RESPONSE_PHASE_CONTRACT_V1_9_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_9_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_9_analysis_20260927.json), [raw trace](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_9_20260927.trace.jsonl)).

**V1.9.1 native replay of global joint-sheet trace caps (2026-09-27):** the eight both-sheet global cases that hit v1.9's 512-pass Python trace horizon were rerun from exact initial graph/input hashes with native cycle detection and a 1.6M progress ceiling. Seven integrity gates pass, including exact stimulus hash parity (the first exploratory replay rounded π/2 and was excluded). One case reaches fixed point; seven reach exact repeated states in 1,416–8,850 passes and 1,421–8,855 progress steps. The v1.9 pass cap was an instrumentation horizon, not unresolved engine termination. Combined distribution on this single learned graph is root-scoped 5 fixed points/20 cycles and global 9 fixed points/16 cycles. Cycle periods from these native reports are unavailable. Next repeat the phase-aware panel across independently adapted seeds and visual task families before prevalence or scale claims ([v1.9.1 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_9_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_VISION_SHARED_SUBSTRATE_MULTI_SHEET_TASK_PILOT_V1_9_1_AMENDMENT_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_9_1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_vision_shared_substrate_multisheet_task_pilot_v1_9_1_20260927.json)).


**Vision multi-seed response replication v2.1 (2026-09-27):** three independently adapted checkpoints completed the same 25-condition orientation/phase panel under the v1.9 response contract. The 11/11 analysis gates pass across 75 trials: phase-appropriate rate equations and stamps hold, all inputs match pixel counts, every outcome is an exact fixed point or cycle, and none hits the 512-pass/1.6M progress bound. Adaptation work is similar (429k/442k/439k progress), and response medians are 194/192/192 passes; yet termination class differs by seed in 9/25 matched conditions (seed fixed/cycle counts 4/21, 4/21, 7/18). This supports bounded progress counts on the small fixture and exposes seed-sensitive attractors; no reliable per-condition wall-clock or peak-memory capture was made, so it is not a commodity-CPU latency result. It does not establish natural-task prevalence, cheap-cut locality, scaling, or learning tractability. Next test independent visual task families and grow frame size while recording graph width/separator and CPU/memory costs, then isolate rung-3/4 induction rather than extrapolating from closure ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_SEED_RESPONSE_REPLICATION_V2_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [response preregistration](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_SEED_RESPONSE_REPLICATION_V2_1_PREREG_20260927.md), [checkpoint preregistration](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_SEED_CHECKPOINT_REPLICATION_V2_0_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_multiseed_response_replication_v2_1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_multiseed_response_replication_v2_1_20260927.json)).


**Vision multi-seed timed response replay v2.2 (2026-09-27):** the 75-trial replay passes seven integrity gates and exactly reproduces all v2.1 response vectors, work counts, termination classes, and cycle indices. Across three isolated seed workers, median native `run_rules_report(1, False)` call time is 60–62 ms per stimulus (about 1.50–1.56 s summed across 25 trials); peak process RSS is 228–237 MiB. The full exact-cycle audit loop takes 426–446 s per seed because it materializes/hashes the graph after every pass and extracts state; the non-engine portion is not separately attributed and must not be called substrate latency. The evidence narrows the small-fixture CPU/memory envelope only. Next repeat call-level time and memory under a current-source size/rule-count sweep with exact output parity, then compare the audit hash implementation separately ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_SEED_RESPONSE_COST_REPLAY_V2_2_RESULTS_20260927.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_MULTI_SEED_RESPONSE_COST_REPLAY_V2_2_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_multiseed_response_cost_replay_v2_2_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_multiseed_response_cost_replay_v2_2_20260927.json)).


**Vision two-sheet current-source scale sweep v2.3 (2026-09-27):** 96 isolated cells cross 3 seeds, 4 frame sizes, global/root-scoped fold, two B-sheet states, and audit counters off/on. All eight analysis gates pass; all audit-on/off outputs and progress counts match exactly, and the independent equation oracle and counter conservation pass. At 512 total SUnits, global `NodesOfType` work is 524,800 (`T(2T+1)`) versus 512 (`T`) under root scoping. Counter-off median closure time is 24.846 ms global versus 11.391 ms scoped; peak process RSS medians rise from 42 to 51 MiB through this limited size range. The selected global fold therefore defeats execution locality even though the two frame roots are disjoint. Root scoping removes cross-root traversal but leaves dense within-sheet coupling. This is controlled one-rule evidence at 512 SUnits, not a natural workload or large-scale bound. Next grow local frame size and capture the runtime dependency width, then test independently authored rules from a second task family ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_TWO_SHEET_CURRENT_SOURCE_SCALE_V2_3_RESULTS_20260927.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_TWO_SHEET_CURRENT_SOURCE_SCALE_V2_3_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_two_sheet_current_source_scale_v2_3_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_two_sheet_current_source_scale_v2_3_20260927.json)).


**Vision two-sheet current-source scale extension v2.4 (2026-09-27):** 144 isolated worker cells extend the same controlled factor to 8,192 SUnits across 3 seeds, 6 sizes, 2 scopes, 2 B states, and audit off/on. Nine analysis gates pass, including exact counter-off/on semantic parity, equation oracle, work-count reconciliation, and stable per-worker source locks. From 2,048 to 8,192 units, total Term evaluations increase 15.9× as unit count increases 4×, for both global and root-scoped folds; counter-off call time rises about 15.4×. At 8,192 units, global/scoped times are 6.297/2.786 s and process peak RSS is about 207 MiB. Root scoping halves this one rule's constant factor but retains quadratic work within each sheet because every output reads a whole-sheet mean. These are controlled-rule measurements, not natural workload prevalence or arbitrary-rule scale. The master completion flag was stale at 96; the complete 144-cell aggregate was independently rebuilt from each locked worker file and that reconciliation is included in analysis. Next measure a second task family and dynamic dependency widths, then run broader rule sets at larger graph sizes ([results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_TWO_SHEET_CURRENT_SOURCE_SCALE_V2_4_RESULTS_20260927.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_VISION_TWO_SHEET_CURRENT_SOURCE_SCALE_V2_4_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_two_sheet_current_source_scale_v2_4_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/vision_two_sheet_current_source_scale_v2_4_20260927.json)).


**Live describe-scene access measurement status (2026-09-27):** v2.3 is censored: all six workers time out before selection without Goal callbacks, so its recorded access/timing rows are unusable for substrate locality or cost. A separate diagnostic passes with the observed-lock proxy, audit wrapper, and nested public lock combined when access tracing and selection barriers are off, but it used different `world_adapter.py` and `conversation_action.py` hashes than v2.3. It does not isolate the failure. Current status: live-task behavior is demonstrated by earlier captures; request-level dynamic locality remains unmeasured. See [censored result and next gates](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_3_CENSORED_20260927.md), [censor analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_3_censored_analysis_20260927.json), and [combined-hooks diagnostic](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_scene_measurement_hooks_combined_diagnostic_v1_20260927.json).


**Live describe-scene dynamic trace (2026-09-27):** trace-only isolation and the full v2.4 paired access capture both pass their integrity gates on stable source locks. The latter measures selected access activity across a shared Scene+Percept input and request lifecycle projections, but request cuts expose 18–20 external nodes and 32–35 boundary incidences; induced lifecycle width 2 alone does not establish frame locality or dynamic factorization. Public response text and graph outbound Message remain misaligned in all traced workers. Producer audit/readback contaminates timing for baseline CPU claims. Current status: one valid task fixture, not natural-task prevalence or scale evidence. See [result limitations and follow-ups](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_4_RESULTS_20260927.md) and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_analysis_20260927.json).


**Uninstrumented live request cost (2026-09-27):** on the same source closure/native build as v2.4, one uninstrumented two-call capture records 10.746 s and 11.021 s caller wall intervals after a 12.013 s boot. Both requests return the expected fixture response and resolve a Goal. This is a single concurrent two-request run without per-thread CPU, RSS, or replicate distribution; it does not support stable latency or scale claims. Public output still lacks matching graph outbound evidence. See [timed result boundaries and next gates](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_UNINSTRUMENTED_TIMED_RESULTS_V1_20260927.md).


**Repeated uninstrumented CPU and memory envelope (2026-09-27):** five same-source processes pass request/lifecycle gates. Median seed boot is 12.284 s wall/13.248 s CPU; median combined CPU for two concurrent requests is 11.370 s; peak RSS is 2.508 GiB. A phase sample shows 1.342 GiB current RSS after boot and 0.352 GiB after requests; 1.673 GiB is the timing runner high-water RSS at boot, not current resident memory. GNU `time -v` independently confirms peak RSS. This is one full-seed scene fixture at 25,201 final graph nodes, not a general scale estimate; memory attribution and minimal-profile comparison remain open. See [results and next gates](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_UNINSTRUMENTED_TIMED_REPLICATION_V2_RESULTS_20260927.md).


**Memory phase profile (2026-09-27):** measured current RSS is 1.342 GiB after full seed boot and 0.352 GiB after the two-request schedule, while high-water RSS reaches 2.508 GiB. Seed boot adds about 1.295 GiB anonymous RSS; attaching the scene changes current RSS by only about 1.2 MiB. The earlier timing artifact's `rss_after_boot_bytes` is a high-water value, not current RSS. Phase deltas locate memory growth and release but do not attribute ownership. See [phase results and correction](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_BOOT_MEMORY_PROFILE_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md) and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_scene_boot_memory_profile_v1_analysis_20260927.json).


**Seed corpus and reduced-profile capability (2026-09-27):** 837 manifests total 288.15 MB, heavily skewed into ten files (89.4%); the 110.73 MB grounded lexicon uses a nested legacy graph payload. Four- and eight-seed dependency-closed conversation profiles cut measured peak RSS from 2.508 GiB to 0.129 GiB, but both fail scene-description behavior and produce no request-bound resolved Goal. This is a measured memory/capability tradeoff, not a working small profile. Runtime dependency extraction plus held-out prompt validation is still needed. See [corpus footprint](SUBSTRATE_SEED_MANIFEST_FOOTPRINT_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md) and [profile result](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_SELECTIVE_SEED_PROFILE_RESULTS_20260927.md).


**Current live route seed/rule provenance (2026-09-27):** a source-stable current capture joins every selected native access UID to a seed or a runtime-construction phase. Of 2.139M selected accesses, 59.2% come from 18 rules lazily added by first-use describe-faculty construction, 40.8% from seed-installed rules, and six conceptualizer rules add another 568 accesses; the second call reuses the built faculty. `_act_describe_scene` invokes the builder before evaluating the request in the held scene basis, so this path does not wait for a measured transform failure before minting. This is an implementation-path finding, not a general task-frequency estimate or an ablation result. See [provenance results and limits](SUBSTRATE_SEED_RULE_PROVENANCE_LIVE_CAPTURE_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md) and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/seed_rule_provenance_live_capture_v1_analysis_20260927.json).

**Faculty construction cost and cache reuse (2026-09-27):** on three source-locked replicates per arm, lazy first-request medians are 11.112 s wall/11.063 s CPU and cached second requests are 0.278/0.279 s. Explicit prebuild takes 10.907/10.910 s; the following request takes 0.388/0.384 s. The first request adds six conceptualizer rules in either arm; explicit prebuild adds 18 faculty rules before that request. Full output and Goal lifecycle gates pass. This supports deferred one-time setup with cheap repeated use for this single route; frequency across task families, transfer, causal necessity, and scaling remain unmeasured. The v1 failed-gate pilot and corrected v1.1 confirmation are both retained ([results](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_FACULTY_FIRST_USE_REUSE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_faculty_first_use_reuse_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json), [pilot](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_faculty_first_use_reuse_v1_pilot_20260927.json)).

**Cold prompt-family generalization (2026-09-27):** the integrity-checked 10-process panel yields the expected scene Goal/reply only for the canonical formulation. Two paraphrases invoke the scene handler, incur the 18-rule faculty build, and finish on the self-report route with “I am Jabberwock”; another paraphrase returns a gap reply without a Goal. The self-identity control avoids scene-faculty construction and resolves its `report_self` Goal, despite six unrelated closure rules. Capability gate is failed. This selected prompt panel shows setup cost can be paid on unsuccessful requests and cached-call timing does not establish language generalization ([results](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_FACULTY_PROMPT_FAMILY_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_faculty_prompt_family_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_faculty_prompt_family_v1_20260927.json)).

**Cross-route live conversation capability (2026-09-27):** in two source-locked full-seed workers per task, scene description, held self-knowledge answering, self-report action, and unknown-word clarification each pass their selected contracts 2/2. Live concern authoring fails 0/2: interpretation chooses repair for unresolved “two,” so no concern-authoring action occurs. Scene description costs a median 10.354 s wall and adds 18 lazy faculty rules, while other selected tasks take 0.205–0.279 s; the scene's returned reply still lacks a matching graph-linked outbound Message. This is a small selected panel, not general capability prevalence. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_CAPABILITY_PANEL_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md) and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_capability_panel_v1_analysis_20260927.json).

**ProblemShape recurrence-guard scaling through N=64 (2026-09-27):** the source-locked 12-cell larger-population sweep passes state, mint, fixed-point, and independent-analysis checks. Recognition-only dynamic guard counts follow N(N+1)/2 (136/528/2,080); frozen mode removes the work. Total ProblemShape candidate pairs approach 4× per doubling from N=16 to 64. One recurrent residual mints once at each size, while frozen controls leave it residual. This is a controlled mechanism envelope, not natural residual prevalence or general rung-3 tractability; the historical N≤8 capture has a different native build and is kept separate ([results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_POPULATION_SCALE_V2_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_population_scale_v2_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_population_scale_v2_20260927.json)).

**ProblemShape schema acquisition probe (2026-09-27):** ten fresh locked workers and independent analysis pass. Singleton unseeded `solve_can_relax` and `solve_can_bp` Goals remain distinct residuals; repeated examples mint one family-specific shape and re-decode the first residual; mixed families stay distinct. This supports only two authored categorical schema cases, not open-ended concept invention, natural residual frequency, or safe abstraction across omitted parameters ([results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_UNSEEDED_SCHEMA_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_UNSEEDED_SCHEMA_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_unseeded_schema_transfer_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_unseeded_schema_transfer_v1_20260927.json)).

**ProblemShape parameter-sensitivity boundary (2026-09-27):** a preregistered paired audit on three resident CAN networks (six workers) finds `max_product` and `sum_product` share a structural key but produce different beliefs in all six pairs (max absolute delta 2.0); max-product fails to converge in four while sum-product converges in six. Thus ProblemShape recognition is structural and does not promise behavioral substitutability across omitted parameters. This is not comparative solver-quality evidence ([results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_BP_PARAMETER_SEMANTICS_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_BP_PARAMETER_SEMANTICS_V1_1_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_bp_parameter_semantics_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_bp_parameter_semantics_v1_1_20260927.json)).

**Live describe-scene declared-boundary audit (2026-09-27):** after counting both request lifecycle nodes and the shared Scene+Percepts inputs, 99.64% of A and 99.39% of B selected trace events touch none of those declared sets. Repeated `name` reads from three recognition rules and `state`/`is_ambivalent` reads from the curiosity rule dominate. This identifies an incomplete operational projection; omitted descendants/shared hubs and unrelated scans remain confounded, so it does not establish a complete cut or task locality ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_V2_4_BOUNDARY_TOUCH_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_boundary_touch_v1_20260927.json), [base trace results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_4_RESULTS_20260927.md)).

**Live describe-scene rule-produced descendants (2026-09-27):** concrete native mint/edge-upsert traces identify seven additional directly connected rule-minted nodes for request A and five for B. Including them raises measured-frame touches to 7,775/1.14M and 8,808/998k selected events respectively. The descendant set is a lower bound; host-created and untraced dependencies remain unresolved, so frame completeness and scoped-execution safety are still open ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_V2_4_RULE_DESCENDANTS_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_rule_descendants_v1_20260927.json), [source capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_workers_raw_20260927.json)).

**Describe-scene dominant-rule source map (2026-09-27):** the three dominant `name` readers are authored in `arc_self_report.py::build_describe_faculty`; they join each Scene Percept with Concept nodes reached through a Microtheory `has_concept` relation, test a label/Holds predicate, and add classification edges. The curiosity rule similarly iterates Microtheory concept members for state predicates. These concrete joins expose candidate-domain growth as the next cost variable; no current measurement isolates vocabulary-size scaling or proves a narrow dynamic interface ([rule descendant analysis](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_V2_4_RULE_DESCENDANTS_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [trace analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_boundary_touch_v1_20260927.json)).

**Describe-scene ownership correction (2026-09-27):** refined source-based edge ownership assigns five temporary rule-produced descendants to each request and classifies request A's SelfConcern/GapRecurrence as persistent shared Agent-state writes. Task/input nodes plus temporary descendants touch ~0.64% of selected events for each request; B also touches A lifecycle nodes in 2,400 events. The policy is explicit but post-hoc and incomplete; the earlier 7/5 connected-mint count is retained as candidate evidence, not an ownership claim ([amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_V2_4_RULE_DESCENDANTS_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_rule_descendants_v1_1_20260927.json)).

**Describe-rule matcher population scale (2026-09-27):** three source-derived recognition rules retain exact output on a fixed three-Percept fixture while distractor Concepts grow to 4,096. Candidate-pair and Term counts follow exact per-rule affine formulas through 4,099 Concepts (`C+3` and `4C+18`). This supports linear measured work for this isolated matcher motif; full-route repeat activity, other Microtheories, and natural task scaling remain unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_RULE_CONCEPT_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_RULE_CONCEPT_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_rule_concept_candidate_scale_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_rule_concept_candidate_scale_v1_20260927.json)).

**Describe-rule matcher scale in full-seed graph (2026-09-27):** eight workers pass source, fixed-point, and output-parity gates while varying 1/4 Scenes and 0/4,096 distractor Concepts. With only the three target rules active, per-rule candidate work follows `S × (M + 3)` exactly across the four cells; aggregate counts are 18,969–125,028 candidate pairs. This supports linear measured work for these motifs in a full-seed graph, not full active-rule or live-route tractability: 2,334 other rules were tombstoned and the added inputs are synthetic ([results](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_RULE_FULL_SEED_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_DESCRIBE_RULE_FULL_SEED_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_rule_full_seed_candidate_scale_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/describe_rule_full_seed_candidate_scale_v1_20260927.json)).

**Live describe-scene current-source replication (2026-09-27):** three fresh traced/untraced pairs on the current `world_adapter.py` pass lifecycle, source-stability, exact graph/reply parity, and zero-drop gates. Whole-route selected access traces reproduce at 1,140,892 and 997,848 events; audited Goal-event closure intervals total 139,279/198,600 candidate tests for A/B, but exclude other closures during `converse`. The public reply still has no matching graph-visible outbound Message. This is reproducibility evidence for one concurrent fixture, not a complete work census, local-frame proof, or general task result ([current-source replication](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_4_CURRENT_SOURCE_REPLICATION_20260927.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_20260927.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_4_analysis_20260927.json)).

**Historical live describe-scene whole-request audit v1 (2026-09-27; superseded):** its summary identified ambiguous-ID Term rows and missing no-rule attribution. The original raw rows/lock are no longer present after the v1.1 rerun overwrote them; use v1.1 for reproducible current evidence ([historical results/limits](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_WHOLE_REQUEST_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_WHOLE_REQUEST_AUDIT_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md)).

**Live describe-scene whole-request audit v1.1 (2026-09-27):** after a rebuild, three audited/uninstrumented pairs pass all 12 independent gates with exact output and graph parity. The revised native counter withdraws ambiguous rule-ID rows and reports unattributed Term evaluations, yielding exact candidate and Term conservation at 678,432 and 3,676,862 respectively. A separate access trace records 1,140,892 events with no drops. The public reply still lacks a matching graph-visible Message. This closes audit accounting for one fixture only, not task locality or scaling ([v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_WHOLE_REQUEST_AUDIT_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_describe_scene_whole_request_audit_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/live_describe_scene_whole_request_audit_v1_1_workers_raw_20260927.json)).

**Live describe-scene full-rule membership scale v1 (2026-09-27):** a 12-worker paired sweep passes all 13 independent gates with every rule active. At fixed 3-Percept input, 0→4,096 noise memberships raises candidate tests 678,432→1,092,128, Term evaluations 3,676,862→5,819,070, and selected access events 1,140,892→1,632,412; each added membership contributes exactly 101/523/120 in these tested cells. The three recognition rules and one curiosity rule account for all observed work growth, while outputs remain identical. This is controlled route-level vocabulary scaling, not evidence of task locality, natural prevalence, or general capability ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_LIVE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_describe_scene_live_scale_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/live_describe_scene_live_scale_v1_workers_raw_20260927.json)).

**Independent live self-report membership scale v1 (2026-09-27):** a 12-worker paired sweep over the full-seed `report your organisation` route passes all 14 gates with exact reply, route, Goal lifecycle, and audited/untraced graph parity. From 0 to 4,096 noise memberships, candidate tests rise 66,095→98,863, Terms 716,057→1,076,505, and selected access events 333,203→423,315.
