Every tested added membership contributes exactly 8/88/22, all growth attributed to `curiosity_targets_ambivalent_nodes`. Compared with describe-scene's 101/523/120 slope and four affected rules, this shows task topology matters. It is a controlled synthetic axis and says nothing by itself about natural prevalence, runtime frame locality, asymptotic behavior, or general capability ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_LIVE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_self_report_live_scale_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/live_self_report_live_scale_v1_workers_raw_20260927.json)).

**Live self-report task-dimension frame scale v1.1 (2026-09-27):** the current-source 20-worker paired sweep passes all 16 corrected gates for 4/8/12/16/24 held dimensions. Structured reflection retains every dimension, but the reply surface caps output at 12 (coverage falls to 75% at 16 and 50% at 24). In this explicitly injected frame, each dimension adds 8 candidate tests, 88 Terms, and 22 selected accesses; aggregate/per-rule counters attribute that growth to `curiosity_targets_ambivalent_nodes`. Concrete trace node IDs show frame nodes receive 22 selected accesses per dimension, only 0.026–0.158% of full-route selected trace events; the shared Agent root stays at 3,726 accesses. Frame-touch events instead map to `time_infer_mint` and `resolve_slang_variant`, exposing an unresolved attribution gap between counter and access-trace instrumentation. The aggregate frame has one unique external Agent separator by construction, so this does not establish learned cheap cuts or engine factorization. Wall timings are nonmonotone; RSS remains dominated by the full seed graph ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_DIMENSION_FRAME_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_DIMENSION_FRAME_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_self_report_dimension_frame_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/live_self_report_dimension_frame_v1_workers_raw_20260927.json)).

**Self-report route regression v1 (2026-09-27, exact locked snapshot):** three fresh full boots on the snapshot documented in the source lock fail `report your organisation` identically: no communication route, no Goal, generic `no faculty` reply. This is a result for that source version, not the live worktree; a later worktree panel was rejected because `boot_all.py` and `boot_profiles.py` changed during capture. Boot reports 11 repeatable manifest failures while loading 731 seeds; their causal role is unproven ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_ROUTE_REGRESSION_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_self_report_route_regression_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [report](../../../evaluation/reports/live_self_report_route_regression_v1_20260927.json)).

**Self-report route contrast v1 (2026-09-27, exact locked snapshot):** 30 fresh workers pass all eight independent gates. Request forms with `organisation` wordplay-preempt; self-account verbs with other nouns ask back; `what do you do?` reaches an instruction-gap route. The positive coinage controls dispatch to wordplay, but produce no usable answer in two cases. This is an authored ten-stimulus panel for the hash recorded in its source lock, not a current-worktree estimate or natural distribution ([results](SUBSTRATE_SELF_REPORT_ROUTE_CONTRAST_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/self_report_route_contrast_v1_analysis_20260927.json)).

**Seed provider-scope route intervention v1.1 (2026-09-27):** six fresh locked workers compare the route-failing baseline against the same file with only the typed-provider-selection hunk applied. Both conditions load the same 731 manifests and produce the same graph digest and wordplay route. The hunk alone has no measured effect and is insufficient to explain the later route change. Subsequent content-selection/scaffold-retention edits remain unisolated; route behavior is source-version-sensitive and current worktree claims require a newly frozen panel ([results](SUBSTRATE_SEED_PROVIDER_SCOPE_SELF_REPORT_INTERVENTION_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/seed_provider_scope_self_report_intervention_v1_analysis_20260927.json)).

**Self-report route contrast with complete runtime closure v1.2 (2026-09-27):** audit found two omissions in preceding captures: v1 lacked `data/slabs`; v1.1 lacked `scripts/collapse_engine.py`, silently preventing action execution. Their route conclusions are superseded. V1.2 locks source/seeds, all 36 slab files, and all 3,355 script Python modules. Thirty fresh workers reproduce the route/reply panel. The original exact digest flags J, but follow-up comparison shows its only difference is serialization order in `Interlocutor.believed_concepts.__fset__`; after sorting set values the full graph digest repeats. In this snapshot, requests for organisation route to `self_report` and return a 12-area inventory; purpose/self paraphrases get only `I am Jabberwock.`; `what do you do?` remains an instruction gap; wordplay definition yields no answer and a wordplay statement defers. This remains one narrow conversation family, not general capability evidence. Next standardize set-aware graph hashing, measure answer coverage/quality beyond these prompts, and apply the complete lock to multiple task families ([v1.2 and v1.3 amendments](SUBSTRATE_SELF_REPORT_ROUTE_CONTRAST_V1_3_AMENDMENT_20260927.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_SELF_REPORT_ROUTE_CONTRAST_V1_2_RESULTS_20260927.md), [canonical graph analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/route_j_graph_repeat_diagnostic_v1_1_canonical_analysis_20260927.json)).


**Reusable measurement provenance and graph canonicalization (2026-09-27):** `scripts/measurement_provenance.py` hashes explicit source/runtime file sets and deterministically filtered trees; missing or out-of-root paths fail closed. `scripts/measurement_graph_digest.py` canonicalizes node/edge row order and values wrapped as unordered set/frozenset attributes while preserving ordinary list order and the established volatile `SystemModel` fields. Rehashing the v1.2 lock’s exact 3,355 script paths and 36 slab paths reproduces its recorded hashes; the canonical J diagnostic yields identical semantic graph digests across three workers. These are measurement primitives, not yet migrated through existing benchmark runners. Next adopt them in a multi-family capability panel and require complete runtime closure, stable source locks, canonical graph parity, task-level correctness, and resource attribution as separate gates ([provenance helper](../../../scripts/measurement_provenance.py), [graph digest helper](../../../scripts/measurement_graph_digest.py), [route results](SUBSTRATE_SELF_REPORT_ROUTE_CONTRAST_V1_2_RESULTS_20260927.md), [canonical diagnostic](../../../evaluation/reports/route_j_graph_repeat_diagnostic_v1_1_canonical_analysis_20260927.json)).

**Live conversation capability panel complete-runtime replication v1.1 (2026-09-27):** the five-task panel was rerun on an isolated full-runtime snapshot after adding hashes for all 3,359 Python files under `scripts/` and all 36 `data/slabs` files to the earlier domain/substrate/native/seed lock. Ten fresh workers pass source stability, unique-process, boot-integrity, and independent analysis gates. Four selected task contracts pass both workers; concern authoring passes neither. Scene replies still lack matching graph-linked outbound Messages. This repairs the environment-closure flaw in the older panel but remains two replicates across five authored conversation prompts, not a general capability rate or current-worktree claim. Next repair the concern route and scene message alignment independently, then evaluate separately scored inference, vision, memory, and synthesis families ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_CAPABILITY_PANEL_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_CAPABILITY_PANEL_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260927.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_capability_panel_v1_1_20260927.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_capability_panel_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_conversation_capability_panel_v1_1_lock_20260927.json)).

**Cross-family capability evidence ledger (2026-09-27):** consolidated bounded results for native rule execution, shared-state vision dynamics, episodic and durable memory, list/string program synthesis, and live conversation while preserving each oracle, run grade, cost boundary, and failure mode. No pooled capability score is justified: the families use different task units, novelty axes, and source/runtime snapshots. The ledger's common gaps are natural task sampling, cross-family transfer, learned cheap-cut locality, and comparable resource accounting. Use it to select the next independent family protocols, not to upgrade pilots into general capability claims ([ledger](SUBSTRATE_CROSS_FAMILY_CAPABILITY_EVIDENCE_LEDGER_20260927.md)).

**MetaShape rung attribution and fixed-relation discrimination v2.1 (2026-09-27):** a source-stable replication on a read-only snapshot passes the full 8-worker provenance gates across four schema families and one positive/four perturbation probes per worker. Three families mint after two examples, transfer to both novel positives, and leave all six fixed-value/missing/type negatives residual and uncovered. MotionRule is consistently claimed by a preexisting `spatial_dynamics` keyword prior: it mints no shape, transfers no exact structure, and prior-attributes all six strict negatives. New extra-field probes match the acquired shapes in all six positive-acquisition workers; after capture this is reclassified as open-world extension tolerance, not a semantic false-positive rate. The original v2 run is preserved as censored because `boot_all.py` changed mid-capture. This is a replicated synthetic schema mechanism, not natural false-positive precision or downstream usefulness ([v2.1 results](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_RUNG_ATTRIBUTION_PANEL_V2_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [censored run](SUBSTRATE_METASHAPE_RUNG_ATTRIBUTION_PANEL_V2_CENSORED_RESULTS_20260927.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/metashape_rung_attribution_panel_v2_1_20260927.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/metashape_rung_attribution_panel_v2_1_analysis_20260927.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/metashape_rung_attribution_panel_v2_1_lock_20260927.json)).

**ProblemShape transfer through the real reasoning bridge v1 (2026-09-27):** two full-seed fresh workers pass the immutable source/runtime lock and reproduce the transition. A seeded CAN exact Goal matches its held shape; two resolved `can_to_deduction` Goals on distinct settled residents cause one direction-plus-target-edge ProblemShape mint and re-decode the first residual; a third held-out resident is recognized by that same shape. A resolved `deduction_to_can` near-neighbor remains residual and mints nothing. Full boot takes 11.86–11.93 s wall and peaks at 1.754 GiB; instrumented ProblemShape observation calls take 13.04–17.28 ms with per-stage candidate/Term counts recorded. This measures real solver-Goal structural reuse, not bridge answer quality, natural rung frequency, or general induction ([results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_SOLVER_FAMILY_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_SOLVER_FAMILY_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20260927.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_solver_family_transfer_v1_20260927.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_solver_family_transfer_v1_analysis_20260927.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/problem_shape_solver_family_transfer_v1_lock_20260927.json)).

**ProblemShape solver-family transfer v1.1 on updated boot source (2026-09-27):** after the live source changed in `boot_all.py` and `boot_profiles.py`, a read-only full-runtime replication passes all provenance and capability gates on the updated snapshot. The seeded exact-CAN control, one recurrence mint, first-observation re-decode, third-resident transfer, and opposite-direction residual all match v1 in both workers. Full-seed boot remains 731 loaded / 11 failed; boot wall is 12.81/14.88 s with 1.752/1.753 GiB peak RSS. This is not a causal before/after optimization comparison. Results and raw artifacts keep source versions separate ([v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_SOLVER_FAMILY_TRANSFER_V1_1_RESULTS_20260927.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_SOLVER_FAMILY_TRANSFER_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260927.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_solver_family_transfer_v1_1_analysis_20260927.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/problem_shape_solver_family_transfer_v1_1_lock_20260927.json)).
## Independent software source extension (2026-09-27)

The source-linked [Stacks Project code-reading panel](SUBSTRATE_STACKS_CODE_READING_PANEL_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md) processes all 37 functions from six locally vendored Apache-2.0 helper scripts through the production `code_reading.read_function` path in two source-stable full-runtime workers. Construction coverage is 37/37; Term emission is 0/37; each worker has one cold-start mint and no subsequent mint. This establishes route coverage on one real repository slice only. No behavior oracle or semantic correctness scoring is present, so it does not establish code competence. Full-runtime boot remains 731/742 manifests with 11 failures and ~1.805 GB peak RSS. The next missing evidence is behaviorally scored transfer on independent code with fixed input contracts, followed by multi-repository sampling and held-out composition.

The source-linked [Stacks string-predicate behavior panel](SUBSTRATE_STACKS_STRING_PREDICTION_PANEL_V1_RESULTS_20260927.md) provides that first external behavior score: 0/20 Stacks calls return a prediction, including 0/16 held-out calls, while a separate arithmetic control returns both outputs correctly. The AST shape and Python `.find` call are not lowered into the existing Term algebra's `StringIndexOf`, `Let`, comparison and `IfThenElse` nodes; `StringIndexOf` numeric offsets also use bytes rather than Python code points. For the panel's `< 0` and `== 0` predicates, only search presence/start is observed, so this offset distinction does not affect those outputs on string inputs. This measures a concrete lowering/composition gap; it neither suggests wrong-answer risk nor generalizes beyond these five related predicates. The next capability intervention is a constrained lowering with explicit string-type evidence, frozen-baseline comparison, and replication on an independent repository family.

The targeted follow-up [v2 panel](SUBSTRATE_STACKS_STRING_PREDICTION_PANEL_V2_RESULTS_20260927.md) yields 25/25 exact outputs across five inspected functions, including five Unicode-prefix probes; v1's paired 20 cases scored 0/20, and the arithmetic control remains 2/2. This is an engineered grammar extension with concrete input-type gating, not rung-three learning. The implementation was designed after the function family was inspected, so its within-family held-out score is not blind transfer evidence. Generalization remains open pending a frozen candidate on a newly selected second family.

## External dependency predicate surface (2026-09-28)

The source-linked census finds 18 `UseTool` nodes in `where` predicates across nine rules in `figure_grounding.json`, with zero in effects; all referenced names are among the registered symbolic handlers. The full-corpus seed loader includes this manifest when called without selection arguments. The optional AST-to-rule migrator can also lower nested NumPy calls inside no-else guards into predicate `UseTool` Terms. A hard predicate ban therefore requires migrating an existing full-boot seed and constraining a generator seam. Registration does not prove handler purity or external-state stability. Next evaluate declared predicate-safe handlers and versioned external dependencies/invalidation against a source-locked full-boot compatibility oracle ([plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md), [audit capture](../../../evaluation/reports/use_tool_predicate_surface_v1_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/audit_use_tool_predicate_surface_v1.py)).

Source review further qualifies those handlers: WordNet and the NLTK opinion lexicon can download resources on a cold miss and cache them process-locally; Wiktionary and ConceptNet read mmap slabs whose absence changes lookup outputs. The fixed lexical-set handlers avoid those resources. Predicate determinism therefore needs resource snapshot and initialization guarantees, not only callback read-only intent. Cold/warm and resource-version behavior remains to be measured ([plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md)).

The offline handler probe now passes all seven integrity/repeatability gates in three fresh processes: 14/15 handlers give stable representative outputs; `text_valence` errors and attempts to download the missing opinion lexicon twice per worker. WordNet/OMW ZIP data is present and fingerprinted, correcting an initial directory-only presence check. SentiWordNet fallback and resource-version invalidation remain untested ([v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_USE_TOOL_HANDLER_RESOURCE_PROBE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_use_tool_handler_resource_probe_v1_2_analysis_20260928.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/use_tool_handler_resource_probe_v1_2_lock_20260928.json)).

The file-backed external invalidation probe closes that gap for one `UseTool` predicate path. Three fresh workers show that changing the backing file from false to true without graph mutation triggers zero callbacks and no effect; adding a second match after changing it back triggers two callbacks that see the current value. `reads_any` covers graph changes but not external file changes. Correct external invalidation needs an explicit version fact/event, and already committed effects need a separate compensation or retraction policy ([results](SUBSTRATE_EXTERNAL_RESOURCE_INVALIDATION_PROBE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/external_resource_invalidation_probe_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/external_resource_invalidation_probe_v1_20260928.json)).

The paired resource-version/scoped-closure scale panel passes at N=4/16/64. A graph revision explicitly awakens the target match; global callback work rises 8→32→128, while `run_rules_scoped` on that frame remains four calls, with identical final projection and one accepted target. This makes hierarchical scheduling a concrete mitigation for external callback work, conditional on a caller-maintained version fact and correct scope selection. The probe does not establish automatic resource watching, retraction, or linear total engine work ([v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_RESOURCE_VERSION_SCOPED_INVALIDATION_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/resource_version_scoped_invalidation_v1_2_analysis_20260928.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/resource_version_scoped_invalidation_v1_2_20260928.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/resource_version_scoped_invalidation_v1_2_lock_20260928.json)).

The positive-to-negative compensation panel shows that resource revisions can re-evaluate rules but do not undo prior outputs. Without a false-case rule, `accepted=1` persists. An authored `set_attr_if_different(..., 0)` rule provides explicit attribute compensation; an authored `del_edge` rule removes an asserted positive edge. There is no `DelAttr` effect in the current effect vocabulary. This supports explicit expiry/tombstone design for external evidence; it is not general truth maintenance ([results](SUBSTRATE_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_COMPENSATION_PROBE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/external_effect_compensation_probe_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/external_effect_compensation_probe_v1_20260928.json)).

Reversing insertion order and applying opposite transitions simultaneously preserves final output for the tested disjoint guards, in both attr-compensation and edge-retraction arms. Callback counts vary with order even though results match: 9–12 calls for the initial/positive phases, and 12 calls for every simultaneous phase. This supports a bounded confluence result for those guards while showing rule order affects work. Conflicting overlapping writes remain unmeasured ([results](SUBSTRATE_COMPENSATION_ORDER_SIMULTANEOUS_TRANSITIONS_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/compensation_order_simultaneous_transitions_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/compensation_order_simultaneous_transitions_v1_20260928.json)).

The overlapping-write probe closes that boundary: two identical predicates that write `accepted=1` and `accepted=2` alternate indefinitely. Insertion order controls the observed value; cycle detection stops on repeated graph state after two passes, while the undetected run reaches its progress budget. Termination protection exists for this graph-state cycle, but there is no confluence or conflict-resolution guarantee ([results](SUBSTRATE_OVERLAPPING_RULE_CONFLICT_PROBE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/overlapping_rule_conflict_probe_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/overlapping_rule_conflict_probe_v1_20260928.json)).

The conflict-policy comparison shows that global `order` metadata does not resolve competing writes. A narrow same-match/same-guard checker can reject the exact fixture before installation, but is only a runner prototype. Graph-resident `ConflictProposal` nodes preserve both outputs and reach fixed point under either rule order; selecting or abstaining on a proposal remains a separate capability. General overlap detection and false-positive rates are open ([results](SUBSTRATE_RULE_CONFLICT_POLICY_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/rule_conflict_policy_comparison_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/rule_conflict_policy_comparison_v1_20260928.json)).

## Proposal lifecycle and tie-aware arbitration (2026-09-28)

The source-locked [lifecycle scale v1.2 panel](SUBSTRATE_CONFLICT_PROPOSAL_LIFECYCLE_SCALE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md) passes all gates in three workers through N=64: proposal creation is deduplicated on repeat closure; unique maxima resolve correctly; tied maxima reach fixed point with an explicit ambiguity flag and no selected proposal. This uses existing `Count`, `Filter`, and `MaxOver` Terms plus ordinary graph rules, so tie handling has a working construction without an engine change. The earlier unguarded resolver silently chose a tied proposal, demonstrating that a raw `MaxOver` guard is insufficient. Generation counters scale linearly in this fixture, while ambiguity-aware resolver Term evaluations rise near quadratically over measured sizes (26,058 at N=64 for unique maxima). The construction-level capability is present; low-cost arbitration at larger scale, alternative formulations, natural tie rates, and learned proposal quality remain unmeasured. This result does not support a general inference-complexity claim.

The matched [materialized resolver comparison](SUBSTRATE_CONFLICT_PROPOSAL_RESOLVER_MATERIALIZATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md) shows that the quadratic measured cost is avoidable for this fixture: computing `MaxOver` and `Count(Filter(...))` once per target, annotating top proposals, and then resolving reduces all 12 cells against the nested v1.2 construction. At N=64, unique/tied Term evaluations fall from 26,058/17,480 to 1,293/1,035. This is a practical existing-Term construction with near-linear counted work through N=64, not an asymptotic guarantee. Its unresolved lifecycle issue is invalidation when the proposal set or scores change after derived attributes have been materialized; stale decision state is not covered by the passing fixed-set panel.

That lifecycle gap was directly tested in the [revision invalidation panel](SUBSTRATE_CONFLICT_PROPOSAL_REVISION_INVALIDATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md). Across three workers, adding a tie left `ambiguous=false` despite `top_count=2`; changing a score afterward triggered zero Term evaluations and retained the old winner. The fixed-set optimization must therefore not be treated as a correct mutable resolver. Explicit revision events, dependency ordering, and retraction/reset semantics remain capability gaps; the next evaluation should test a revision-token or event-driven design against the independent score oracle.

The [explicit revision pipeline](SUBSTRATE_CONFLICT_PROPOSAL_REVISION_PIPELINE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md) now demonstrates a correct caller-orchestrated path: a revision node wakes three ordered closures that materialize the maximum, count maxima, and write ambiguity/selection. It passes score-oracle checks for adding a better proposal, creating/removing a tie, and rescoring the proposal. The caller must signal every update and execute all stages. Retained revision nodes increase later closure work substantially (N=64 rises from 789 Term evaluations initially to 4,005 on the fifth revision), so bounded history/compaction and interrupted-stage recovery remain open.

The [bounded-retention follow-up](SUBSTRATE_CONFLICT_PROPOSAL_REVISION_COMPACTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md) removes the prior revision node and its five event/stage edges before opening the next revision. All mutation oracles still pass and one live event remains; at N=64 the fifth-revision cost falls 4,005→801 Term evaluations relative to retained history. Host cleanup measured about 0.002 ms in these small fixtures. This supports caller-managed event compaction as a solution to history-dependent closure work, while built-in compaction, durable audit retention, atomic stage completion, and interrupted-pipeline recovery remain absent or unmeasured.

The [revision recovery v1.1 panel](SUBSTRATE_PROPOSAL_REVISION_RECOVERY_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md) adds field-level revision stamps and a commit marker. At every injected cut between max/count/decision closures, the old committed revision remains visible; the resume routine runs only missing stages and commits after all three stamps match. Final state is oracle-correct across all tested sizes/cuts. This covers interruption between completed closures in one live process only. Consumers are not yet shown to enforce the commit marker, and neither durable process recovery nor in-closure partial-effect behavior is established.

The source-locked [consumer gate v1.2](SUBSTRATE_COMMITTED_RESULT_CONSUMER_GATE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md) closes the authored-rule consumer case: its consumer refuses incomplete and complete-but-uncommitted fields, then awakens after `resolved_revision` commits and reads the score-oracle result. This is not substrate-wide access enforcement; direct host reads and unguarded production rules can still consume stale fields. The initial v1/v1.1 captures are exploratory due analyzer defects; v1.2 is the confirmatory record.

The [process-restart recovery v1.1 panel](SUBSTRATE_PROPOSAL_REVISION_PROCESS_RESTART_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md) confirms that a fresh Python process can restore graph, grammar, and semantically equivalent rule storage from the source-linked JSON checkpoint, resume a cut pipeline, commit, and awaken a consumer. It preserves 36 hashed/fsync'ed checkpoints and passes every cut/size oracle. Rule import normalizes match-clause order and recalculates content hashes, while graph and grammar are exact. Consumers also need a `consumed_revision` stamp: the prior label remains stored during a newer uncommitted revision. This is process-boundary recovery on one local persistence path, not power-loss or concurrent-writer durability; recovery from an OS kill during a native closure remains untested.

The [progress-budget overshoot panel v1.1](SUBSTRATE_RULE_RUN_PROGRESS_BUDGET_OVERSHOOT_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md) measures an important run-control boundary: `run_rules_report(max_steps=1)` processes its entire eligible dirty batch before checking the outer pass budget again. Across 1–64 independent rules, all N writes occur and overshoot is N−1; the following call reaches fixed point. This budget is not a strict rule/effect cancellation cap, so callers cannot infer a hard interruption bound from `max_steps` alone. The panel measures one authored batch shape, not a universal overshoot limit.

The [budgeted stage-order probe](SUBSTRATE_BUDGETED_REVISION_PIPELINE_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md) shows why this matters for revision commits. A reverse-ordered unguarded pass writes all stage stamps and commits a wrong empty selection, then remains stably wrong; normal ordering happens to hide the bug. Readiness guards make the reverse order fail closed at the budget cut and recover on the next settle. Equal revision stamps do not prove derived values were computed from current upstream state; each stage needs an upstream-current guard. These guards remain a rule-authoring contract rather than an engine-enforced dependency type.

The [budget-cut process-restart panel](SUBSTRATE_BUDGET_CUT_PROCESS_RESTART_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md) confirms that guarded partial state survives a clean process boundary: reverse-order guarded closure preserves the old committed/consumed revision, then a fresh process resumes to the correct score winner. Normal order can finish within the batch; the unguarded reverse-order negative control checkpoints and restores its false commit unchanged. All 106 gates pass across three workers and N=2/64. This adds budget-limited restart coverage, but does not test interruption inside native execution, power loss, migration, concurrent writers, or other rule families.

## Current-source matcher-order replication (2026-09-28)

The updated-source anaphora replay passes all 293 analyzer gates across 48 workers; all task outputs and terminal graph digests agree, and the eight realized orders cost 8–16 target candidate tests. Paired with v1, deterministic per-cell outputs and non-timing counters match exactly. The v1.1 run's logs verify against their hashes; its runner reused and overwrote v1 child-log paths, so v1's original individual logs are no longer independently verifiable. This strengthens repeatability for one task-derived linear pattern only; broad topology coverage and charged estimator evaluation remain open. See the [current-source result](SUBSTRATE_TASK_DERIVED_ANAPHORA_JOIN_ORDER_V1_1_CURRENT_SOURCE_RESULTS_20260928.md) and [paired comparison](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_derived_anaphora_join_order_v1_source_replication_comparison_20260928.json).

## Captured runtime graph structure (2026-09-28)

A deterministic post-hoc census of the source-linked full-profile graph capture finds 25,142 nodes, 11,762 typed edges, and 14,071 components after projecting to an undirected simple graph. There are 13,711 isolated nodes; the largest component has 6,201 nodes (24.7%); maximum degree is 393. Removing `has_concept` edges shifts the largest component to 1,885 nodes. This supports global sparsity with relation-specific connectivity, but says nothing conclusive about bounded inference separators or learned frame locality. Next map explicit ownership to rule/factor dependencies and measure separator dimensions, induced width, and partition maintenance cost ([result](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_GRAPH_STRUCTURE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_live_runtime_graph_structure_v1.py), [record](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_runtime_graph_structure_v1_20260928.json)).

## ProblemShape structural recognition and behavior boundary (2026-09-28)

A post-hoc join of two passing panels confirms recurrence reuse of the BP structural key across four max/sum parameter pairs. A separate six-pair behavior panel shows every same-key pair has different beliefs (max absolute delta 2.0); sum-product converges in six and max-product in two. This supports narrow structural schema acquisition while establishing that the key is not a behavior-equivalence certificate. Keep `combine` and other behavior-affecting values explicit in any downstream reuse. Test held-out consumer use with an output-quality oracle before making broader learning claims ([crosswalk](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_KEY_BEHAVIOUR_CROSSWALK_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [join record](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_key_behavior_crosswalk_v1_20260928.json)).

## ProblemShape integration audit (2026-09-28)

A static AST scan of 833 production Python files found no statically linked consumer of `domains.problem_shape`. The growth rules currently decode and classify observations and mint structural shapes, but no production domain uses the key to select or replace behavior. Learning mechanics are demonstrated on narrow fixtures; downstream capability impact remains unimplemented and unmeasured. Next define and evaluate a test-only consumer with parameter preservation, abstention, quality oracle, and charged cost before considering production integration ([audit report](SUBSTRATE_PROBLEM_SHAPE_PRODUCTION_CONSUMER_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [scanner](../../../scripts/audit_problem_shape_production_consumers_v1.py), [record](../../../evaluation/reports/problem_shape_production_consumer_audit_v1_20260928.json)).

## Native graph storage at 10⁷ nodes (2026-09-28)

The public Python-to-native path constructs a 10,000,000-node, 9,999,999-edge sparse chain in median 15.66 s with 2.15 GB incremental RSS (2.393–2.394 GB process peak), across two fresh workers; the full 10k/100k/1M/10M panel passes 8 runner gates and 11 independent checks. This is storage-only with empty node attributes on one host. It does not establish closure, inference, or realistic-workload tractability. Next add representative graph facts/rules and measure closure separately ([results](SUBSTRATE_GRAPH_STORAGE_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_graph_storage_scale_v1_1.py), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_graph_storage_scale_v1_1.py), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_graph_storage_scale_v1_1_20260928.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_graph_storage_scale_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/graph_storage_scale_v1_1_lock_20260928.json)).

## Native single-rule closure through 10⁷ nodes (2026-09-28)

One attributed ring with a single monotone status-transition rule reaches exact fixed point through 10M nodes/edges. Across the four sizes, audit work is exactly 2N candidate tests, N attr-set attempts, and 8N Term evaluations; at 10M audit-off closure takes 8.85 CPU seconds. Current live RSS after closure rises 3.29 GB; the 6.13 GB process high-water includes a validation query returning 10M node IDs. Sixteen source-locked workers and 11 independent gates pass. This establishes one favorable workload envelope, not general closure complexity, interacting-rule behavior, or inference scaling. The interacting topology panel below covers a two-rule topology contrast through 1M nodes; higher rule diversity and larger interacting closures remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_RULE_CLOSURE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_RULE_CLOSURE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_rule_closure_scale_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_rule_closure_scale_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/rule_closure_scale_v1_lock_20260928.json)).

## Interacting closure across tree topology through 10⁶ nodes (2026-09-28)

Two monotone rules propagate `pending → ready → active` over balanced binary trees and high-fanout stars. The 36-worker source-locked panel and all 11 independent analyzer checks pass at N=10k/100k/1M, with exact full-state fixed-point oracles. At 1M, audit-off median closure is 6.11 CPU seconds on the balanced tree versus 1.68 seconds on the star; audit-on candidate tests are 39,999,960 versus 3,999,996, while both perform 1,999,998 attribute-set attempts. The balanced tree takes 20 passes versus 2. This identifies topology-sensitive work for this rule pair, not a general closure law. Live RSS and validation-inclusive peak are reported separately ([results](SUBSTRATE_INTERACTING_TREE_CLOSURE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_INTERACTING_TREE_CLOSURE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_interacting_tree_closure_scale_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_interacting_tree_closure_scale_v1.py), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_interacting_tree_closure_scale_v1_20260928.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_interacting_tree_closure_scale_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/interacting_tree_closure_scale_v1_lock_20260928.json)).

## Interacting-tree construction bottleneck (2026-09-28)

Post-hoc analysis of those same locked workers finds that 1M-star edge construction takes a median 456.86 s, versus 1.68 s for the balanced tree; star construction grows 163.2× from 100k to 1M. The 10M star pilot emitted no result and was interrupted after more than nine minutes. Source inspection finds duplicate detection scans each `(source, edge type)` adjacency bucket, which is a plausible explanation for the high-fanout curve, but this is not a measured causal intervention. The construction analyzer passes 13/13 checks. Treat the existing 1M closure result as valid, while recognizing that extending this fixture to higher fanout first requires a high-degree duplicate-check optimization or equivalent builder ([results](SUBSTRATE_INTERACTING_TREE_CONSTRUCTION_COST_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_interacting_tree_construction_cost_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_interacting_tree_construction_cost_v1.py)).

## Finite-key termination restriction on four admission routes (2026-09-28)

The static finite-key checker rejects a fresh-`add_node` rule, but direct `add_rule`, `import_rules`, rule-patch revision, and graph-resident self-mint each accept it in 3/3 source-locked workers. The self-minted rule is installed but never fires because its match edge is absent. This confirms that the proven finite-key termination fragment is not mandatory on these paths; it does not demonstrate nontermination. V1's default-cycle fixture failure and the unscored setup pilot are retained and disclosed ([v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_MINT_FRAGMENT_ADMISSION_PATH_PROBE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [source audit](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_FRAGMENT_SOURCE_AUDIT_V1_20260928.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_MINT_FRAGMENT_ADMISSION_PATH_PROBE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260928.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mint_fragment_admission_path_probe_v1_1_20260928.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mint_fragment_admission_path_probe_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/mint_fragment_admission_path_probe_v1_1_lock_20260928.json)).

## Mint-termination restriction audit (2026-09-28)

The existing finite-key checker supports a finite-universe argument for a narrow insertion-only fragment, with a coarse progressing-fact bound `S + T(N₀+S)² − M₀`; fixed rule/pattern sizes give polynomial data complexity. It is not weak acyclicity and is not enforced by the native rule loader. Native parsing admits computed effect Terms, general `where` Terms, and a wider effect language, so arbitrary production-rule termination remains unproved. See the [source crosswalk and proof conditions](SUBSTRATE_FINITE_KEY_CLOSURE_FRAGMENT_SOURCE_AUDIT_V1_20260928.md).

## High-degree edge lookup and 10M interacting star (2026-09-28)

An adaptive target lookup for high-degree `(source, edge type)` buckets preserves low-degree scans and changes 1M-star edge construction from 456.86 s to 1.66 s (275.6×); duplicate replacement, deletion, index demotion/recreation, and sparse lookup controls pass. The 10M star replay then passes exact graph and two-rule fixed-point oracles in three source-locked workers. Median node/edge construction is 23.07/19.93 s; closure is 17.78 s; live post-closure RSS is 4.14 GB and validation-inclusive peak is 7.02 GB. The 10M balanced-tree extension also passes three workers: construction is similar (23.17/20.92 s) but closure is 77.89 s (4.38× star), with 5.10 GB live post-closure RSS and 6.33 GB process peak. Both concern one fixed monotone rule pair on one host; audit is off, high-degree deletion churn and wider rule diversity remain open ([star results](SUBSTRATE_INTERACTING_TREE_EDGE_INDEX_V1_3_SCALE_RESULTS_20260928.md), [balanced results](SUBSTRATE_INTERACTING_TREE_EDGE_INDEX_V1_4_BALANCED_SCALE_RESULTS_20260928.md), [1M causal replay](SUBSTRATE_INTERACTING_TREE_EDGE_INDEX_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [star analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_interacting_tree_edge_index_v1_3_analysis_20260928.json), [balanced analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_interacting_tree_edge_index_v1_4_1_balanced_analysis_20260928.json), [implementation](../../../runners/dsl/src/graph.rs)).

## High-degree edge deletion and reinsertion cost (2026-09-28)

Exact full-bucket deletion and reinsertion passes at N=5k/20k/80k across nine locked workers. Delete-all medians are 35 ms/551 ms/8.70 s; every 4× size increase multiplies deletion time about 15.7×, and per-edge deletion cost rises from 7.0 to 108.7 µs. Reinsertion remains near-linear over these sizes. The original single-edge path repeatedly retains over source, typed-source, and global edge-type vectors, identifying deletion as the operation-cost bottleneck. This is one controlled bucket, not mixed-degree churn ([churn results](SUBSTRATE_HIGH_DEGREE_EDGE_DELETE_CHURN_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_HIGH_DEGREE_EDGE_DELETE_CHURN_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_high_degree_edge_delete_churn_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_high_degree_edge_delete_churn_v1.py)).

A paired nine-worker sequential-vs-batch replay passes graph, hook, duplicate/absent-key, and remaining-adjacency-order checks. Batch deletion takes 3.53/9.16/42.71 ms at N=5k/20k/80k, compared with 35.94/569.54/8,740 ms sequential (10.2×/62.1×/204.6×). Stable compaction preserves order and avoids a permanent position table. The public `remove_node` route is also measured in nine fresh workers across those sizes: all 8 runner gates and 13 independent checks pass, with medians 0.559/2.68/13.36 ms (112/134/167 ns per incident edge). This validates one outgoing-hub node deletion, not general mixed-bucket mutation. Rule-heavy dirty propagation, cursor invalidation, mutation-log parity, incoming edges, self-loops, and mixed types remain to be measured ([batch results](SUBSTRATE_HIGH_DEGREE_EDGE_DELETE_BATCH_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [node-removal results](SUBSTRATE_HIGH_DEGREE_REMOVE_NODE_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [node-removal analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_high_degree_remove_node_scale_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_HIGH_DEGREE_EDGE_DELETE_BATCH_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [batch analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_high_degree_edge_delete_batch_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_high_degree_edge_delete_batch_v1.py)).
A three-worker mixed-topology semantic replay closes the listed incoming-edge, self-loop, mixed-type, and attribute teardown cases on one deterministic fixture. All 7 runner gates and 8 independent checks pass; canonical post-state equals an independent filter, the unrelated edge survives, and public hook behavior matches the node-delete contract. This is correctness evidence only; rule-driven invalidation and mutation-log parity remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_MIXED_NODE_DELETE_SEMANTICS_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mixed_node_delete_semantics_v1_1_20260928.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mixed_node_delete_semantics_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_mixed_node_delete_semantics_v1.py)).
A pre-fix paired replay identified a default-mode stale-state bug: deleting the only edge read by a typed `Edges` Term yielded a zero-work fixed point with derived count 1 while the graph count was 0. A source-level fix now always notifies readers of edge types removed as part of `remove_node`. In the post-fix replay, default and precise modes both enumerate the reader once and update the derived count to zero; all 8 runner gates and 8 independent analysis checks pass across three workers. This validates the direct counterexample after correction, while where-only negation, other Term families, mixed-type batches, and larger reader sets remain open ([pre-fix results](SUBSTRATE_DELETE_ACTIVE_RULE_INVALIDATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [fix results](SUBSTRATE_DELETE_ACTIVE_RULE_INVALIDATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_delete_active_rule_invalidation_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_delete_active_rule_invalidation_v1.py), [source](../../../runners/dsl/src/substrate.rs)).
A follow-up where-only reader panel finds that full rescan, incremental, and the unsound type-local falsifier all reopen N slots after blocker-node deletion, with identical graphs and exactly N candidate pairs at N=8/32/128. The reason is the generic node-mutation fallback clears all where-rule cursors; deletion correctness is preserved at linear reader cost, and this node-delete panel cannot isolate the type-local invalidator ([results](SUBSTRATE_DELETE_WHERE_CURSOR_INVALIDATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_delete_where_cursor_invalidation_v1_1_20260928.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_delete_where_cursor_invalidation_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_delete_where_cursor_invalidation_v1.py)).
An edge-only follow-up removes that masking fallback. Full rescan and sound incremental matching reopen all N slots with identical graphs, while the unsound cursor falsifier reopens none. This validates type-local where-reader invalidation for one Count/Edges family. Candidate work is N for sound mode, so the operation still needs a full reader rematch ([results](SUBSTRATE_DELETE_EDGE_WHERE_CURSOR_INVALIDATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_delete_edge_where_cursor_invalidation_v1_20260928.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_delete_edge_where_cursor_invalidation_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_delete_edge_where_cursor_invalidation_v1.py)).

### Where-reader fan-out measurement

A 36-worker panel expands deletion-triggered where readers to `Count(Edges)` and `Count(InNeighbours)`, N=32/128, and R=1/4/16. Full rescan and sound incremental outputs match; the unsound falsifier misses every newly enabled slot. Sound candidate work is exactly N×R and reader enumerations R, documenting a linear full-rematch cost rather than incremental savings for these where flips ([results](SUBSTRATE_WHERE_READER_FANOUT_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_where_reader_fanout_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_where_reader_fanout_v1.py)).

### Transitive and negated where-reader scaling

A 36-worker v1.1 panel validates sound/full-rescan parity and unsound-falsifier divergence for `ReachableFrom` and `Not Exists` readers at N=128/512 and R=1/16/64. At N=512,R=64 both have 32,768 candidate pairs, while initial transitive evaluation charges 16.78M reachable-edge visits (site-defined units) versus 32,768 units for `Not Exists`. This surfaces repeated graph traversal under binding fan-out; it is a synthetic topology measurement, not natural-task prevalence ([results](SUBSTRATE_WHERE_READER_TERM_FANOUT_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_where_reader_term_fanout_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_where_reader_term_fanout_v1.py)).

### Repeated ReachableFrom query reuse

A 27-worker paired panel measures the experimental same-graph `ReachableFrom` cache at N=32/128/512 and R=1/16/64. Across all nine cells, exact adjacency-work counts match N²R with cache off and N with cache on; at N=512,R=64, this is 16.78M versus 512 visits. Fixed-point reports, final graphs, runtime access traces, and dropped-event counts match exactly. A post-insertion freshness control changes from zero to eight expected rule outputs in both arms; post-mutation work is 72 visits off versus 9 on. The cache is experimental and default-off. This is constructed repeated-query evidence, not natural reuse prevalence or full mutation-API coverage ([results](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_FROM_CACHE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_FROM_CACHE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_reachable_from_cache_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_reachable_from_cache_v1_analysis_20260928.json)).

A second paired panel loads the actual `browser_affordance_reach` seed rules and crosses N=2/8/32/128 nested affordances with B=1/4 direct wrappers. Across 24 fresh workers, graph, progress, matcher counters, and runtime access traces are exact between cache arms; all authored mirror edges appear. At N=128, reachable-edge work falls 2.10M→257 (B=1) and 587,776→260 (B=4), with audited median wall reductions of 2.57× in both cells. This is source-authored rule evidence on controlled DOM-shaped graphs, not live browser pages or natural workload frequency ([results](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_browser_reachable_cache_v1_analysis_20260928.json)).

The historical source census v1 found one executable seed Term AST; a fresh current-source census searches all 1,016 seed manifest paths and finds two typed Terms, both in the two-rule browser-affordance family, plus five helper call sites in 880 Python files. Direct `PySubstrate.evaluate()` still uses a fresh default cache, and the high-level wrapper still lacks a toggle. This is source frequency, not dynamic task activation; the browser-profile cache captures remain synthetic DOM-shaped runs, not live pages ([current census](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_FROM_CACHE_USAGE_CENSUS_V2_RESULTS_20261005.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_reachable_from_cache_usage_v2_20261005.json), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/audit_reachable_from_cache_usage_v2.py), [historical census](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_FROM_CACHE_USAGE_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md)).

A full five-seed `WorldAdapter.tick()` replay then confirms integration: all eight rules from the profile manifests are loaded, every N affordance receives its mirror edge, and cache arms match in graph, progress, matcher work, and access traces. At N=128, reachable work falls 2.10M→257 (B=1) and 587,776→260 (B=4); audited tick speedups are 2.67×/2.76×. This uses DOM-shaped records without launching Chromium. A launch preflight fails under the current sandbox, so live-page latency and prevalence remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_PROFILE_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_PROFILE_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_browser_profile_reachable_cache_v1_analysis_20260928.json)).

Telemetry v1.1 records exact cache hits/misses against every `ReachableFrom` Term call and high-water payload estimates during that same tick. At N=128, peak payload is 8.46 KB for B=1 (16,382 hits/2 misses) and 8.91 KB for B=4 (16,763/5), across two/five entries. Entries are cleared by tick completion. The estimate excludes hash-table and allocator memory; it is not RSS. This closes query-count and fixture-payload attribution, but not overall process/cache memory on live pages or other workload families ([results](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_PROFILE_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_PROFILE_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_browser_profile_reachable_cache_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [source](../../../runners/dsl/src/term.rs)).

Cache v1.2 removes the cached-footprint copy from each hit by sharing result/footprint storage and skips trace-footprint replay when access tracing is disabled. After fixing a same-pass stale-read counterexample and the browser mirror cleanup condition, the profile was rerun on the corrected seed. The source-locked v1.3 24-worker audit/quiet factorial passes semantic parity, exact audited trace parity, counter accounting, and provenance checks. Quiet N=128 tick medians improve 12.81× (139.9→11.8 ms, B=1) and 7.46× (47.0→6.1 ms, B=4); N=2 is near parity with three replicates. N=128 estimated peak payload is 8.51/9.03 KB over two/five entries. Timing and payload remain scoped to synthetic DOM-shaped records in the five-seed adapter profile; the run does not establish live-page performance or prevalence ([v1.3 results](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_PROFILE_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_3_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_PROFILE_REACHABLE_CACHE_V1_3_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_browser_profile_reachable_cache_v1_3_analysis_20260928.json), [source](../../../runners/dsl/src/term.rs)).

The mutation invalidation panel primes a live cache entry before each intervention, then exercises ten public surfaces: node insert/delete, checked/unchecked edge insertion, single/batch edge deletion, scalar and bulk attribute writes, and type change. Across 30 fresh workers, each cache-on arm records a fresh miss and the recomputed reachable set has the expected cardinality; graph, rule progress, and mirror parity match cache-off. That historical seed lacked reachability-based mirror retraction; the truth-maintenance repair now passes its dedicated seven-checkpoint replay in both cache modes ([mutation results](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_CACHE_MUTATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [truth-maintenance protocol](SUBSTRATE_BROWSER_AFFORDANCE_TRUTH_MAINTENANCE_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/browser_affordance_truth_maintenance_v1_analysis_20260928.json)).

A separate minimal monotone three-rule probe found a serious internal case: after one rule inserted an edge read by a later same-pass `ReachableFrom` consumer, the cached engine returned a false fixed point and omitted a derived edge in 5/5 workers. The fix clears graph-dependent memo state after each progressing rule attempt, before the next rule. The v1.1 locked rerun passes in 5/5 workers with exact cache-on/off graph and progress parity; a follow-up 40-worker matrix passes across eight effect families (add/delete edge, add/conditional node, delete node, set/conditional attr, and replace value), and the public mutation panel still passes. Deferred effects, rule patches, dynamic Term dependencies, and other rule families remain open. The v1 falsifier is a source-locked reproducibility capture, not a preregistered test; its protocol status is corrected in the [amendment](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_CACHE_IN_PASS_MUTATION_V1_AMENDMENT_20260928.md) ([v1 falsifier](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_CACHE_IN_PASS_MUTATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [v1.1 result](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_CACHE_IN_PASS_MUTATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [effect matrix](SUBSTRATE_REACHABLE_CACHE_EFFECT_MATRIX_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [implementation](../../../runners/dsl/src/substrate.rs)).

## Held-topic request-form capability panel (2026-09-28)

A preregistered seven-prompt/two-worker panel confirms that answer availability is not the same as request-form capability. Both workers exactly answer “What can you tell me about copper?”, “What do you know about copper?”, and “What is copper?” with turn-linked supported evidence/receipts; both preserve the unresolved/no-leak unknown-topic control. “Tell me about copper” and its polite imperative are parsed as `instruction` and return a self-identity report. “Can you tell me about copper?” routes to `honest_capability`, asks for grounding information, and leaves the copper answer-topic Goal unresolved despite the held answer. This is a narrow arbitration gap across imperative and polar request forms, not evidence of a general retrieval failure. The lock includes the current source/native closure, runtime data, worktree context and exact prompt set; all stability/repetition gates pass. Next conduct blinded intent adjudication and held-out minimal paraphrase tests before changing route/oracle behavior ([results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_REQUEST_FORM_PANEL_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_REQUEST_FORM_PANEL_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_request_form_panel_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_request_form_panel_v1_20260928.json), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/held_topic_request_form_panel_v1_lock_20260928.json)).

## Public native-module inventory coverage audit (2026-09-28)

A deterministic audit compares every top-level `pub mod NAME;` declaration in `runners/dsl/src/lib.rs` against the exact Rust source paths linked by the machine-readable capability map. The crate exposes 90 such modules, all 90 now map to one or more of 23 capability rows, every declared source file exists, and no module remains unclassified. The seven added capability rows cover cognitive runtime/scheduling, feedback control/viability, motor program synthesis, auditory processing, video codec/transcoding, graph neural/pipeline execution, and hypothesis admissibility; supporting rule/Term/serialization, vision/spatial, and learning modules attach to existing rows. The map builder resolves 184 source links, 56 test references, and 224 linked runners with no unresolved sources or missing evidence references. The rows retain explicit gaps: several results are historical or narrow, one row has no linked test file, and test links do not imply audited coverage. This is public top-level Rust module coverage only; private/inline modules and the Python domain surface are outside this census. Next audit whether linked tests actually exercise each newly mapped path, then prioritize current-source workload replication and independent quality/resource measurements ([audit script](../../../scripts/audit_substrate_native_module_inventory.py), [machine-readable audit](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/native_module_inventory_audit_20260928.json), [map builder](../../../scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py)).


**Scheduler work-trace overhead scale v2 (2026-09-28):** a source-locked three-worker factorial on warmed 8/32/128-rule positive fixtures passes semantic, graph, scheduler-counter, trace-presence, and no-drop gates. With 256 fixed-point calls per arm, median trace-only deltas are 194–499 ns/call, but ranges are noisy and cross zero for one size. This is a narrow steady-state native pilot, not a general engine overhead bound; mutation-to-fixpoint and matcher-dense costs remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_TRACE_OVERHEAD_SCALE_V2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/scheduler_trace_overhead_scale_v2_analysis_20260928.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_TRACE_OVERHEAD_SCALE_V2_PREREG_20260928.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_scheduler_trace_overhead_scale_v2.py), [raw report](../../../evaluation/reports/scheduler_trace_overhead_scale_v2_20260928.json)).


**Mutation-to-fixpoint trace overhead v1 (2026-09-28):** three source-locked process blocks cover 18 cells across sparse and matcher-dense positive closure at 8/32/128 rules. Semantic, graph/report, candidate-work, counter-parity, and trace-drop gates pass; candidate counts confirm one sparse and 72 dense matcher candidates per fixture. Timing deltas change sign and have wide ranges, so trace overhead remains unestimated. Synthetic-family result only; natural workload prevalence and scaling remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_MUTATION_CLOSURE_TRACE_OVERHEAD_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/mutation_closure_trace_overhead_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_mutation_closure_trace_overhead_v1.py), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/mutation_closure_trace_overhead_v1_20260928.json)).


**Rooted-frame operation-interface crosswalk v1 (2026-09-28):** independent reconstruction of 24 joined candidate/reference frames passes graph identity and typed-cut checks. Radius-2 BFS candidates of 409–410 nodes touch 5.99%/12.01% of selected trace events, with 49–51 observed RuleUID footprints spanning each cut. This confirms state-graph proximity is not operation isolation for the tested prompt pair; trace completeness, finite task interfaces, and learned-cut prevalence remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_ROOTED_FRAME_OPERATION_INTERFACE_CROSSWALK_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/rooted_frame_operation_interface_crosswalk_v1_20260928.json), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_rooted_frame_operation_interface_crosswalk_v1.py)).
**Live self-report scheduler-work and matcher accounting v1.2 (2026-09-28):** three fresh workers from a double-read, archived source snapshot reproduce both prompts and all operation accounting. Across six turns, 72–78 all-scope work rows per prompt balance scheduler/deferred snapshots, and candidate/Term attribution conserves the recorded totals. The prior positive `report your organisation` action route has regressed on this current snapshot: both prompts return gap replies with no route or Goal in all workers. The capability gate fails despite successful instrumentation checks; claims are limited to cost accounting on this gap route, with no causal regression explanation or general cost bound ([results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_SELF_REPORT_WORK_TRACE_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_self_report_work_trace_v1_2_analysis_20260928.json), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_self_report_work_trace_v1_2_lock_20260928.json)).
**Python implementation-source coverage audit v1 (2026-09-28):** across `domains/`, `substrate/`, and `runners/dsl/python/`, the source-linked capability map directly links 32/833 Python files (3.8%). Static AST imports from five runtime entrypoints reach 247/833; only 15 files are both linked and import-reachable. These counts do not prove execution: import traversal misses dynamic dispatch, and many domain modules are seed-selected. The per-file inventory records hashes, top-level public symbols, import edges, reachability, and capability-row links. This exposes an important map-coverage gap; it does not mean the other files are dead or capable. Next classify unlinked modules into runtime capability, shared support, generated/experimental, or legacy/excluded, and validate dynamic reachability from seed manifests and observed runtime loads ([audit results](SUBSTRATE_PYTHON_SOURCE_COVERAGE_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [machine inventory](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/python_source_coverage_audit_v1_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/audit_substrate_python_source_coverage_v1.py)).
**Python source coverage audit v1.1 (2026-09-28):** the dynamic-load extension adds the conversation profile’s 65 declared `importlib` preloads and hashes all 838 loader-discovered seed manifests. Static import closure reaches 247 files; the explicit profile list expands it to 248, adding only `domains/reference_slab.py`. No exact dotted Python module references occur in 742 loader-eligible seed manifests, consistent with native graph-rule/data loading. The map’s direct Python links remain 32/833; none of these counts establishes per-task execution. See [results](SUBSTRATE_PYTHON_SOURCE_COVERAGE_AUDIT_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_PYTHON_SOURCE_COVERAGE_AUDIT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260928.md), [per-file inventory](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/python_source_coverage_audit_v1_1_20260928.json), and [runner](../../../scripts/audit_substrate_python_source_coverage_v1_1.py).
**Conversation-profile module-load observation v1 (2026-09-28):** three fresh source-locked workers actually invoked the full 65-entry profile preload list; all 65 resolved in declared order and were newly imported. The profile selected 74 seeds without reported loader failures, built a 2,100-node/1,390-edge graph, and added 86 domain modules including transitive imports. All observed module additions fall within the static-plus-declared reachability inventory. Profile-call times were 558–2,797 ms and peak RSS 127–386 MiB; the first-run spread, small n, and uncalibrated observer overhead make these descriptive startup measurements only. A hash-verified 1,778-file source/native/seed replay bundle is preserved. This validates one profile's import map, not module necessity or task capability ([results](SUBSTRATE_PROFILE_RUNTIME_IMPORT_OBSERVATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_PROFILE_RUNTIME_IMPORT_OBSERVATION_V1_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/profile_runtime_import_observation_v1_analysis_20260928.json), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/profile_runtime_import_observation_v1_lock_20260928.json), [bundle](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/source_bundles/profile_runtime_import_observation_v1_20260928.tar.zst)).

### Conversation task module activation v1.1 (2026-09-28)

Five authored conversation task conditions were profiled in two fresh workers each with an independently checked task oracle. All 12 integrity/source/cache gates pass; task outcomes are 8/10 (scene description, held self-knowledge, self-report action, unknown-word clarification each 2/2; concern authoring 0/2). Per-condition Python module, function, and call-edge sets repeat exactly; route traces cover 25–32 domain modules and 3,240–83,070 Python domain calls. WordNet corpus and warmed index were locked and stable. A post-hoc join finds that the panel called 40 distinct Python domain modules, all present in the 833-file source inventory, but only 3 of the 40 have direct capability-map file links. This identifies a source-linking gap on the observed routes; it does not make the other modules independent capability areas. See the [crosswalk](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_source_crosswalk_v1_20260928.json) and [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_substrate_conversation_task_module_source_crosswalk_v1.py). This is route activation evidence, not causal necessity, native-work coverage, natural task prevalence, or an uninstrumented cost estimate. V1 is exploratory because it omitted the corpus/cache lock. A first-pass source adjudication now groups the 37 unlinked modules into language/communication (25), code authoring (2), concept/frame/context representation (4), host/perceptual adapters (2), and lexical/reference support (4). One path, `domains/comprehend_text.py`, changed after the locked capture, so its current-source map link is deferred. The other proposed groupings preserve distinctions between code authoring and enumerative synthesis, frame ontology and low-width cuts, and lexical support and stand-alone cognition ([adjudication](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_MODULE_SOURCE_ADJUDICATION_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [machine record](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_adjudication_v1_20260928.json), [analyzer](../../../scripts/adjudicate_substrate_conversation_module_sources_v1.py)). The v1.2 refresh below materializes the adjudicated links after current-source replay; the next measurement broadens the panel across perception, reasoning, learning, and planning with independent oracles and native-work counters ([v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_1_PREREG_20260928.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_activation_v1_1_20260928.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_activation_v1_1_analysis_20260928.json), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_module_activation_v1_1_lock_20260928.json), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_conversation_task_module_activation_v1_1.py)).

### Current-source task activation to source-map join v1.2 (2026-09-28)

The five-route conversation panel was repeated under a current-source lock after `domains/comprehend_text.py` drifted. All 12 task/source/cache gates pass and the task outcomes remain 8/10. A fresh Python census now links 69/833 files (8.3%), compared with 32/833 in the historical v1.1 map; static and profile-declared reachability stay at 247/833 and 248/833. The independent source join passes six checks and finds that all 40 Python domain modules called by this panel have direct capability-map links. The mapping change improves source navigation and does not improve substrate capability or establish causal necessity. The known task boundary remains: concern authoring fails 0/2 and scene description takes about 11 s under profiling ([v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_2_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_activation_v1_2_analysis_20260928.json), [source census](SUBSTRATE_PYTHON_SOURCE_COVERAGE_AUDIT_V1_2_RESULTS_20260928.md), [module crosswalk](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_source_crosswalk_v1_2_20260928.json)). Next profile independently scored capability families outside conversation with native-work counters and calibrated observer overhead.

### Conversation task panel v1.3 source-drift censor (2026-09-28)

A post-v1.2 current-source replay was censored because `substrate/world_adapter.py` changed during the ten-worker schedule. Two workers finished on stable inputs; the third detected within-worker source drift and seven workers rejected the stale lock. The analyzer fails completeness and source-stability gates, so none of the partial outcomes or timings are pooled. The wrapper-aware profiler did capture one conversation extent in each completed worker. V1.2 remains valid for its own locked snapshot but is not the latest adapter state; see the [censor report](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_3_CENSORED_RESULTS_20260928.md) and [raw/analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_activation_v1_3_analysis_20260928.json). A refreshed static census is run separately; repeat the live panel after the adapter source can remain stable for a full schedule.

### Current source census after the task-panel censor (2026-09-28)

The v1.3 static source census completes with stable Python, seed, and map hashes: 69/833 Python files have direct capability-map links (8.3%), six-root import reachability is 247/833, and declared profile-load reachability is 248/833. A seven-check join to the complete v1.2 conversation capture confirms all 40 activated domain modules remain directly linked and their per-file Python hashes match the census. `substrate/world_adapter.py` changed after that capture, so the join does not make v1.2 a current end-to-end route result; the later panel attempt is censored. See the [v1.3 census](SUBSTRATE_PYTHON_SOURCE_COVERAGE_AUDIT_V1_3_RESULTS_20260928.md), [module crosswalk](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_source_crosswalk_v1_3_20260928.json), and [censor report](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_3_CENSORED_RESULTS_20260928.md).

### Current-source conversation task activation v1.4 (2026-09-28)

The v1.4 replay completed after the v1.3 source-drift censor. All 12 source/task/cache gates pass across ten unique workers; the serialized `WorldAdapter.converse` wrapper is recognized exactly once per task. Oracle outcomes are 8/10: four conditions pass 2/2, while the concern prompt asks back about “two” in both trials before creating a goal or invoking a concern action. A six-worker matched-phrasing diagnostic reproduces the unresolved-dependency route for “the two letters,” “the pair of letters,” and “letter A and letter B”; the clarified token changes, but all six trials produce zero goals and no concern action. This is an input interpretation/grounding gap for these phrasings ([diagnostic results](SUBSTRATE_CONCERN_PROMPT_GROUNDING_DIAGNOSTIC_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/concern_prompt_grounding_diagnostic_v1_analysis_20260928.json)). A separate preregistered direct-dispatch probe confirms that a preconstructed compare instruction invokes concern induction and grounding, produces the same `experiment_rule` concern signature in both workers, and adds one rule; the exact unrelated `walk` control stays on the goal-only route. A follow-up executes that generated judge rule against an independent score-sign oracle: positive, negative, zero, and nonterminal cases all match in two workers, including the `done` guard and one-shot `analysed` edge. This validates rule execution semantics, not correctness of the concern for a real trial. Grounded induction and downstream judge execution are demonstrated for one authored signature, while natural-language interpretation and real-task semantic accuracy remain open ([dispatch results](SUBSTRATE_GROUNDED_CONCERN_DISPATCH_V1_3_RESULTS_20260928.md), [judge semantics results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_EXPERIMENT_JUDGE_SEMANTICS_V1_RESULTS_20260928.md), [judge analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/generated_experiment_judge_semantics_v1_analysis_20260928.json)). Paired v1.4 module, function, and call-edge key sets are exact. The activation panel refreshes the baseline for locked `world_adapter.py` source, but remains a small authored family with profiler-instrumented times and no native-work counts ([results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_4_RESULTS_20260928.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_MODULE_ACTIVATION_V1_4_PREREG_20260928.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_module_activation_v1_4_analysis_20260928.json), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_module_activation_v1_4_lock_20260928.json)). The next activation panel must cover separately scored non-conversation families and native work.

### Anaphora operation-boundary locality capture v1.3 (2026-09-29)

A source-audited retrospective analysis of 11 frozen assistant-authored anaphora tasks across ten fresh workers passes all 12 integrity checks. Seed warmup correction attributes 6–20 task nodes across turns; the owned state plus pairwise-trace dependency graph has min-fill width 2. Authored turn boundaries show 0/2/4 cross-frame state edges and 0/1–2/2 pairwise trace crossings for turns 0/1/2. The cuts also touch global graph nodes and global unary/pair accesses, while six wildcard readers and 22 wildcard writers prevent a safety claim from these graph cuts. Stable-ID comparison is confounded by turn-ordered allocation; blind dependency-minfill partitions split turn ownership substantially. This does not establish learned or naturally prevalent cheap cuts, exact inference factorization, or safe scoped execution. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ANAPHORA_OPERATION_BOUNDARY_CAPTURE_V1_3_RESULTS_20260929.md), [v1.3 source-audited analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/anaphora_operation_boundary_capture_v1_3_analysis_20260929.json), [v1.2 raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/anaphora_operation_boundary_capture_v1_2_raw_20260929.json), and [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_ANAPHORA_OPERATION_BOUNDARY_CAPTURE_V1_3_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20260929.md).

### Current-source held-out operation boundary v2.5 (2026-09-29)

Three paired traced/untraced `describe_scene` replicates pass the task/source/lifecycle/graph parity gates. Request identity is derived from Goal-to-inbound-Message links; five rule-minted children per request are classified by explicit edge policy, independently of allocation order. The lifecycle-only graph cut has width upper bound 2, 33–36 incidences and 19–21 external nodes, but the capture did not retain the expanded descendant graph projection. Only 0.45–0.66% of 1.40–1.43M selected access events per request touch the named request, other-request, shared Scene/Percept, or persistent-side-effect nodes. Most events are unclassified by those sets; the trace is partial and does not show those accesses to be unnecessary. This route cautions against using small lifecycle width as a proxy for cheap operation. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_5_RESULTS_20260929.md), [source-current analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_5_analysis_20260929.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_access_trace_v2_5_workers_raw_20260929.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_ACCESS_TRACE_V2_5_PREREG_20260929.md).

### Native scheduler/deferred work trace v2.6 (2026-09-29, censored)

The operation-work API exposes per-request scheduler/deferred counters: A records 37 scheduler passes and 54,371 selected-rule slots; B records 24 passes and 37,442 selected-rule slots. Three traced workers repeat the same counts with zero counter drops. All work is attributed to producer UID 0 (outside an active rule). Two untraced workers detect source drift, and one traced worker differs from the prior source snapshot, so this is diagnostic-only and does not establish reproducible scaling or locality. Preserve the capture and repeat after source stabilization under one exact source baseline. See [censored result](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_OPERATION_WORK_V2_6_CENSORED_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_operation_work_v2_6_analysis_20260929.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_live_describe_scene_operation_work_v2_6_workers_raw_20260929.json), and [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_OPERATION_WORK_V2_6_PREREG_20260929.md).

### Current-source operation-work replication v2.7 (2026-09-29)

Three paired traced/untraced fresh-process captures pass all 12 runner gates with source stable and exact task/whole-graph parity. All three traced workers repeat scheduler/deferred counters per serialized request; all work rows have UID 0 (outside active rule context), and no trace rows drop.
