### Live full-seed Percept-count scaling v1 (2026-09-29)

A source-locked 16-worker 3/6/12/24 Percept sweep with 4,096 fixed distractor Concepts passes 13 corrected independent checks. Every Percept gets its expected class, the three-fact semantic reply stays exact, and pair graphs are identical. Candidate tests grow by exactly 448,780 and Term evaluations by 1,796,720⅔ per added Percept over this fixture; three recognition rules account for almost all added candidate work. Scheduler/deferred counters remain constant, confirming they do not reflect matcher/Term growth. This is one authored route, not a general complexity or locality result; outbound graph-message alignment remains false. The runner's final PID guard expected 12 rather than 16 and the locked analyzer had one wrong invariant; both defects are disclosed and the corrected analysis passes. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_PERCEPT_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20260929.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_describe_scene_percept_scale_v1_analysis_20260929.json), [raw](../../../evaluation/reports/live_describe_scene_percept_scale_v1_workers_raw_20260929.json), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_PERCEPT_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20260929.md), and [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_DESCRIBE_SCENE_PERCEPT_SCALE_V1_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20260929.md).

## 2026-10-02 Recurrence candidate-admission audit

A three-worker source/native-locked audit scores all eight unchanged-generator candidates on disjoint recurrence training and held-out episodes. The task oracle requires exactly one offer for each of two due candidates and a fixed point. `dedup_identity_fix` passes both splits in all workers; seven other variants fail held-out utility. All 12 independent review gates pass. This establishes a measured candidate set for recurrence, but not ranker quality or economic benefit; one further independent family is needed before the four-family proposal-utility panel. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANDIDATE_ADMISSION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANDIDATE_ADMISSION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_admission_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_admission_v1_lock_20261002.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_admission_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [review](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_admission_v1_analysis_review_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Generic edge-idempotence proposal generation

A generic rule-structure scan derives guard proposals from each bound `add_edge` effect, with no call to `_default_candidates` and no supplied `dedup_identity_fix`. The native graph-Term selector chooses the candidate-scoped `has_mint_offer` guard across four workers using training evidence; the selected proposal passes eight held-out episodes, and tie/all-wrong controls abstain. This demonstrates generic candidate derivation in the evaluation harness plus substrate-side selection for one recurrence family. The transformation, task cue, and labels remain externally authored, so this is not yet substrate-side operator invention. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERIC_EDGE_IDEMPOTENCE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERIC_EDGE_IDEMPOTENCE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_generic_edge_idempotence_v1_analysis_20261002.json), and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_generic_edge_idempotence_v1.py).

## 2026-10-02 Repeatable edge-output counterexample

On the seeded conversation rule, the source emits one reply per inbound message; after removing its per-message guard, the generic `Not Exists(agent.sent)` proposal emits one reply total. Both proposals fail the multi-message training oracle, and the native evidence selector abstains; the rejected generic guard under-replies on three held-out messages in all four workers. This establishes a false-suppression boundary for coarse source/edge idempotence, not a natural-task error rate. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDGE_IDEMPOTENCE_REPEATABLE_BOUNDARY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDGE_IDEMPOTENCE_REPEATABLE_BOUNDARY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_edge_idempotence_repeatable_boundary_v1_analysis_20261002.json), and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_edge_idempotence_repeatable_boundary_v1.py).

## 2026-10-02 Thoughtexperiment CoW fanout at one million nodes

The retained-fanout workload now passes its complete 18-worker matrix at one million unrelated host nodes. At fanout 16, shared graph pages use 41.9 MiB median PSS delta after candidate validation versus 1,457.3 MiB for full-copy branches, and fork medians are 0.573 ms versus 184.7 ms. One validated 41-node/2-edge residual is returned while host rules stay unchanged. This is partial-CoW evidence for one authored navigation task; peak RSS includes a 4+ GiB baseline fixture, PSS does not identify live allocations, and parallel throughput, other task families, and general self-improvement remain unmeasured. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_FANOUT_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_FANOUT_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_residual_cow_fanout_scale_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_residual_cow_fanout_scale_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_cow_fanout_scale_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_fanout_scale_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Recurrence repair CoW scale and explicit release

A second Thoughtexperiment family now passes at one million linked noise nodes. Both backends restore the authored recurrence guard, produce exactly one offer, reach fixed point, and preserve primary graph/rules/revision. The corrected v1.1 worker deletes the branch wrapper and inner substrate before post-release sampling: full-copy PSS remains 193.1 MiB above baseline after a 360.0 MiB sampled peak; shared pages remain 1.3 MiB above baseline after a 19.3 MiB peak. V1's inner-substrate alias kept the branch live during its purported drop sample, so those values are not reclamation evidence. PSS does not separate live allocations and allocator-retained pages, and fast shared-page timing leaves a sparse 10 ms peak sample. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_SCALE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_scale_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json). The preserved v1 branch-resident capture is available as [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/measure_thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_scale_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_scale_v1_lock_20261002.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_scale_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_scale_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Million-node recurrence allocator attribution

In the corrected recurrence repair lifecycle, glibc-accounted in-use bytes return within 0.04 MiB of baseline after explicit branch release. Full-copy PSS remains about 193 MiB higher because glibc free arena pages retain residency; `malloc_trim(0)` releases them. Shared-page allocations likewise return near baseline. These process-wide glibc counters do not establish behavior for other allocators or attribute allocation to graph objects. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_allocator_reclamation_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Million-node concurrent recurrence wave

At fanout 8 over 64 recurrence candidates, the shared-page backend increases throughput from 10.73 to 15.47 experiments/s (1.44×), with sampled peak PSS increasing from 142 MiB sequential to 1,129 MiB concurrent. Full copy slows from 1.90 to 0.75 experiments/s and peaks at 2,053 MiB. Branch fork/setup stays Python/GIL-serialized while native closure overlaps. This measures one synthetic recurrence workload, not broad parallel efficiency. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_scale_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Million-node recurrence branch cancellation

At fanout eight, four in-progress branches are dropped after repair and before closure; all four survivors pass the exact-once/fixed-point oracle for 64 candidates. PSS decreases after cancellation, but shared-page concurrent setup peaks at 534 MiB while the all-live checkpoint is 72 MiB. After all references clear, full-copy residency remains 1.65 GiB over baseline and requires allocator attribution. This does not cancel native closure mid-operation. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_cancel_scale_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Concurrent recurrence cancellation allocator attribution

After eight-branch fanout and half-cancellation, full-copy in-use bytes return near baseline after release while free glibc arena rises about 1.62 GiB. `malloc_trim` releases about 1.49 GiB of resident PSS. Shared-page allocations also return close to baseline. This is process-wide glibc/Linux evidence from one workload, not general allocator behavior. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_ALLOCATOR_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_ALLOCATOR_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_cancel_allocator_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Shared edit-op hypothesis generation

Four workers per family use the canonical edit-op site finder and guard applier for recurrence and conversation. The graph-Term selector picks the correct candidate scope in all eight cells and selected candidates pass held-out episodes; broader scopes fail and controls abstain. This removes evaluator-local proposal construction but still receives the target, cue, training evidence, and oracle from the harness. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_EDGE_HYPOTHESES_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_EDGE_HYPOTHESES_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_editops_edge_hypotheses_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Trace-guided rule-site discovery pilot

Three fresh source/native-locked workers use `run_rules_delta_report` to narrow an active-rule-store scan to the rule that made a graph write during a one-step recurrence fault. The observer returns 12 firing events, no dropped events, and three edit-op guard sites from that writer; the active rule store contains substantially more than three candidates. The target oracle and cue are not arguments to the discovery function, and primary rules remain unchanged during candidate construction. This is candidate search narrowing on one authored fixture, not causal diagnosis: rule names remain semantically visible, activity is not necessity, and no ranking, held-out utility, or abstention is measured. See [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLIND_RULE_SITE_DISCOVERY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_blind_rule_site_discovery_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_blind_rule_site_discovery_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blind_rule_site_discovery_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Anonymized trace-guided proposal selection

Four fresh workers receive opaque rule aliases during discovery and generate six candidate edge guards from an exact-task writer and a correlated shadow writer. An attempted empty-effect control is observed but produces no candidates. Training yields one unique exact-once winner; the graph-Term selector chooses it in all four workers, and it passes both disjoint held-outs in every worker. Tie/all-wrong controls abstain. This measures a narrow host-orchestrated selection path with external task labels; relation names and rule structure remain available to edit generation, so it does not establish semantic cue blindness or autonomous causal diagnosis. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ANONYMIZED_CAUSE_SITE_DISCOVERY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ANONYMIZED_CAUSE_SITE_DISCOVERY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1.py), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Anonymized cause/site transfer across recurrence and conversation

The same trace-guided candidate generation and native selector pass in recurrence and multi-message conversation, four workers per family. The recurrence traces yield six candidates; conversation traces yield 18. Each family/worker has one unique exact-task training winner, selected by the native graph-Term mechanism; every selected candidate passes its disjoint held-outs, all candidates are held-out scored, and tie/all-wrong controls abstain. Correlated shadow writers and attempted no-op writers are present in both families. This is evidence of transfer between two authored task fixtures with host-supplied labels, not autonomous diagnosis or a natural-task tractability estimate. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ANONYMIZED_CAUSE_SITE_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ANONYMIZED_CAUSE_SITE_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_transfer_v1.py), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_transfer_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_transfer_v1_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Exact inference through certified width 9

Forty-eight current-native workers measure binary pairwise exact MAP at N=120, using a same-binary q=6 control and q=7/8/9 grids and sparse partial q-trees with six replicates per cell. All exact-oracle, extraction, width, profile, no-mutation and source-lock gates pass; the independent analyzer passes 40/40. Certified q=9 JT medians are 2.48 s on grids and 210 ms on sparse q-trees; q=6→9 certified-time ratios are 9.00× and 3.81×. Min-fill overestimates every q=7–9 grid width but is exact on all q-trees, showing safe-gate false refusals in the grid family. This is a width/topology sensitivity measurement on two synthetic families, not a natural graph distribution. See [results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_4_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_4_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4_20261002_analysis.json).
# 2026-10-02 Thoughtexperiment per-rule write attribution

A source-locked three-worker evaluation validates bounded per-rule attribution of graph write targets on direct mixed writes and overlapping/no-op writers. All semantic checks pass against the exact graph snapshot oracle; paired timing medians show 1.52× and 1.60× attribution overhead on these small fixtures. The trace still lacks binding and field-level value attribution, deferred-effect ownership, and evidence for target-blind diagnosis. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RULE_WRITE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RULE_WRITE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_rule_write_attribution_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_rule_write_attribution_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_rule_write_attribution_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_rule_write_attribution_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 Deferred TrackIdentity producer attribution

The engine carries the producing rule identity through one deferred TrackIdentity queue and labels the drain event as deferred. Three fresh workers reconcile all five changed percept nodes against the exact graph oracle while retaining parity across the ordinary and traced settlement paths. The panel is deliberately capped after one drain and does not establish fixed-point termination, causal uniqueness, other queue-family provenance, or target-blind diagnosis. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_TRACK_IDENTITY_ATTRIBUTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_TRACK_IDENTITY_ATTRIBUTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_track_identity_attribution_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_deferred_track_identity_attribution_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_track_identity_attribution_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_track_identity_attribution_v1_20261002.json).

## Current exact-inference cost evidence (2026-10-02)

Current-native exact binary MAP has been source locked through certified q=9 and N=504 on two synthetic families; a paired domain panel covers the supported binary and ternary vocabularies at q=4–6. Ternary JT ranges from 4.41× to 16.26× the binary time as q rises, reaching 4.48 s at q=6 on an N=120 grid. The full 100-resident Bigville cast also passes a 3-state exact-MAP census, but its frames have width 2 and are disconnected by model construction. These results improve the conditional engine cost envelope; the natural cheap-cut hypothesis remains untested on a predefined connected task-derived sample. See [high-width results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_4_RESULTS_20261002.md), [fixed-width node scaling](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_NODE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [domain cost](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_DOMAIN_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), and [Bigville population census](BIGVILLE_POPULATION_EXACT_INFERENCE_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

**Higher-width ternary extension (2026-10-02):** the supported 3-state exact solver passes a current-native, source/fixture-matched q=6/7/8 panel. At q=8, median certified JT is 44.06 s on the grid and 2.59 s on the sparse q-tree; matched binary ratios are 35.55× and 24.02×. Min-fill overestimates q=8 grid width as 10–12 despite certified width 8. This narrows the tested operating envelope and does not establish natural graph prevalence ([results](CAN_TERNARY_HIGH_WIDTH_INFERENCE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](CAN_TERNARY_HIGH_WIDTH_INFERENCE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_ternary_high_width_inference_v1_20261002_analysis.json)).

## 2026-10-02 Qwen3 cross-model transport

A preregistered comparison independently fits mode routers and four adjacent-layer transports in Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3-1.7B on a shared 60/10 text split. Three workers per model pass exact graph inference across 960 total fan-in cases. Both model scales show the same explicit fan-in width/cost profile: widths 1/2/4–6/8 and median-of-hop JT times near 0.15/0.37/9/538 ms. This is cross-scale repeatability for an authored sparsification intervention; held-out sign accuracy is roughly 0.78–0.83 and does not improve monotonically with pruning. Natural cut discovery, task utility, and cross-model basis transfer remain open. See [results](LLM_CROSS_MODEL_TRANSPORT_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](LLM_CROSS_MODEL_TRANSPORT_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [source-lock amendment](LLM_CROSS_MODEL_TRANSPORT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/llm_cross_model_transport_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_cross_model_transport_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-03 Conversation task-scope closure parity pilot

A source-locked three-worker pilot compares monolithic closure against complete repeated sweeps of two existing Microtheory scopes on one concurrent two-goal conversation case. All 24 checkpoint graphs, final graphs, and goal statuses match; all 48 per-scope quiescence reports show zero progress and no deferred effects. The runtime continues to report frame notifications incomplete, so this relies on manually sweeping every scope until a global round is quiescent. The scoped path performs 132 calls and has a descriptive median checkpoint time of 0.969 ms versus 0.045 ms monolithic; each scoped call still matches over the full graph and the scoped arm includes additional quiescence reports. This is a parity positive control, not a factorization, optimized speed, or natural-task prevalence result. The task ownership edges require unchecked insertion, so ordinary vocabulary admission and learned frame discovery remain gaps. Expand to common-ground stress, 4/8 goals, dynamic owner changes, deletion/reuse, and cross-frame negative controls before making a general scoped-closure claim. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [v1.1 amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_1_lock_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Conversation scoped-closure parity expansion

The v1.2 source-locked pilot adds one task Microtheory per frame and compares monolithic closure with exhaustive full-frame resweeps at 2/4/8 concurrent goals plus a four-frame common-ground stress case. Three workers pass exact graph parity at all 198 checkpoints and final state. All 1,005 per-scope quiescence records show zero progress and no deferred work, while the API reports frame notifications incomplete. Scoped calls rise from 126 total on two goals to 1,944 on eight goals; median scoped checkpoint time rises from 0.954 ms to 1.425 ms, with full-graph matching on each scope call. This broadens parity coverage but does not demonstrate a factorized computation or speedup. The capture has a disclosed claim-string typo (“three-frame”); the actual case matrix uses 2, 4, and 8 goal frames and a 4-frame stress case. Its source lock also omits `term.rs` as a direct Rust-source entry, so the next confirmatory run should close that manifest gap and independently validate workload matrix labels. See [v1.2 results and limits](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_2_RESULTS_20261003.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_2_lock_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_2_analysis_20261003.json).

### V1.3 source-complete parity confirmation

The v1.3 repeat adds direct `term.rs` source locking and independent matrix/frame-count validation. It reproduces exact closure parity across the same 12/4-case panel and 198 checkpoints, with 1,005 quiescent per-scope reports. This strengthens reproducibility of the state-parity finding; full-graph matching, exhaustive resweeps, unchecked ownership annotations, and the measured scoped-call cost remain. See [v1.3 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md), [source-lock amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_3_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_3_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_3_analysis_20261003.json).

### 2026-10-03 Scoped closure matcher-work attribution

V1.4 preserves exact state across monolithic/scoped and scoped trace-on/off arms for the 2/4/8-goal plus common-ground panel. Counters show the scoped schedule performs 9.34× candidate visits and 11.18× Term evaluations because it repeats full-graph matching per frame. The partial scoped trace records 51,246 events, mostly involving shared/unowned endpoints. Thus current scoped closure has a semantic parity result but no demonstrated computational factorization; this implementation adds work on the selected panel. Corrected independent analysis v1.4.1 verifies the actual 198 checkpoint total after the original analysis had a shadowed count. Next evaluate localized candidate enumeration/indexing with exact parity and omitted-cross-frame controls. See [v1.4 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_4_RESULTS_20261003.md), [analyzer correction](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_4_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_4_20261003.json), and [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_4_1_analysis_20261003.json).

### Scoped matcher path attribution

The current conversation rules have four-edge match patterns, so every scoped matcher call falls back to global matching. V1.4 counters show 11,640 full-fallback invocations and zero calls to either one-edge optimized path. The 9.34× candidate and 11.18× Term-work increases are therefore consistent with the current implementation's post-match scope filter. A Goal-seeded multi-edge matcher is a testable next hypothesis, contingent on preserving global effect order and shared dependencies. See [v1.4 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_4_RESULTS_20261003.md) and [source](../../../runners/dsl/src/rewrite.rs).

### 2026-10-03 Scoped frame-order robustness

Three complete scope visitation schedules (ascending, descending, outside-in), each replicated by three fresh workers, preserve monolithic state at 594/594 checkpoints and 36/36 final states. This is useful protection against one scheduler order creating the earlier parity result. It does not establish order-equivalent binding/effect traces for all rules. Candidate and Term work remain about 9× and 11× monolithic because four-edge discourse rules still use full fallback. See [v1.5 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_ORDER_PARITY_V1_5_RESULTS_20261003.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_5_analysis_20261003.json), and [multi-edge index audit](SUBSTRATE_SCOPED_MULTI_EDGE_CANDIDATE_INDEX_AUDIT_20261003.md).

**Exploratory multi-edge ownership seeding v1.6 (2026-10-03):** an all-pattern-variable ownership-seeded matcher with EdgeIdx-sequence order restoration is exercised 8,520 times under a no-prior-deletion guard; fallback remains 26,400 calls. It preserves all 594 checkpoint states and 36 final states, including all three frame orders. It does not improve work: candidate visits increase from 164,985 to 170,853 (9.13× to 9.45× monolithic), and Term evaluations from 575,808 to 654,438 (11.17× to 12.69×). Repeated seed enumeration and subsequent fallback explain why path uptake alone is not a performance result. Traced matcher multiplicity also rises because each seed traversal visits patterns again. Treat this implementation as an exploratory result, not an adopted optimization. Next attribute overhead by rule and closure epoch, then evaluate a semantically justified single-anchor path or rollback this approach if it cannot beat the full-fallback control ([v1.6 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_LOCAL_PATTERN_V1_6_RESULTS_20261003.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_6_analysis_20261003.json), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_6.py)).

**Per-rule cost attribution v1.7 (2026-10-03):** independent analysis of the same panel identifies all four discourse lifecycle rules as the measured cost center; their candidate counts sum to 170,853 and Term counts to 654,438, exactly matching scoped totals. `discourse_goal_resolve` is highest at 46,467 candidates/171,306 Terms. This does not yet split local-pattern from fallback work or report epoch-specific cost, so it identifies where to instrument next rather than a fix. See [v1.7 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_COST_ATTRIBUTION_V1_7_RESULTS_20261003.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_7_analysis_20261003.json).

**Scope-path attribution v1.8 (2026-10-03):** per-rule path counters reconcile 8,520 local-pattern attempts and 26,400 full fallbacks (24.4%/75.6%). The fallback split is nearly even across all four lifecycle rules. A graph-global deletion-generation guard causes fallback after any edge deletion, explaining why the local path's uptake remains limited. Exact state parity is retained (594 checkpoints/36 finals), but aggregate work is still worse than v1.5. Next implement/evaluate a deletion-safe order key or sound declared-anchor path; require lower candidate and Term counts than full fallback on the same source. See [v1.8 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PATH_ATTRIBUTION_V1_8_RESULTS_20261003.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_8_analysis_20261003.json).

**Deletion-safe order reconstruction v1.9 (2026-10-03):** a live candidate-slice rank key removes the global deletion guard and preserves 594/594 checkpoint and 36/36 final-state parity; all 34,920 scoped match calls use the local pattern path. Work worsens to 12.52× candidate and 22.62× Term evaluations (from v1.8's 9.45×/12.69×), due to repeated all-variable seeded traversal and rank reconstruction overhead. The initial analyzer's nonzero-fallback expectation was stale; amended analysis v1.9.1 validates the capture with zero fallback. This closes an ordering restriction but rejects this algorithm as an optimization. Next test a single declared scope anchor or a reordered single-pass join, preserving fallback for rules without a sound scope contract. See [v1.9 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DELETION_SAFE_ORDER_V1_9_RESULTS_20261003.md), [amended analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_9_1_amended_analysis_20261003.json), and the [v2.0 preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DECLARED_ANCHOR_V2_PREREG_20261003.md).

**Declared scope-anchor matching v2.0.1 (2026-10-03):** the source-locked 54-case run passes checkpoint/final parity, goal-state equality, trace-on/off parity, declared-path coverage, and cross-frame/orphan-owner guard controls. Across 738 checkpoints, anchor and full-fallback arms tie at 81,228 Candidate pairs and 313,842 Terms; each costs 4.02× and 5.34× monolithic work. Thus the path is semantically validated for this authored Goal/Event contract, but it is not a performance improvement. The first v2.0 capture is retained as a failed implementation attempt because the guard rejected aligned frames. Next instrument clause-level joins and avoid repeated candidate expansion; keep both work reductions as adoption gates. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DECLARED_ANCHOR_V2_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol/amendments](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DECLARED_ANCHOR_V2_PREREG_20261003.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_anchor_pilot_v2_0_1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_anchor_pilot_v2_0_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_anchor_pilot_v2_0_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Semantic-edge expansion attribution v2.0.2 (2026-10-03):** new native counters attribute expansion calls and Candidate pairs by stable rule-edge signature, surviving clause reordering. The 54-case run passes all prior parity and ownership gates; attribution exactly reconciles to aggregate Candidate work. Anchor and fallback have the same 44,862 expansions and 81,228 candidates, with identical counts for every semantic edge. This rules out hidden/unattributed candidate work as the reason for the v2.0.1 tie and shows that this rule/workload combination gains nothing from clause reordering. Next test controlled branching, decoy relations, and degree skew while holding solutions fixed; if order still does not change the expansion tree, measure indexed candidate access or shared-work reuse.
