**Correction and selectivity result v2.1.1 (2026-10-03):** source review found the v2.0 fallback arm also used anchor metadata because `boot_core.manifest_for` was not patched; withdraw v2.0 anchor/fallback work interpretations. V2.1.1 explicitly patches both references and gates zero anchor-path counters in fallback. Its 81 paired cases/954 checkpoints pass exact state, trace, and safety gates. Anchor reduces total Candidate work 33.7% and Term work 34.2% against verified full fallback. At 1,024 unowned complete decoys reductions are 32.3% and 33.3%; anchor nevertheless remains 2.61×/2.80× monolithic work across the matrix. This validates scoped pruning on a controlled authored graph distribution only. Next test partial decoys, degree skew, and naturally harvested ownership. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SELECTIVITY_V2_1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SELECTIVITY_V2_1_PREREG_20261003.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_selectivity_pilot_v2_1_1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_selectivity_pilot_v2_1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_selectivity_pilot_v2_1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Degree-skew/partial-selectivity v2.2 (2026-10-03):** 126 paired cases and 1,314 checkpoints pass exact state/lifecycle/trace, fallback-isolation, candidate-path coverage, and counter-reconciliation gates. Against verified full fallback, anchor matching reduces Candidate pairs 33.7% and Term evaluations 33.8%. The reduction falls to 13.6%/18.1% when decoys omit `has_active_goal`; other missing-edge profiles remain near 32–33%. Anchor is still 2.67×/2.77× monolithic work. This supports sensitivity to controlled decoy topology, not a general speedup or natural-task cheap-cut claim. Next measure size/memory scaling at fixed decoy profiles and then test independently labeled ownership on naturally harvested task graphs. See [v2.2 preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DEGREE_SELECTIVITY_V2_2_PREREG_20261003.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DEGREE_SELECTIVITY_V2_2_RESULTS_20261003.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_degree_selectivity_pilot_v2_2_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_degree_selectivity_pilot_v2_2_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_degree_selectivity_pilot_v2_2_analysis_20261003.json).

**Shared-event size scaling v1 (2026-10-03):** 45 fresh workers at five graph sizes pass state/lifecycle parity, verified fallback isolation, anchor-path coverage, and trace-on/off parity. At 16,384 decoys (median 16,647 nodes/49,392 edges), anchor reduces Candidate and Term work by one-third versus fallback but remains 2.73× monolithic work; aggregate wall time is 19.79 s anchor, 23.31 s fallback, and 2.73 s monolithic. An amended independent analyzer corrects the original replicate-label validation while preserving the capture and original lock. Whole-worker peak RSS is confounded by retained checkpoint/trace payload (2.5 GB capture); substrate memory cost is not yet measured. This confirms scaling of an authored matcher workload only, not natural-task tractability. Next use one arm per fresh process with no trace and streamed summaries to isolate memory, then validate on naturally harvested task graphs. See [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SIZE_MEMORY_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SIZE_MEMORY_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_size_memory_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_size_memory_v1_20261003.json.gz), and [amended analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_size_memory_v1_amended_analysis_20261003.json).

**Memory-isolated size scaling v1.1 (2026-10-03):** the 45-worker rerun passes source-lock, assignment, exact-state/lifecycle, and fallback-isolation gates using untraced compact summaries. Across 0–16,384 shared-event decoys, median whole-worker peak RSS rises 32.6→222.1 MiB (about 11.8 KiB per decoy for this workload). At 16,384, anchor retains one-third less matcher work and is 19% faster than fallback, but still 2.73× the monolithic work and 6.61× its wall time. This end-to-end worker figure does not separate native graph storage or individual arm allocation. V1 RSS is withdrawn because parent-held captures contaminated child-worker high-water; compressed original capture and corrected analysis remain available. Next measure each arm in its own process and evaluate independently owned natural task graphs. See [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SIZE_MEMORY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SIZE_MEMORY_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_size_memory_v1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_size_memory_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Per-arm process memory v1.2 (2026-10-03):** 135 unique workers, one arm per process, pass exact matched checkpoint/final graph and lifecycle parity at five decoy sizes. Median RSS for monolithic/fallback/anchor differs by at most 0.62 MiB across sizes; at 16,384 decoys it is 201.6/202.0/201.9 MiB. This points to graph construction/runtime as the dominant measured footprint in this synthetic workload, without isolating native storage. Anchor saves one-third of matcher work and is 17% faster than fallback at maximum size, but stays 2.73× work and 7.11× wall time versus monolithic. The isolated-arm protocol supersedes earlier combined-arm RSS; v1 RSS is withdrawn. Next evaluate naturally harvested task graphs with independently reviewed ownership. See [v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SIZE_MEMORY_V1_2_RESULTS_20261003.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SIZE_MEMORY_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_size_memory_v1_2_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_size_memory_v1_2_analysis_20261003.json).

**Live task-graph ownership census v1 (2026-10-03):** ten source-locked processes capture five live-route graphs and pass exact same-condition replicate digests plus provenance gates. Independent ownership uses only prompt Message identity and explicit Goal/Event links. Only two conditions expose a Goal assignable to the prompt (one has the wrong task Goal kind); three routes have zero Goal owner coverage. One request per process forces one frame, so zero cross-frame edges are not cheap-cut evidence. Frames have 1–5 nodes in graphs around 25k nodes and 16–30 direct unowned neighbors. Current task oracles pass 6/10, with scene description misrouted to self-report. The first analyzer missed an import; amended v1.1 analysis passes and preserves the capture lock. Next resolve runtime Goal/Message identity coverage and capture multi-turn sequences. These repository-authored prompts do not estimate natural-task distributions. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_TASK_GRAPH_HARVEST_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_TASK_GRAPH_HARVEST_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_graph_harvest_v1_20261003.jsonl.gz), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_graph_harvest_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Live multi-turn frame scaling v1 (2026-10-03):** 12 fresh source-locked runs of one repeated self-report route verify distinct, resolved Message-owned task Goals across 1–8 turns. Each frame has six owned nodes. Boundary-mediated frame interactions become complete by eight turns (28 frame-pair edges; exact treewidth 7), so repeated discourse continuity is not a low-width example under the current ownership rule. Direct cross-frame edge counts (seven) alone hide most coupling. The ~1.72 GiB peak includes full-seed boot; later turns usually take 130–160 ms. Exact raw graph digests vary only in generated SystemModel timing attrs; amended semantic replicate gate passes while v1 failure remains visible. This is one repetitive authored route, and neither establishes a general locality distribution. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_MULTITURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_MULTITURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_multiturn_frame_scale_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Distinct-turn live frame scale v1 (2026-10-03):** six source-locked workers compared independent prompts with context-dependent follow-ups. The independent arm had three Message roots but only one Goal explicitly linked to its own prompt; the follow-up arm had three linked Goals, but both follow-up `answer_topic` Goals remained active and failed to provide grounded answers. Both three-frame graphs have complete pairwise interactions (treewidth 2), so this small experiment does not support low-width locality. Raw replicate digests vary only in generated SystemModel timing fields; the v1.1 semantic comparison passes with those fields excluded and raw failures preserved. Next test follow-up binding with contrastive prior-answer and fresh-first-turn controls. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_DISTINCT_TURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_DISTINCT_TURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_distinct_turn_frame_scale_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Live follow-up context binding v1 (2026-10-03):** nine source-locked runs compare a fixed follow-up after a known organisation answer, an unknown-fact answer, and in a fresh turn. The follow-up reply is identical across all conditions and has zero lexical overlap with the preceding answer; explicit cross-turn `follows_event`/`responds_to` edges still appear in both sequential graphs. This measures structural continuity without evidence of answer-content conditioning on these prompts. The raw replicate gate fails for timing attrs in the two-turn arms; the v1.1 semantic gate passes while preserving the failure. Next use counterbalanced prior values and an externally checked follow-up answer. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_FOLLOWUP_CONTEXT_BINDING_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_FOLLOWUP_CONTEXT_BINDING_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_followup_context_binding_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Counterbalanced live value recall v1 (2026-10-03):** nine runs provide a secret code (14 or 29) then ask the same question, plus fresh controls. Every call returns a communication-generation error; correct recall is 0/6 and corrected substantive abstention is 0/3. Graphs contain question Goal identity and links to the earlier Message, but no value recovery or counterfactual sensitivity. The initial fresh-control oracle falsely counted errors as abstention; amended analysis corrects it. Raw digests vary only in serialized order of an unordered believed-concepts set; v1.1 preserves raw failures and passes semantic comparison. This is a specific live-conversation route failure, not a test of the direct episodic API. The graph-native episodic seed requires an explicit observed event bound to context/schema, so the natural-language bridge remains unverified. Next measure direct API retrieval and conversation integration separately. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_COUNTERBALANCED_VALUE_RECALL_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_COUNTERBALANCED_VALUE_RECALL_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_counterbalanced_value_recall_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Structured episodic recall v1 (2026-10-03):** nine isolated, source-locked runs pass exact graph-native binding, known-cue retrieval, and unknown-cue rejection. Explicit `EpisodicEvent`/`MemoryContext`/`MemorySchema` inputs produce one Episode; known cues retrieve the correct event at confidence .85/uncertainty .15 with `recalled/not_observed` provenance, while unknown cue yields explicit insufficient information at uncertainty 1.0. This validates bounded rule-based memory under structured inputs. Paired live conversation recall remains 0/6 with communication errors, indicating the natural-language-to-memory/reply bridge is unestablished. Do not compare minimal-graph timings with full-seed conversation costs. Next inspect live event and request creation before considering a bridge. See [results](SUBSTRATE_STRUCTURED_EPISODIC_RECALL_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_STRUCTURED_EPISODIC_RECALL_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/structured_episodic_recall_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

**Episodic memory integration boundary (2026-10-03):** raw-graph inspection confirms the live value-recall workers never create `EpisodicEvent`, `MemoryContext`, `MemorySchema`, `MemoryRetrievalRequest`, `RecallCandidate`, or `RecallOutcome`; existing `Episode` nodes are legacy message episodes. The structured memory rules independently pass exact bind/retrieve/unknown-cue checks. The measured gap is bridge activation from natural-language utterance to structured memory inputs, followed by response use—not a failure of the isolated retrieval rules. Next define and evaluate that bridge with provenance and ambiguity controls. See [structured recall results](SUBSTRATE_STRUCTURED_EPISODIC_RECALL_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [live recall results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_COUNTERBALANCED_VALUE_RECALL_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), and [live graph capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_counterbalanced_value_recall_v1_20261003.jsonl.gz).

**Cross-capture episodic bridge audit v1 (2026-10-03):** independently rechecks raw conversation and structured-memory capture digests. All nine conversational graphs omit the event/context/schema/retrieval/request-result nodes that all nine structured workers explicitly use. The live parser extracts `14.` in the value-14 statement but produces no object for the counterbalanced value 29; both lack relation support and remain unresolved. `What is my secret code?` parses as `table:know`, with no `MemoryRetrievalRequest`. The bridge gap therefore includes inconsistent assertion extraction and absent query handoff before memory retrieval. See [audit](../../../evaluation/reports/live_structured_memory_boundary_v1_audit_20261003.json) and [script](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/audit_live_structured_memory_boundary_v1.py).

**Semantic ingress source contract (2026-10-03):** `WorldAdapter.ingest_semantic_event()` accepts already-adapted semantic mappings; raw text is rejected, it does not create a turn or reply, and source search found no production caller under `substrate/`. Its optional proposition bridge materializes caller-supplied neutral propositions with provenance, but does not extract them from language or accept them as true. This is a concrete substrate integration seam, not demonstrated self-directed extraction. Next measure raw-text extraction, neutral semantic ingestion, and memory/query integration as independent stages. See [source audit](SUBSTRATE_SEMANTIC_INGRESS_SOURCE_AUDIT_20261003.md).

**Semantic ingress contract v1 (2026-10-03):** a minimal-seed source-locked panel passes all 9 rows: valid explicit propositions materialize with evidence/event provenance and remain proposed; text-bearing proposition payloads are rejected; raw text frames raise `TypeError` without mutation. This measures the substrate's explicit semantic input contract, not language extraction, provider trust, or self-improvement. See [results](SUBSTRATE_SEMANTIC_INGRESS_CONTRACT_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_SEMANTIC_INGRESS_CONTRACT_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/semantic_ingress_contract_v1_raw_20261003.json).

**Text-to-proposition extraction v1 (2026-10-03):** direct raw-text comprehension locates exact values in 15/18 statement calls, but complete speaker/predicate/value/polarity proposition accuracy is 0/18. The counterbalanced value 29 is absent in all repetitions; polarity is absent for negation. Query classification selects the `my secret code` target 3/3, while the separately audited live graph has no memory request. This is parse-stage evidence, not full conversation integration. See [results](SUBSTRATE_TEXT_TO_PROPOSITION_EXTRACTION_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_TEXT_TO_PROPOSITION_EXTRACTION_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/text_to_proposition_extraction_v1_raw_20261003.json).

**Accepted proposition to episodic memory boundary v1 (2026-10-03):** all nine explicitly structured propositions are accepted with native lifecycle rules and preserve their semantic source-event edge, but no graph creates `EpisodicEvent`, context, schema, Episode, or retrieval request even with episodic rules loaded. This isolates the missing bridge from accepted semantic content into episodic memory; a communicated claim must retain its provenance class and cannot silently become a direct observation. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ACCEPTED_PROPOSITION_TO_EPISODE_BOUNDARY_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ACCEPTED_PROPOSITION_TO_EPISODE_BOUNDARY_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/accepted_proposition_to_episode_boundary_v1_raw_20261003.json).

**Proposition-to-polar-answer capability (2026-10-03):** a 21-row source-locked native-rule panel passes: nine accepted, evidenced, exact proposition/question matches produce `yes`/`no` plans with source proposition/evidence support links; proposed, suspended, mismatched-predicate, and no-evidence controls produce none. This is a real graph-native partial retrieval capability, and existing tests cover its named live route. The current rules do not require lifecycle `uptake_applied=accept`, a `Proposition.originated_in` edge, or speaker attribution. They do not establish authenticated uptake or lineage, and do not implement wh-query claim retrieval or episodic memory. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ACCEPTED_PROPOSITION_POLAR_ANSWER_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ACCEPTED_PROPOSITION_POLAR_ANSWER_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [seed rules](../../../seeds/communication_intent_arbitration.json).

**Proposition polar-conflict boundary (2026-10-03):** a source-locked follow-up passes unique affirmed/negated claims and excludes a retracted contradiction, but fails conflict abstention in 6/6 graphs. Two accepted opposite-polarity propositions both receive support; the engine then chooses a `yes` plan whether affirmed or negated claim is inserted first. Conflict is therefore not safely resolved by current rules. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ACCEPTED_PROPOSITION_POLAR_CONFLICT_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ACCEPTED_PROPOSITION_POLAR_CONFLICT_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/accepted_proposition_polar_conflict_v1_raw_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Explicit GenInfer scope

The corrected, source-locked production-path panel passes all 69 cases and
five gates: 279/279 positive observations are exact and 24/24 required-scope
controls acquire no target generator. At K=16, frame scope reduces candidate
checks 16-fold and median closure time is 258 ms versus 1,057–1,068 ms globally
(about 4.1×). This is a synthetic 2-D integer-linear workload result on one
host; scope discovery, assignment errors, scheduling, and end-to-end boundary
cost are still unmeasured. V1.2 remains preserved as a harness failure because
it omitted the production `observed` edge. See [v1.3 results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_EXPLICIT_SCOPE_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_EXPLICIT_SCOPE_V1_3_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_explicit_scope_v1_3_analysis_20261003.json).

### GenInfer scope-token contract qualification (2026-10-03)

A separate 36-worker source-locked probe holds the matching scope token
constant while varying its `FrameScope` record. All 12/12 valid, 12/12 missing,
and 12/12 contradictory-record cases infer and acquire the exact target. Native
GenInfer checks exact `scope_id` membership and fails closed only when the token
itself is absent; it does not validate provenance records. V1.3 externally
verified its synthetic assignments, but that did not establish runtime scope
validation. Boundary assignment and provenance enforcement remain gaps. See
[probe results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPE_PROVENANCE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_EXPLICIT_SCOPE_V1_3_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_scope_provenance_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

### GenInfer cross-frame dependency boundary (2026-10-03)

A 36-case paired production-path comparison measures local, explicitly
included cross-frame, and excluded cross-frame bases at K=2/4/8/16. Global
closure acquires the exact generator in 36/36 cases. Scoped closure acquires
in 24/24 when the needed basis is in scope, but in 0/12 when the valid basis is
present only outside the supplied scope. At K=16, in-scope closure is about
4.5× faster; the excluded-scope path is 13.3× faster because it misses the
explanation. This demonstrates a real locality/coverage trade-off. Frame and
dependency assignment are authored by the evaluator, not discovered by the
substrate. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_CROSS_FRAME_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_CROSS_FRAME_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_cross_frame_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

### GenInfer scope selection capability boundary (2026-10-03)

Source audit finds a consumer but no substrate producer: the native Term reads
the observation's caller-supplied `scope_id` and filters codebook parts by exact
attribute equality. No GenInfer path derives scopes from `frame_id`, connected
components, separators, or dependency edges, and no runtime provenance
validator was found. The v1.3/v1.4 speed gains therefore measure applying
provided partitions, while the costly capability—discovering safe partitions
and keeping cross-frame dependencies—is still absent. See [source audit](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FRAME_SELECTION_SOURCE_AUDIT_20261003.md) and [cross-frame results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_CROSS_FRAME_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md).

### GenInfer scoped population work trace (2026-10-05)

A current-source, three-arm K=2 production-rule probe passes nine fresh-process
captures with exact outputs and zero trace drops. Scoped, explicit-global, and
unscoped-global modes each enumerate all 76 typed population members across
two GenInfer queries. Per-frame scope reduces selected attributes from 320 to
28, parsed parts from 76 to 4, and candidate work from 19,200 to 9,600 versus
unscoped global mode. This exposes the remaining whole-population lookup cost
while confirming downstream work reduction on this authored fixture. The
scope IDs are supplied by the harness; this is not automatic frame discovery
or a natural-task estimate. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPED_POPULATION_TRACE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md),
[protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPED_POPULATION_TRACE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_geninfer_scoped_population_trace_v1.py),
[lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_scoped_population_trace_v1_lock_20261005.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_scoped_population_trace_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

### GenInfer optional scope index (2026-10-05)

The current native loader uses the existing graph attribute index when the
caller declares `(ShapeSignature, scope_id)`. In twelve fresh K=2 captures,
indexed per-frame scope avoids all typed-population node visits and returns the
same four scoped parts/9,600 candidate checks as scan-based scope. Its median
index build is 6.36 µs on this small graph, but closure time is 38.03 ms vs.
28.49 ms for scan-based scope, so this fixture establishes avoided node
enumeration, not a runtime speedup. Index use is opt-in and harness supplied;
index maintenance/clone/restore cost at scale and safe scope discovery remain
open. V2's wrong expected call count and v2.1's sparse-counter analyzer defect
are retained and disclosed with the corrected independent analysis. See
[results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPE_INDEX_TRACE_V2_1_RESULTS_20261005.md),
[amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPE_INDEX_TRACE_V2_1_AMENDMENT_20261005.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_geninfer_scope_index_trace_v2_1.py),
[lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_scope_index_trace_v2_1_lock_20261005.json),
and [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_scope_index_trace_v2_1_corrected_analysis_20261005.json).

### Articulation-based frame-discovery baseline (2026-10-03)

A label-blind articulation-removal proposal, scored against held-out runtime
owner annotations on 78 captured graphs, performs differently by family. It
recovers pure components in 63 Bigville snapshots (median pairwise F1 0.674,
with 0.508 recall and 0.396 same-owner-edge cut rate), but over-joins all
annotated frame owners in 12 conversation and 3 discourse graphs (median pairwise
F1 0.406 and 0.549; zero cross-owner edges touch the cut). This rules out
articulation points alone as a general frame-discovery method on this selected
panel. It is a topology-only Python baseline, not substrate-native learning;
labels and graph projections have limits. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ARTICULATION_FRAME_DISCOVERY_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_ARTICULATION_FRAME_DISCOVERY_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_articulation_frame_discovery_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

### Typed frame interactions (2026-10-03)

The 78-snapshot, source-locked typed census independently validates all graph
edge occurrences and frame-membership classifications. Disjoint-frame
conversation/discourse edges are exclusively `follows_event` (90 and 6); the
Bigville capture has 7,758 `causal_influence` cross-owner edges. Shared-node
boundary incidence is substantial in conversation (966 edges) and discourse
(177). Between 35% and 70% of edges have an unassigned endpoint, depending on
family, limiting the observed boundary. These relation types give concrete
rule-level coupling targets, but graph edges alone are not complete factor
scopes. See [results](SUBSTRATE_TYPED_FRAME_INTERACTION_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_TYPED_FRAME_INTERACTION_CENSUS_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_typed_frame_interaction_census_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

### Rule-level event predecessor dependency crosswalk (2026-10-03)

The four discourse lifecycle rules in the captured access-trace panel did not
read any of the 90 cross-frame `follows_event` links. That capture loaded only
the `conversation_discourse_state` seed. A separate source scan finds three
other multi-edge rules in communication seeds that do read `follows_event`,
including predecessor-based action-target carry, correction grounding, and
reported-content recall. They were not dynamically measured in the lifecycle
panel, so its no-cross-owner access result is limited to those four rules. See
[crosswalk results](SUBSTRATE_RULE_EDGE_SCOPE_CROSSWALK_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_rule_edge_scope_crosswalk_v1_analysis_20261003.json), and [seed rules](../../../seeds/communication_intent_arbitration.json).

### Predecessor-interface carry behavior (2026-10-03)

In a locked 60-case native-rule panel, action-target carry was exact for the
retained predecessor interface (10/10), rejected wrong-speaker, topic-bearing
interruption, missing-edge, and omitted-target-interface controls (0/10 each),
and propagated through a two-event topicless chain (10/10). This establishes
the specific rule's local interface dependence and its transitive behavior
through eligible events. The ~82–102 μs medians apply only to tiny synthetic
graphs. The first experiment explicitly loaded the seed manifest because it
was absent from `domains/seed_registry.py`; that gap is now fixed and verified
through `load_seeds_into`.
Follow-on scoped closure matches monolithic carry for 10/10 retained-interface
pairs and rejects 10/10 wrong-speaker, topic-interruption, and omitted-target
controls. The predecessor is outside frame ownership but visible in the shared
graph; this is not physical isolation. Tiny graph median closure times are
109 μs monolithic and 50 μs scoped, insufficient for a scaling claim. Neither
experiment measures Cow branches, residual merge-back, or broad partition
parity. See [scoped runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_scoped_closure_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_scoped_closure_v1_lock_20261003.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_scoped_closure_v1_20261003.json), in addition to the [initial rule runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_interface_v1.py) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_interface_v1_20261003.json).

### Definite-correction grounding across a scoped predecessor boundary (2026-10-03)

A second multi-edge rule grounds an unresolved definite correction from a
matching action-target head on the immediate predecessor. With the current
event and interpretation owned by the active Microtheory and the predecessor
outside ownership, monolithic and scoped closure grounded 10/10 valid cases.
Wrong-speaker, wrong-head, missing-edge, topic-bearing interruption, and
omitted-target controls were all rejected in 10/10 each. The predecessor
remained globally readable, so this tests scoped firing and mutation ownership,
not partition isolation. Median tiny-fixture closure was 171 μs monolithic and
71 μs scoped, with no scaling implication. The first interruption assertion
was corrected after it was found to inspect the original target event rather
than the immediate predecessor; the failed capture was discarded. See
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_grounding_scoped_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_grounding_scoped_v1_lock_20261003.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_grounding_scoped_v1_20261003.json).

### Multi-event correction trace across frame ownership (2026-10-03)

The follow-up uses the production discourse event materializer to create
1/2/4/8 instruction/correction pairs and assigns each event plus its
interpretation to separate frames. Three sweep orders across nine fresh
processes produce 36 monolithic/scoped pairs. Canonical full-graph node/edge
facts and exact grounding evidence match in all 36. The corrected v1.1
ownership audit measures two semantic nodes plus the scope root per frame. At
eight pairs/16 frames, scoped closure takes 25.3 ms versus 2.48 ms monolithic
(10.2×), with 14.5× Candidate enumeration, 13.4× Term evaluation, and 40.1×
rule enumeration.
This confirms semantic parity for the selected trace while showing that the
current repeated full-graph scoped matcher does not gain computational
factorization. Serialization order diverges in some sweep orders despite
canonical state equality. The ownership annotations are authored; they are
not discovered by the substrate. Prior thoughtexperiment CoW studies already
measure separate branch/residual workflows, but this trace does not test CoW
integration. Treat the first v1 capture as a diagnostic. See the current [v1.1 runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1.py), [independent analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1.py), [work analysis](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_work_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

### Declaring an owner anchor on a chained correction trace (2026-10-03)

A rule-level anchor on the current correction interpretation safely preserves
canonical graph state, but its proof guard has low selectivity on a chained
transcript. At eight instruction/correction pairs, it takes the declared
anchor path for 3 target-rule evaluations and fails closed to generic matching
for 53; aggregate Candidate/Term work drops only 0.5%/0.7% relative to
fallback. Both paths remain roughly 14× monolithic work. The current event can
also occupy the predecessor role for a later frame, which explains why the
co-ownership proof must reject many scopes. This points to multi-role ownership
contracts or separate discourse partitions as the next engine design question.
See [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1.py), [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

### Ownership audit amendment for the materialized correction trace (2026-10-03)

The v1 trace runner misreported `owned_nodes_per_frame` as four. V1.1 records
actual Microtheory membership queried from the native graph: each frame has two
semantic nodes (one DiscourseEvent and one CommunicationInterpretation), and
three nodes in the engine's owned set when the Microtheory root is included.
The corrected capture preserves 36/36 canonical graph parity, exact grounding,
and prior work counts; corrected N=8 timing is 25.3 ms scoped versus 2.48 ms
monolithic. Treat the v1 capture as diagnostic and v1.1 as authoritative for
ownership validation. See [v1.1 runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).
## 2026-10-03 thoughtexperiment candidate generation and scale updates

The source-locked node-identity proposal gate generates eight edge/attribute
guards from the shared edit-op algebra. A native graph-Term selector picks the
actor+verb guard in all four workers; it passes all held-outs and seven
alternatives fail. The v1 duplicated-recipient fixture is retained as a
diagnostic only. V1.1 still uses harness-supplied task/fault/examples and
fresh task contexts, so CoW branch execution and residual adoption are the next
integration step. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_NODE_IDENTITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_NODE_IDENTITY_V1_1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_editops_node_identity_v1.py),
[lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_editops_node_identity_v1_1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_editops_node_identity_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

An unpreregistered predecessor-aware fanout checkpoint reaches 100,511 nodes.
Shared graph pages reduce median candidate-fork time and retained allocator
growth substantially, while total lifecycle time remains close to full copy
because whole-graph auditing dominates. Treat it as exploratory, not a scaling
law. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_FANOUT_100K_EXPLORATORY_20261003.md)
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_100k_exploratory_analysis_20261003.json).
The v1.1 integration runs the generated pool in isolated full-copy and
shared-page branches, selects the actor+verb repair from training evidence,
and returns a selected-proposal receipt through the revision-checked graph
delta API. The primary rule remains unchanged; this is evidence handoff, not
production rule adoption. V1 is diagnostic because its analyzer field name was
wrong; v1.1 is the locked rerun. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_NODE_IDENTITY_COW_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_NODE_IDENTITY_COW_V1_1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_editops_node_identity_cow_v1_1.py),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_editops_node_identity_cow_v1_1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_editops_node_identity_cow_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

### Adaptive factored search and singleton audit (2026-10-04)

An amended 20-panel run reduces median search time to 3.0–5.8 seconds per
task family (from 11.1–11.7 seconds for the fixed four-branch route), while
accepting 19 panels and requesting help on one. Independent fractional
holdout scoring finds seven false acceptances among the 19 accepted singleton
models, even though each matched two integer-valued audit labels. The policy
therefore demonstrates a work reduction with an inadequate acceptance gate;
it does not demonstrate safe adoption. The corrected source lock enforces the
six-active-label stop. Next compare integer audits with balanced
task-conditioned continuous audits and help/abstention. See [results](ADAPTIVE_FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](ADAPTIVE_FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/adaptive_factored_operator_search_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_adaptive_factored_operator_search_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/adaptive_factored_operator_search_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/adaptive_factored_operator_search_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/adaptive_factored_operator_search_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Factored operator search (2026-10-04)

On the same 20 panels, four separate one-operator grammar searches recover
and resolve an exact candidate in all 20 cases (5/5 for each of four task
families). The paired 13-operator search found exact candidates in 5/20, all
from absolute-value tasks. Median factored search cost is 11.1–11.7 seconds
per panel versus the shared search's 12-second cap; 50/80 branch searches
themselves time out but return useful partial candidates. This supports
partitioning the declared search space for these numeric tasks, not a general
scaling theorem or open-ended invention. See [results](FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/factored_operator_search_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_factored_operator_search_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/factored_operator_search_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/factored_operator_search_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/factored_operator_search_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Shared multi-operator search (2026-10-04)

With a shared 13-operator grammar, bounded synthesis reached the 12-second
cap in every one of 20 panels. Exact candidates appeared in all five
absolute-value panels and active selection resolved those five. No exact
candidate appeared before the cap for additive, product, or minimum equality;
the finite selector ended at false singletons in 13/15 of those panels and at
ambiguity in the other two. This is evidence of useful `Abs` search plus a
search-cost and false-singleton failure under the registered cap, not proof
that the larger grammar cannot express those targets. V1 does not resynthesize
after new labels. See [results](MULTI_OPERATOR_FAMILY_SEARCH_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](MULTI_OPERATOR_FAMILY_SEARCH_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/multi_operator_family_search_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_multi_operator_family_search_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/multi_operator_family_search_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/multi_operator_family_search_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/multi_operator_family_search_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Blinded operator-family selection (2026-10-04)

On five additive and five product panels, bounded synthesis runs both
`Plus` and `Times` expansions from the same examples. With task identity
withheld from candidate search, the joint candidate pool plus native
discriminating queries leaves a task-appropriate exact singleton in all ten
panels after 1–5 labels. Single-expansion controls yield false singletons and
empty sets on the mismatched task. Joint synthesis costs about 3.8–3.9 seconds
per panel versus 1.7–2.1 seconds for one expansion. This demonstrates choice
among two declared primitives only; it is not open-ended primitive invention.
See [results](OPERATOR_HINT_BLINDING_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](OPERATOR_HINT_BLINDING_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/operator_hint_blinding_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_operator_hint_blinding_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/operator_hint_blinding_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/operator_hint_blinding_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/operator_hint_blinding_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Term-derived probe generation (2026-10-04)

In 120 locked cells spanning two relation families, three bounds, and four
comparison encodings, an authored syntax walker extracted the effective bound
from each candidate Term without a separate threshold field. Native selection
and one oracle label rejected the bounded false candidate while retaining two
exact Terms in every cell. The fixed seeded wider set exercised 17,726 bounded
candidate errors while both exact members remained correct. The study is a
small affine-fragment result, not generic Term interpretation or candidate
invention. See [results](TERM_DERIVED_PROBE_GENERATION_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](TERM_DERIVED_PROBE_GENERATION_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/term_derived_probe_generation_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_term_derived_probe_generation_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/term_derived_probe_generation_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/term_derived_probe_generation_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/term_derived_probe_generation_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Boundary-probe policy comparison (2026-10-04)

Across 30 additive/product panels at three hidden thresholds, the current
coverage policy returns a restricted false candidate in all 30. Candidate-
directed probing rejects it with one oracle label per panel, retaining both
exact Terms; the fixed 17-point grid reaches the same result with 5.7× as many
candidate/probe evaluations. Safe abstention prevents false acceptance but
does not solve. Independent finite wide-domain rescoring confirms these
outcomes. This remains a harness-authored arithmetic study and does not prove
generic boundary extraction or autonomous induction. See [results](BOUNDARY_PROBE_POLICY_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [preregistration](BOUNDARY_PROBE_POLICY_COMPARISON_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/boundary_probe_policy_comparison_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_boundary_probe_policy_comparison_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/boundary_probe_policy_comparison_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/boundary_probe_policy_comparison_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/boundary_probe_policy_comparison_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Continuous singleton audit (2026-10-04)

On the same 20 fixed proposals, a post-search audit presents up to four
balanced, task-conditioned continuous examples. It accepts the 12 proposals
that are exact on the sealed fractional holdout, rejects all seven false
integer-audit acceptances, and requests help on those seven plus the one panel
with no candidate. It uses 62 labels (median four when a candidate exists),
compared with 38 labels for the integer audit; immediate help avoids false
acceptance but yields no accepted proposals. This is evidence that audit
distribution matters, not autonomous adequacy detection: the example generator
knows task structure, four examples cannot certify equivalence, and mismatches
are not fed back into resynthesis. Next compare generic domain sampling and
Term-directed proposals without task-informed generation, then integrate
residuals into resynthesis and charge its full cost. See [results](CONTINUOUS_SINGLETON_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](CONTINUOUS_SINGLETON_AUDIT_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/continuous_singleton_audit_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_continuous_singleton_audit_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/continuous_singleton_audit_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/continuous_singleton_audit_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/continuous_singleton_audit_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Generic continuous singleton audit (2026-10-04)

On the same 20 fixed proposals, task-agnostic uniform sampling over the
four-dimensional continuous input box fails to detect any of the seven false
candidates, even after 64 labels each. At 4/16/64-label caps it accepts 12
holdout-correct and seven false candidates, with one help request; the cap-64
run costs 1,216 labels. The paired task-conditioned audit catches all seven
false candidates using 62 labels total, but depends on task-informed examples.
This is a negative result for the registered random sampler and domain, not a
claim about all random policies. Per-query wall time was not captured. Next
measure syntax-directed proposals and connect residuals to resynthesis. See
[results](GENERIC_CONTINUOUS_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](GENERIC_CONTINUOUS_AUDIT_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/generic_continuous_audit_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_generic_continuous_audit_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/generic_continuous_audit_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/generic_continuous_audit_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/generic_continuous_audit_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Live runtime store state coverage (2026-10-04)

The source-matched v1.2 capture records the first graph seen and cumulative
rule slots for 14 matcher stores across a fixed two-request route. An
independent analyzer validates all payloads and reconciles 4,745/4,745
store-qualified identities, with zero unmatched rule slots. Route outputs and
behavioral counters match the v1.6 parent; timing counters are retained but
excluded from parity. This closes state-body coverage for that sample, while
independent per-store replay, simultaneous secondary-store boundary images,
natural workload frequency, and broad scaling remain unmeasured. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_STORE_STATES_V1_2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_STORE_STATES_V1_2_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_runtime_store_states_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_live_runtime_store_states_v1_2.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_store_states_v1_2_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_store_states_v1_2_analysis_20261004.json).

### Primary closure-entry replay (2026-10-04)

The v1.1 capture records ten and five primary `run_rules` entry states across
the two requests; all 15 restore with exact graphs and ordered rule bodies.
Independent replay reaches only 50.5% exact per-rule metric-cell agreement.
Candidate and Term work totals are 10–21% higher than live. Live calls select
1,272 and 927 rules where fresh replay selects all 2,357, exposing persistent
dirty-rule scheduler state as a missing checkpoint component. The replay
binding also clears mutation-protection metadata. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_ENTRY_REPLAY_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_closure_entry_states_v1_1_20261004.json), and [replay analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_closure_entry_replay_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Live dirty-rule entry state (2026-10-04)

The source-locked audit observes dirty-rule counts/names before all 15 primary
closure calls. Counts match first-pass selected rules exactly in all five
single-pass calls. In the ten two-pass calls, later-pass selections account
for 5,661 and 1,993 extra rows by request. This confirms dirty bits explain
the first-pass fresh-replay mismatch; dirty-node sets, incremental cursors,
mutation protection, and exact per-rule replay remain open. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_DIRTY_RULE_ENTRY_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_DIRTY_RULE_ENTRY_AUDIT_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_dirty_rule_entry_audit_v1_20261004.json), and [amended analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_dirty_rule_entry_audit_v1_1_analysis_20261004.json).

### Live scheduler-state replay (2026-10-04)

An opt-in native checkpoint now captures and restores each rule's dirty bit
and dirty-node set, edge-type cursors, deletion versions, and the global
`any_mutation` flag. On the same 15 primary closure calls, exact rule and
scheduler restoration reproduces all 13,376 per-rule metric cells and the
compared aggregate work counts. Graph serialization is exact in 13/15 states;
the remaining two have one timestamp differing by one binary64 ULP, which is
reported and bounded rather than described as bit-exact. This closes the
previous replay gap on one route; cache/configuration state, other workloads,
natural prevalence, and scaling remain open. See [v4 results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_ENTRY_REPLAY_V4_RESULTS_20261004.md), [checkpoint implementation](../../../runners/dsl/src/py.rs), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_closure_entry_states_v2_20261004.json), and [replay analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_closure_entry_replay_v4_analysis_20261004.json).

The next source-locked transfer gate passes on two separately authored rule
families: productive CAN coactivation and retinal percept synthesis. Four
fresh/post-closure states reproduce graph, rules, scheduler, global counters,
per-rule work, and final state exactly. A retinal external activation change
exercises predicate retraction with one dirty node and five cursors; replay
matches that path exactly. These are small synthetic fixtures; natural-task
frequency and scaling remain open. The checkpoint still omits evaluation
cache entries and incremental per-edge deletion-version maps. See [transfer
v3 results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_CHECKPOINT_TRANSFER_V3_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/scheduler_checkpoint_transfer_v3_20261004.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/scheduler_checkpoint_transfer_v3_analysis_20261004.json).

### COW value-shard write amplification (2026-10-06)

A corrected source-locked component profile shows first branch writes clone the
selected value shard: at 50,771 entries, observed structural copy estimate
falls from 793 at 64 shards to 97 at 512. Fork-plus-delete median falls from
48.9µs to 5.3µs in this map-only workload. This motivates a graph-level shard
count comparison; it does not establish end-to-end speed or memory benefit and
does not change the 64-shard default. See [results](COW_VALUE_SHARD_WRITE_AMPLIFICATION_V6_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](COW_VALUE_SHARD_WRITE_AMPLIFICATION_V6_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/cow_value_shard_write_profile_v6.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/cow_value_shard_write_profile_v6_lock_20261006.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_value_shard_write_profile_v6_analysis_20261006.json).

### TypedGraph COW shard-count replication (2026-10-06)

Three fresh-process replicates passed all graph mutation, exact query/order,
parent/sibling, and NodeID reuse/compaction controls at each of 64/128/256/512
shards. On 50,771 nodes, 512 shards reduced branch write medians to 18–29% of
64-shard times, with similar exact query sweep and fixture RSS. The small
graph insert-plus-validation timing is non-monotonic: 128/256 shards were
7.6×/12.0× slower than 64, while 512 stayed near baseline. The timed region
includes the exact post-insert lookup, so its cause is not yet localized. The
production default remains 64 pending phase timing and real closure/full-
Substrate replication. See [results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_SHARD_COUNT_V7_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_SHARD_COUNT_V7_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_shard_count_v7.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_shard_count_v7_lock_20261006.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_shard_count_v7_analysis_20261006.json).

### TypedGraph insert-phase isolation (2026-10-06)

A phase-separated source-locked capture (three fresh processes, 41 repeats)
finds the exact post-insert lookup takes 0.09–0.14µs. Isolated indexed insert
at 7,164 nodes stays 8.6–11.1µs across shard counts, so the v7 small-graph
insert-plus-lookup anomaly does not reproduce in this isolated workload. At
50,771 nodes, indexed insert falls from 41.8µs at 64 shards to 13.1µs at 512;
no-index insert remains 3.2–3.5µs. The v7 anomaly may depend on the preceding
mutation/eager-clone sequence; next replay that order with phase timers. See
[results](TYPED_GRAPH_INSERT_PHASE_PROFILE_V8_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_INSERT_PHASE_PROFILE_V8_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_insert_phase_profile_v8.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_insert_phase_profile_v8_lock_20261006.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_insert_phase_profile_v8_analysis_20261006.json).

### Exact-schedule insert phase replay (2026-10-06)

A three-process v9 replay preserves the v7 set-attribute/insert/delete/retype
and alternating shared/eager order while splitting insertion from validation.
It does not reproduce v7's small-graph 128/256 timing spike: combined insert
and query is 10.97–11.57µs, of which lookup is about 0.12µs. Treat the earlier
spike as non-replicating; it is not a stable shard-count penalty. At 50,771
nodes the large-shard write trend reproduces. Keep the default at 64 pending
counterbalanced rule-closure and representative Substrate runs. See [v9 results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_INSERT_SCHEDULE_PROFILE_V9_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_INSERT_SCHEDULE_PROFILE_V9_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_insert_schedule_profile_v9.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_insert_schedule_profile_v9_lock_20261006.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_insert_schedule_profile_v9_analysis_20261006.json).

### Full-seed GenInfer closure by shard count (2026-10-06)

The paired 64/512-shard gate passes exact acquisition, training, held-out,
corruption, closure-work, and transformation-receipt parity over 15 cases and
30 source-stable fresh workers. At relation counts 4/16/64, training medians
are 52.469/211.641/873.071 ms for 64 shards and 52.531/216.731/865.018 ms
for 512. These differences (−0.9% to +2.4%) are small and inconsistent; the larger-shard
graph mutation gain does not materially change this GenInfer closure route.
Held-out closure is 0.7–4.8 ms, while predicate-corrupt held-out controls are
about 1.3 s and must be tracked as tail cost. Keep the production default at
64. Next profile training closure phases and repeat shard-count comparison on
a live whole-Substrate route with fork, mutation, and closure. This synthetic
family neither establishes natural-task prevalence nor bounds rung-three
search generally. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_COW_SHARD_CLOSURE_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_COW_SHARD_CLOSURE_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_geninfer_cow_shard_closure_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_geninfer_cow_shard_closure_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_cow_shard_closure_v1_analysis_20261006.json). A source-locked native work profile finds exactly 29,280 matrix attempts at bound 5 for N=4/16/64, with two eligible bases each; training time rises about 16× as relation count rises 16× because candidate transformation/residual work scans the relations. The follow-up N×B factorial passes all 27 semantic and counter gates: attempts rise from 4,800 at B=3 to 101,248 at B=7, and `GenInfer` term units equal 4N per attempt in every cell. Runtime tracks the product of bound-driven attempts and relation scans, reaching 3.0 s at N=64/B=7 with audit enabled. The current implementation skips coordinate counts other than two; it has fourth-power B enumeration in this implemented 2D path, while higher-dimensional enumeration remains unsupported. This is a bounded workload model, not evidence about natural B distributions ([v1 work-profile results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_TRAINING_WORK_PROFILE_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [v2 factorial results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_TRAINING_WORK_PROFILE_V2_RESULTS_20261006.md), [v2 protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_TRAINING_WORK_PROFILE_V2_PREREG_20261006.md), [v2 runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_geninfer_training_work_profile_v2.py), [v2 analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_substrate_geninfer_training_work_profile_v2.py), [v2 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_training_work_profile_v2_analysis_20261006.json)).

### Sorted identity join key semantics (2026-10-06)

The matched-solver six-worker differential passes exact training and held-out
receipt/status parity for integer-to-float equal IDs, float IDs, and boolean-
to-integer equal IDs with unique predicate/key pairs. The join uses its sorted
path only when ordering yields complete unambiguous alignment; otherwise it
falls back to exact baseline key equality. This validates those key cases,
not all `Value` variants or natural-task prevalence. See [results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_KEY_SEMANTICS_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_KEY_SEMANTICS_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1_lock_20261006.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1_20261006.json).

The v1.1 extension adds unique compound, duplicate, and missing-key controls.
All 12 workers pass train/held-out status and receipt parity; positive float
and bool/int cases still acquire and reuse the transform, while the other
controls match baseline refusal. This adds no natural prevalence or timing
evidence. See [v1.1 results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_KEY_SEMANTICS_V1_1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_KEY_SEMANTICS_V1_1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1_1_lock_20261006.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1_1_20261006.json).

### Live-runtime dimension-inference frequency pilot (2026-10-06)

A source-locked re-analysis of three audit-on replicates over two repeated
self-report prompt routes finds `DimInfer` and `ShapeDecode` activity on
`report your organisation` in every replicate; the other route has zero such
events. Generic `GenInfer` is zero in both routes. This is a six-execution
slice of one narrow runtime scenario, not a natural-task prevalence estimate
or timing result. See [results](LIVE_RUNTIME_DIM_INFER_FREQUENCY_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](LIVE_RUNTIME_DIM_INFER_FREQUENCY_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_runtime_dim_infer_frequency_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_runtime_dim_infer_frequency_v1_lock_20261006.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_dim_infer_frequency_v1_20261006.json).
